# 2026-10-22 — I Built the Prop Firm I Wish Existed Years Ago w/ $2M INSTANT FUNDING...

|---|---|

## 1. What this video covers
This is mostly an announcement for his rebranded prop firm (Trade Prime / formerly "Prime Time Funding"). It covers lower prices and new instant, one-step and two-step accounts up to $2M. The meta title on file is "I Rebranded My Prop Firm and Added a $1M, $1.5M, and $2M INSTANT Account To Change Your Life...". There is no chart work. Strategy content is limited to:
- the statement that he switched to position trading after realising he "missed out on $5 million by simply not holding";
- that position trading needs larger accounts;
- percentage-return framing (10%/month);
- a Q4 2026–Q1 2027 plan: get one or two six-figure accounts, don't yolo, be consistent.

It reflects his current (late 2026) position-trading era.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Position trading, referenced only: "So many people always ask me, 'Well, how am I supposed to position trade with a 100K account?' Well, you for damn sure can do it with some seven figure accounts" [4:06].
- No timeframes or holding periods stated.

Not covered in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Don't yolo.** He defines "yolo" as "putting a crazy high lot size and just guessing" [1:05]. His firm still pays yolo traders but warns them, and "leverage may have to be lower if you're going to just guess and yolo your trades" [1:05].
- **Account size over trading harder:** "Sometimes the answer isn't trading better. Sometimes you're already trading good enough. You're just on too low of an account size" [8:07].
- **Return framing:** 10%/month is the benchmark he uses. 10% on 25K = $2,500/month; on 500K = $50,000; on $1M = $100,000; on $2M = $200,000 [7:07]. He mentions traders on his firm making "10% 20% 25% even 50%" on small accounts.
- **The plan (Q4 2026 into Q1 2027):** "find one or two accounts that fit you. Get both of them. Make sure they're six figure accounts. Even a 100K account is better than nothing. Spend the next 3 to four months trading on these accounts… Don't yolo your trades and try to get a payout day one because you may end up breaching your account. Focus on being a consistent trader" [8:07].
- **Student example ($471K, January 2026):** +66% on a 500K one-step account ($6,300 cost) and +137% on a 100K instant-funding account ($6,300 cost). That is about $12.6K of fees for $471K profit. He scales it: the same % on $1M/$2M accounts would be about $2.69M [5:06–6:06]. The same trader is now at $130K on a new 100K account (+30%). Another trader, "Alfredo", is +12% on 100K (a $12K payout). Another is +6% on 5K ($300) [6:06–7:07].
- **Firm offerings (context):** instant funding now at 100K, 250K, 500K, $1M, $1.5M, $2M; one-step and two-step up to $1M, $1.5M, $2M [4:06]. Previously 100K two-step was about $900 and 500K one-step $6,300 [1:05–2:05]. New prices were shown on screen, not stated. Promo test runs "up until the 25th" [9:08].
- No per-trade risk % or drawdown limits stated.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
No trades walked through.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Don't yolo: "Don't yolo your trades and try to get a payout day one because you may end up breaching your account." [8:07]

# 2026-07-28 — In 12 Months, You Will Be Profitable Trader By Doing This...

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UXGnJ76wssE |
| Uploaded | 2026-07-28 |
| Length | 13:34 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2025-26 position trading (daily/weekly trend, multi-day to multi-week holds) |

## 1. What this video covers
A short (13-minute) argument for position trading over day trading and scalping, using the same weekly-vs-15-minute "bar replay" exercise he uses with mentorship students. He shows that the same Fibonacci A-B-C structure is easy to read on the weekly chart and unreadable on the 15-minute chart. He gives the success-rate figures he quotes for each trading style, a real GBPAUD short he called in his mentorship in mid-March 2026 (about 900 pips over about two months), and lot-size and pip profit maths. He states his current trade frequency (5-6 trades a month) and holding period (anywhere from 5 to 14 days up to a couple of months). He ends with the "check your old trades 3 weeks later" P&L exercise. This is his current (2026) position-trading strategy. There is very little on entry or stop mechanics.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Style:** position trading, always with the higher-timeframe (weekly) trend. Day trading on the 15-minute chart is the approach he says he "used to" use and that cost him years.
- **Analysis timeframe:** weekly. "If I just went to the weekly time frame … it's a lot easier to figure that out" [6:07]. The 15-minute (and 5-minute) chart is the counter-example: unpredictable noise.
- **Holding period:** flexible. "I'm going to take five trades a month, six trades a month, and … hold them" [10:15]. "You can alter it for 5 days, 7 days, 14 days, 12 days" [10:15]. He suggests beginners "start small" with holds of about 8-12 days rather than two months. Those who are more patient can hold for months; the GBPAUD example was held for about two months.
- He explains why the 15-minute chart is misleading: four 15-minute candles make an hour, and you need 24 hours of them for a day and a full week of them for a week [2:05]. A big 15-minute drop can just be the "C" leg of a weekly ABC.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **GBPAUD ("GA")**: the main live example (short, mid-March 2026).
- **Gold (XAUUSD)**: "Gold looks too ugly for me right now. That's why I'm not trading it right now" [8:14] (said around July 2026). He told people not to "fight the tide" trading gold when GA was trending cleanly.
- EUR-style 1.15xxx prices are used in a hypothetical P&L example (pair not named).
- **Sessions:** Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **TradingView** with **Bar Replay** (random start point) for the 15-minute vs weekly exercise.
- **Fibonacci A-B-C(-D)** structure read on the weekly: "It's very clearly it's a Fibonacci, right? A, B, C, and then go up to D" [1:04]. No Fib settings are given.
- **Psychological (round-number) levels**: the GA target was "past this psych level and then … down to this psychological level" [8:14] (the exact levels are shown on the chart, not stated; the move was 1.94 → 1.85).
- Calculator for pip/lot P&L.
- No moving averages or other indicators are mentioned in this video.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Start on the **weekly** timeframe and identify the overall trend and structure (e.g. a Fibonacci A-B-C pattern pointing to a D leg) [1:04].
2. Decide which way the market is going on that higher timeframe. Trade only in that direction ("trading with the overall trend") [1:04, 6:07].
3. Ignore 15-minute and 5-minute moves against that direction. They are part of the larger structure, not a new trend [2:05].
4. Use psychological levels as the waypoints/targets (GA: past one psych level, down to the next) [8:14].
5. Filter by how clean the chart looks: skip ugly charts (gold, July 2026) and trade the clean trend (GA) [8:14].
6. Enter, hold for days to weeks, and let the trade run (5-6 trades a month) [10:15].

## 6. Entry rules
- Enter in the direction of the weekly trend only. A counter-trend 15-minute signal is a trap.
- In the GA example he told mentorship students "GA is coming back up. We need to enter. We should enter right now basically" [6:07]. This reads as selling into the pullback up within a weekly downtrend. The exact entry price, order type (market vs limit) and confirmation are not stated.
- Not covered in this video: limit orders, Fib entry levels, confirmation candles.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
Not covered in this video in specific terms. The implied point is that a tight 15-minute-based stop gets hit by noise ("it did exactly what I said it was going to do, but then it still stopped me out. Yeah, because you're not trading with the overall trend" [1:04]). In the bar-replay demo, a 15-minute buyer was down 50 pips and "probably would have" been stopped out [6:07].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Targets are psychological levels on the higher timeframe. For GA: "watch it go all the way down past this psych level and then go all the way down to this psychological level" [8:14] (1.94 → 1.85, about 900 pips).
- No TP ladder is given in this video.

## 9. Trade management
- Hold the trade. "All you had to do was enter … and just held it and all it did was just go straight down" [8:14].
- Holding period is up to you: 5, 7, 8, 9, 11, 12 or 14 days, or months [10:15].
- No break-even, partials or trailing rules in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Trade frequency:** "five trades a month, six trades a month" [10:15].
- **Win rate claim:** a caller said "I know you win, you know, more than 90% of your trades" [1:04]. That is the caller's claim, repeated without correction.
- **Pip values he uses:** 1 lot = $10/pip; 3 lots = $30/pip; 5 lots = $50/pip.
- **Lot sizes:** on his prop firm (Trade Prime / Prime Time Funding), traders on larger accounts use "10 lots, 15 lots, 20 lots" [8:14]. A 5-lot is usable on his prop-firm accounts.
- **Success-rate figures he quotes (per style):** scalping 1-3%, day trading 5-10%, swing trading 15-20%, position trading 25-30% [3:05].
- No risk-% rule or drawdown rule in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: Weekly vs 15-minute Fibonacci ABC (illustration) (1:04–2:05)
- A drawn sketch, not a real trade. On the weekly, price makes A, B, C and then goes up to D: an obvious buy.
- The same pattern on a 15-minute chart, viewed at point C, looks like "the biggest drop ever and a little bit going up", so a 15-minute trader reads it as a downtrend and sells. The point: timeframe context decides the read.

### Trade 2: Bar-replay exercise, unnamed pair, 15-minute (5:07–6:07)
- TradingView Bar Replay to a random point, scaled down to the 15-minute chart. Pair not stated (shown on chart).
- Question 1, buy or sell? Played forward: buyers "would be down 50 pips right now. That probably would have stopped you out."
- Question 2: price drifted up 10-15 pips, then "dropped another 100 pips". The people who said up lost again.
- Contrast: going to the weekly chart and refreshing makes direction "a lot easier" to see.
- He tells viewers to repeat this themselves on 5- or 15-minute charts.

### Trade 3: GBPAUD short (mid-March 2026, roughly 2 months) (6:07–8:14)
- **Context:** "when the market was going down for GA around this time in mid-March, hey guys, boom, GA is coming back up. We need to enter" [6:07]. Called live to his mentorship.
- **Direction:** short (price fell from 1.94 to 1.85).
- **Entry:** around 1.94 [approximate; "194"]. Order type not stated.
- **Stop:** not stated.
- **Target:** down past one psych level to the next psychological level (1.85 area) [8:14].
- **Result:** "The market went from 194 all the way down to 185. That is 900 pips over two months" [7:07]. On a 5-lot ($50/pip): 50 × 900 = **$45,000**. The transcript has a gap between [7:07] and [8:14]. The surviving fragment "…per month. Do you make 22.5K right now from your job?" implies $45,000 / 2 months = $22.5K per month.
- **Management:** simply held.

### Trade 4: Hypothetical "3 weeks later" P&L review (11:15–12:18)
- Example: a 2-lot entry at 1.1500, stopped out at about 1.1480 (garbled: "1, you know, uh, 14 800"). Price 3 weeks later reached 1.1780 ("117800").
- 1.1500 → 1.1780 = 280 pips. On a 3-lot ($30/pip): 30 × 280 = $8,400 (he doesn't say the total out loud; the transcript drops it).
- Point: do this for every past trade and you'll see the "half a million dollars you're trying to chase".

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Trade with the weekly trend: "Yeah, because you're not trading with the overall trend." [1:04]
- Don't predict on the 15-minute: "go to a five or 15 minute time frame and tell me where the market's going to go. You're going to have a hard time doing that." [6:07]
- Use the weekly: "It's a lot less stressful as well because you're trading with the overall trend." [6:07]
- Skip ugly charts: "Gold looks too ugly for me right now. That's why I'm not trading it right now." [8:14]
- Low frequency: "I'm going to take five trades a month, six trades a month, and you know, hold them." [10:15]
- Flexible hold: "You can alter it for uh 5 days, 7 days, 14 days, 12 days." [10:15]
- Beginners meet in the middle: "Let's try to do 12 days, 11 days, 9 days, 8 days versus, you know, two months. Start small." [10:15]
- Review old trades: "look at the entry and just go on TradingView and see where it went three weeks later." [11:15]
- Don't fight the market: "Just do where the percentage gains lie at in terms of the odds stacking in your favor." [9:14]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- He used to trade 15-minute-chart setups: "people try to trade this way. And again, like I said, I used to trade this way. That's why it took me years to get to where I wanted to get to" [2:05].
- He says it took him "six years" to figure out [4:06] and that, knowing what he knows now, he "could have gotten there in less than a year" [2:05].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Captions are missing between [7:07] and [8:14]. Part of the GA explanation (the per-month maths) is lost.
- GA entry: he says "GA is coming back up. We need to enter", yet the trade made money as price fell 194 → 185. Most likely he meant a sell after a pullback up, but the audio alone doesn't confirm the order type or exact entry.
- GA prices are given as "194" and "185" (= 1.9400 / 1.8500). The psych levels are on the chart, not stated.
- The bar-replay pair and dates are not stated.
- The hypothetical stop "1, you know, uh, 14 800" is garbled. Read as 1.1480 [?].
- "More than 90%" win rate is a caller's statement, not a verified stat.
- The style success-rate percentages are numbers he says you can "ask ChatGPT" about (in other videos). No source is given.
- No stop-loss, Fib-level, risk-% or TP-ladder specifics in this video. See the 2026-04-04 masterclass (787JlWMtCwU) for those.

## 15. Key quotes
- "Trading is about transferring the money from the impatient to the patient." [0:03]
- "If you are looking at a weekly time frame … It's very clearly it's a Fibonacci, right? A, B, C, and then go up to D." [1:04]
- "Four of these candles represent 1 hour and then you need 24 more of them to represent one day." [2:05]
- "Day trading … has a 5 to 10% success rate … swing trading … 15 to 20% and position trading is 25 to 30%." [3:05]
- "Those who said buy, you would be down 50 pips right now. That probably would have stopped you out." [6:07]
- "The market went from 194 all the way down to 185. That is 900 pips over two months." [7:07]
- "Let's just say it was a five lot. So $50 per pip. 50 time 900 is $45,000." [7:07]
- "Gold looks too ugly for me right now. That's why I'm not trading it right now." [8:14]
- "I'm going to take five trades a month, six trades a month, and you know, hold them and see what happens." [10:15]
- "There can be a balance between the patience of holding for months and not having patience at all." [10:15]
- "Just go on TradingView and see where it went three weeks later." [11:15]

# 2026-04-04 — The EASIEST Way To Profitable Trading For Beginners (Full Video)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=787JlWMtCwU |
| Uploaded | 2026-04-04 |
| Length | 27:42 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2025-26 position trading (daily/weekly, Fib 38.2/50/61.8 limit orders, wide stops, extension TPs) |

## 1. What this video covers
This is his "position trading master class" for 2026 and the most mechanical description of his current method. He defines the four trading styles and their timeframes and argues for position trading using style success-rate stats. He then walks through his USDJPY chart: psychological round-number levels drawn as boxes, trend lines, 8 and 14 EMAs, and a Fibonacci A-B-C. He gives his entry method (laddered buy limits at 38.2 / 50 / 61.8, with the biggest lot at the deepest level), stop placement (midway between 78.6 and 100), TP ladder (0%, -27, -61.8, next big psych level) with alerts, moving the stop into profit after a higher low, and re-entries. He also covers swap costs, prop-firm account-size rules and a GBPAUD short. He also states the core directional rule: decide where price will be in 3 months and trade only in that direction.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
His definitions of the four styles [1:03–2:04]:
- **Scalping:** 1-minute chart, in and out every 10-15 minutes.
- **Day trading:** about the 15-minute chart, out by end of day.
- **Swing trading:** about the 2-hour / 4-hour chart, out in a couple of days to maybe a week.
- **Position trading (his method):** daily / weekly chart. Hold "a week or 2 weeks or 3 weeks or 4 weeks or 6 weeks or 8 weeks … position trading goes longer and longer" [2:04].
- He reads the Fib and sets TPs on the **weekly** ("I go to the weekly time frame" [17:10]; "Let me go back to the weekly" for GA [21:13]).
- **Directional horizon:** 3 months. "Look at where you believe price will be 3 months from now, and only trade in that direction" [8:08].
- Example hold: a USDJPY move from late January to about mid-March ("a month and a month and a half") [19:12].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **USDJPY ("UJ")**: main worked example. "Shows how much I really focus on UJ versus, you know, gold and GU" [18:12].
- **Gold**: he believes gold will get to $6,000/oz [18:12]. Gold alerts are hard to act on late ("if you miss that alert by a couple hours, it's curtains" [19:12]).
- **GBPUSD ("GU")** and **GBPAUD ("GA")**: also traded. GA short shown on the weekly [21:13].
- Round-number levels matter "especially [for] majors" [5:06].
- **Sessions:** not used. He places limits and isn't watching the screen. Not covered otherwise.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Psychological / round-number levels** drawn as **rectangles (zones)**, not lines: USDJPY 160, 155, 150 (and 145 "just to show you") [5:06]. For an exact line: click it → Coordinates → type e.g. 160.000 [6:06]. "Price never stops at a particular number. It'll stop at a area… so you kind of draw boxes around them" [6:06]. The next big psych levels for UJ: 163, 167, 170 [9:08, 18:12].
- **Trend lines:** inner and outer trend lines; "just play around with it". He simplified to a single line ("one-liner") to focus [7:07]. He uses "third trend line bounce" as an entry confluence [23:14].
- **Moving averages:** "we already have our moving averages on … the 8 and 14 EMA" [7:07]. Colours and other settings not stated (he refers viewers to his AI). Used for re-entry on "retest the moving average" [11:08].
- **Fibonacci retracement/extension**, drawn A → B on an A-B-C structure. Levels he names: **0% (TP1), 38.2, 50, 61.8 (entries), 78.6 and 100 (stop goes between them), -27 extension (TP2), -61.8 extension (TP3)**. Full Fib settings are not given ("what my Fibonacci settings are … [ask the AI]" [8:08]).
- **Price alerts** at TP levels (and optionally at entry levels instead of limits) [18:12, 19:12].
- TradingView Bar Replay used for the demo [15:09].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Strip the chart clean [5:06].
2. Draw the major psychological round-number levels as boxes (USDJPY: 150, 155, 160, plus higher targets) [5:06–6:06].
3. Add trend lines (inner/outer) to frame the trend [7:07].
4. Have the 8 and 14 EMAs on [7:07].
5. Draw the Fibonacci on the higher-timeframe A-B-C swing to see "exactly where price wants to go" [8:08].
6. Ask where price will be **3 months from now**, and only trade in that direction. For UJ he expected 163, then 167 by June/July, so only buys [8:08–10:08].
7. Plan the entry zone (38.2-61.8 limits), find confluence with trend-line bounces and psych levels, set the stop between 78.6 and 100, and set the TP ladder/alerts at 0, -27, -61.8 and the next big psych level [15:09–18:12, 23:14].
8. Check the reward is at least 3-5× the risk [6:06].
9. Take only A+ setups. If none exist, don't trade, even for a month [25:18].

## 6. Entry rules
- **Direction:** only with the 3-month bias. "Every trade I take should only be buys in that direction. There's no need for countertrend trading, no need for selling" [9:08].
- **Order type:** mostly **limit orders** (buy limits in an uptrend). "Your entries at least me as a position trader is more predicated on limits" [14:09].
- **Laddered limits across the Fib retracement:** once price has made its high (Fib 0%) and he expects a pullback, "I will get, you know, buy limits around this entire area… one that's around here, one that's around here, one that's around here" at about the **38.2, 50 and 61.8** [15:09].
- **Lot sizing across the ladder:** "the lowest one always has the highest lot size because … you're closer to where your stop loss is" [15:09]. The 38.2 entry gets a "more conservative" lot size [15:09]. The shallow limits make sure you're "at least in that trade" if price turns from 38.2 or 50. "If you're someone who only gets into the 61.8, you may miss it" [16:10].
- Sometimes he only uses 61.8 and 50 and misses a trade that turns at 38.2 [22:14–23:14]. Sometimes he places the limit on a **third trend-line bounce**, which may coincide with a psych level [23:14].
- **Confluence:** "maximize my confluences with Fibonacci, trend line … extensions or trend line bounces … and factor it all together to get where my entry should be" [23:14].
- **Alternative to limits:** set **alerts** at those levels and market-execute when notified. Fine for USDJPY/GU, where price is often still nearby hours later, but bad for gold, which moves too far [19:12–20:12]. "You probably got to have a balance of limits and alerts. But … I'll just do limits for a lot of the times and I may miss some trades" [22:14].
- **Re-entries:** if you don't want to hold one position for a month, take profit along the way and re-enter "when price gives you a higher low… retest the moving average, reenter" [11:08].
- "You are no longer looking for sniper entries" [6:06]. "Your entries do not matter when you're position trading… Cuz your stop loss is wide enough" [14:09].
- Several trades in the same direction are fine: "why would you not find 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 trades in that direction and just hold them?" [9:08].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- **Placement:** "I usually have my stop losses in the middle between the 78.6 and the 100" [16:10]. The reason: price sometimes reaches 78.6 and still goes up.
- The same stop applies to all laddered entries (38.2, 50, 61.8) [17:10]. He mentions you could optionally raise it ("if you want to get it, you know, lower to the 61.8 or even a 50" [17:10]; wording unclear).
- **Width:** "You're no longer trying to have a 15 or a 25 pip stop loss… Your stop loss can be as big as you want it to be as long as it gives your trade enough breathing room, it fits the criteria, and your reward is at least three, four, five x that" [6:06].
- Example widths: about 400 pips on the UJ weekly example [20:12]. Typical ranges "80 pip stop loss, 400 pip TP… 50 pip stop loss, 250 pip TP… 100 pip stop loss, but a 500 pip TP", depending on pair [21:13].
- "A lot of you guys are not bad traders. Your stops are just way too tight" [16:10].
- Size the position to the wide stop: "you can't just have a 10 lot if your stop loss is this wide" [20:12].
- **Embrace drawdown:** be fine with 20, 30, 40 pips of drawdown [23:14].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- He doesn't use just the -27 extension. "I'm not putting my TP as a 27 extension. I'm putting my TP higher … the -61.8. So now it's a one and 3.4 risk-to-reward ratio" [17:10].
- **TP ladder (with alerts at each):** **TP1 = Fib 0%** (the prior high), **TP2 = -27 extension**, **TP3 = -61.8 extension**, **TP4 = next big psychological level** (UJ 170, new all-time-high territory). With TP4 included it becomes about 1:4 R:R [18:12].
- Targets are big psych levels: "160, 150, 140 … 170" [18:12].
- GA weekly short: target 1.78, "before it gets back to two flat" (2.00). About 1:5.6 R:R [21:13].
- Minimum reward of 3-5× the risk [6:06].

## 9. Trade management
- **Partials:** "I will take TPs along the way" at each ladder level, "take money off the table" [18:12].
- **Stop into profit:** once price moves up and forms a higher low ("gave us a higher low"), move the stop into profit below it. Example: entries around 153, stop moved up so the trade is "400 pips in profit" (153 → 157) and "no longer a overall stop loss" (he jokes "1 in 17,000 risk reward") [18:12–19:12]. The worst case is then a stop-out in profit.
- **Let it play out over time:** "What are we being so impatient about? Let the market go" [19:12].
- **Alerts** at 0%, -27, -61.8 and 170 [18:12].
- **Re-enter** on higher lows / EMA retests if you've taken profit [11:08].
- **Overnight swaps:** "like a dollar, two dollars … At the end of a month, you may have paid what, 60, 80, 100 dollars in overnight fees. Not a big deal" [11:08]. Holding overnight and for weeks is normal.
- **Invalidation:** if price double-bottoms without a new low, the trend is intact (the January UJ example) [10:08].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **R:R:** at least 1:3 to 1:5; examples 1:3.4 and 1:4 (UJ) and 1:5.6 (GA) [6:06, 17:10, 18:12, 21:13].
- **Lot sizing:** pick a stop you are comfortable losing in money terms. The deepest Fib entry (61.8) gets the largest lot and the shallow 38.2 the smallest [15:09, 20:12].
- **Pip value:** 3 lots = $30/pip. 700 pips × $30 = **$21,000** [10:08].
- **Account size, brokerage:** position trading works on a small personal brokerage account: $2,000-$10,000 ("$5,000 … $3,000, $4,000, $8,000, $10,000") [14:09, 22:14].
- **Account size, prop firm:** "only do this on a six-figure prop firm account, like a 100K account, 250K account, 500K account" [12:08]. Reason: daily loss limits. "If your daily loss on a 10K account is $500, like 5%, you're not going to be doing this because it's easy to lose 500 in a day" [12:08]. On a 500K account the max loss of 10% is $50,000, which he says you have never lost in a month [13:08].
- Prop-firm prices quoted (his firm, tradeprime.com): 100K = $825 (about $412-475 with codes); 500K two-step = $3,685 (about 30-50% off with codes; "about $2,000" discounted) [12:08–13:08, 22:14].
- **Plan:** pass and withdraw from a big prop account, then put part of the first withdrawal into a personal brokerage account to position trade on your own [13:08–14:09].
- **Trade selection:** "If it's not an A+ setup, I'm not trading. Even if that means I can't trade for a month, so be it because month number two will pay for months 1 2 3 4 and 5" [25:18].
- **Success-rate stats he quotes (2-year period):** scalping 1-3%, day trading 5-10%, swing 15-20%, position 25-30% [3:05].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: USDJPY long, the 3-month bias (Jan–Mar 2026) (7:07–11:08)
- **Timeframes:** daily/weekly chart, with psych boxes at 150/155/160, trend lines and 8/14 EMA.
- **Setup:** Fib A-B-C. His view: UJ goes to 163, then 167 "by June or July", so only buys [9:08–10:08].
- **Entry:** at the 61.8 retracement in January ("if you got in at 61.8 and got in, you know, in January, you were in profit") [10:08]. Exact price shown on chart, not stated.
- **Path:** in profit, "price came back down, double bottom, didn't create a new low, and then it pushed all the way back up 700 pips over a month span" [10:08].
- **Result:** 700 pips. On 3 lots ($30/pip) = $21,000 "from simply just holding it" [10:08].
- Stop not stated for this specific example.

### Trade 2: Bar-replay demo of the Fib limit ladder (unnamed, presumably USDJPY) (15:09–17:10)
- Price has made its high (Fib 0%) and is expected to pull back.
- **Entries:** buy limits at about 38.2, 50 and 61.8, with the biggest lot at 61.8 and a conservative lot at 38.2.
- **Stop:** midway between 78.6 and 100.
- Exact prices shown on chart, not stated.

### Trade 3: USDJPY weekly long, full TP ladder (17:10–20:12)
- **Timeframe:** weekly.
- **Entries:** 38.2 / 50 / 61.8 limits ("you're here"). Price area about 153 [19:12].
- **Stop:** midway between 78.6 and 100. Width about 400 pips ("if this is a 400 stop loss, so what?") [20:12].
- **TPs (with alerts):** TP1 Fib 0%, TP2 -27 ext, TP3 -61.8 ext (1:3.4), TP4 170 psych level (1:4) [17:10–18:12]. He says "1,800 … Sorry, 18,000 pip TP" [20:12]. The real distance from about 153 to 170 is about 1,700 pips, so this is a misspeak [?].
- **Management:** after price rallied and made a higher low, the stop was moved into profit: "153 all the way up to 157. Now we are 400 pips in profit" [19:12].
- **Timeline:** "January 26th, came back down, came back up… That's a month and a month and a half ago" [19:12].
- **Result:** still running at recording (open, risk-free).

### Trade 4: GBPAUD short, weekly (21:13)
- **Timeframe:** weekly.
- **Setup:** "the market is coming down, it's coming back up, boom" (a pullback in a downtrend). Sell.
- **Target:** 1.78, "before it gets back to two flat" (2.00).
- **R:R:** 1:5.6. Entry and stop prices shown on chart, not stated.

### Trade 5: Generic stop/target examples (21:13)
- 80-pip SL / 400-pip TP; 50-pip SL / 250-pip TP; 100-pip SL / 500-pip TP, depending on the instrument (UJ, gold, GU, GA). Illustrative only.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Trade the 3-month direction only: "Look at where you believe price will be 3 months from now, and only trade in that direction." [8:08]
- No countertrend: "There's no need for countertrend trading, no need for selling." [9:08]
- Psych levels as zones: "Price never stops at a particular uh number. It'll stop at a area." [6:06]
- No sniper entries: "You are no longer looking for sniper entries. Get that out of your mind." [6:06]
- Wide stops: "You're no longer trying to have a 15 or a 25 pip stop loss." [6:06]
- Reward multiple: "your reward is at least three, four, five x that." [6:06]
- Use limits: "Your entries at least me as a position trader is more predicated on limits." [14:09]
- Ladder sizing: "the lowest one always has the highest lot size because that's you're closer to where your stop loss is." [15:09]
- Stop placement: "I usually have my stop losses in the middle between the 78.6 and the 100." [16:10]
- TP beyond -27: "I'm not putting my TP as a 27 extension. I'm putting my TP higher… the -61.8." [17:10]
- TP ladder: "TP1 at point zero. TP2 -27 extension. TP3 -61.8. TP4 here." [18:12]
- Stop to profit after a higher low: "price done went up and gave us a [higher] low. So now we can move our stop loss into profit." [18:12]
- Limits beat alerts for gold: "gold, if you miss that alert by a couple hours, it's curtains." [20:12]
- Size for the stop: "you can't just have a 10 lot if your stop loss is this wide." [20:12]
- Prop firm: "only do this on a six-figure prop firm account." [12:08]
- Embrace drawdown: "if you … was fine with the 20, 30, 40 pips … of a drawdown … your trading will be a whole lot better." [23:14]
- A+ only: "If it's not an A+ setup, I'm not trading. Even if that means I can't trade for a month, so be it." [25:18]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- "The strategy is kind of the same, the entries and the exits are different… the psychological levels are the same, you're just holding it longer" [5:06]. Same psych-level/Fib/EMA toolkit as before; the change is in execution and holding time.
- No more sniper entries or 15-25 pip stops [6:06].
- Old concepts don't work: "You guys are taking old outdated concepts and try to apply it to today's market" [22:14]. NBA-centre / three-pointer analogy for adapting [0:02–1:03].
- He says he is "leaving this industry next year" (2027) [22:14].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Fib settings (exact levels drawn, colours) and EMA colours/timeframe are not given. He points to "Swaggy's AI".
- Which timeframe the Fib is drawn on: he says he "go[es] to the weekly" for TPs. The entry example in bar replay doesn't say.
- "Or if you want to get it, you know, lower to the 61.8 or even a 50" [17:10]. Unclear whether this means raising the stop or something else.
- "1,800 … sorry, 18,000 pip TP" [20:12] doesn't match 153 → 170 (about 1,700 pips). Likely a misspeak.
- "1 in 17,000 risk reward" is hyperbole for "no risk left".
- How the lot size is split across 38.2/50/61.8 (exact ratios) is not stated.
- Prices for the GA short (entry/stop) are on the chart only. Target 1.78 and 2.00 are stated.
- Prop-firm daily/max loss figures: 10K → $500 (5%) daily; 500K → 10% max = $50K. He says "max loss is, you know, 5% … max loss is 10%" [13:08], mixing daily and max.
- The style success-rate stats are unsourced ("ask ChatGPT").

## 15. Key quotes
- "Position trading over a 2-year period equals 25 to 30%." [3:05]
- "The strategy is kind of the same, the entries and the exits are different… you're just holding it longer." [5:06]
- "Your stop loss can be as big as you want it to be as long as it gives your trade enough breathing room… and your reward is at least three, four, five x that." [6:06]
- "We already have our moving averages on… the 8 and 14 EMA." [7:07]
- "Look at where you believe price will be 3 months from now, and only trade in that direction." [8:08]
- "Why would you not find 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 trades in that direction and just hold them?" [9:08]
- "Your entries do not matter when you're position trading… Cuz your stop loss is wide enough." [14:09]
- "I will get, you know, buy limits around this entire area." [15:09]
- "The lowest one always has the highest lot size." [15:09]
- "I usually have my stop losses in the middle between the 78.6 and the 100." [16:10]
- "TP1 at point zero. TP2 -27 extension. TP3 -61.8. TP4 here. Now it's a one and four risk-to-reward ratio." [18:12]
- "So now we can move our stop loss into profit right here." [18:12]
- "I like to place limits so that I'm at least in the trade no matter what. And I just hold them." [20:12]
- "If it's not an A+ setup, I'm not trading." [25:18]
- "The industry does not reward you for how many times you show up. The industry rewards you for showing up correctly." [24:16]

# 2026-04-03 — Most Trading Strategies Are GARBAGE... Here's One That Actually Works

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oUzc4qeRIhg |
| Uploaded | 2026-04-03 |
| Length | 39:27 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2025-26 position trading (3-month directional bias, limit orders, wide stops, hold winners) |

## 1. What this video covers
He presents position trading as his "primary strategy … for 2026 into 2027" and argues that other strategies, "including my former one" (day trading / screen-glued trading), are trash. Most of the video is conceptual. It covers the "check where price was 3 weeks after you exited" P&L exercise, matching analysis timeframe to trading style, and a bar-replay game on USDCAD (weekly 3-month prediction vs 15-minute 5-hour prediction). It also covers his core rule of only trading in the direction you expect price to be in 3 months (invalidated only by a break in market structure), wide stops vs 20-30 pip drawdown, lot sizing, and why you should use six-figure prop accounts. He sketches his weekly routine: analyse on Sunday/Monday, place a buy/sell limit, 1-2 trades in that direction, move the stop to break-even then into profit, check periodically. He says a full step-by-step technical breakdown will come in a separate video (that is the 2026-04-04 masterclass, 787JlWMtCwU).

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Style:** position trading on the macro/higher timeframes. "Trading is simply looking at the macro side of things and predicting where it goes. And most of you guys look at the micro, but want the macro side of money" [16:20].
- **Directional horizon:** 3 months. "If you believe it's going to be there in 3 months, why is every single trade you take not in that direction?" [13:19].
- **Holding:** not literally 3 months. "I'm not telling you to look at what the markets are going to do over the next 3 months and just hold it for 3 months" [13:19]. You can take "10 trades over those 3 months" [14:19]. The benchmark exercise compares results with holding for about 3-4 weeks.
- **Timeframe must match style:** don't analyse on 5-minute and then swing trade, analyse on 1-minute while holding for days, or start a day-trade analysis on the weekly [8:13].
- The weekly chart is used for the 3-month read [11:18].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **USDCAD**: main chart example (bar replay, and his live view in spring 2026).
- **GBPUSD**: December (2025) trade call example.
- **Gold**: example bias "Gold is at, you know, 4,500, it's going to get to 5,000 an ounce" [13:19].
- **USDJPY**, **EURUSD**: mentioned as examples.
- **Sessions:** Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- TradingView **Bar Replay** (5× and 3× speed) for the prediction game [10:16–11:18].
- **Boxes (zones)** and **trend lines**: in the USDCAD outlook price may "test the overall box" and then "break through this trend line" [32:32].
- **Market structure** (higher lows / lower highs / lower lows) as the only invalidation of the bias [32:32].
- Buy limit / sell limit orders [34:32].
- Fibonacci and EMAs not discussed in detail here (deferred to the next video). He mentions his AI can answer "What is Swaggy's Fibonacci rules?" [37:37].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. On the weekly/higher timeframe, decide the direction you expect over the next **3 months** [10:16–13:19].
2. Mark the key zone (box) and trend line that price may test or break (USDCAD example) [32:32].
3. Take every trade in that direction only. Buy only if bullish, sell only if bearish, no counter-trend [13:19–14:19].
4. Change the bias only if **market structure breaks**: "the market comes down and then up and then down again, lower high, lower low, boom, lower high, lower low. Then it's like, 'Oh, we're clearly in a downtrend'" [32:32].
5. Do the analysis once, on a Sunday or Monday: "you can just look at a chart on one Monday or Sunday, analyze for a good amount of time, and … place a buy limit or sell limit" [33:32–34:32].
6. Place one or two trades in that direction and let price come to you [34:32].
7. Check periodically over the next couple of days and manage (break-even → profit) [34:32].

## 6. Entry rules
- Only in the 3-month direction [13:19–14:19].
- **Order type:** buy limit or sell limit placed after the weekend analysis. "Place it, let it, you know, kind of come to you, place, you know, one or two trades in that direction" [34:32].
- No sniper entries: "as a position trader, you're not really worried about your entries" [17:22]. Entries are said not to be the problem; "your dedication … to trying to push through a failing concept is" [17:22].
- Detailed entry levels (Fib) not covered here.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Wide stops with breathing room. Example: "This is a 80-pip stop loss" against "well over … 300 pips" TP [17:22–18:22].
- "I'm not worried about a 20-pip 30-pip drawdown, but some of you guys have a 20-pip 30-pip stop loss, so being 5 pips in … drawdown scares you out" [18:22].
- The bar-replay game shows 15-minute traders who "they're not going to hold 50 pips in drawdown. They're going to either get out or get stopped out" [12:19].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Target the 3-month objective level (USDCAD: 1.4050 / 1.4150, "up here around 141-ish" by about June) [32:32].
- Example R:R: 80-pip SL vs about 313-pip TP = "1 and 3.92, basically 1 and 4" [18:22].
- No TP ladder in this video.

## 9. Trade management
- "Periodically check it over the next couple of days, move your stop loss into break even, then move your stop loss into profit, so it's a risk-free trade, and just let it get there" [34:32].
- Hold through early drawdown. In the December GBPUSD call, students were 30-40 pips down for 2 days before a 200-250 pip move [18:22].
- Hold winners longer. The 3-weeks-later exercise is about how much more holding would have made. The goal is to capture "20% … 30% … 50%" more of it, not all of it [5:11–6:12].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **"Your lot sizes are wrong"** is named as a real reason for not being profitable [22:25]. Exercise: imagine every past trade at double the lot (2 → 4, 1 → 2, 0.5 → 1) [22:25]. His point is that sizing correctly on a large account multiplies results (his student example below).
- **Pip value:** 2 lots = $20/pip [4:10].
- **Prop-firm account size:** avoid small accounts with tiny drawdown limits. "trade a 10K prop firm account when the drawdown the max drawdown is like what? $400? Right? 4%? 5%?" [30:28]. Use 100K (drawdown $4,000-$5,000) or 500K: "your daily drawdown is 25K … Your max drawdown is 50K" [31:31]. "It is much better to take the next month and a half to stack up money and then get a 500K account" than keep blowing 5K/10K accounts with a $250 daily loss limit [31:31].
- Persistence: the student who made $471K had blown five prior accounts (10K, 25K, 50K: all under six figures) [30:28].
- **Polls he cites:** 96% would rather earn $500K/yr with free time than $750K glued to screens [7:12]. Barriers: 32% stress, 45% entries/exits, 5% lot size, 80% "I don't know what I'm doing sometimes" [16:20].
- No explicit risk-% per trade in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: Hypothetical EURUSD long, "3 weeks later" exercise (3:08–5:11)
- Entry 1.10000, stopped out at 1.09700 (30 pips) within about a day.
- 3.5-4 weeks later price reaches 1.15000 = 500 pips. On 2 lots ($20/pip) = $10,000 missed. He admits EU doesn't usually move that much in 3 weeks; illustrative only.
- His own version of the exercise: on about $210K of realised profits he "would have made over 10 million dollars" had he held [5:11].

### Trade 2: USDCAD bar replay, weekly 3-month prediction (10:16–11:18)
- Bar replay back to around July (year not stated), weekly chart. Group mostly said "up". Played at 5× through about 3 months: USDCAD went up. Most were right.

### Trade 3: USDCAD bar replay, 15-minute 5-hour prediction (11:18–13:19)
- 15-minute chart at 3× speed: "where price is going to go in the next 5 hours". The chat split up/down.
- Price "shot up about … 10 uh 20 pips, came down … from … 1.4455 all the way up to 140, you know, 600 basically, all the way down to 1.4100 about 50 60 pips down". The price numbers are garbled (see Gaps). The "up" camp would have lost.
- Next round: "down" callers went 20 then 30 pips into drawdown as price rose. Over 10 rounds, everyone won some and lost some.

### Trade 4: Unnamed long, 80-pip SL vs 300+ pip TP (17:22–18:22)
- Chart example: "If I get in right here… the market comes down in drawdown… This is a 80-pip stop loss… well over … 300 pips" TP = 1:3.92 (about 1:4). Instrument and prices shown on chart, not stated.

### Trade 5: GBPUSD call, December (2025) (18:22–19:23)
- Called out to his group. First 2 days: 30-40 pips of drawdown, with complaints. Next 3 days: up "well over 200 250 pips". A student passed a prop challenge and got a $15,000 payout. Direction implied long. Entry/stop not stated.

### Trade 6: USDCAD current outlook, long bias (spring 2026) (32:32–33:32)
- Bias: by about June, USDCAD "might be up here … around 141-ish". It may first "come back down … to test the overall box, come up, boom, higher low, finally break through this trend line and come up".
- Targets: 1.4050 or 1.4150 ("140,500 … 141,500").
- Rule: every trade long. Flip only if structure breaks into lower highs and lower lows.
- Entry/stop not stated (box and trend line shown on chart).

### Trade 7: Student's $471K prop-firm run (Oct 2025–Jan 2026) (22:25–25:26)
- Context: lot-sizing lesson. 100K account bought October 17 (after his October 13-17 boot camp). Made about $20K in the first few days, then did nothing for two months.
- From January 19 (after his guidance), took the account from about 120K to $236K (about $116K in 5-6 days).
- 500K one-step account from January 23: $500K → $834K. He then corrects the profit on one account to about $136-137K. Total said to be about $470-471K in about a week and a half.
- With half the lot size he'd have made about $235K. Pairs, entries and stops are not given.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Match timeframe to style: "What the hell does a weekly time frame got to do with you being a scalper or day trader?" [8:13]
- Trade the 3-month direction only: "If you believe it's going to be there in 3 months, why is every single trade you take not in that direction?" [13:19]
- Buy-only / sell-only: "You're only buying if you believe it's going to go up. If you believe it'll be down in 3 months, you're only selling." [13:19–14:19]
- No counter-trend: "Why am I countertrend trading? … You're literally trading against the trend." [14:19]
- Only exception: "The only way I will go against it, in my opinion, is if market structure breaks." [32:32]
- Wide stop / tolerate drawdown: "I'm not worried about a 20-pip 30-pip drawdown." [18:22]
- Weekly routine: "look at a chart on one Monday or Sunday, analyze for a good amount of time, and … place a buy limit or sell limit." [34:32]
- Few trades: "place, you know, one or two trades in that direction, and just let it get there." [34:32]
- Management: "move your stop loss into break even, then move your stop loss into profit, so it's a risk-free trade." [34:32]
- Lot size: "the true real reasons are A, your lot sizes are wrong and B, you don't know what you're doing." [22:25]
- Prop accounts: "It is much better to take the next month and a half to stack up money and then get a 500K account." [31:31]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- He calls other strategies trash "including my former one" [1:04]. Former = active, screen-glued trading: "I really was glued to the charts 12 hours a day and I was making money, but like I didn't have time for nobody… I was in Cabo glued to the screen" [33:32].
- He says he has tested "every single strategy under the sun over the last 12 years, but only one stuck out" [1:04].
- Countertrend: "back in 2019 and 2020, I'm like, 'Yeah, countertrend trading is I don't believe it anymore'" [14:19]. That dates his rejection of counter-trend trading to 2019-2020.
- Using old information in today's market (NBA centres who can't shoot threes) [8:13–10:16].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- USDCAD 15-minute prices garbled: "1.4455 all the way up to 140, you know, 600 … down to 1.4100 about 50 60 pips down". 1.4455 → 1.4100 would be 355 pips, not 50-60. Possibly 1.4055 → 1.4060 → 1.4000, or the captions mis-heard [?].
- USDCAD targets "140,500 … 141,500" read as 1.4050 / 1.4150 [?].
- The student's numbers don't add up cleanly (334K vs 136-137K on the 500K account; total 471K). The 2026-10-22 video says +66% on the 500K one-step and +137% on the 100K instant.
- The poll percentages total more than 100% (as stated).
- No Fib/EMA/stop-placement specifics. He defers those to the next video (787JlWMtCwU).
- The "December" GBPUSD trade direction is implied long ("skyrocketed"). Entry/stop not given.
- Year for the USDCAD bar-replay "July" is not stated.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I'll show you the primary strategy that will work for you in 2026 into 2027 and why other strategies are trash including my former one." [0:03–1:04]
- "Look at your lot sizes your entry and your close price. Go on the charts and see where price was at 3 weeks later." [3:08]
- "You are looking at a 5-minute time frame but then trying to swing trade." [8:13]
- "Do you see how difficult it is to try to predict what the market's going to do over a 3 4 5-hour span versus what is it going to do for the next 3 months?" [13:19]
- "Why is every single trade you take not in that direction?" [13:19]
- "Most of you guys look at the micro, but want the macro side of money." [16:20]
- "As a position trader, you're not really worried about your entries." [17:22]
- "This is a 80-pip stop loss … We're over a 300-pip TP… 1 and 3.92, basically 1 and 4." [17:22–18:22]
- "The only way I will go against it, in my opinion, is if market structure breaks." [32:32]
- "Place a buy limit or sell limit. Place it, let it, you know, kind of come to you." [34:32]
- "Move your stop loss into break even, then move your stop loss into profit, so it's a risk-free trade, and just let it get there." [34:32]
- "The true real reasons are A, your lot sizes are wrong and B, you don't know what you're doing." [22:25]

# 2025-08-15 — I'VE BEEN TRADING WRONG FOR YEARS....

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TaH782wuq7Q |
| Uploaded | 2025-08-15 |
| Length | 19:36 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 2:04-11:17; 15:19-16:20; 17:21-19:23) |
| Strategy era | 2025-26 position trading (announces the switch from swing/day trading) |

## 1. What this video covers
This is his explanation of why he switched to position trading in 2025. He tells a story about a trader who held a USDJPY position from 102 and would have made $3.5M had she not exited and reversed. He then describes reviewing his own trades (especially a large gold long) and backtesting longer holds. The method itself barely changes: the same Fibonacci and trend-line analysis, now done on the daily/weekly, with trades held for weeks to months, fewer than 10 trades a quarter, and partial profits along the way. He gives his recent win-rate figures and says wrong timing with 20-pip stops cost him about half his losses. Large parts are promotion for a $47 "5-day position trading intensive" (Aug 18) and a "position" document on Instagram, which are not documented here.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Style:** position trading ("positioning"). "Positioning, you don't have to worry about that. you're just focused on where is it going to get to whether it takes two weeks, whether it takes two months, where is it going to get to by the end of this quarter" [8:11].
- **Holding period:** weeks to months; he reviewed his trades asking what would happen with "an extra week, an extra month, an extra two months" [2:04].
- **Timeframes:** daily and weekly. "what does this want to do from a long-term point of view, from a macro point of view? Looking at a weekly or a daily" [3:07]. "doing it on a daily time frame and just looking at it and then getting in the trade and just holding it" [9:13].
- Against the lower timeframes: analysing on the daily/weekly "is a lot smoother because you see so much more than just a bunch of candles going up and down like you would on a fivem minute time frame" [4:07].
- You still monitor the trade, but "not looking for a day or two or three" [9:13].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Gold is the main instrument he reviewed ("I went back and reviewed some of my trades, gold specifically" [2:04]).
- USDJPY in the opening story (102 to 120; it later reached 150) [0:03-1:04, 15:19].
- Sessions: not relevant to positioning; "you come in when you want. You take profit when you want" [18:22]. Day trading and scalping force you to "change your schedule to fit the market schedules" [18:22].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Same tools as before: "using Fibonacci trend lines all the same stuff but doing it on a daily time frame" [9:13].
- TradingView mentioned for reviewing charts [15:19].
- No indicator settings given in this video.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Look at the market from a macro/long-term view on the weekly or daily [3:07].
2. Apply the same Fibonacci and trend-line analysis on the daily [9:13].
3. Decide where price is likely to get to "by the end of this quarter", not when [8:11].
4. Pick only the few best setups ("two good pitches, three good pitches, five good pitches, 10 good pitches over a quarter" [8:11]).
- No more detail on the process in this video (he refers to his intensive and the "position" document).

## 6. Entry rules
- Not covered in this video in terms of exact triggers or order types. The only entry principle: wait for the best pitch: "I wait for the pitch to come to me. I'm not swinging at every single pitch" (paraphrasing Warren Buffett) [5:07].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Stated problem with his old stops: of his last 130 trades, in "at least 81" his direction was right but "like half of my loss because why? I had a 20 pip stop loss. I got in too late. I got in too early" [7:09-8:11]. With positioning "you don't have to worry about that" [8:11].
- No new stop size is given.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- The target is where price gets to over the quarter [8:11]. He uses big moves as examples: USDJPY 102 to 120 (and 150) [0:03-1:04, 15:19]; gold "skyrocketed 10,000 pips" after his trade [3:07].
- No Fibonacci target levels named in this video.

## 9. Trade management
- **Hold the trade:** the moral of the USDJPY story is to not touch the position: "She just let it ride" [1:04].
- **Partial profits while holding:** "You can still take partial profits. If you're up 10, 20, 30, $40,000, you can literally go to your phone or computer and literally take partial profits. Take 10% out, 20, 30, 40% out and still let the trade run" [9:13-10:16].
- **Weekend holding and costs:** "We're talking about commissions, rollover fees, swap. Like, pay it. Hold it over the weekend. Pay it" [15:19].
- Monitor, but don't watch every candle [9:13].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Trade frequency:** "literally take less than 10 trades a quarter" [5:07]. "Can you imagine how much simpler life would be if you took 10 trades in three months as opposed to 10 trades in one day?" [8:11].
- **Win-rate claims since switching:** "I won 10 out of my last 12 trades ... I won 20 out of my last 24 trades ... I won my last 30 out of my last 38 trades" [9:13].
- **Old record:** "at least 81 trades out of my last 130 where I literally said the market was going to do this and it did that" [7:09].
- **Lot size example:** a 50-lot gold position (see Trade 2).
- Goal framing: "I would rather make a million dollars a year off of six trades ... versus making a million dollars a year off of 400 trades" [18:22].
- No risk % stated.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: USDJPY long (someone else's position trade) (0:03-1:04, 15:19)
- **Context:** a woman trader, late 2020 / early 2021, believed USDJPY would go from 102.00 to 120.00. She was a position trader.
- **What happened:** price went 102 to 108; she exited, "secured that 700 racks" ($700,000), then reversed and shorted. Price kept going to 120. She lost the $700k and more. She said she "would have made $3.5 million had she just stayed in the trade" [1:04]. USDJPY later reached 150, so holding further "would have been 8 n 10 million" [15:19].
- **Lesson:** one trade on one pair, held, beats many trades.

### Trade 2: Gold (XAUUSD) long, his own (2:04-3:07)
- **Position:** "I had a 50 lot. A 50 lot on gold" [3:07].
- **Status at the time:** "I was up over $40,000. And my goal was to get it to $108,000" [2:04]. He implies he exited (it is a "what if I had held" review).
- **What-if:** gold "has skyrocketed 10,000 pips" since; held until now it would be "$5 million. Not 500K, 5 million" [3:07].
- **Entry, stop, exit price:** not stated.
- **Note:** the "$40,000 up, goal $108,000" details match the live gold trade in video 7YmARnoIJ7I (uploaded 2025-03-02), where his maximum profit was $108,000 and the trade peaked at about $40,000. But that video shows about 22.5 lots, not 50, so it may or may not be the same trade [?].

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Trade less: "People won't tell you, but you're actually a good trader. You're just swinging too many times" [4:07].
- Wait for the pitch: "every single pitch is not worth swinging" [5:07].
- Fewer than 10 trades a quarter: "literally take less than 10 trades a quarter" [5:07].
- Hold for the full move: "where is it going to get to by the end of this quarter" [8:11].
- Take partials but keep the runner: "Take 10% out, 20, 30, 40% out and still let the trade run" [10:16].
- Pay the swap and hold over weekends: "Hold it over the weekend. Pay it" [15:19].
- If you call direction right but get stopped: "you know how to trade. You just don't know how to day trade" [7:09].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- **Headline:** "I have been trading wrong for the last few years" [0:03]. The story "is why I kind of switched my entire strategy around" [3:07].
- **From day/swing to position:** in Secure the Swag season 2 (LA) he was "still ... trading more like a day trader and then like slowly getting into swing trading" [1:04]. Now he holds weeks to months.
- **Why:**
  1. Reviewing past trades showed that holding an extra week/month/two months would have made far more (gold: $5M vs ~$40k) [2:04-3:07].
  2. Too-tight 20-pip stops and bad timing caused about half his losses even when direction was right (81 of 130 trades) [7:09-8:11].
  3. Backtesting and live testing gave 10/12, 20/24, 30/38 wins [9:13].
  4. Lifestyle: positioning fits around life; day trading forces you onto market hours [14:19, 18:22].
  5. Influences: Warren Buffett ("Day trading is a no strike game" paraphrase [4:07]; a Buffett book saying day trading is an uphill battle [12:18]), and Alex Hormozi's (captioned "Alexi") advice to double down on strengths [7:09].
- **What stayed the same:** "using Fibonacci trend lines all the same stuff but doing it on a daily time frame" [9:13].
- **When:** "this is something I developed earlier this year. I've kind of kept it more to myself. I didn't feel it was fair to make a video on it if I didn't back test it myself" [17:21]. He also says he wishes he had started "in 2021 and 2022" [10:16].
- Learning time: "all you got to do is learn for a ... couple of weeks, let's say eight weeks of of an intensive study" [18:22].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- No concrete entry, stop, or target rules for the new style here; he points to his paid intensive and an Instagram "position" document.
- The 50-lot gold trade has no entry/exit prices or dates; the link to the 7YmARnoIJ7I trade is unconfirmed (lot sizes differ).
- "81 trades out of my last 130 ... like half of my loss" is loosely worded; it is unclear whether "half" refers to half of the 81 or half of all losses.
- The win records (10/12, 20/24, 30/38) are not tied to specific dates or instruments.
- "Alexi" is assumed to be Alex Hormozi [?].

## 15. Key quotes
- "I have been trading wrong for the last few years and I want to tell you exactly how." [0:03]
- "what does this want to do from a long-term point of view, from a macro point of view? Looking at a weekly or a daily." [3:07]
- "People won't tell you, but you're actually a good trader. You're just swinging too many times." [4:07]
- "literally take less than 10 trades a quarter. Less than 10 trades in a quarter." [5:07]
- "You just don't know how to day trade." [7:09]
- "I had a 20 pip stop loss. I got in too late. I got in too early. Whereas positioning, you don't have to worry about that." [8:11]
- "where is it going to get to by the end of this quarter" [8:11]
- "I won 10 out of my last 12 trades ... I won my last 30 out of my last 38 trades." [9:13]
- "using Fibonacci trend lines all the same stuff but doing it on a daily time frame" [9:13]
- "Take 10% out, 20, 30, 40% out and still let the trade run." [10:16]
- "Hold it over the weekend. Pay it." [15:19]
- "this is something I developed earlier this year" [17:21]

# 2025-03-02 — Making $30,000+ In 4 Hours in Dubai With FULL TRADE MANAGEMENT

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7YmARnoIJ7I |
| Uploaded | 2025-03-02 |
| Length | 18:14 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 0:01-12:12; 14:22-16:23) |
| Strategy era | Transition: late-2024/early-2025 swing trading moving toward daily-based (position-style) trades. Daily Fib ABC to the 61.8 with 4H/1H candle confirmation and scaling in |

## 1. What this video covers
A Dubai vlog in which he places and manages a live gold (XAUUSD) long. He identifies a daily-timeframe Fibonacci ABC pullback to the 61.8, waits for a 4H candle close as confirmation, and enters with a small market order first. He then layers in larger pending orders: a buy limit below price and a buy stop above a counter-trend line / inverse head and shoulders neckline. He states lot sizes, potential profit and loss in dollars, and a ~1:5 R:R on the starter position. Later he reports the floating P/L ($20k, $27k, $30k, $40k peak, then back to $10k), his plan to move the stop into profit after a higher low prints, and letting the New York session play out. The video ends without a final result. Promotional segments (a $47 3-day boot camp) are not documented.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Analysis timeframe:** daily ("for gold um I'm looking on a daily uh time frame" [0:01]).
- **Confirmation timeframes:** 4H candle close ("going down to the four-hour time frame" [0:01]; "waiting to see how this 4-Hour Candlestick wants to close" [2:02]), 1H ("I don't like how this 1 hour Candlestick looks" [1:02]), and he also glances at the 15-minute and a 5-minute candle ("oh it's a 5 minute time frame we still got an extra 5 minutes ... before this candle closes" [5:11]).
- **Holding:** at least through the New York session close; he talks about possibly closing and re-entering, and a follow-up vlog for the outcome [15:23-16:23]. Intended as a multi-session hold to the $108k target.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **Instrument:** gold (XAUUSD) only.
- **Session:** the trade runs into the New York session: "it's New York session right now so um honestly you just got to let New York session play out ... by the end of the day which is uh 5:00 p.m. New York session uh which is about 2:00 in the morning here [Dubai]" [15:23]. London session had already passed [15:23].
- **News:** at the end, "news just dropped it a little bit" [16:23] (no specific release named).

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Fibonacci retracement** drawn as an ABC on the daily, with the 61.8 as the entry zone [0:01-1:02].
- **Projection tool** (TradingView long/short position or projection) showing the target at 2681 [0:01-1:02].
- **Counter-trend line** (the short-term down trend line of the pullback) and an **inverse head and shoulders** pattern on the lower timeframe [1:02, 4:10, 6:11].
- **Candlesticks:** a doji on the 4H; he wants the 4H to close as a doji or with a small body as confirmation [1:02, 2:02].
- No moving averages mentioned in this video.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. **Daily structure:** "I can see price higher low in comparison to this low higher low did another higher low right here" [0:01]; the daily is in an uptrend making higher lows.
2. **Daily Fibonacci ABC:** "I see a Fibonacci clear as day a BC is right to 61.8" [0:01-1:02]; the C leg has pulled back to the 61.8.
3. **Drop to the 4H** for the confirmation candle: he wants the current 4H candle, which looked like a doji, to close as a doji / small body [1:02].
4. **Check 1H and lower** for structure: the 1H "looks kind of ugly", so he expects some consolidation "before breaking through this counter trend line and then skyrocketing up to our projection of 2681" [1:02].
5. **Identify the trigger levels:** a break above 2633 (counter-trend line / inverse H&S neckline area) for a buy stop; a lower level (2623) for a buy limit "in case it wants to get that low" [2:02-4:10].
6. **Plan the layered entries** (see section 6) to "trap price" from both sides.

## 6. Entry rules
- **Confirmation needed:** "if this doji can close and print in the next 10 minutes ... that's a confirmation I need to actually get in" [1:02]. Candle-body rule: "waiting to see how this 4-Hour Candlestick wants to close um whether it wants to close 50% of the body or 10 20% cuz if we get close to 10 or 20% Then um we're entering fully fully" [2:02]. (A small body, 10-20%, i.e. a doji-like close = full entry.)
- **Starter position before confirmation, with small size:** "there's a chance I may get in now with a lower lot size like I usually do" [1:02]; "price wants to pull up then I'm in the trade regardless um and then scale in once I get that extra extra extra confirmation" [2:02].
- **Three order types used together ("trap price"):** "I'm going to get a buy limit a market execution and then a buy stop to trap price cuz I really believe it's about to pull up" [5:11].
  1. **Market execution:** 2.5 lots [5:11].
  2. **Buy limit:** 5 lots at 2623 (later replaced; see below) [3:09-4:10].
  3. **Buy stop:** 10 lots at 2634, just above 2633, "if I can break above 2633 that'd be great" [2:02, 6:11].
- **Adjustment:** he later moved the buy limit up to "maybe 10 Pips below price" with 10 lots, to get "the last little bit of draw down I possibly can get" [9:12-10:12]; if price hesitated and didn't clear those pips he would market-execute instead [9:12]. That limit filled shortly after [10:12].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Stops go below the daily higher low / pullback structure.
- **Market order (2.5 lots):** potential loss $2,500 [2:02]. At 2.5 lots ($250 per $1 move) that is about $10 below entry. He later says "my St was 2672 on the other one", which is most likely 2612 misheard [?] (2672 would be above entry on a long).
- **Buy limit 2623 (5 lots):** potential loss $4,000 [4:10]. He says "I don't see getting this 12 let's do 15" [3:09-4:10], which is most consistent with a stop at 2615 (2623 − 2615 = $8 × $500/$ = $4,000) [inferred].
- **Buy stop 2634 (10 lots):** stop placement as spoken: "stop 26 at that point ... the B could be at 2630 basically the stop loss", reasoning that if price breaks the counter-trend line, the new higher low forms there and "my stop loss being down here is more than feasible" [6:11-7:12]. Exact stop price unclear [?].
- **Overall:** with about 22 lots on, maximum loss "I lose 14" (~$14,000) vs maximum profit $108,000 [12:21].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- **Single target for all positions: 2681**, the projection ("skyrocketing it up to our projection of 2681" [1:02]; buy stop "TP 261 same" = 2681 [6:11]).
- Stated potential profits:
  - 2-3 lots at market: "potential profit of 15,000 and a potential loss of 2500 which is a 1 in5 Risk Reward ratio" [2:02].
  - 5-lot buy limit: "potential profit of 30,000 or 29,000 and loss of 4,000" [4:10].
  - Both pending orders: "76,000"; with the market order "it'll make it to 88,000" [7:12].
  - After switching to two 10-lot pendings: "the most I can make is 108,000" [12:21].
- **R:R:** 1:5 on the starter; overall about 1:7.7 ($14k risk vs $108k), "it's not like it's a one like a one in 10" [12:21].

## 9. Trade management
- **Scale in:** small first, add after confirmation and with pending orders at better/confirmed prices [2:02, 5:11].
- **Minimize drawdown:** "I like to minimize draw down as much as possible" [10:12]; that is why he used a limit a little below price rather than a second market order.
- **Move stop to profit after a higher low:** "I'm waiting for a higher low to be printed and then once that higher low is printed then I can move my stop loss into profit so now it's a complete risk- free trade" [14:22].
- **Hold through red candles:** "I'm up $30,000 and I've seen a bunch of red candles I could have gotten out at any point in time ... but it's like I feel like it's going to go higher" [14:22].
- **Let the New York session play out** to 5 pm New York before judging [15:23].
- **Close and re-enter option:** after it fell back to $10k: "I may close the trade and then reenter or watch it go back up" [15:23]; "I can close at 10 and get back in because gold is looking crazy right now" [16:23].
- He comments on greed at the start: "I'm up a certain amount of money if I would just close right now I could go on vacation ... but I don't want to close cuz I want to double that and then you go to sleep you wake up and now you just lost it all ... you can't just be greedy" [0:01].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Sizes used:** 2.5 lots market, 10 lots buy stop, 5 lots buy limit (replaced by 10 lots), total "22" lots [12:21] (2.5 + 10 + 10 = 22.5).
- **Dollar risk:** about $2,500 on the starter; $4,000 on the original limit; about $14,000 total at full size [2:02, 4:10, 12:21].
- **R:R:** 1:5 on the starter; ~1:7.7 overall.
- Lot size is small before confirmation, larger on pending orders ("I may get in now with a lower lot size like I usually do" [1:02]); same principle as his 2024 strategy video.
- Account size and risk % not stated.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: XAUUSD (gold) long (0:01-16:23)
- **Date/context:** uploaded 2025-03-02; filmed in Dubai. Prices around 2620-2680 suggest it was filmed in late 2024 / early 2025, not at upload time (gold was ~$2,850+ in March 2025) [?].
- **Timeframes:** daily (structure and Fib), 4H (confirmation candle), 1H/15m/5m (timing, counter-trend line, inverse H&S).
- **Setup and reasoning:** daily uptrend with successive higher lows; a daily Fib ABC pullback "right to 61.8"; a 4H doji forming at the 61.8; a lower-timeframe inverse head and shoulders with a counter-trend line; he expects consolidation and then a break of the counter-trend line, rallying to 2681 [0:01-2:02, 4:10-7:12].
- **Entries:**
  1. Market buy, **2.5 lots**, before full confirmation (he considered 2-3 lots) [2:02, 5:11]. Entry price not stated (about 2621-2625 if the $15k/$2.5k numbers are taken as stated [inferred]).
  2. Buy limit, **5 lots @ 2623**, "a precautionary measure in case it wants to get that low" [3:09-4:10]; he doubted it would fill.
  3. Buy stop, **10 lots @ 2634**, to catch a break above 2633 / the counter-trend line; "the 10 lot I know will hit" [6:11-7:12].
  4. Revised: buy limit **10 lots** about "10 Pips below price" replaced the low limit; it filled ("boom open okay cleared") [9:12-10:12].
- **Stop loss:** starter about $10 below entry (~2612 [?]); 5-lot limit 2615 [inferred]; buy stop near 2630 or below the new higher low [?]. Max total loss ~$14,000.
- **Target:** 2681 for all orders. Max profit $108,000.
- **Management:** watched the floating P/L (−$200, +$100, −$30, +$500 shortly after entry [10:12]); planned to move the stop into profit once a higher low printed [14:22]; let the NY session play out [15:23].
- **Running result:** "I'm 20,000" [12:21-13:21]; "currently up $27,000 I was up 30" [13:21]; "close to 30,000 again" [14:22]; then "the $30,000 trade went to 40,000 then it went all the way back down to 10,000" [15:23]. Final result not shown; he considered closing at $10k and re-entering [16:23].
- **Shown on chart, not stated:** the daily Fib anchor points, the counter-trend line, the inverse H&S levels, and the exact stop prices.
- **Possible link:** in TaH782wuq7Q (Aug 2025) he describes a gold long that was "up over $40,000" with a goal of $108,000, which matches this trade. But he calls that one a 50-lot position [?].

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Wait for the candle close as confirmation: "that's a confirmation I need to actually get in" [1:02].
- Small starter before confirmation: "I may get in now with a lower lot size like I usually do" [1:02].
- Scale in after confirmation: "scale in once I get that extra extra extra confirmation" [2:02].
- Use limit + market + stop together: "a buy limit a market execution and then a buy stop to trap price" [5:11].
- Minimize drawdown on entries: "I like to minimize draw down as much as possible" [10:12].
- Move the stop to profit only after a higher low: "once that higher low is printed then I can move my stop loss into profit" [14:22].
- Let the New York session finish before judging: "you kind of let it play out and by the end of the day which is uh 5:00 p.m. New York" [15:23].
- Don't be greedy: "you can't just be greedy" [0:01].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- He does not state a change in this video. The trade shows the bridge between eras: the daily-timeframe Fib 61.8 entry (the core of his 2025-26 position approach), combined with the 2024 habit of a small market order plus larger pending orders, and lower-timeframe candle confirmation. The ~$70 (700-pip) target and ~$10 stop on gold are much wider than his 2024 "40-50 pip" forex stops.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Filming date unknown; prices suggest late 2024 / early 2025.
- Market-order entry price is not stated.
- "My St was 2672" is probably 2612 [?]. "I don't see getting this 12 let's do 15" is interpreted as a stop at 2615 for the 2623 limit [inferred from the $4,000 loss figure].
- The buy-stop stop loss is unclear ("stop 26 ... B could be at 2630").
- Gold pip usage is loose: in his 2024 video $1 = 10 pips, but here "10 pips below price" and "10 pips away from losing" may mean $1 or $10.
- Whether the 2623 buy limit was cancelled or kept alongside the new 10-lot limit is not fully clear; the "two pending 10 lots" and the total of ~22 lots suggest it was replaced.
- Final outcome not shown. The link to the "$40k, goal $108k" trade in TaH782wuq7Q is likely but the lot size differs (22 vs 50).
- No mention of risk % or account size.

## 15. Key quotes
- "for gold um I'm looking on a daily uh time frame" [0:01]
- "I see a Fibonacci clear as day a BC is right to 61.8" [0:01-1:02]
- "if it can close as a doji ... that's a confirmation I need to actually get in" [1:02]
- "there's a chance I may get in now with a lower lot size like I usually do" [1:02]
- "scale in once I get that extra extra extra confirmation" [2:02]
- "a potential profit of 15,000 and a potential loss of 2500 which is a 1 in5 Risk Reward ratio" [2:02]
- "if we get close to 10 or 20% Then um we're entering fully" [2:02]
- "I'm going to get a buy limit a market execution and then a buy stop to trap price" [5:11]
- "I like to minimize draw down as much as possible" [10:12]
- "the most I can make is 108,000" [12:21]
- "once that higher low is printed then I can move my stop loss into profit so now it's a complete risk- free trade" [14:22]
- "you just got to let New York session play out" [15:23]
- "the $30,000 trade went to 40,000 then it went all the way back down to 10,000" [15:23]
