# 2024-11-23 — The Day Trading Forex Strategy That Changed EVERYTHING For Me At 29 (FULL)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Q6WiIOUcR8k |
| Uploaded | 2024-11-23 |
| Length | 38:31 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2024 swing trading (key levels + Fibonacci retracement entries); he says it is essentially the same as his early-2023 strategy |

## 1. What this video covers
His first full strategy walkthrough since early 2023, recorded the day after he spoke at an FX Summit and launched his prop firm. He builds a EURUSD chart from blank: "quarter level" key levels on the weekly, shorter-term 4H levels, a Fibonacci ABC retracement, and explains his entry method (small market order between the 38.2 and 50 retracement, larger pending limit order at the 61.8). He lists the five parts of the strategy (key levels, Fibonacci, trend lines, Japanese candlesticks, moving averages / 8 EMA) and applies the same thinking to GBPAUD, GBPUSD, USDJPY and gold. It is the 2024 swing-trading version; he states that the chart work has not changed since 2023, and only his psychology and execution have changed. Despite the "day trading" title, he describes himself as a swing trader and only describes his old day-trading layout in passing.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Style:** swing trader. "I am also a swing Trader" [6:08]. He defines swing trading loosely: "swing trading doesn't necessarily mean holding for two or three weeks it just means longer than a day" [14:18].
- **Expected duration:** a EURUSD retracement move "may take you know one or two weeks" [12:13]. A GBPUSD idea is held "for a good 250 300 Pips" [35:28].
- **Swing timeframes:** weekly to get key levels and trend ("I look at the weekly just to just to get my you know key levels on there see if there's any Trends" [7:08]); daily (he switches to the daily briefly at [8:08]); 4H for shorter-term support/resistance ("these can be looked at as 4our support levels" [11:12]).
- **His old day-trading layout (for contrast):** he "wasn't analyzing on the weekly or the daily as an overall day trader"; he had the 15-minute and 2-hour charts side by side, sometimes the 4-hour [6:08-7:08]. He says the same method "still works as a day trading strategy" [6:08].
- He says he still does "a little bit of intraday trading" [7:08].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **EURUSD** is the demonstration pair: "the most heavily traded pair" and "so easy to predict in my opinion", but slow: "while GBP AUD may you know move 200 Pips in like two days EUR USD may move 200 Pips in 3 weeks" [1:04].
- Also discussed: GBPAUD, GBPUSD ("GU"), USDJPY ("UJ"), EURJPY ("EJ", "looks ugly right now" [18:24]), gold (XAUUSD).
- **Gold pip count:** "2200 to 2210 is not 10 Pips The 100 Pips gold" [10:12], i.e. he counts $1 on gold as 10 pips; $10 = 100 pips.
- **Correlations he uses:** DXY (dollar index) against gold: "if this is heading up that means the US dollar is heading up right which means gold should be heading down" [9:10]. Japanese yen futures against USDJPY: "anytime this is heading up the Japanese Yin Futures Market is heading down ... which means u.j is heading up" [19:24] (as spoken; the logic is that yen futures and USDJPY move inversely).
- **Sessions:** not covered in this video.
- **News:** see section 9.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Platforms:** TradingView for analysis ("My Sanctuary" [0:03]) and TradeLocker for execution, often side by side [1:04]. Broker named: 24K Markets (via TradeLocker); prop firm: his own ("prime time" / Trade Prime, www.tradeprime.com [?]) [16:22, 37:28].
- **Horizontal lines / rectangles:** levels drawn at quarter numbers, with the coordinate rounded ("you can click it twice go to coordinates and make it zero" [4:06]), then a rectangle drawn around the level so it is treated as a zone ("I use this as an area" [4:06]).
- **Fibonacci retracement / extension:** levels named: 38.2, 50, 61.8, 78.6 (retracement) and "27" and "61" extensions (i.e. -0.27 and -0.618 extension targets) [12:13-13:14].
- **Moving averages:** "I only have one right now the 8 EMA" [17:22]. Sometimes 8 and 16 EMA, sometimes a "longer terms EMA ... sometimes I even have a 20 or 50 EMA", "but for the most part it's usually just a simple 8 EMA" [17:22]. Rule: if the moving average is above price, the market is heading down [17:22].
- **Trend lines** and **Japanese candlesticks** (shooting star, morning star, reversal candles) [16:22, 36:28].
- **Chart style:** for day trading he liked "black charts" (monochrome candles) or Heikin Ashi so he is "robotic" and is not swayed by colour; Heikin Ashi "shows the overall trend instead of getting faked out by the candlesticks" [7:08].
- **Other:** projection/text labels on chart ("weekly resistance 1.10", "weekly support 1.05" [8:08]); TradingView alerts that text his phone [29:28]; TradingView news "lightning bolt" (ForexLive articles) [29:28].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Start from a clean chart on TradingView ("strip my charts clean and go from top down analysis" [2:04]).
2. **Weekly:** mark quarter-level key levels ("I look at the market in levels right and levels for me comes in you know quarters" [4:06]): for EURUSD 1.1000, 1.1025, 1.1050, 1.1075 and the major ones he uses here: 1.1000, 1.0750, 1.0500 [4:06-5:07]. Turn each line into a rectangle (zone). Check history to confirm the zone has been respected ("wick wick wick" [5:07]). Label weekly resistance/support.
3. **Identify the trend or consolidation** on the weekly/daily. EURUSD is "one big consolidation area" between 1.10 and 1.05 [7:08-8:08].
4. Note chart patterns (e.g. an inverted head and shoulders inside the range [10:12]) and dollar context (DXY) and news/fundamentals.
5. **Drop to the 4H** and mark shorter-term support/resistance boxes where price wicked several times [11:12-12:13].
6. **Draw a Fibonacci ABC** on the current swing to find the retracement zone (38.2-61.8) and the extension target (-0.27 / -0.618) [11:12-13:14].
7. **Look for trend lines** (only if there is a real trend; "you can try to force a trend line right here but it's too tight" [16:22]).
8. **Check candlesticks and the 8 EMA** for confirmation. "I use as many confluences as possible when it comes to making an actual bias" [17:22].
9. **Build if-then scenarios** rather than a prediction: "if the market does this then I'll do that" [36:28].
10. Set alerts at the area and let the market come to you ("I don't chase the market I let the market come to me" [19:24]).

## 6. Entry rules
- **Location:** a Fibonacci retracement of the latest leg, in the direction of his bias, ideally at or near a key level / trend line.
- **Market execution:** "I usually will Market order somewhere between the 38.2 and the 50 and just be in a little bit of draw down because I'm a swing Trader" [13:14]. Done with a **low lot size**.
- **Pending limit order:** at the 61.8 retracement, "nine times out of 10 price usually will retrace at the highest point at 61.8" [15:20]. Done with a **high lot size** because there is less drawdown at that price.
- Rule as he dictates it: "on Market executions I enter with a low lot size ... on sell limits ... pending orders I am enter with a high lot size" [13:14-14:18].
- The 78.6 is treated as the point where the idea is failing: "if it get to the 78.6 and at that point we may as well get out the trade it price usually doesn't retrace that high" [13:14].
- So there are typically **two entries** on one idea: a small market order at 38.2-50 plus a larger limit at 61.8.
- **GBPAUD example:** a pending **buy limit** at a mid key level that is also a trend-line touch (ABCD) [18:24].
- **GBPUSD example:** wait for a break below and a **retest of the trend line**, then enter, with a Japanese candlestick reversal (e.g. a shooting star) as the trigger [35:28-36:28].
- **News filter:** "if I haven't gotten in the trade yet and I see a trade looks you know beautiful but news is coming out in 20 minutes I won't get in the trade" [29:28].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Stop goes above/below the Fibonacci structure (for the EURUSD short, above the 61.8/78.6 area; "my stop loss is probably somewhere up here" [13:14], exact price shown on chart, not stated).
- With the limit entry at 61.8 the stop is "much much tighter" because the entry is closer to the stop [13:14].
- No pip size is given in this video.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Targets are Fibonacci extensions: "TP is right here this 27 or the 61 or right in the middle" [13:14] (i.e. between the -0.27 and -0.618 extensions).
- Next key level / opposite end of the range (e.g. GBPAUD back to the top of the consolidation [18:24]; EURUSD 1.0750, 1.05).
- **R:R quoted:** market execution between 38.2 and 50 gives "around one in 2.8 one and3 risk reward ratio"; the 61.8 sell limit gives "a 1 and eight risk reward ratio" [13:14].
- GBPUSD: hold "250 300 Pips" to trend-line point B, then point A [35:28].

## 9. Trade management
- **Accepting drawdown:** the market order is expected to go into drawdown before working: "how many times you guys as a Trader got in a trade and the market was in draw down for 8 hours straight and ... you just got out with a break even and then the market went exactly in your direction" [14:18]. He blames impatience, not "smart money stop hunting".
- **Partials:** in the GBPUSD scenario, if price breaks the first target level "I'm going to take partial profits and hold to here" [36:28] (percentage not stated).
- **Early full exit on reversal pattern:** "if it just reverses and gives me like a ... W or ... inverted Head and Shoulders a Morning Star anything like that then okay yeah I'm stopping right here taking all profits" [36:28].
- **News while in a trade:** "if I'm already in a trade and news is coming out like a major news announcement whether it's CPI NFP fomc anything like that I won't get out the trade unless it's completely drastic" [28:27].
- **Alerts:** "alerts are my best friends where if I feel like I'm tired or anything I will set an alert and it will text my phone when the market is coming to my area" [29:28-30:28].
- Fundamentals check: read the TradingView/ForexLive news (technical, fundamental, sentiment analysis); "it takes you five minutes" [29:28].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Lot size is varied by entry type (small on market orders, large on pending limits) [13:14].
- Grow by increasing lot size over time on a working style: "you'll see your your profits you know scale up if you just increase the lot size as time goes on" [4:06].
- **Profit expectations:** $250,000 a year puts you in the top 1%; $10,000 a month is "great" [20:24-22:25]. Prop firm math: "10% of this right and that's $25,000 of a $250,000 account ... do that four times there's your 100K" [25:26-26:26].
- **Capital:** "if you have no money you need to get ... funded"; "10% on a ... $25,000 account $100,000 account is a lot more money than 100% on a $100 account" [37:28].
- Do not trade with rent money; have another income (he babysat) [23:25-24:25].
- No risk % per trade stated in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: EURUSD short (weekly range call, past) (8:08-10:12)
- **Context:** About 1.5 months before the video he told people EURUSD was "on a collision course with 1.07 500", "heading about 300 Pips down" [8:08].
- **Result:** price reached 1.0750, retested the moving average, "and then came collapsing to 1.05" [8:08]. No entry, stop, or lot size given; this is a directional call.

### Trade 2: EURUSD short on a Fibonacci retracement (current idea) (10:12-15:20)
- **Timeframes:** weekly levels, 4H support boxes, Fib on the current leg.
- **Setup:** weekly resistance 1.1000, weekly support 1.0500, middle level 1.0750; a possible inverted head and shoulders inside the range; 4H support boxes where price wicked several times [shown on chart, not stated]. Price already retraced "all the way up to 1.06 100 flat" (a wick, "80 Pips of a push up") and came back down [11:12].
- **Bias:** a retracement up as an ABC to around 1.06 ("maybe get a little bit of strength before being false and coming back down") then continuation down [11:12]. He says he doesn't think price reaches 0.96 [9:10].
- **Entry:** market sell between the 38.2 and 50 retracement (small lot) plus a pending sell limit at the 61.8 (larger lot) [12:13-15:20].
- **Stop:** above the structure [shown on chart, not stated].
- **Target:** -0.27 / -0.618 extension or between them [13:14].
- **R:R:** ~1:2.8-1:3 on the market order, ~1:8 on the limit [13:14].
- **Duration:** "this may take you know one or two weeks" [12:13].
- **Result:** not stated (forward-looking idea).

### Trade 3: Gold (XAUUSD) fall (commentary) (9:10-10:12)
- Dollar strength after the US election (DXY rallying); gold "collapsed from 2733 all the way 2544 which is right around 2,000 Pips" [9:10-10:12]. Commentary only; no entry stated.

### Trade 4: GBPAUD long via buy limit (17:22-18:24)
- **Setup:** pair consolidating; he expects a sell-off to "this 1.29 5 area" [?] (as captioned; GBPAUD traded around 1.95 at the time, so this is probably 1.95 or a mishearing; exact level shown on chart). Two confluences: "number one a mid level right a mid key level number two a bounce off this trend line one two 3 4 a b c d" [18:24].
- **Entry:** pending buy limit at that area.
- **Expected path:** "GBP AUD comes crashing down and then ... higher low boom Off to the Races and back to the top of this consolidation area" [18:24].
- **Stop / target:** target is the top of the consolidation [shown on chart, not stated]; stop not stated.

### Trade 5: GBPUSD short, if-then plan (34:28-37:28)
- **Setup:** a down trend line (he first says there is none, then finds one) and an ABC; "Mark is heading down" [35:28].
- **Plan:** "sit on your hands": if price drops below the level and retests the trend line, then enter short; if there is a reversal candle ("shooting star anything") at the retest, enter [35:28-36:28].
- **Targets:** "hold it for a good 250 300 Pips To here point B on the trend line and then all the way down here to point a" (he doubts A is reached right now) [35:28].
- **Management:** if it breaks the first target, take partials and hold to the next; if it reverses with a W / inverted H&S / morning star, close everything [36:28]. If it bounces and hesitates, trade it back up (the opposite scenario) [36:28].

### Trade 6: USDJPY / EURJPY (commentary) (18:24-19:24)
- UJ "pushing straight up"; EJ "looks ugly"; yen futures used as confluence for UJ. No setup details.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Stick to the one style that works: "completely eradicate that other style and just focus on the one that's working" [3:04-4:06].
- Treat levels as zones: "I use this as an area" [4:06].
- Small size on market orders, big size on limits: "on Market executions I enter with a low lot size ... pending orders I am enter with a high lot size" [13:14-14:18].
- Accept drawdown on swing entries: "you guys were not patient enough this is why I adapted to the swing trading mindset" [14:18].
- Use multiple confluences: "I use as many confluences as possible when it comes to making an actual bias" [17:22].
- Don't force trend lines: "you can try to force a trend line right here but it's too tight" [16:22].
- Don't chase: "I don't chase the market I let the market come to me" [19:24].
- No entries right before news: "news is coming out in 20 minutes I won't get in the trade" [29:28].
- Don't exit on news unless drastic: "I won't get out the trade unless it's completely drastic" [28:27].
- Think in if-then terms: "talk in terms of if the market does this then I'll do that" [37:28].
- Get funded if undercapitalized: "if you have no money you need to get ... funded" [37:28].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- **Chart work unchanged since 2023:** "with my strategy nothing has really changed ... the things that are change really overall ... is the overall psychology behind it all and the execution behind it all" [3:04].
- **2020 vs 2023 vs 2024:** "you guys can compare this video to the 2023 video to the 2020 video ... most of it is is very very similar this is the only one where it's more ... intraday the day trading the 2020 strategy but 2023 is more or less the same as now" [26:26].
- **Day trader to swing trader:** as a day trader he used the 15m and 2H (sometimes 4H), not the weekly/daily [6:08-7:08]; now he analyses weekly/daily/4H. He moved to swing trading because traders (including him) closed trades at break-even after hours of drawdown: "this is why I adapted to the swing trading mindset" [14:18].
- **Predictions:** "you can't make predictions on the market get away from that I I've done that my first three or four years" [36:28].
- Says he took nine courses and tried smart money concepts; "it didn't work for me" [26:26].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Quarter levels as captioned ("110 11225 uh 2505 11725" [4:06]) are garbled; interpreted as 1.1000 / 1.1025 / 1.1050 / 1.1075.
- GBPAUD level "1.29 5" is probably a mishearing (GBPAUD was ~1.95 in Nov 2024) [?].
- Stop-loss placement and all target prices for the EURUSD and GBPUSD ideas are shown on chart, not stated. No pip stop size given.
- The "27 or the 61" extension targets are interpreted as -0.27 and -0.618 Fibonacci extensions.
- "Prime time" / "trade prime.com" is presumably his prop firm brand (Trade Prime) [?].

- The title says "day trading" but the content is his swing method.
- No risk % per trade, no win rate, no session timing in this video.

## 15. Key quotes

# 2024-08-01 — 42 Mins of The BEST PSYCHOLOGY Lessons From All TRADING Books

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3EJFLAOsGzg |
| Uploaded | 2024-08-01 |
| Length | 42:24 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 1:02-3:04; 11:10-13:11; 23:20-24:20) |
| Strategy era | 2024 swing trading (fixed 1:3 R:R) |

## 1. What this video covers
He reads 20 (plus one bonus) psychology lessons from trading books, mostly "Trading in the Zone" (Mark Douglas), "Market Wizards" (the Marcus story), and "The Forex Mindset" (Jared Martinez), and comments on each. The strategy-relevant parts are short. He gives his win rate, says his R:R is always 1:3 and he takes every 1:3 setup, lists what a written trading plan must contain, and says to check the downside before every trade (never risk more than 2/5/10%). He also stresses committing to an exit and stop on every trade, being selective, the if-then mindset, and cutting a trade that violently reverses. This reflects his 2024 swing-trading era.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
Not covered in this video (beyond the general rules below).

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- No specific pairs or sessions for his own trading. EURUSD is used only as a hypothetical (price at 1.08-1.09) [23:20-24:22].
- A trading plan must list "the pairs you only trade the time frames you trade on the sessions you trade on what news affects the certain pairs" [12:10-13:10].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
Not covered in this video. (He says technicals drive timing and price: "you need to be a technical Trader" [3:03]; "technicals run it all" [17:14].)

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Not covered in this video. Relevant principle: fundamentals matter, but technical analysis is needed "for the timing of trade" [16:14-17:14]. Use an if-then mindset: "if the market does this then I would do that" [35:37-36:39].

## 6. Entry rules
- **Take every valid 1:3 setup:** "my risk to word ratio no matter what is 1 to three if I see a 1 to three setup I'm going to trade it" [11:10].
- **Be selective:** wait for trades "in which all the key elements line up in One Direction"; marginal trades are "more to do with entertainment than trading success" (Market Wizards quote, endorsed) [15:11-16:14].
- **Trust your own bias** over your community or mentor [8:09-9:10].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- **Always have an exit / stop:** commit "to an exit point on every trade"; "protective stops like stop losses are very important because they force this commitment on the trader" [13:10-14:11].
- **Cut losers that violently reverse:** don't "breathe the sigh of relief" on small pullbacks; "sometimes you may want to just cut your losses cuz you know it's a loser because ... it violently turned against you" [27:25-28:28]. He accepts the alternative of "just letting it hit your stop loss" [27:25].
- Liquidate positions to regain mental clarity when losing and confused (Market Wizards, endorsed) [14:11].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Fixed **1:3 R:R** [11:10]. No pip targets in this video.

## 9. Trade management
- Cut losers early when the market violently reverses through your entry (see section 7) [27:25-28:28].
- **Withdraw and reward yourself:** "every time you make money I don't care if it's $30 take 12 out and go buy something with it" [33:34].
- No break-even/partial rules in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Win rate:** "I want to say I have like a 80 or maybe even a 70% win" [1:02] (as captioned, uncertain). He uses a webinar attendee's "65%" as the worked example: "you take 10 trades you're going to win six ... or six and a half seven" [2:03]; "I can lose the next five trades but out of 20 that's my 35%" [2:03].
- **R:R:** always 1:3 [11:10].
- **Max risk / check the downside:** before entering, ask "how much can I lose if I use a standard lot if I use a two lot if I use a three lot am I going to blow my account ... am I not risking more than 10% more than 5% more than 2% if I'm not doing that place the trade" [23:20]. "There should be no reason you should be blowing your account" [23:20].
- **Money you can afford to lose:** trade only with money that has "little or no value in your life" [28:28]; he funded his early trading with $4-6k/month from babysitting [29:29].
- **Written trading plan required:** a document with "a detailed list on what you're looking for in terms of setups how many Pips you're looking for the pairs you only trade the time frames you trade on the sessions you trade on what news affects the certain pairs ... and like if I if this happens I do this and like outline your psychology in certain situations" [12:10-13:10]. "Metrics are your best friend" [13:10].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
No trades walked through. (Hypotheticals only: a book author's position that went from +$67k to +$35k under his rules [9:10-11:10]; a sell that reverses violently through entry [26:24-28:28]; a far-away EURUSD order at 2.00 or 0.50 to illustrate "you win or you lose" [23:20-24:22].)

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Think in probabilities: "you take 10 trades you're going to win six" [2:03].
- Fixed R:R: "my risk to word ratio no matter what is 1 to three if I see a 1 to three setup I'm going to trade it" [11:10].
- Written rules: "if you don't have any written rules for making a trade then everything about that trade is a mistake" [12:10].
- Track everything: "metrics are your best friend" [13:10].
- Commit to an exit: "committing to an exit point on every trade" [13:10-14:11].
- Be selective: wait for "all the key elements line up in One Direction" [15:11].
- Check the downside: "am I not risking more than 10% more than 5% more than 2%" [23:20].
- Cut violent reversals: "no more sigh reliefs cut your losses" [27:25].
- If-then mindset: "if the market does this then I would do that" [36:39].
- Withdraw profits: "every time you make money ... take 12 out and go buy something with it" [33:34].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- "just 7 eight years ago I was blown accounts" [23:20]; now he checks the downside before every trade. No other strategy changes stated.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Win-rate wording is unclear: "like a 80 or maybe even a 70% win" [1:02]; the classification summary reads it as ~65-70%, but 65% is the webinar attendee's number, which he uses as an example.
- The risk ceiling is phrased as a sequence ("not more than 10% more than 5% more than 2%"), not a single fixed %. The classification summary's "2-5%" is the strictest reading.
- No setups, pairs, timeframes, or pip numbers for his own trades.
- Most content is quoted from books (Trading in the Zone, Market Wizards, The Forex Mindset by Jared Martinez), with his endorsement.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I want to say I have like a 80 or maybe even a 70% win" [1:02]
- "you take 10 trades you're going to win six basically" [2:03]
- "my risk to word ratio no matter what is 1 to three if I see a 1 to three setup I'm going to trade it" [11:10]
- "if you don't have any written rules for making a trade then everything about that trade is a mistake" [12:10]
- "the pairs you only trade the time frames you trade on the sessions you trade on what news affects the certain pairs" [12:10-13:10]
- "metrics are your best friend" [13:10]
- "protective stops like stop losses are very important because they force this commitment on the trader" [14:11]
- "am I not risking more than 10% more than 5% more than 2% if I'm not doing that place the trade" [23:20]
- "sometimes you may want to just cut your losses cuz you know it's a loser" [27:25]
- "if the market does this then I would do that" [36:39]

# 2024-07-25 — 10 Years of Brutal Trading Advice in 47 Mins (NONSTOP HONESTY)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WZvdQah_hyI |
| Uploaded | 2024-07-25 |
| Length | 47:43 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 5:06-8:07; 28:22-29:22) |
| Strategy era | 2024 swing trading (40-50 pip stops, 185-250 pip targets, 1:3-1:4 R:R) |

## 1. What this video covers
A list of "brutally honest" lessons from his first decade, mostly about discipline, mindset, money goals, and his businesses. The strategy-relevant parts are short. He says small stop losses don't work long term and that he now uses 40-50 pip stops with 185-250 pip targets (1:3-1:4) as a swing trader. He also says you don't need to trade every day, week, or month, and that swing trading is less stressful than day trading (one to three trades every two weeks, held a week or two). He adds that risk management and psychology matter more than technicals. This reflects his 2024 swing-trading version.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Style:** "I am a swing Trader" [6:07]. "I have switched from day trading to swing trading" [14:14]. "even though I swing trade now I don't day trade" [15:14].
- **Holding period:** "make the same exact trade but swing trade it and hold it for a week or two weeks and then get out" [24:18-25:20].
- **Frequency:** "focus on one two or three trades every two weeks versus one two three trades every single day" [25:20].
- **Timeframes:** not covered in this video.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- No specific pairs or sessions given. One example mentions preparing for Brexit (a GBP event) hypothetically [7:07].
- **Calendar:** "I don't trade near Christmas"; people treat roughly "December 9th through January 9th" as not a trading month [8:08-9:09].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
Not covered in this video.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Not covered in this video. (He says smart money concepts "works" but "I don't like trading that way I like trading the way I trade" [3:05]; he lists styles: price action, SMC, EAs, indicator-based, naked trading [4:05].)

## 6. Entry rules
Not covered in this video.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- **Wide stops, 40-50 pips:** "my experience I've seen having stop losses like 40 and 50 pip stop losses give you the breathing room you need because again I am a swing Trader" [6:07].
- **Small stops fail over years:** "small stop losses do not work over a multi-year track record" [4:05]. A small stop "get you out way earlier than you're supposed to"; the market "retrace a little bit seven Pips five Pips stopped you out and then came crashing down your analysis was right" [5:06].
- Stop size only matters relative to target: "all that matters is your risk reward ratio" [6:07]. Example: a 200-pip stop with a 1,000-pip target (preparing for Brexit) "is a good trade that's a one in5" [6:07-7:07].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- "my take profit is always like 200 Pips 250 225 185 right so it's always a 1 to three to four risk reward ratio" [6:07].

## 9. Trade management
- He contrasts traders who "know when to walk away": "if they lose a few trades they know when to walk away if they win a couple trades they know when to keep going or stop for the day or for the week" [28:22-29:22].
- Step away after losses: professionals know "if they lose a few trades to step away from the market" [2:03].
- No break-even/partial rules in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **R:R:** 1:3 to 1:4 standard [6:07].
- **Risk %:** professionals "keep their risk under a certain percentage" [2:03] (no number given). Bad example: traders "risking 30 40 50%" [28:22].
- **Leverage:** "you have too high of a leverage you risk entirely way too much" listed as a core problem [1:02].
- **Priority:** "someone who has their psychology and risk management down packed ... but has iffy technicals is going to be so much better far off than someone who is a technical Master" [28:22].
- **Account size:** don't try to flip $10/$100 accounts; save "two3 $4,000" for an account or get a prop account [22:17-24:18].
- **Trading breaks:** "every single day is not a trading day every single week is not a trading week and every single month ... is not a trading month" [7:07]. He might "trade for three weeks straight and then ... take two weeks off one week off" [7:07]; or "trade for four months straight and then take two months off" [8:08]. Breaks should be a week, two weeks, or a month, not many months [35:23].
- **Goal-based stopping:** set a goal (e.g. $10,000 in a day); when you reach it, "do not try to trade the next day or day after that like stop celebrate" [30:22-31:22].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
No trades walked through. (Only the hypothetical 200-pip stop / 1,000-pip target Brexit example [6:07-7:07].)

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Wide stops: "having stop losses like 40 and 50 pip stop losses give you the breathing room you need" [6:07].
- Targets 185-250 pips: "my take profit is always like 200 Pips 250 225 185" [6:07].
- R:R over stop size: "all that matters is your risk reward ratio" [6:07].
- Don't trade every day: "you do not need to trade every single day" [7:07].
- Skip the holidays: "I don't trade near Christmas" [9:09].
- Trust your own analysis over the community: "always use trust your own analysis no matter what" [34:23].
- Stop after hitting a goal: "when you get that do not try to trade the next day" [31:22].
- Don't flip tiny accounts: "there's no chance in the world you are going to flip a $10 account to $10 million" [21:16-22:17].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- **Stops:** "I was somebody who had small stop loss in 2014 2015 I had the 10 pip stop losses the five pip stop losses the I listened to people out there who said if you have more than a 20 pip stop loss you don't know how to trade I did all of that" [6:07]. Now 40-50 pips.
- **Day trading to swing trading:** "I have switched from day trading to swing trading" [14:14]. He admits "you didn't get rich from swing trading you got rich from day trading that's a fact" [24:18]. Day trading suited him when he had "no wife no kids no Mansion no money" and could be on the charts all day; with family and businesses, swing trading the "same exact trade" held a week or two is "so so much less stressful" [24:18-25:20].
- **Future:** he might later stop day-to-day trading and just hold longer-term investments [14:14-15:14]. This foreshadows his 2025 move to position trading.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- No pairs, timeframes, entry triggers, or tools are given.
- The risk % threshold is not stated ("under a certain percentage").
- "1 to three to four" is read as 1:3 to 1:4. With a 40-50 pip stop and a 185-250 pip target, the actual ratio would be about 1:3.7-1:6, so the numbers are approximate.

## 15. Key quotes
- "small stop losses do not work over a multi-year track record" [4:05]
- "I had the 10 pip stop losses the five pip stop losses" [6:07]
- "having stop losses like 40 and 50 pip stop losses give you the breathing room you need because again I am a swing Trader" [6:07]
- "my take profit is always like 200 Pips 250 225 185 right so it's always a 1 to three to four risk reward ratio" [6:07]
- "all that matters is your risk reward ratio" [6:07]
- "you do not need to trade every single day" [7:07]
- "I have switched from day trading to swing trading" [14:14]
- "hold it for a week or two weeks and then get out" [25:20]
- "focus on one two or three trades every two weeks versus one two three trades every single day" [25:20]
- "someone who has their psychology and risk management down packed ... but has iffy technicals is going to be so much better" [28:22]

# 2024-01-01 — 85 MINS OF THE BEST FOREX ADVICE (Trading Study Compilation for 2025)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mvGXdxGXUu8 |
| Uploaded | 2024-01-01 |
| Length | ~84:30 (approx.; last caption marker [83:59]) |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 9:09-11:11; 17:15-27:24; 32:27-33:27; 40:31-42:32; 46:34-47:34; 50:36-52:37; 63:46-66:47) |
| Strategy era | Mixed compilation. Most strategy clips are from 2022-2023 (2023 swing: Asian-session market entries, scale-ins, 40-70 pip stops). The stop-to-structure tutorial clips look older (2020-21 style teaching on MetaTrader) [?]. Business/podcast clips are from 2021-2023. |

> **Compilation warning.** This video stitches together clips from different videos and years. Several clips are word-for-word repeats of other videos:
> - [9:09]-[11:11] small-account lot scaling, [27:24]-[31:26] emotions/plan checklist and [71:55]-[72:55] "what is your strategy" are from **8ub5s9-Cd-A (2023-09-09)**.
> - [17:15]-[21:19] (enter immediately, Sept 9/10 record, alerts, second and third entries) is from **0yoVaP_NTFA (2023-12-20)**. That clip itself refers to September/October 2023.
> - Some clips are interviews/podcasts where **someone else is speaking** (e.g. [11:11]-[12:11], [13:13]-[15:15] and [69:52]-[70:53] include guest voices). The "close at 150k floating profit" rule at [11:11]-[12:11] is the guest's. At [59:44] Swaggy attributes it to Q Banks.
>
> Clip boundaries are not marked in the captions, so the year of each clip is inferred from what is said.

## 1. What this video covers
This is an 85-minute New Year compilation mixing motivation, business/podcast talk and strategy clips. The strategy content covers:
- stacking confluences on a gold short (trend line, wicks to the trend line and to the 1950 psych level, a 2H lower high, an MA crossover)
- scaling a small account by slowly raising lot size
- taking profit when price is within a few pips of TP
- the 2023 routine of entering at market immediately, adding a lighter second entry near the stop and a much bigger third entry once there is a lower high
- moving the stop only to market structure (below the last higher low), or to break-even when 40-50 pips up
- market execution plus a buy/sell limit as the second entry
- risk of 2-4% (5-8% aggressive, he sometimes goes to 9%), 1:3+ R:R and win-rate maths
- wider stops (40-70 pips) aiming at 140-230 pips
- journaling and changing bias when the market disagrees

Overall it reflects his 2023 swing approach. The structure-based stop tutorial may come from his earlier teaching.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Swing trader who accepts drawdown: "me as a swing trader, being in drawdown does not scare me at all" [40:31].
- Typical stops: "my stop losses are usually 40, 50, 60 pips because I aim for 180, 200, 230 take profits" [26:24], and "chances are my stop loss is probably going to be like 50 pips, 60 pips, 70 pips" [40:31].
- Hold for 1-3 days: give the stop "an extra 20, 25, 30 pips ... and move the take profit to an area where you can hold it over a span of 1, 2, 3 days" [51:37].
- Timeframes mentioned: 2-hour (used to spot a lower high on gold) [9:09], and daily ("my charts right now ... on a daily timeframe") in the journaling clip [74:56].
- Scalpers with 7-10 pip stops can't use his drawdown/limit approach [40:31].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Gold (XAUUSD) is the main example throughout. He also mentions USDJPY ("UJ"), EURJPY ("EJ", a "$25,000 in one day" video), GBPAUD and GBPUSD ("GU").
- Gold pip convention: the take-profit clip treats 1933 → 1947 as 140 pips (1 point = 10 pips) [16:15-17:15]. He notes "the calculation of gold is different" [17:15].
- Session: in the repeated routine clip, entries are made before London. He mocks traders who say "it should move during London session" and go play basketball, then miss the move [41:32].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- One moving average ("I use just one, a moving average") [72:55]. Period not stated. A "moving average crossover" is listed as a confluence in the gold example [9:09], which implies two MAs [?] (8/14 EMA per the 2023 era; **not stated here**).
- Psychological levels and key levels ("very very very important") [72:55]. Example: gold 1950.
- Trend lines, and counting wicks into a trend line and a psych level [9:09].
- Market structure (higher highs/higher lows, lower highs) [21:19-25:24, 63:46-66:47].
- Fibonacci retracement: drawn once price pulls back and turns up again, while trailing the stop [22:21, 24:23]. Levels are not specified.
- TradingView alerts [20:18]. TradingView news feed ("lightning bolt") read about an hour Mon-Wed [71:55].
- MetaTrader for moving the stop [22:21]. MT4 mentioned [53:37].
- Journaling/tracking: notes app, notebook, later journaling software. He credits it with becoming profitable [73:56-74:56].
- No order blocks or "complex terms" [71:55].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Pieced together from the clips:
1. Read the news/sentiment (TradingView news feed) so the technicals don't contradict the fundamentals [71:55-72:55].
2. Draw the trend line and count wicks into it, and into the nearest psychological level (e.g. 1950 on gold) [9:09].
3. Drop to the 2-hour chart to look for a lower high (short) [9:09].
4. Check for a moving-average crossover [9:09].
5. Count confluences. He is happy to execute with 2-3. Waiting for 4-5+ is a psychology problem [5:06, 33:28, 39:31-40:31].
6. Check against the plan: 1:3 R:R (1:2 / 1:4 depending on the plan) and 2-3 confluences [30:26-31:26].
7. Be ready to flip bias if the market disagrees ("UJ is going down ... a day later ... UJ is going up") [72:55].
8. Journal every trade and review on the chart why it won or lost [73:56-74:56].

## 6. Entry rules
- **Market-execute early with 2-3 confluences.** "If I got two or three and I feel like ... the market is not ready to move yet but I know what it's going to do, sometimes I for sure will just market execute for my first trade" [40:31].
- **Second trade as a limit order.** "Then my second trade, what I will do is put a sell limit if I think it's going to go up a little bit, retrace and then come back down, or place a buy limit if I think it's going to come back down and then go up" [40:31]. So he is often in two trades that are close together, or three with one further away [41:32].
- **Routine version (repeat of 0yoVaP_NTFA)** [17:15-21:19]:
  1. Market entry now so he doesn't miss the move.
  2. A TradingView alert near the stop. If price reverses at the psych level, add a lighter second entry, "whether it's half the lot size".
  3. After a new lower high, move the stop there and add "a way bigger lot size".
- **Don't enter late.** Losing traders get in "eight confluences late ... as opposed to getting in on the second or third confluence" [5:06]. Don't chase. Example: gold 1897 → 1917, "the old me would have tried to get in at 1913" [30:26].
- **Entering too early can also fail.** See Trade 4 (USDJPY).

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Typical stop size is 40-70 pips (see section 2). Give trades "room to breathe". A 5-10 pip stop is "one of the biggest issues that I see traders face daily". Add "an extra 20, 25, 30 pips" [50:36-51:37].
- **Trail only to structure** [21:19-25:24]. Once price retraces and turns back in your direction, move the stop "a few pips below" the last higher low (longs) [23:23]. For shorts, above the last lower high [65:47]. "You can't just move it anywhere because price can easily come up and then stop you back out; it's not in alignment with structure" [23:23-24:23].
- **Keep the stop where it started until structure forms** [63:46-64:46]. "Keep your stop loss in the negatives, like wherever it was where you started, until price creates structure."
- **Break-even exception.** If you are 40-50 pips in profit and price starts retracing, "you can at least move your stop loss into break-even, which is your entry" [64:46]. Then move from break-even to below the new higher low once price plants and turns up again.
- Why: if a structure-based stop is hit, "it is no longer an uptrend" [64:46-65:47]. Every trade can't be risk-free right away, "you'll get stopped out all the time" [65:47].
- In the routine clip: stop at 1961 on a 1943 gold short. Moved to the new lower high to make positions risk-free [19:16-21:19].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Target sizes: 180/200/230 pips with 40-60 pip stops [26:24]. In one example he was 140+ pips in profit after 30 pips of drawdown on gold ("like a 1 to 5") [50:36].
- **Take profit when price is close to TP.** "If the market is two, three, four pips or 40 pips [on gold] ... take profit if it's close to it" [16:15]. Example: TP 1950, price at 1947, entry 1933. Don't walk away and let it reverse into a loss. "The market gave you 140 plus pips, take the profit" [17:15].
- Partial profit at a psychological level or "mid level" is mentioned but not explained ("this is not the video for partial profit") [24:23].
- Gold short example target 1910/1900 [20:18].

## 9. Trade management
- Set and forget after entering. Alerts near the stop (1950/1952 on the gold example) [20:18].
- Scaling in: lighter second entry in drawdown (≈half size), much bigger third entry after the stop moves to a new lower high [20:18-21:19]. Or market entry plus a limit-order second entry [40:31].
- Trail the stop below each new higher low (above each lower high for shorts), drawing a Fibonacci retracement at each pullback [22:21-24:23, 64:46-65:47].
- Break-even when 40-50 pips up and price retraces [64:46].
- Close near TP rather than waiting the last few pips [16:15-17:15].
- Partials at psych/mid levels (no % given) [24:23].
- Guest clip [11:11-12:11] (not Swaggy): close a floating profit once it passes a written goal ("150 plus close").

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Risk % per trade** [46:34-47:34]: "if you want a risk number, 2 to 4% is fine, 5 to 8% is aggressive." He doesn't stick to 1-2%: "sometimes I'll risk three or four. If the trade is amazing I'll even risk 5 to 8%, even 9%". He says this is "not the norm" and not what you should do. Example: on a $100,000 account he may lose $6,000-$8,000 on a trade, but winners are $30,000-$40,000, "so I can lose two or three and then have two winners and I'm golden".
- **R:R**: "always trade with the 1 to 3 plus risk reward ratio, 1 to 2 if you have to" [45:34-46:34].
- **Win rate**: "you can have a 30% win rate and still be profitable ... is it a 1 to 4, 1 to 5, 1 to 6" [32:27].
- **Small-account scaling** [9:09-11:11] (repeat of 8ub5s9): $300 account at 0.04 lots → +$150 to $450 → 0.05-0.07 lots toward $700. No standard lot on a small account ("all it takes is 40 pips and your account is blown"). The captions here say "0.004/0.005", a mishearing of 0.04/0.05.
- **Don't up size after a win streak** [25:24-27:25]. Lab members went 6/6 at small size ($10-40 wins at 0.10 lots), then jumped to 0.60 lots on trade 7 (USDJPY). With a 60-pip stop that cost $360 and blew small accounts. "Who told you to add a higher lot size and not 0.01 or 0.04?"
- Don't up size to copy big days: "you shouldn't look at it as I'mma up my lot size just to get to that 25k in a day" [32:27].
- Never blow an account after year 3-4 of trading. That is "really reckless", risking the whole account on one or two trades [33:28].
- Funded accounts: a $50k-100k account lets you use bigger size ("standard lot, 50 [0.50?]") and be happy with $900 or $1,800 wins [49:36-50:36]. Prop firms are "by far the easiest way" to become profitable [61:45-62:46].
- Track-record claims: 6/6 in the lab and up about $110k floating [1:03]. Sept 2023: 9/10 [18:16]. 7 of the last 8 trades [25:24].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: XAUUSD short, confluence stacking (around [8:09]-[9:09])
- Context: people in his community said there were "no confluences" to get into gold around 1937 [captions also say "1930"; level uncertain].
- His confluences: (1) a trend line coming down; (2) "one, two, three, four wicks to the trend line"; (3) a wick to the major psych level at 1950; (4) on the 2-hour chart, a lower high; (5) a moving-average crossover. He counts "five confluences".
- Direction: implied short (descending trend line, rejection wicks at 1950, lower high). Not explicitly stated.
- Entry, stop and target not stated in this clip. Result not stated.

### Trade 2: XAUUSD long, take profit near TP (around [16:15]-[17:15])
- Entry 1933, TP 1950. Price reached 1947 (about 140 pips of profit). Point: close it instead of waiting the last 3 points. Otherwise it "tanks all the way back down" below the entry. Illustrative. No actual result given.

### Trade 3: XAUUSD short, routine example (around [17:15]-[21:19]; same as 0yoVaP_NTFA)
- Market sell at 1943, stop 1961, target 1910/1900.
- Expected drawdown to 1948-1950. TradingView alert at 1950/1952. If price reverses off the 1950 psych level with a wick, add a lighter second entry (≈half size).
- After price breaks back below 1943 and prints a lower high, move the stop to that lower high and add a much larger third entry at about 1934. Illustrative.

### Trade 4: USDJPY long, loss (around [25:24]-[27:25])
- Context: "trade number seven" in the lab after a 6/6 run (7 of the last 8 won).
- Setup: price in consolidation "for a very very long time", cleared the high, came back and retested, a new trend line, a key level he did not want broken, a wick. "All the signs of bullish momentum". He still thinks it is bullish long term.
- Entry: "I got in at the wrong time". He entered early, expecting a drawdown to the trend line before a move up [level shown on chart, not stated].
- Result: price "just cleared through" and stopped him out. Stop size not stated; the lot-size example uses a 60-pip stop.
- Lesson: members who raised lot size on this trade blew their accounts.

### Trade 5: XAUUSD (direction not stated), 30 pips drawdown → 140+ pips (around [50:36])
- He was 30 pips in drawdown, then 140+ pips in profit. That is a realized ~1:5. "Not even including GU" (he also had a GBPUSD position). No levels given.

### Trade 6: XAUUSD long, not taken (around [29:26]-[30:26]; repeat of 8ub5s9)
- Called a gold rally from about 1897 in his free Telegram. It went to 1917 (200 pips). He didn't enter and warns against chasing at 1913.

### Example: illustrative long, stop trailing ($ example) (around [21:19]-[24:23])
- Entry at break-even, risk $500, target $2,500 (1:5). Price goes up to about +$900-1,000, retraces to about +$500, then turns up. Draw a Fibonacci retracement and move the stop a few pips below that new higher low. Worst case is now about +$600. Repeat at each higher low. A possible partial at a psych/mid level.

### Example: illustrative uptrend, stop to structure / break-even (around [63:46]-[66:47])
- Keep the original stop until a higher low forms. If 40-50 pips up and retracing, move to break-even. After price plants and turns up, trail below the new higher low. Mirror image for shorts. A break of the last higher low means the trend is over.

### Mentioned only: EURJPY, "$25,000 in one day" (around [32:27])
- He refers to an earlier video. It "was days of preparing, like a week of monitoring". No details.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Execute on 2-3 confluences: "worry about why you get in ... eight confluences late ... as opposed to getting in on the second or third confluence" [5:06].
- Market-execute plus a limit: "I will just market execute for my first trade and then my second trade ... put a sell limit ... or place a buy limit" [40:31].
- Don't miss the move: "I don't want to miss the trade, so me as a swing trader being in drawdown does not scare me at all" [40:31].
- Take profit when near TP: "take profit if it's close to it" [16:15].
- Second entry lighter: "that second position is a little bit lighter even though it's the better entry" [20:18].
- Trail to structure only: "your stop has to be below the last higher low" [24:23].
- Don't trail too early: "keep your stop loss in the negatives ... until price creates structure" [63:46].
- Break-even when well up: "if you are 40, 50 pips in profit you don't want to ... lose money" [64:46].
- Wider stops: "give your trade some room to breathe" [51:37].
- R:R: "always trade with the 1 to 3 plus risk reward ratio, 1 to 2 if you have to" [45:34].
- Risk: "2 to 4% is fine, 5 to 8% is aggressive" [46:34].
- Don't raise size after a winning streak: "who told you to add a higher lot size?" [26:24].
- Scale small accounts slowly: "slowly raise the lot size every now and then" [10:11].
- Change bias when the market disagrees: "it's okay to change your bias" [72:55].
- Journal: "I wasn't profitable until my fourth year and a half because I wasn't tracking and journaling" [73:56].
- Don't try to flip tiny accounts: "stop trying to turn a $20 account into $2,000" [38:30].
- Check the news: "to make sure I am not trading based off the technicals alone" [71:55].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- He found a 2016-2017 video of himself trading and says "6 years later, 7 years later I still trade the same way" [42:32-43:33]. (This clip is probably from about 2022-2023.)
- He became profitable only after he started journaling and tracking (about year 4.5). Before that he used notes, Snapchat screenshots and a red notebook [73:56-74:56].
- Earlier in his career he would plan a trade, walk away and miss the move. Entering at market immediately fixes that [17:15-18:16].
- He used to blow accounts trying to flip $20-$100 accounts (2014-2015) [38:30-39:31].
- He once was a day trader and then stopped, before the $5,000 TV-money account that he scaled up (goal: $1,300/month for bills) [3:04-4:05].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- **Clip dating.** The compilation doesn't mark sources or years. The stop-to-structure tutorials ([21:19]-[25:24], [63:46]-[66:47]) use MetaTrader and promise later videos on partials, so they may come from 2020-2021 [?]. The lab/USDJPY clip and the recession/"stay liquid" clip are probably 2022-2023. The confluence clip references MyForexFunds' troubles, so it is probably late 2023.
- **Guest voices.** Interview segments ([11:11]-[12:11], [13:13]-[15:15], [69:52]-[70:53]) include other traders. The "withdraw at least $100,000 a week" and "150 plus close" statements are the guest's (Q Banks per [59:44]), not Swaggy's.
- MA period/type isn't stated. "Moving average crossover" suggests two MAs, but it is not defined here.
- In the gold confluence clip, the price ("1937" vs "1930") and the direction are not stated outright.
- Lot sizes: "0.004/0.005" in the captions = 0.04/0.05 (confirmed by 8ub5s9). "A 10 to a 601" = 0.10 to 0.60 lots, consistent with $10 per 10 pips and $60 per 10 pips, and the $360 loss on a 60-pip stop. He first misspoke "3.6k" and corrected it to $360.
- "Standard lot 50" [50:36] is unclear (0.50 lots? 50 lots?).
- Fibonacci levels for the trailing method aren't specified. The partial % isn't specified.
- The gold routine example's "1:4" isn't consistent with its numbers (about 1:2), as noted in the 0yoVaP_NTFA write-up.

## 15. Key quotes
- "One confluence is there's a trend line coming all the way down ... a wick to the trend line and a wick to the psych level ... we scale onto the 2-hour timeframe ... we got this lower high, that's four confluences, moving average crossover, that's five" [9:09]
- "You can't really get into big trades with a small account unless you slowly scale that account and slowly raise the lot size" [10:11]
- "Take profit if it's close to it" [16:15]
- "That second position is a little bit lighter even though it's the better entry" [20:18]
- "I'll move my stop loss into where that lower high is now, it's a risk-free trade, and then I'll enter with a way bigger lot size" [21:19]
- "Structure means if higher highs and higher lows are being created in an uptrend, your stop has to be below the last higher low" [24:23]
- "My stop losses are usually 40, 50, 60 pips because I aim for 180, 200, 230 take profits" [26:24]
- "You can have a 30% win rate and still be profitable" [32:27]
- "I will just market execute for my first trade and then my second trade what I will do is put a sell limit ... or place a buy limit" [40:31]
- "Always trade with the 1 to 3 plus risk reward ratio, 1 to 2 if you have to" [45:34-46:34]
- "If you want a risk number, 2 to 4% is fine, 5 to 8% is aggressive" [46:34]
- "Give your trade some room to breathe ... an extra 20, 25, 30 pips of a stop loss" [51:37]
- "Keep your stop loss in the negatives, like wherever it was where you started, until price creates structure" [63:46]
- "I wasn't profitable until my fourth year and a half because I wasn't tracking and journaling" [73:56]
- "I don't use too many indicators, I use just one, a moving average, I use psychological levels ... and I trade trend lines" [71:55-72:55]

# 2023-12-20 — I'm a Multi-Millionaire By Following This Exact Schedule... (My Daily Routine)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0yoVaP_NTFA |
| Uploaded | 2023-12-20 |
| Length | ~11:30 (approx.; last caption marker [11:13]) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2023 swing trading with Asian-session entries (recorded around autumn 2023: he cites "last month of September") |

## 1. What this video covers
He walks through his trading routine. He starts his day around the Asian session (about 7:00 pm US Eastern), reviews a core watch list of four pairs, and enters swing trades between 7:30 and 9:30 pm ET. He enters early, at market, so he does not miss the move, and he accepts drawdown. Then he walks away and relies on stop loss, take profit and TradingView alerts. If price reaches a better level near his stop he adds a smaller second position. Once price makes a new lower high (on a sell) he moves the stop there, so the trade is risk-free, and adds a third, larger position. He takes profit around the New York session or trails the stop and holds for 2-7 days. This is his 2023 swing / Asian-session version. He contrasts it with his 2020 "sniper entry" style.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- He calls himself a swing trader who "can afford to be in drawdown" [2:06].
- Holding period: "sometimes these moves may take two, three, four, five, six, seven days" [9:11]. His most recent trade was entered on a Monday and stayed in drawdown until Wednesday, then went into big profit.
- He does not chase "sniper entries" anymore: "I don't care about sniper entries, right, I did in 2020" [8:11]. He goes for "the most pips" and tries not to miss trades.
- No chart timeframes are named in this video.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Watch list: "I only like have four on my watch list each week that I'm taking seriously" [2:06]. There are usually 8 or 9 pairs on the list in total. The other 4 or 5 serve as confluences for the four main pairs. If one main pair "looks ugly" he swaps in another one.
- Pairs named: gold (XAUUSD), GBPAUD, GBPUSD, AUDUSD, and sometimes EURJPY [1:04-2:06].
- Session: his day starts around the Asian session. He comes in around 7:00 pm, sometimes doing a webinar in his paid group ("the lab").
- Entry window: "I usually enter my trades from between 7:30 and 9:30 at night, sometimes 10:30 ... I'm talking Eastern Standard Time" [7:10].
- Why Asian: liquidity is lowest, but "I find better setups that way", and with a calm market he can enter and relax [1:04]. The typical pattern he describes: a small move against him during Asia, then "London just massively reversed" in his direction [8:11].
- Exits: "I usually either take profit at the New York session or continue to move ... my stop loss in the profit" [9:11].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- TradingView price alerts: "TradingView alerts works miracles" [6:08]. He sets them near his stop-loss level (e.g. 1950/1952 on a gold short with the stop at 1961) to catch a possible reversal there.
- Psychological ("psych") levels, e.g. gold 1950 [6:08].
- Market structure (lower highs) for stop placement and adding.
- No indicator settings are given in this video.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Around 7:00 pm ET, open the charts (sometimes live in a lab webinar) and go through the watch list pairs [1:04].
2. Focus on the four core pairs and use the other 4-5 pairs on the list as confluence. Swap out a pair that looks ugly [2:06].
3. Look for a setup where the confluences "already playing out" point one way, even if price is "not ready yet" [2:06].
4. Decide entry, stop and target in advance: "if I lose it's going to be here, if I win it's going to be here" [5:08]. Write it into the trading plan.
5. Enter during the Asian session (7:30-9:30 pm ET, sometimes 10:30), set alerts, then walk away [7:10].

## 6. Entry rules
- **Entry 1 – market execution, early.** If the confluences are there and he believes the direction, he enters right away rather than waiting: "I will literally enter for a sell right then right there because I don't want to miss the trade" [2:06-3:06]. He expects drawdown after this entry.
- **Entry 2 – smaller add at the better level.** If an alert near his stop fires and he sees price "print a reversal" at a psychological level (e.g. a bounce off 1950 that tried to wick up and came back down), he enters again: "that second position is a little bit lighter even though it's the better entry" [6:08], "whether it's half the lot size" [7:10]. Reason: he does not want to double his exposure while already in drawdown.
- **Entry 3 – bigger add after structure.** Once price comes back past entry 1, "creates more structure and gives me a lower high", he moves the stop to that lower high (positions 1 and 2 are now risk-free). Then he enters "with a way bigger lot size" because "this is the best possible entry" [6:08-7:10].
- He tells lab members who don't like drawdown to "be a little bit more patient and then get in when the market feels right to you" [4:07].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Set a fixed stop before entry and accept drawdown up to it. The market "can only do one of two things: hit that take profit or hit that stop loss" [5:08].
- Example stop: gold short at 1943, stop 1961 (about 18 points [~180 pips in his gold pip convention]).
- After a new lower high forms (shorts), move the stop to that lower high to make the trade risk-free [6:08].
- Keep moving the stop into profit while holding [9:11].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Example target: gold short from 1943 to 1910 or 1900 flat, which he estimated "should be a good one-to-four reward ratio ... just off the top of my head" [2:06]. (Arithmetic check: 33-43 points of reward against 18 points of risk is about 1:1.8-1:2.4, not 1:4. He was estimating loosely.)
- Takes profit at the New York session, or trails the stop and holds for days [9:11].

## 9. Trade management
- Set and forget: after entering he is "off my phone" and does not watch the trade. He only finds out in the morning [7:10-8:11].
- He uses a TradingView alert near the stop to know when to consider entry 2 [6:08].
- Scaling: smaller (≈half) second entry in drawdown, then a much bigger third entry once there is a new lower high and the stop has moved there [6:08-7:10].
- Moves the stop into profit and holds for 2-7 days, or closes at New York [9:11].
- Doesn't trade on vacation [10:13].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Lot size must be small enough that a loss doesn't hurt. Traders get emotional in drawdown "because you place way too high of a lot size that if you lose you're going to blow your account or blow half your account" [5:08].
- He assumes a "50% chance of winning, 50% chance of losing" on every trade [5:08].
- Relative position sizes: entry 2 is about half of entry 1, and entry 3 is "way bigger". Exact risk percent is not given.
- Track-record claim: September 2023, about 14-15 lab webinars, 10 trades, 9 winners, "over six figures". With a win on October 1 that becomes 10 of 11 [3:06-4:07].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: XAUUSD short (illustrative; [2:06]-[7:10])
- Context: a hypothetical/representative example ("let's say"), not a dated trade.
- Setup: he believes gold is heading down and the confluences are already playing out, but price is "not ready yet". The confluences themselves are not described.
- Entry 1: market sell at 1943.
- Stop: 1961. Target: 1910 or 1900. He calls it about 1:4 (see note in section 8).
- Expected path: drawdown to 1948, 1949, 1950. A TradingView alert at 1950/1952 fires. Price bounces off the 1950 psych level, tries to wick up and comes back down. He adds entry 2 there with about half the lot size.
- Price falls back below 1943 and makes a lower high. He moves the stop to that lower high, which makes entries 1 and 2 risk-free, and adds a bigger entry 3 at about 1934.
- Result: illustrative, no result given.

### Trade 2: unnamed pair, direction not stated (mentioned at [9:11])
- His most recent trade before the video: entered Monday, in drawdown until Wednesday, then "massively flew into profit". No pair, levels or result given.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Enter during the Asian session and walk away: "I usually enter my trades from between 7:30 and 9:30 at night, sometimes 10:30" [7:10].
- Keep a core four pairs: "I only like have four on my watch list each week that I'm taking seriously" [2:06].
- Don't wait and miss the move: "I will literally enter for a sell right then right there because I don't want to miss the trade" [2:06].
- Use alerts: "set a TradingView alert for 1950, 1952 once it's closer to that stop-loss level" [6:08].
- Second entry is smaller: "that second position is a little bit lighter even though it's the better entry" [6:08].
- Don't match the original size while in drawdown: "what I don't want to do is mirror the exact same lot size" [6:08].
- Risk-free first, then size up: "I'll move my stop loss into where that lower high is now so it's a risk-free trade and then I'll enter with a way bigger lot size" [6:08-7:10].
- Accept the binary outcome: "it can only do one of two things: hit that take profit or hit that stop loss" [5:08].
- No trading on vacation: "I don't trade on vacation" [10:13].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- 2020 vs now: "I don't care about sniper entries, right, I did in 2020, that's why I made sniper entry videos part one, part two, part three, part four" [8:11-9:11]. Now he goes for "the most pips" and doesn't miss trades. He only gets a "sniper" entry on the third, post-lower-high add.
- Early in his career he would write a setup in his plan, check back hours later and find he had missed the move. Entering at market immediately is "a way for me to combat that" [3:06].
- He says he has used this routine "for the last few years" and doesn't see a need to change it [10:13].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- The confluences behind the gold example are not described. No timeframes, indicators or MA settings are mentioned.
- The 1943/1961/1900-1910 numbers are a "let's say" example, and his "1:4" estimate doesn't match the numbers. The real R:R is about 1:2.
- He doesn't say exactly how big entry 3 is ("way bigger"), or what risk % entry 1 uses.
- "Agent session" in the captions = Asian session. He says "Eastern Standard Time", meaning US Eastern time.
- The gold drawdown sequence "1943 1948 1949 1950" is hypothetical.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I usually enter my trades from between 7:30 and 9:30 at night, sometimes 10:30 ... I'm talking Eastern Standard Time" [7:10]
- "With Asian session it's not that liquid but I find better setups that way" [1:04]
- "I only like have four on my watch list each week that I'm taking seriously" [2:06]
- "I'm a swing trader, I can afford to be in drawdown" [2:06]
- "I will literally enter for a sell right then right there because I don't want to miss the trade" [2:06]
- "TradingView alerts works miracles" [6:08]
- "That second position is a little bit lighter even though it's the better entry" [6:08]
- "I'll move my stop loss into where that lower high is now so it's a risk-free trade and then I'll enter with a way bigger lot size" [6:08-7:10]
- "Asian, then going into London, and then London just massively reversed" [8:11]
- "I usually either take profit at the New York session or continue to move ... my stop loss in the profit" [9:11]
- "Sometimes these moves may take two, three, four, five, six, seven days" [9:11]
- "I don't care about sniper entries, right, I did in 2020" [8:11]
