# 2023-11-29 — The Best Technical Analysis Video You'll Ever See On YouTube 🏆

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=koKRQ_pPhjQ |
| Uploaded | 2023-11-29 |
| Length | ~49:00 (last caption marker 48:54) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2022 swing trading (he says "how I trade in 2022"); appears to be recorded around April 2022 as Academy lessons and uploaded in late 2023. Already uses the 8 & 14 EMA crossover that defines his 2023 approach. |

## 1. What this video covers
This is three top-down analysis lessons in a row, each starting from a blank chart: GBPAUD (0:00–19:00), AUDUSD (19:28–37:40) and GBPUSD (37:42–end). He explains how he marks monthly, weekly and daily levels with round numbers and "quarters", then adds the levels where price actually reacted, trendlines, chart patterns, Fibonacci retracements and extensions, the 8 and 14 EMAs and Heikin Ashi candles. He then sets a bias and waits for an entry, or sets an alert. He says it shows "how I trade in 2022" and calls it an update of what he taught in his "Gold Academy". The GBPAUD lesson includes a full worked long setup with R:R figures for each target and a step-by-step trailing stop. All three lessons push swing trading over intraday counter-trend trading.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Style:** swing trading. He holds a few positions ("1 2 3 4 five trades") and lets them run through pullbacks instead of taking many intraday trades. "This is why I like swing trading… once you get in right here you can just hold it hold it hold it" [15:24].
- **Weekly:** looks at roughly the last 12 weeks (one quarter, about 3 months) only. Older history "does absolutely nothing" for the weekly read [1:01–2:02]. He keeps a "3-month" marker on the chart for this.
- **Weekly/daily:** where he draws support and resistance. He prefers to draw on the weekly "just cuz it's a lot easier" [2:02].
- **4H / 2H:** he zooms in for structure, Fibonacci and entries [3:02, 12:22].
- **Side-by-side charts:** he usually runs the daily, 4H, 2H and sometimes the 15-minute next to each other with mirrored drawings. The 15m is for when he is "trying to really get… a great entry and kind of scale it back in" [30:38].
- He does not let the weekly EMAs drive the decision because the weekly "takes so long". He reads the EMAs on the daily, 4H and 2H [32:38].
- **Routine:** he reviews and redraws every week. He calls the daily check-in the "30 minutes or less" rule: analyse, trade if the setup is there, otherwise set an alert [30:38–31:38, 48:54].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- GBPAUD ("my favorite pair") [1:01], AUDUSD [19:28] and GBPUSD [38:43]. He also mentions gold levels from another video [45:52].
- Sessions: not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **8 EMA and 14 EMA.** Blue line = 8, red line = 14. A blue-over-red cross is bullish. Price trading above the EMAs means "we're heading up", below means "heading down" [13:23]. If he shows only one line, it is the 8 [13:23]. All EMAs pointing down on the daily, 4H and 2H means sells only [31:38–32:38, 42:49].
- **Round numbers ("quarters" / psychological levels).** He uses major whole levels with a mid level ("quarter") between them, for example GBPAUD 1.70 / 1.725 / 1.75 / 1.775 / 1.80, AUDUSD 0.70 / 0.725 / 0.75, and GBPUSD 1.30 / 1.325 / 1.35 / 1.275. He says "170 could mean 169900 it could mean 17200 [1.7200]". In other words, each level is a zone, not an exact price [4:04]. He says he partly moved away from quarters in the Gold Academy and then "went back to it, not like fully" [3:02].
- **Horizontal lines and horizontal rays.** He uses lines for long-term levels and rays to keep the chart uncluttered. He now prefers rays: "I just don't like lines anymore I like rays" [22:32].
- **Labels.** He writes text such as "monthly support 1.70", "weekly mid-level of support 1.725" and "key area of support" [5:07–6:12]. Colours: black or blue for monthly levels, gray or yellow borders for daily levels [24:32].
- **Ellipse tool.** He circles major areas and pattern points instead of drawing long lines [9:17, 10:17].
- **Trendlines** labelled A-B-C-D, and a ray on the key swing low.
- **Fibonacci retracement:** 38.2, 50 and 61.8 [12:22, 17:26].
- **Fibonacci extension:** 1.27 ("27 extension"), 1.618 ("61.8 extension") and 1.80 ("1.80 extension") [12:22–13:23].
- **Long-position (R:R) tool** to measure R:R [12:22–13:23].
- **Heikin Ashi candles** to read the trend without the alternating red/green noise of normal candles [33:39–35:41]. He also checks a line chart for structure [32:38].
- **Chart colours:** purple for up candles and black for down candles. He keeps the charts dark ("black charts") to take emotion out [34:41].
- **Alerts** for when the market reaches the area he expects [31:38, 48:54].
- **Measured distances ("Forex is art"):** he measures how far each recent swing travelled and clones that distance to project the next move. On GBPUSD the drops were repeatedly about 90 pips, so a new drop of about 90 pips becomes a take-profit area [45:52–46:52].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Remove all drawings and indicators and start from a blank chart [1:01, 38:43].
2. **Weekly:** look at only the last ~12 weeks (one quarter) for the overall picture [2:02].
3. Read the current price on the right axis and mark the round-number levels above and below it: major levels (e.g. 1.70, 1.75, 1.80) plus the mid level (e.g. 1.725). Label them monthly, weekly or mid-level support/resistance [3:02–6:12].
4. Look in between those levels and mark where price actually stopped in the past, using wicks and closes. "Sometimes round numbers aren't enough sometimes you got to see exactly where price stopped in the past" [21:31]. He scrolls back through the history to see where price "stopped here, stopped here, stopped here" [8:16–9:17]. If a level breaks, he expects price to run to the next level it has respected [9:17].
5. **Weekly close check:** a wick above a major level that closes back below is a fakeout. "Once you get a close below at a major support or resistance area you know it's going to head all the way back in the opposite direction" [23:32]. He stresses waiting for the weekly candle to close [22:32].
6. **Daily:** read the candles and structure (lower highs and lows vs. higher highs and lows). Look for patterns such as an inverted head and shoulders or a double bottom/top, and draw trendlines [10:17–11:19].
7. **4H/2H:** place the Fibonacci. Find the A-B swing, check where C bounced (38.2/50 retracement) and project the D target with the 1.27, 1.618 and 1.80 extensions [12:22].
8. Check the 8/14 EMAs: the direction of the cross, and whether price is above or below them [13:23].
9. Check Heikin Ashi and/or the line chart to confirm the overall trend [32:38–35:41].
10. Count confluences. He lists "four to five different confluences" on the GBPAUD setup [12:22].
11. Decide a bias (buys only or sells only). Then either enter (step 12) or wait and set an alert [31:38, 48:54].
12. **Entry timing:** wait for a retracement, then take the first, second or third confirming candle in your direction, such as a bullish engulfing or morning star [17:26–18:26].

## 6. Entry rules
- Do not enter while the move still looks like the opposite direction. You need an actual retracement and a bounce. "Could you have gotten in right here no because this is still a sell… you need actual retracement" [12:22].
- After the retracement, enter on the first confirming candles. "That first bullish engulfing or or or Morning Star enter because you have all the confluences you need to get in" [18:26]. He also says "first second or third Candlestick" [17:26].
- On the GBPAUD example the entry is the 4H candle that bounced after the inverted head-and-shoulders, trendline and Fib confluence [12:22].
- **Trend filter:** while the key swing low (the ray on point B) holds, look for buys only [14:24]. When the EMAs point down on the daily, 4H and 2H, "you would only look for sales" [42:49].
- **Re-entry plan on GBPAUD:** wait for a higher low, place a new Fib from A to B, and look for C in the 50–61.8 retracement, where monthly support lines up. Then enter on the first bullish engulfing or morning star [17:26].
- **Don't chase.** If the only possible stop is far away (above the last lower high), don't enter. Wait for a pullback or for a break-and-retest [32:38, 43:50].
- **GBPUSD double bottom:** don't count it as a reversal until price clears the level, comes back down to retest it, and prints a candle you can act on [42:49].
- Order type: not stated. The entries are described as entering on the candle, i.e. a market order at the confirmation candle.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Put the stop beyond the structure swing: below the higher low or swing low for buys ("stop loss right below here", [12:22]), above the last lower high for sells [32:38].
- He wants a wide stop that gives the trade "room to breathe". A stop that is "so close… gives you no room to breathe" [35:41–36:41].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Fib extensions from the A-B-C swing: 1.27 (about 1:3 on the example), 1.618 (about 1:5) and 1.80 (about 1:7) [13:23].
- The next round-number or "stopped here before" level, e.g. GBPAUD 1.80 [7:15], AUDUSD around 0.7170 [26:34] and GBPUSD 1.275 [42:49].
- The measured-move distance (about 90 pips on GBPUSD) [46:52].
- Swing traders take profit or partial profit at each level along the way [35:41].

## 9. Trade management
- Trail the stop to each new higher low once it forms. Because that low sits above the entry, the stop ends up in profit: "now our stop loss right here can be placed in profit" [16:24].
- Take partial profits at each target level: "take profit take profit take profit or take partial profit take partial profit take partial profit" [35:41].
- Hold through pullbacks and let the trade play out [15:24].
- Use alerts so you don't have to watch the chart [31:38, 48:54].
- **Trendline invalidation rules (GBPAUD):** a break of the trendline alone does not end the trend, especially near support. To invalidate it, price must (1) break the trendline, retest it and continue down to take out the low, and (2) take out the support and the swing low B. "Until it takes B out… it may not be a valid trend line but still a valid Trend" [14:24]. The same idea on AUDUSD and GBPUSD: break the mid level, then take out B, then A [26:34, 44:52].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- R:R on the GBPAUD example: 1:3 at the 1.27 extension, 1:5 at 1.618 and 1:7 at 1.80 [13:23].
- He holds only a few positions and avoids counter-trend trades. "Counter trend trading is very very dangerous" [15:24].
- Risk %, lot sizes and account sizes: not covered in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPAUD long, example setup and plan (1:01–18:26)
- **Context:** current price about 1.76902 on the weekly [2:02]. The previous quarter (January–April) was strongly bearish for GBPAUD ("the pound was very very weak compared to the Australian dollar") [2:02].
- **Levels:** 1.70 monthly support (major), 1.725 weekly mid-level support, 1.75 monthly support (price is above it), 1.775 and 1.80, which he calls "very very strong", along with 1.60 and 1.90 [6:12–7:15]. Price is trading between 1.75 and 1.80. In the past, 1.725 held price several times, and price stopped short of it more than once [8:16]. 1.675 is noted as a minor mid-level where price also stopped [9:17].
- **Daily read:** lower high, lower low, then a higher low that matched an earlier high. An inverted head and shoulders forms (marked with ellipses). Price reverses at 1.725, which holds, and there is a second trendline bounce. He draws an uptrend trendline [10:17–11:19].
- **4H:** Fib A-B, with C bouncing between 38.2 and 50. Price looks set to break above the 1.27 extension and head for the 1.618 extension [12:22]. The 8 EMA crossed above the 14 at the reversal "same time as the candlestick burst right through", and price is above both EMAs [13:23].
- **Entry:** "this candlestick right here, even this one right here", the 4H bounce candle after the retracement. Price not stated, but later implied at about 1.7467 [16:24].
- **Stop:** just below the swing low "right below here" [shown on chart, not stated].
- **Targets:** 1.27 extension = 1:3, 1.618 extension = 1:5, and 1.80 extension (beyond the 1.80 round number) = 1:7 [13:23]. Best case about 400 pips at the 1.618 extension [17:26].
- **Management:** after price makes a higher low and continues up, move the stop under that low. The example stop moves to about 1.7570, roughly 110–120 pips in profit, "from 174 670 to 1757" [16:24] (the prices are garbled in the captions and read as 1.7467 entry → 1.7570 stop [?]). Worst case once trailed is a stop-out about 120 pips in profit. He compares this with forcing a counter-trend sell for about 120 pips "with no guarantee" [15:24–16:24].
- **Near-term expectation:** the current 4H candle (about 1h40m left to close) may push up. He then expects a pullback to retest the trendline, or even a break of it down to support before price heads back up [13:23–14:24].
- **Follow-up plan:** wait for a higher low and a new Fib A→B. C would be at 38.2, but monthly support lines up with the 50–61.8 retracement, "this pool of liquidity". Enter on the first, second or third bullish candle (engulfing or morning star) [17:26–18:26].
- **Result:** not stated (it is an analysis example).

### Trade 2: AUDUSD short bias (19:28–37:40)
- **Context:** "the market is open". He expects the week to "drop drop drop" [19:28]. He notes a trendline he had drawn since October, January and February. If you delete your old drawings you would miss it, so keep them on the chart [20:30–21:31].
- **Levels:** 0.70 at the bottom, 0.725 and 0.75. 0.75 is a major round number that has been respected back to 2006 [22:32–23:32]. There was a big weekly wick above 0.75 that did not close above it, "a quick fake out", then a strong close below. "Stupid, you didn't wait for the candle to close on a weekly time frame" [22:32]. 0.70 shows a double bottom [23:32]. He adds a 0.725 mid-level ray and a daily yellow-border level where price hesitated before breaking through [24:32–25:33].
- **Daily:** a trendline from A to B, with price approaching it as a confluence [25:33].
- **Bias and targets:** short. He expects price to head down to about the 0.7335 area [?], come up and retrace, then pull back toward 0.70, "maybe a little bit higher probably 71700 [0.7170]", where earlier wicks deserve respect. The distance between those two areas is about 140 pips [25:33–26:34]. He also thinks the current weekly candle could drop about 100 more pips [22:32].
- **Intraday trigger:** price breaks the trendline and the mid level, then takes out B and then A [26:34–27:35].
- **Execution:** the EMAs point down on the daily, 4H and 2H, so trade down only. "You just can't get in right now because your stop loss [would] have to be right here, the last lower high" [32:38]. He wants side-by-side daily/4H/2H charts for the entry [29:37–30:38].
- **Confirmation:** a possible double top was invalidated once price took out the low [33:39]. Heikin Ashi is "pure red" [33:39–34:41].
- **Entry, stop, result:** no entry was taken. "Is it retracing? Okay, leave it alone, set an alert" [31:38].

### Trade 3: GBPUSD short bias (38:43–48:54)
- **Levels:** price is at or near 1.30, "a major major major area", which is why it is hesitating. He also marks 1.35, the 1.325 mid level and the 1.275 area below (he calls it essentially the mid level). Price stopped at 1.275 several times, including a morning star right on the level [39:43–42:49].
- **Trend:** a long-term downtrend trendline A-B-C-D that "missed it a little bit" for E [40:49–41:49]. If price rallies 200–300 pips he would expect it to reach this trendline before breaking. To flip bullish it would need higher lows and to take out D, C, B and A [41:49].
- **Signals:** a double bottom at the current level, but he will not anticipate a reversal until price clears the level and retests it with a candle [42:49]. The EMAs are down on every timeframe, so sells only, with a target of 1.2750. He calls the recent price action consolidation because there are no true lower highs and lower lows [42:49–43:50].
- **Entry plan:** don't sell now, because the stop would have to be "way up here". Either wait for a retrace up and sell from there, or wait for a break below, a retest and then sell, targeting 1.2750 [43:50].
- **If wrong:** if price breaks the resistance and the trendline, the upside targets are the next levels, each about 90–100 pips apart [44:52].
- **Measured move:** recent drops were about 90 pips each (one was 99–100), so he expects about 90 pips per leg and uses that as a take-profit area. Heikin Ashi is pointing down [46:52].
- **Alternative scenario:** if price rises, look for a higher low and an 8/14 EMA crossover, then a continuation up to the trendline [47:53].
- **Result:** not stated.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Only the last ~12 weeks matter on the weekly. "What matters on the weekly is probably the last 12 weeks like the last three months" [1:01–2:02].
- Treat round numbers as zones. "Every time we say 170 it's the area around 170" [4:04].
- Mark where price actually reacted, not just round numbers. "Sometimes round numbers aren't enough sometimes you got to see exactly where price stopped at in the past" [21:31].
- Wait for the weekly candle to close at major levels. "You didn't wait for the candle to close on a weekly time frame" [22:32].
- Keep old drawings on the chart. "Leave your drawing tools on because you never know if it'll come back in a month or two months" [20:30].
- Don't trade counter-trend. "Counter trend trading is very very dangerous" [15:24].
- Look for buys only until the key swing low breaks. "Until it takes this out we are looking for buys only" [14:24].
- When the EMAs are down, sell only. "In that scenario you would only look for sales" [42:49].
- A trendline break alone is not a trend change. "It may not be a valid trend line but still a valid Trend" [14:24].
- Price must be on the right side of the 8 EMA. "Whenever the candlesticks are above it we're heading up" [13:23].
- Wait for a retracement, then enter on the first engulfing or morning star. "That first bullish engulfing or Morning Star enter" [18:26].
- Give trades room. "The one thing you need is market area to breathe" [36:41].
- If there is no setup, set an alert. "Please set an alert before you go" [48:54].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- Quarters/round numbers: he had partly moved away from the "quarter" levels in the Gold Academy and has since gone back to them, but not exclusively. "I kind of went away from it but I kind of went back to it not like fully" [3:02].
- He now prefers rays over full horizontal lines [22:32].
- He calls this lesson "updated from the actual Gold Academy" and describes it as "how I trade in 2022" [36:41].
- He mentions possibly making a full Heikin Ashi course, which suggests Heikin Ashi was becoming more central [34:41].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Many prices are shown on the chart and not said aloud: the GBPAUD entry candle, the stop, and the exact A/B/C points.
- "174 670 to 1757" is read as a 1.7467 entry and a 1.7570 trailed stop [?]. AUDUSD "7335" and "71700" are read as 0.7335 and 0.7170 [?].
- The upload date (2023-11-29) differs from the content date. He mentions April and "how I trade in 2022", and the prices fit spring 2022.
- The EMA colours (blue = 8, red = 14) are inferred from "blue cross over the red" plus "the black one… the eight by itself". The colour of the 8 may differ between charts.
- The video has no session or time-of-day rules and no risk %.
- He mentions other courses (the Gold Academy, the "archive", a "trading Bible" and a trendline video). Content from those is not in this transcript.

## 15. Key quotes
- "What matters on the weekly is probably the last 12 weeks like the last three months." [1:01–2:02]
- "Every time we say 170 it's the area around 170… that's just the area, that's not a specific number." [4:04]
- "The 8 and the 14 EMA, you have the blue cross over the red… the candlesticks are trading above the moving averages right now which is very important." [13:23]
- "1.27 [extension] is one to 3, 61.8 is a 1 to 5 and 1.80 is a 1 to 7 reward ratio." [13:23]
- "Until it takes this out we are looking for buys only." [14:24]
- "Counter trend trading is very very dangerous." [15:24]
- "You're not in 30 different trades, you're in 1 2 3 4 five trades and you're just holding them." [15:24]
- "Now our stop loss right here can be placed in profit." [16:24]
- "That first bullish engulfing or Morning Star, enter, because you have all the confluences you need." [18:26]
- "Once you get a close below at a major support or resistance area you know it's going to head all the way back in the opposite direction." [23:32]
- "I usually have the daily here, the 4 hour here, 2 hour here and even the 15-minute here." [30:38]
- "Is it retracing? Okay, leave it alone, set an alert." [31:38]
- "Heikin Ashi candles make it so much easier to trade for me." [34:41]
- "You just take profit, take profit, take profit, or take partial profit." [35:41]

# 2023-09-23 — CHART WORK: The 2023 Strategy That Made Me Over $100K Last Month

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MEgXUxqSPQ4 |
| Uploaded | 2023-09-23 |
| Length | ~31:30 (last caption marker 31:21) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2023: 8 & 14 EMA crossover, psychological levels, early entries in the Asian session, TP1/TP2 partials |

## 1. What this video covers
This is his stated "2023 strategy" video, the yearly strategy explainer he had skipped earlier in 2023. He lays out a numbered list of points: take 60–75% off at TP1; enter only between 6:30 and 9:30 pm, in the low-volume Asian session; get in early and accept some drawdown rather than miss the move; close manually when price is a few pips from TP; use psychological levels; read candlesticks; use the 8 & 14 EMAs as his only indicators; and read fundamentals and sentiment, including DXY for gold. He illustrates the points with a live gold long that had just won and a GBPUSD trade, then places a real GBPUSD sell limit on camera with all the numbers. He says his strategy is "on a foundational level… the same" as 2020–2022 but different "from a technical level".

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Style:** swing trading with some day-trading elements. Aimed at "swing traders and even day traders", not scalpers [7:05]. Trades are placed in the evening and often left running overnight and through the next day; the gold trade was entered Monday and moved on Wednesday morning [5:05].
- **Timeframes:** daily for psychological levels and the TP2 view [12:08, 24:17]; 4H and 2H for DXY and trendlines [18:12, 22:17]; Fibonacci on the lower-timeframe structure [23:17]. Entry timeframe: not stated.
- **Stops and targets:** stops are usually 40–60 pips, because "my take profit is so massive" [26:18–27:18]. Example R:R runs from 1:2.3 to 1:4.2.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **Gold (XAUUSD)** is "my favorite pair always has been". He made about $25–26K on gold in the recent trades, plus $7.8K on GBPUSD [2:03].
- **GBPUSD** is used for the live example.
- **Entry window:** "I usually enter my trades between 6:30 p.m. and around 9:30 p.m.… like 99% of my trades" [4:04]. He calls this the Asian session [6:05]. Time zone not stated, presumably US Eastern [?].
- **Reason:** low volume and liquidity while the market consolidates. By London he has "already forgot about the trade", which helps his discipline [4:04–5:05].
- He calls himself "a London-ish New York overlap trader", meaning the moves happen then, "but I will never ever ever enter during those times" [4:04].
- **News:** FOMC was mentioned as a reason to exit GBPUSD at TP1 [3:04]. He watches UK CPI and the Fed rate decision [17:11].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **8 EMA and 14 EMA crossover.** "I got my eight and my 14 crossover two EMAs; these are the only indicators I use" [16:11]. He no longer uses RSI, MACD or stochastics; he used to, "eight years ago seven years ago when I first started trading".
- **Psychological levels (round numbers).** GBPUSD: 1.20, 1.25, 1.30, 1.35, 1.40 are the major whole levels, with mid levels such as 1.225. Minor levels like 1.23 or 1.24 count but are "not as major" [12:08–14:10]. Gold: 1850, 1900, 1950, 2000 [21:15]. A level is a zone: "price stopping at 1.25 and price stopping at 1.2547, that's the same thing" [12:08].
- **Extra rays and small boxes** where price clearly reacted, even if the level is not a psychological one (e.g. GBPUSD 1.185 and 1.237; gold 1925) [20:12–23:17]. He keeps these visually different from the major levels so he doesn't confuse them [21:15].
- **Trendlines and channels:** long-term trendlines, a descending parallel channel and consolidation boxes. He keeps an unbroken trendline on the chart even after price leaves it [21:15–22:17].
- **Fibonacci extension:** 27 (1.27) and 61.8 (1.618) used as TP1 and TP2. "Anywhere between this area 27 and 61" [23:17–24:17].
- **Heikin Ashi** candles to confirm the overall trend [28:18].
- **Candlestick patterns and closes:** what candle printed at the level and how it closed [15:10–16:11].
- **Fundamentals:** the TradingView news (lightning bolt icon) linking to DailyFX analyst articles, plus ForexFactory red-folder events [16:11–17:11].
- **DXY (dollar index)** on the 4H/2H as a confluence for gold [18:12].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Mark the psychological levels: major round numbers, mid levels, and minor round numbers to be aware of [12:08–15:10].
2. Add rays or small boxes where price has reacted in the past, plus trendlines, channels and consolidation boxes [20:12–23:17].
3. Read the candlesticks at those levels: the pattern, and how the candle closed [15:10–16:11].
4. Check the 8/14 EMA crossover [16:11].
5. Read the fundamental sentiment: the TradingView news, DailyFX analyst opinion and ForexFactory high-impact events. For gold, check the DXY: a weaker dollar means stronger gold [16:11–19:12].
6. Determine the structure (last lower high or higher low) for stop placement. Use Fib extensions to confirm that targets line up with psychological levels [20:12–24:17].
7. Check Heikin Ashi for trend direction [28:18].
8. Enter between 6:30 and 9:30 pm, early and before the move, accepting a little drawdown (see §6).

## 6. Entry rules
- **Timing:** enter only between 6:30 and 9:30 pm, during the low-volume Asian session [4:04].
- **Get in before the move.** Entering when the market is already moving is "a few confluences too late". "It is better to get in if you are confident the market is going to go in a direction and it's close to it but it may have a drawdown" [7:05]. He would rather market-sell now than wait for a perfect sell limit that may never fill [8:05].
- **Accept drawdown.** "I expect a little bit of drawdown" [8:05–9:05]. In the gold example he was about 20 pips in drawdown [5:05].
- **Don't miss trades.** "A lot of times you guys would be profitable if you just didn't miss the trade" [9:05].
- **Gold-specific confluence:** gold was already moving up and had reached the 1928 level, "all I had to wait for was a retest", backed by DXY weakness and the Fed pause expectation [19:12].
- **Retracement needed when the stop would be too far.** "I would need some type of retrace", otherwise the stop would sit too far up at the last lower high [20:12].
- **Order types:** market orders for the early entries. A sell limit at a reaction level plus Fib confluence for the GBPUSD example [23:17–26:18].
- **Number of entries:** the gold trade was two positions, a "13 lot and the six lot" [5:05] (13 and 6 lots as heard [?]).

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- The stop goes beyond structure: "my stop loss is always in accordance with structure. This is the last lower high right here, that's my stop loss, 10, 20 pips above that" [9:05].
- He loses because he was wrong, never because the stop was too tight: "you'll never see me lose because my stop loss was too tight" [9:05].
- Typical size: "I usually don't have 30 pip stop losses, I usually have 40 and 50 and 60 because my take profit is so massive" [26:18–27:18].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- **TP1 and TP2.** At TP1, take 60–70% off, later stated as 60–75%, and let the other 25% run risk-free [3:04, 27:18].
- **Targets** are psychological levels that line up with Fib extensions: the 1.27 extension for TP1 and the 1.618 for TP2 [24:17].
- **Take profit when price is close.** If price is a few pips from TP (2–3 pips on forex, roughly 10–20 pips on gold), close it manually rather than wait [10:05–11:06].
- **Manual early TP at a minor round number,** e.g. take profit at 1.2300 rather than wait for 1.2250 [14:10–15:10].

## 9. Trade management
- **Partials:** take 60–75% off at TP1 and let the remaining 25% run. "It's risk-free at that point. If it stops you out at break even great" [27:18].
- **Break-even / profit lock:** "when I'm in a trade and I see that I'm in profit 100 pips, 90 pips, 120 pips, chances are structure was created and I'm able to move my stop loss into break even, into profit" [28:18–29:19]. He'd rather be stopped out in profit than give it all back.
- **Early exit for news or consolidation:** he closed GBPUSD at TP1 rather than run to TP2 because "it was consolidating… and FOMC wasn't too good" [3:04].
- **Structure-break exit:** if price prints a higher low and breaks up against a short, "get out get out because this is structure being broken" [25:18].
- **Holding overnight:** yes. Trades are entered in the evening and left to run while he sleeps [5:05].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Lot sizes in the examples: gold "13 lot and six lot" [?] (about $18K profit at a 120+ pip gain) [5:05–6:05]; the GBPUSD sell limit at 0.14 lots [26:18].
- R:R examples: gold 1:2.3 (also described as about 1:2.5 "especially before FOMC") [10:05]; the hypothetical GBPUSD short at 1:3.7–1:4.2 [8:05–9:05].
- He points to a separate risk management and psychology video for risk % and similar topics [29:19–30:20]. Risk % is not covered here.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPUSD short, closed at TP1 (2:03–4:04)
- **Context:** recorded around FOMC week, September 2023 [?]. GBPUSD is "tanking" as he records.
- **Setup:** he "knew GU was going to drop". TP1 and TP2 shown on the chart, prices not stated.
- **Management:** he exited at TP1 because "it was consolidating and it pulled up and FOMC wasn't too good". He would normally take 60–70% at TP1 and run the rest to TP2.
- **Result:** profit at TP1. GBPUSD made $7.8K in the recent period [2:03]. Price then kept falling without him.

### Trade 2: XAUUSD long (5:05–6:05, 10:05, 17:11–22:17)
- **Context:** called on Monday in his lab: "gold 1950… it may draw down a little bit… 1925". The move came Wednesday morning.
- **Reasoning:** analysts expected the Fed to pause rate hikes, so the dollar could weaken and gold strengthen [17:11–18:12]. DXY dropped on the 4H/2H while gold rose, and vice versa; "this drop was all I needed for my gold trade" [18:12]. Gold was already moving, reached the 1928 level, and "all I had to wait for was a retest" [19:12]. There was a long-term downtrend line that price was breaking above, the 1925 level where price reacted, and a trendline from August 21 that he kept on the 4H chart [21:15–22:17].
- **Entry:** 1933, a buy. Two positions, "13 lot and six lot" [?]. Entered in the evening.
- **Stop:** 1925 [10:05].
- **Target:** 1950 [10:05]. R:R 1:2.3 (he mentions about 1:2.5 in the lab).
- **Drawdown:** price dipped to about 1931, roughly 20 pips (he says pips; on gold, $2 of price) [5:05].
- **Result:** he woke up to gold at 1945, "120 plus pips" in profit and "up eighteen thousand dollars right off gold alone", plus 5–6K on GBPUSD [6:05]. Price reached 1946–1947 with TP at 1950 [7:05, 11:06]. Whether TP filled is not stated explicitly. He says he made $25–26K on gold overall [2:03].

### Trade 3: GBPUSD short, hypothetical illustration (8:05–10:05)
- **Setup:** expect price to fall from near a trendline. Sell now, before price reaches the trendline, rather than wait for a sell limit that may never fill.
- **Stop:** 10–20 pips above the last lower high.
- **TP2:** far below.
- **R:R:** 1:4.2 (about 1:3.7 on the second measurement) [8:05–9:05].

### Trade 4: GBPUSD short, live sell limit (20:12–27:18)
- **Levels:** daily 1.25 psychological level, 1.225 mid level, 1.23 minor round number; a reaction level at about 1.2370 [20:12].
- **Setup:** wait for a retrace up to the small reaction level, giving a retest and another lower high. There is a mini trendline A-B-C. Fib extension: D projects to the 1.27 extension at exactly 1.2300 and the 1.618 at about 1.2250 [23:17–24:17].
- **Entry:** sell limit at 1.2365 (first said 1.2360), "because it's a little bit of an area" [24:17–26:18].
- **Size:** 0.14 lots.
- **Stop:** above the last lower high, first 1.2385, then 1.2395, "to be safe": about 30 pips. He notes this is tighter than his usual 40–60.
- **TP1:** 1.2300 (take 60–75% off). **TP2:** 1.2250.
- **Invalidation:** if price comes down, makes a higher low and breaks back up, get out (likely still in profit) [25:18].
- **Caveat:** "this is not a signal". He would analyse it further with the lab [25:18].
- **Result:** not known at recording.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Take most of the position off at TP1. "You take maybe 60 to 70 percent of my trade off if it hits TP1" [3:04].
- Enter in the quiet evening window. "I usually enter my trades between 6:30 p.m. and around 9:30 p.m." [4:04].
- Never enter during London/NY. "I will never ever ever enter during those times" [4:04].
- Get in before the move. "You enter a few confluences too late when the market is moving" [7:05].
- Don't miss the trade. "I'm not missing a trade. Sometimes I'll lose of course" [9:05].
- Put the stop beyond structure. "My stop loss is always in accordance with structure" [9:05].
- Close when it's almost at TP. "Take profit if it's close to it" [10:05–11:06].
- Use only the EMAs. "These are the only indicators I use. I don't use RSI" [16:11].
- Read the fundamentals. "Look at all the fundamentals and see the sentiments" [18:12].
- Gold moves inversely to the dollar. "Anytime the dollar is weak gold is essentially very very strong" [18:12].
- Only mark levels that matter to you. "Make your markings where price stops at… make it where it makes sense to you" [22:17–23:17].
- Exit if structure breaks. "Get out get out because this is structure being broken" [25:18].
- Move the stop to break-even or profit once you are about 90–120 pips up and structure has formed [29:19].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- He had made a strategy video every year (2020, 2021, 2022) but skipped 2023 until now. "On a foundational level [it's] the same but from a technical level… it's a little bit different" [2:03].
- He dropped RSI, MACD and stochastics years ago. The 8/14 EMA is now his only indicator [16:11].
- He says this is the first time he has placed a live lab trade on free YouTube [27:18].
- He mentioned Heikin Ashi in his 2020, 2021 and 2022 strategy videos and still uses it [28:18].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- "13 lot and the six lot" for gold may mean 13 and 6 standard lots, or 1.3 and 0.6 [?]. An $18K gain at about 12 dollars of gold price movement is consistent with roughly 15–19 total lots.
- The time zone for 6:30–9:30 pm is not stated. Presumably US Eastern, given his NY references in other videos [?].
- He numbers his points inconsistently and admits he is "getting mixed up". The order in §6–§9 is reconstructed.
- He says "20 pips drawdown" for gold moving from 1933 to about 1931. His gold pip convention (here $0.10 = 1 pip) differs from some brokers.
- The TP1/TP2 prices for the GBPUSD exit in Trade 1 are not stated.
- The entry timeframe is not stated.
- Whether the gold trade filled at 1950 is not explicitly confirmed.

## 15. Key quotes
- "On a foundational level [my strategy] is the same but from a technical level… it's a little bit different." [2:03]
- "You take maybe 60 to 70 percent of my trade off if it hits TP1." [3:04]
- "I usually enter my trades between 6:30 p.m. and around 9:30 p.m.… like 99% of my trades." [4:04]
- "I am a London-ish New York overlap trader but I will never ever ever enter during those times." [4:04]
- "It is better to get in if you are confident the market is going to go in a direction… but it may have a drawdown." [7:05]
- "My stop loss is always in accordance with structure… 10, 20 pips above that." [9:05]
- "Take profit if it's close to it." [10:05–11:06]
- "I got my eight and my 14 crossover two EMAs; these are the only indicators I use." [16:11]
- "Anytime the dollar is weak gold is essentially very very strong." [18:12]
- "The 27 extension lines up with 1.23000 on the dot." [24:17]
- "I usually don't have 30 pip stop losses, I usually have 40 and 50 and 60 because my take profit is so massive." [26:18–27:18]
- "Take the 60 to 75 off your trade and let the other 25 run. It's risk-free at that point." [27:18]
- "Chances are structure was created and I'm able to move my stop loss into break even, into profit." [29:19]

# 2023-09-10 — A 30 Minute Risk Management Forex Guide That'll Change Your Life

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GgiJOUw7uwk |
| Uploaded | 2023-09-10 |
| Length | ~30:30 (last caption marker 30:14) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2023 swing trading (gold, 40–60 pip stops, 180–230 pip targets) |

## 1. What this video covers
This is a slide-based risk management and psychology guide. He uses a lab member's story as a case study: the member was up $14K on gold, took no partials, closed with $2.25K, then nearly blew the account. The concrete rules: trade only 1:3 or better (1:2 "if you have to"), with 1:4 as the sweet spot; win rate doesn't matter if R:R is high; increase lot size slowly; risk 2–4% normally (he sometimes takes 5–9% on "amazing" trades); never jump lot size on a streak; take profit or partials; keep a main income. It reflects his 2023 swing approach: stops of 40–60 pips aiming for 180–230 pips. Two gold chart examples and one USDJPY loss are briefly shown.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Style:** swing trader. "I'm a swing trader lesson here" [12:07]. He doesn't trade every day: "the issue is you guys are trying to trade every single day" [12:07].
- **Typical trade:** "my stop losses are usually 40 50 60 pips because I aimed for 180 200 230 take profits" [20:10].
- **Timeframes:** not covered in this video.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Gold (both chart examples) and USDJPY (the losing lab trade).
- Advice: don't trade "a pair you've never traded before… don't know anything about the fundamentals don't know anything about how it moves" [13:07].
- Sessions: not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Moving averages: the gold short example "retested the moving averages" [11:06–12:07]. Settings are not stated here; his 8/14 EMAs per other 2023 videos.
- Psychological levels: 1900 and 1925 on gold [12:07, 15:07].
- Trendlines and counter-trendlines [15:07, 19:09].
- An R:R / position tool showing 1:4.09 [11:06].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Not covered in this video. Only brief setup descriptions appear inside the trade examples; see §11.

## 6. Entry rules
- Only take trades whose R:R is at least 1:2, preferably 1:3 or more (see §10).
- He sometimes takes 1:2 "because I got in too late" when highly confident [8:04].
- Don't take random trades that "look good" outside your trading plan [12:07–13:07].
- Entry triggers: not covered in this video.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Always set a stop and a take profit: "this is why you set a take profit and a stop loss because the market can do one or the other" [21:10].
- Typical stop size is 40–60 pips [20:10].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Targets are about 180–230 pips, giving 1:3 to 1:4+ against the stop [20:10].
- Psychological levels serve as TP: gold "TP would have been 1900… it even got lower than 1900 which is my psychological level my key area" [12:07].
- **Take the money.** In the case study he says "you could have taken partials, you could have gotten out at five thousand…" [0:02]. When to close: "the market will tell you… structure is kind of reversing… we need a little bit of a rally" [16:07].

## 9. Trade management
- **Take partial profits** when well in profit; never let a big winner turn into a loss. "There is no excuse to go from being up 14,000 to almost blowing your account in the same trade" [0:02].
- **Close on signs of reversal:** structure reversing, or the market needing "relief… a rally" after a drop [16:07].
- **"Next play" mentality:** move on immediately after a loss or a missed trade [21:10–22:10].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **R:R minimum:** "always trade with the one to three plus risk to reward ratio, one to two if you have to" [27:10]. On the slide, 1:1 is red, 1:2 half red/half green, and 1:3 and 1:4 are green. "Winning three and one to four is your good spot" [8:04].
- **Worked math on a $10K account risking $400 per trade (4%):**
  - 1:1, 5 wins and 5 losses = breakeven [7:03].
  - 1:4, 5 wins and 5 losses: −$2,000 + $8,000 = +$6,000 [9:04].
  - 1:4, 3 wins and 7 losses: −$2,800 + $4,800 = +$2,000. "You can have a 30% win rate and still be profitable" [10:05].
- **Win rate:** "win rates don't move me". A 30% win-rate trader taking 1:4 beats a 70% win-rate trader taking 1:1 [10:05–11:06].
- **Lot size progression:** use a "moderate lot size where you just slowly increase it with time", e.g. 0.5, 0.5, 0.5, and after about four wins that outweigh the losses go to 0.6, then 0.7. With high R:R the account compounds [12:07].
- **Never jump size on a winning streak.** Lab members went from 0.10 to 0.60 lots on trade 7 after six wins in a row and blew their accounts. Example: a 60-pip stop × 0.60 lots = $360 loss [19:09–20:10].
- **Risk % per trade:** "two to four percent is fine, five to eight percent is aggressive". He doesn't risk only 1%. "Sometimes I'll risk three or four. If the trade is amazing I'll even risk five to eight percent, even nine percent, and that's not the norm" [28:11]. On a $100K account he may lose $6–8K per trade but wins $30–40K, so "I can lose two or three and then have two winners and I'm golden" [28:11].
- **Contradictory advice:** keep trading risk "towards the lower mid… most of the lower end of the spectrum" [29:13]. Medium risk is fine in life, not in trading.
- **Main income:** keep a main income while learning. Don't quit your job to trade full-time unless you have saved about two years of bills [25:10–26:10].
- **Recent record:** "my last eight trades… I won seven of them" [18:07].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: XAUUSD short (11:06–12:07)
- **Context:** a trade he called "in early August-ish, late July" (2023).
- **Setup:** price broke through a support level, "came through, retested the moving averages, pulled all the way down".
- **Entry and stop:** shown on chart, not stated.
- **Target:** 1900, his psychological level/key area. Price went even lower than 1900.
- **R:R:** 1:4.09 as shown on the position tool.
- **Result:** win (TP hit).

### Trade 2: XAUUSD, current trade or setup with expanding R:R (14:07–15:07)
- **Setup:** price "bounced off the trend line, broke through this other counter trend line, broke through, cleared 1925, retested 1925".
- **Direction:** most likely long (break above and retest of 1925); the captions are unclear [?].
- **R:R:** originally 1:3. Now about 1:6 to an intermediate level and 1:12 if it reaches the far target.
- **Entry, stop and targets:** shown on chart, not stated.
- **Result:** not stated (in progress).

### Trade 3: USDJPY long, loss (18:07–19:09)
- **Context:** the 7th of 8 lab trades (7 of 8 won).
- **Setup:** price had been in a consolidation area "for a very very long time", cleared the high, came through and retested, and formed a new trendline. There was a key level he did not want to see broken and a wick showing bullish momentum. He believes USDJPY is "bullish long term".
- **Entry:** "got in right here". He expected a drawdown to retouch the trendline before price swooped back up.
- **Stop:** shown on chart, not stated.
- **Result:** stopped out. "I got in at the wrong time". Members who raised their lot size from 0.10 to 0.60 on this trade blew their accounts.

### Case study: lab member's gold trade (0:02–2:02)
- The member was up $14K on gold over 3 days and wanted $30K. He took no partials and ended with $2.25K, then "got cocky and damn near blew my account".
- Lesson: take partials and close when you are well up.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Trade 1:3+ R:R. "Always trade with the one to three plus risk to reward ratio, one to two if you have to" [27:10].
- Avoid 1:1. "You don't want a one-to-one risk reward ratio because that means you have to be right almost every time" [7:03].
- Win rate is not the goal. "You don't have to win more trades than you lose, you just got to make sure your wins outweigh your losses" [11:06].
- Don't force a trade every day. "You guys think you got to trade every single day" [12:07].
- Scale up lot size slowly. "A moderate lot size where you just slowly increase it with time" [12:07].
- Don't up-size on a streak. "Who told you to add a higher lot size and not 0.01 or 0.04" [19:09].
- Stick to your trading plan. "They take any trade that looks good and don't stay consistent with their trading plan" [12:07].
- Risk range: "two to four percent is fine, five to eight percent is aggressive" [28:11].
- Take profit. "You could have taken partials, you could have gotten out at five thousand" [0:02].
- Move on. "Athletes move on after every single play" [6:03].
- Keep a job. "You need a main income" [25:10].
- Leave personal finances out of trading decisions. "The market doesn't give a f[…] you're in debt" [2:02].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- In his past, "it used to be me", he won only 3 of 10 trades, but the winners were large [10:05].
- He made a similar video "three and a half, four years ago" [13:07].
- He notes that in his early days he would be up $3K and give most of it back [1:02].
- No change to the strategy itself is stated.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- The direction of the gold 1:12 example is not clearly stated (likely long) [?].
- Entry, stop and target prices for all chart examples are shown on chart, not stated.
- Risk % advice conflicts: "2–4% fine, 5–8% aggressive" and his own use of up to 9% sit against "keep the risk towards the lower end".
- Lot-size example "0.5, 0.5, 0.5 → 0.6 → 0.7" may mean 0.05 [?]. The number depends on account size, which is not given.
- The 0.60-lot × 60-pip loss is stated as $360 after he first misspoke "3.6K".

## 15. Key quotes
- "There is no excuse to go from being up 14,000 to almost blowing your account in the same trade." [0:02]
- "You don't want a one-to-one risk reward ratio because that means you have to be right almost every time." [7:03]
- "Winning three and one to four is your good spot." [8:04]
- "You can have a 30% win rate and still be profitable." [10:05]
- "Win rates don't move me." [10:05]
- "You don't have to win more trades than you lose, you just got to make sure your wins outweigh your losses." [11:06]
- "A moderate lot size where you just slowly increase it with time, not majorly." [12:07]
- "My stop losses are usually 40, 50, 60 pips because I aim for 180, 200, 230 take profits." [20:10]
- "Nothing is guaranteed in this market." [20:10]
- "Always trade with the one to three plus risk to reward ratio, one to two if you have to." [27:10]
- "Two to four percent is fine, five to eight percent is aggressive." [28:11]
- "If the trade is amazing I'll even risk five to eight percent, even nine percent, and that's not the norm." [28:11]
- "You need to have a main income." [25:10]

# 2023-09-09 — Re-Program Your Trading Brain in 17 Minutes w/ These GREAT Answers To Aide Your Forex Journey 🏆

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8ub5s9-Cd-A |
| Uploaded | 2023-09-09 |
| Length | ~17:20 (approx.; last caption marker [17:08]) |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 2:01-3:01; 6:02-7:02; 10:02-11:02; 15:07-16:07) |
| Strategy era | 2023 swing trading (price action + one MA, psych levels, trend lines; Asian-into-London session) |

## 1. What this video covers
This is an Instagram Q&A. Most of it is about motivation, his life story, jobs and promoting his course. The strategy content covers his favourite trading days (Monday-Wednesday) and the Asian-into-London session on gold, GBPAUD and AUDUSD. He gives a one-line summary of his strategy: price action, news via TradingView, one moving average, psychological/key levels and trend lines. He also explains how to scale a small account by slowly raising lot size, why entries go against people (late entries, too few confluences, tight stops), that he swing trades with about 20 pips of drawdown against a 140-pip target, and that his pre-trade checklist is 1:3 (or 1:2) R:R plus 2-3 confluences. It reflects his 2023 swing approach.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- "I'm a swing trader, and I used to be a day trader obviously, but I'm a swing trader, which means I hold my trade a little bit longer" [10:02].
- He can be "okay with the market going against me 20 pips because I'm aiming for 140" [10:02].
- No chart timeframes are mentioned.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Best days: "believe it or not it is actually Monday through Wednesday" [2:01]. He rejects "don't trade Mondays". The market finds its footing on Sunday, and "the market moves crazy on Monday".
- Pairs: gold (XAUUSD), GBPAUD, AUDUSD. AUDUSD is "a little slower at times" [2:01].
- Session: "Monday night, that Asian session going into London session, that is a very very volatile session and that is the main session" [2:01]. He has traded those pairs and that session since 2019.
- Why not later in the week: by Monday-Wednesday the "midweek reversal" hasn't happened yet. Thursday night into Friday brings news (e.g. the unemployment rate) and falling volatility as the work week ends. Wednesday has FOMC and "good fundamental announcements", but "technical wise Monday through Wednesday is going to be your best friend" [2:01-3:01].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Price action is the core: "simply put it's price action" [3:01].
- News: he reads the news feed (the "little lightning bolt on TradingView") for about an hour, Monday to Wednesday, so he doesn't trade technicals that contradict sentiment/fundamentals. He combines the two [3:01-4:01].
- "I don't use too many indicators, use just one, a moving average" [4:01]. **The MA period/type is not stated here.**
- Psychological levels and key levels: "very very very important" [4:01].
- Trend lines [4:01].
- He doesn't use order blocks or "complex terms" [3:01].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
The video gives no full process. Pieces he mentions:
1. Read the TradingView news feed during the week (about an hour, Mon-Wed) to check sentiment and fundamentals [3:01].
2. Mark the technical picture: psychological/key levels, trend lines, one moving average [4:01].
3. Before taking a trade, check it against the trading plan. Is it 1:3 R:R (or 1:2, "whatever your trading plan is")? "Do I have two or three confluences?" [15:07].

## 6. Entry rules
- Enter after 2-3 confluences, not late: losing traders "got in [after] four confluences, too late, and missed the entire move ... the market done went up 10, 20, 30, 40, 50 pips and you got in at the 50 pips not realizing the market has to correct itself" [10:02].
- Don't chase. In his gold example (1897 → 1917) he did not enter. "The old me would have tried to get in at 1913 ... you done got in 170 pips too late" [14:05].
- Order type is not discussed.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Leave room for drawdown. He accepts about 20 pips against him on a trade aimed at 140 pips [10:02]. Exact stop sizes are not given.
- "Can you be in drawdown for an hour before the market is positive all the way in your favor? I don't think some of you guys can" [10:02].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Example target size: "I'm aiming for 140" pips [10:02].
- R:R in the plan: "is it a one to three risk reward ratio, or one two, whatever your trading plan is" [15:07].

## 9. Trade management
Not covered in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Small-account scaling** [6:02-7:02]: "you can't really get into big trades with a small account unless you slowly scale that account and slowly raise the lot size every now and then". Example:
  - $300 account, 0.04 lots, makes about $150 over a few days → $450.
  - Going from $450 toward $700: move up to 0.05, maybe 0.06 or 0.07.
  - Don't use a standard lot on a small account: "all it takes is 40 pips and your account is blown" [6:02]. The market can move 20-40 pips in minutes on news.
- If you lose while following your plan, accept it. If you keep losing while following the plan, "it may be time to get a whole new plan" [15:07].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: XAUUSD long, not taken (mentioned at [14:05])
- Context: he called in his free Telegram that gold would rally from about 1897. It went to 1917, which he calls "200 pips for gold" (so 1 point = 10 pips in his convention).
- He did not take the trade himself. The point was not to chase a late entry at 1913 (170 pips late). No entry, stop or result for him.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Trade Monday-Wednesday: "technical wise Monday through Wednesday is going to be your best friend" [3:01].
- Asian into London: "Monday night, that Asian session going into London session, that is a very very volatile session and that is the main session" [2:01].
- Check the news: "just to make sure I am not trading based off the technicals alone" [3:01].
- Keep it simple: "I don't use too many indicators, use just one, a moving average" [4:01].
- Scale lot size slowly: "think of it as how can I scale my small account so I can end up doing bigger trades down the line" [7:02].
- No standard lots on small accounts: "all it takes is 40 pips and your account is blown" [6:02].
- Don't enter late: "you got in at the 50 pips not realizing the market has to correct itself" [10:02].
- Pre-trade check: "is it a one to three risk reward ratio ... do I have two or three confluences? Does this all make sense? If it does, trade it" [15:07].
- Don't chase missed moves [14:05].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- "I used to be a day trader obviously, but I'm a swing trader" [10:02].
- He has traded gold/GBPAUD/AUDUSD in the Asian-into-London session since he "came up in 2019" [2:01].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- The moving average period/type isn't given. Other videos from the 2023 era mention 8/14 EMAs, but this video only says "a moving average".
- "Four confluences too late" [10:02] is garbled. It most likely means he got in after too many confluences / too late.
- The captions say "GDP AUD", which is GBPAUD.
- No timeframes, stop sizes, entry order types or management rules are given.

## 15. Key quotes
- "Believe it or not it is actually Monday through Wednesday" [2:01]
- "Monday night, that Asian session going into London session, that is a very very volatile session and that is the main session" [2:01]
- "Simply put it's price action" [3:01]
- "I don't use too many indicators, use just one, a moving average, I use psychological levels ... key levels are very very very important and I trade trend lines" [4:01]
- "You can't really get into big trades with a small account unless you slowly scale that account and slowly raise the lot size" [6:02]
- "I can enter a trade and be okay with the market going against me 20 pips because I'm aiming for 140" [10:02]
- "Is it a one to three risk reward ratio or one two ... do I have two or three confluences?" [15:07]

# 2023-09-01 — How I Made $17,000 in 24 Hours (LIVE RESULTS SHOWN)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CdTM3LGt0dc |
| Uploaded | 2023-09-01 |
| Length | ~14:30 (last caption marker 14:12) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2023: 8/14 MA crossover, psychological levels, higher-low structure, consolidation box breakout and retest, TP1/TP2 |

## 1. What this video covers
He breaks down three trades from the last ~30 hours that made about $17K: a gold long (still open, about $15–17K up), an AUDUSD long and a GBPAUD short (together about $2K, closed). The technical logic: a double bottom or congestion at a level, then a moving average crossover, then higher lows, then entry, with targets at psychological levels and TP1/TP2. For GBPAUD he adds a consolidation box at a major psychological level, a break, and a retest. The second half is psychology and advice, and he stresses trading only one or two pairs you know well. This is the 2023 version of his strategy.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Holding period:** short swing trades lasting hours to about a day. Gold was held overnight and is still open; the GBPAUD retest came about 28 hours after the break on the 4H.
- **Timeframes:** 4H for the GBPAUD box and retest [8:06]; daily for the long-term GBPAUD view [7:04]. Other timeframes are not stated.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Gold is "my favorite pair". He used it in 2019–2020 to flip $5K to six figures [1:02].
- GBPAUD is his "second favorite pair". AUDUSD is third [6:04–7:04].
- He advocates trading "one or two pairs so you know everything it does" [1:02].
- He mentions US30 being popular now, but gold remains his #1 [1:02].
- **Sessions:** at the time of recording it's "10:30, New York session" [6:04]. AUDUSD moves slowly once it's out of the Asian and London sessions. He used to trade AUDUSD "more towards a later Asian session because a lot of the news [for] Australia comes out at nine o'clock, eight o'clock of our time" [6:04].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Moving average crossover,** "blue is over the red" [2:02]. The periods are not stated in this video; elsewhere he uses the 8 and 14 EMAs.
- **Psychological levels:** gold 1900 as major resistance and 1885 as a support area; AUDUSD around 0.6450–0.6500 [?]; GBPAUD 1.95 and 1.93, with 2.00 and 2.20 as long-term levels [2:02–9:07].
- **Trendlines,** including a long-term gold trendline back to the 2067 high [4:03].
- **Boxes** drawn around congestion and consolidation [7:04].
- **Candlestick reading:** marubozu, wicks [5:03, 13:07].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Know the pair intimately; trade one or two pairs [1:02].
2. Mark the major psychological levels or key areas [2:02, 7:04].
3. At a level, look for a double bottom or congestion, where price refuses to break [1:02].
4. Wait for the MA crossover in the new direction [2:02].
5. Look for a higher low (for a long), which shows "market sentiment has switched over" [2:02]. Keep tracking higher lows to confirm "structure has not been broken yet" [2:02].
6. **Range variant (GBPAUD):** at a major psychological level, an immediate reversal followed by congestion. "Simply draw a box on it and see what it wants to do" [7:04]. Expect a fake wick beyond the level before the real move. Check whether the MAs cross before the box breaks [8:06].
7. Enter (see §6). Set TP1 and TP2 at psychological levels.
8. Check the fundamental context, e.g. gold as a safe haven if a US recession is confirmed [4:03].

## 6. Entry rules
- **Gold:** after the double bottom/congestion, the MA crossover and the first higher low, he called the rally at 1897, below 1900 resistance, before any break. He entered at 1925 after another higher low [2:02].
- **AUDUSD:** "if it can safely cross above [the psych level]… I would be entering". It crossed, retested, then printed a big marubozu candle [5:03].
- **GBPAUD:** enter on the box breakout, or on the retest. "Every time price breaks an area it is going to retest, no matter what" [8:06]. The retest came about 28 hours after the break on the 4H.
- **Don't FOMO.** If you missed the breakout, wait for the retest [8:06].
- **Order types:** not stated. These were called as signals at a price, implying market orders.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- No numbers are given. He notes that "the pip count is very very different on gold" and that gold stops "could be a little bit tricky" [3:03].
- Not otherwise covered in this video.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- **Gold:** 1940 (TP1) and 1950 (TP2). TP1 was hit, and he may close TP2 manually [2:02–4:03]. The longer-term trendline target is 2067, but he doesn't expect a break of the highs until a confirmed US recession [4:03].
- **AUDUSD:** he took full profit after about 60–70 pips. He says you could take half or 70% now and let the rest run [5:03].
- **GBPAUD:** TP1 at 1.95 flat, taken. TP2 at 1.93 (about 180 more pips), only if price closes below a nearby wick [9:07].

## 9. Trade management
- **Partials:** "you can take half profits now, maybe 70% profits now, let the others run" [5:03].
- **Manual close for life events:** he may close gold manually because he is travelling and announcing a pregnancy, and "may not have the psychology to try to monitor this" [4:03].
- **Exit warning for GBPAUD:** "you better [watch the] higher low. If you're in GA maybe get out if you didn't take your profit at TP1" [14:12].
- **Drawdown:** expect drawdown and wait it out; don't cut immediately [11:07].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Results:** up about $15K open on gold (fluctuating between $13.9K and $15.4K while recording, about $17K at one point) plus $2K closed on the other trades [0:02, 4:03, 6:04].
- Lot sizes and risk %: not covered in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: XAUUSD long (1:02–4:03)
- **Context:** gold had dropped to about 1885 and others were calling 1850 and 1800. He disagreed.
- **Setup:** a double bottom with "so much congestion" around 1885, where the market was "not breaking below here whatsoever". The MAs crossed (blue over red), then a higher low formed. He called "gold is going to rally" in his free Telegram at 1897, below the 1900 major resistance. Price broke above 1900, came down and made another higher low (structure intact), then made one more higher low, and he called the signal publicly.
- **Entry:** 1925.
- **Stop:** not stated.
- **Targets:** TP1 1940, TP2 1950. He said "I don't think you'll have much of a drawdown" [3:03].
- **Management:** TP1 has been hit. He is holding the rest toward 1950 and may close manually [4:03].
- **Result:** "virtually no drawdown". Up about $15–17K open at recording.

### Trade 2: AUDUSD long (5:03–6:04)
- **Setup:** higher lows and a trendline. "Another higher low, you could have gotten in right here." The trigger: "if it can safely cross above six four… [0.6450 / 0.6500 ?] I would be entering". It crossed above, retested, then printed a "massive marubozu candlestick" that shot up about 60–70 pips, "which is a lot for AUDUSD".
- **Entry:** after the cross and retest; price not stated.
- **Stop:** not stated.
- **Exit:** "I took my full profits" (about 60–70 pips). He thinks it could go higher.
- **Result:** win. AUDUSD is pulling back as he records.

### Trade 3: GBPAUD short (6:04–9:07)
- **Context:** long term he thinks GBPAUD wants to get back above 2.00 (it was at 2.20 in 2009 and pre-pandemic). In the short term there is a clear break of structure at a major psychological level.
- **Setup:** at the major psychological level, an immediate reversal, then congestion and consolidation; he drew a box. Price wicked but didn't break above the level and stayed in the range. The MAs crossed bearish before the box broke. Price broke down out of the box and retested it about 28 hours later on the 4H.
- **Entry:** you could have entered on the breakout, or on the retest. He called his signal later (he wasn't watching GBPAUD at the breakout). Price stayed below the MAs and retested "off of here".
- **Stop:** not stated.
- **Targets:** TP1 at 1.95 flat, which he took. TP2 at 1.93, about 180 pips further, but only if price closes below the nearby wick.
- **Result:** win at TP1 with "barely any drawdown". At the end he warns that a higher low is forming, so get out if you are still in [14:12].

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Trade one or two pairs. "I always advocate to trade one or two pairs so you know everything it does" [1:02].
- A higher low after congestion means a shift. "When you see congestion… and then it gives you a higher low understand market sentiment has switched over" [2:02].
- Box the consolidation at major levels. "Simply draw a box on it and see what it wants to do" [7:04].
- Expect fakeouts. "If price is going to break a key level it's going to try to wick above it, come back down… fake people out" [7:04].
- Breaks get retested. "Every time price breaks an area it is going to retest, no matter what" [8:06].
- Take partials. "You can take half profits now, maybe 70% profits now, let the others run" [5:03].
- Let trades play out through drawdown. "Understanding drawdown happens and then again getting back into profit" [11:07].
- Any style works. "All trading styles work, it just depends on who's implementing them" [10:07].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- Gold was the pair he used in 2019–2020 to take $5K to six figures [1:02].
- He used AUDUSD "four or five years ago" in the later Asian session around Australian news [6:04].
- No change to the strategy itself is stated.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- None of the three trades have stated stop losses or lot sizes.
- The MA periods are not stated here (they are the 8/14 EMAs per other 2023 videos).
- The AUDUSD level is garbled ("six four uh 595… six four six hundred"), probably 0.6450–0.6500 [?].
- The GBPAUD entry price and the major psychological level (probably 1.97–2.00) are shown on chart, not stated [?].
- The "$17K in 24 hours" headline vs. the $15K shown: the total moved during recording.
- "Eight o'clock, nine o'clock of our time" for Australian news is presumably US Eastern pm [?].

## 15. Key quotes
- "I always advocate to trade one or two pairs so you know everything it does." [1:02]
- "We have so much congestion right here, moving averages crossed over, market is not breaking below here whatsoever." [1:02]
- "Anytime you see congestion… and then it gives you a higher low, understand market sentiment has switched over." [2:02]
- "I am getting in at 1925. I do have it going to 1940, 1950." [2:02–3:03]
- "You can take half profits now, maybe 70% profits now, let the others run." [5:03]

- "If price is going to break a key level it's going to try to wick above it, come back down a little bit, fake people out." [7:04]

## 1. What this video covers
This is mostly a personal story: being on Big Brother hurt his trading, and an argument with his wife pushed him to commit fully. The strategy-relevant part is his list of "a-ha" factors from late 2018. He discovered psychological levels in 2017. He studied Sean Lee's public 5K→630K challenge every day for 2–3 months: the lot sizes, entry timing and pairs, which is where his GBPAUD and gold focus comes from. He stopped over-studying technicals and focused on risk management. And he studied for an hour a day. He says his technicals have not changed since he started. No trades are walked through.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- He describes himself in 2017–2018 as "a day trader" [5:02, 16:09].
- Timeframes: not covered in this video.

- **Pairs:** GBPAUD and gold, adopted from studying Sean Lee's challenge. "A lot of [the] pairs Sean trades, I traded GBPAUD, gold" [14:08].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)

- Other tools: not covered in this video.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Not covered in this video.

## 6. Entry rules
Not covered in this video.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
Not covered in this video.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
Not covered in this video.

## 9. Trade management
Not covered in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- The breakthrough was risk management, not technicals: "it was a psychology and risk management part of it all" [14:08–15:08].
- The pattern he fixed: making $5K in January and $6K in February, then losing in March and breaking even in April, because of "risking too much, not being okay with making 5K every single month… you make 5K one month, all of a sudden you're trying to double it the next one" [15:08].
- He studied Sean Lee's challenge lot sizes to learn how a small account can be compounded [14:08].
- **Growth claims:** "3K then 10K, and then that 14K challenge, check into 70K and then 80K and then 100K" [8:05]. He also says he had $14,000 after taxes and rent and "took that six thousand dollar account to 300K and 400K and 500K" (late 2018) [17:09–18:09]. The two figures are inconsistent as stated.
- **Rule on scaling life around income:** "once trading matches your bills, keep going. Once trading doubles your bills, then you can look at… reward[ing] myself" with something luxurious or a week or two off [9:05].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
No trades walked through.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Study a proven account's details: "I literally studied that challenge every single almost every single day for two three months straight… the lot sizes he used, when he entered, the pairs he used" [14:08].
- Don't over-study technicals: "I stopped studying technicals way too much… technicals has not changed" [14:08].
- Focus on risk: "stop focusing on technicals too much and focus on the risk management" [15:08].
- Study daily: "I made sure to at least make time one hour a day" [15:08].
- Don't take long breaks from the charts. During "think weeks" off he still opens a chart and keeps up with the news. A full isolation of about 8 months hurt his skill [4:02, 10:05].
- Reward yourself only once trading doubles your bills [9:05].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- 2017: he discovered psychological levels, and they became central [3:02].
- Late 2018: he shifted focus from technicals to risk management and psychology. He modelled his pairs (GBPAUD, gold) and lot-sizing on Sean Lee's 5K→630K challenge [13:08–15:08].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- He doesn't say what lot sizes, entry times or other specifics he took from Sean Lee's challenge.
