# 2022-04-24 — I May Move to An Island with No Day Trading Taxes... Secure The Swag (Episode 3)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=80mLMGmvr2s |
| Uploaded | 2022-04-24 |
| Length | 44:11 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 11:12–21:17 market segment; also 3:07–4:07 on "30 minutes a day") |
| Strategy era | 2022 — swing/position-leaning, news-driven market (between the 2021 "fewer, longer trades" shift and the 2023 EMA/Asian-session era) |

## 1. What this video covers
A vlog episode; the only trading content is a ~10-minute market segment recorded during the Russia–Ukraine war (gold at ~$2,000, so likely filmed in early March 2022 even though uploaded in April). He goes through gold, GBPAUD, GBPJPY, GBPUSD, EURUSD and AUDUSD, explaining that in a crisis the market trends hard in one direction (400–600 pips a day) and is driven "99.9%" by fundamentals and sentiment, so he reads FXStreet and DailyFX news before trading. Technically, he points to a daily **50 / 61.8 Fibonacci retracement**, a **counter-trendline break**, and level-to-level moves with retests; when there is no retracement, he drops to the **15-minute / 5-minute** to find an entry. Elsewhere he says he needs only "about 30 minutes a day or less" to analyze, place trades and let them run. The rest (academy launch, trading journal, his brother, Puerto Rico, industry collabs) is not strategy.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Time commitment: "All I need every single day is about 30 minutes or less ... to analyze the market, make some trades and then be out and let it run throughout the day" [4:07]. He says this is "not talking about scalping."
- Timeframes in the market segment: **weekly** for the big picture on gold [12:12], **daily** for the Fib retracement and counter-trendline structure [13:14], and **15-minute / 5-minute** to find an entry in a market with no real pullback [20:17].
- Bias held for weeks: "I am continuingly going to buy gold until the market tells me not to anymore" [14:14].
- He mentions taking "my full year of 2021 off" [33:25] — in context about his public/industry presence (the leader/teacher role), not explicitly trading.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Instruments discussed: gold (XAUUSD), GBPAUD ("GA"), GBPJPY ("GJ"), GBPUSD ("GU"), EURUSD ("EU"), AUDUSD ("AU"). His academy also covers US30.
- Sessions: not covered.
- Intermarket notes: gold is a safe haven — rises with uncertainty, war rumours, inflation/interest-rate uncertainty [13:14]; AUD strengthens with gold (Australia's gold mining) [18:17]; GBP hit by the crisis; EUR weak due to dependence on Russian gas [18:17].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Fibonacci retracement: **50 or 61.8** retracement on the daily [13:14].
- Counter-trendline (on the pullback) — break = continuation [13:14], [20:17].
- Horizontal levels and "borders" (zones); retests of broken levels [13:14], [15:14].
- News: **FXStreet** and **DailyFX** news tabs — "this is your best friend" [15:14]. Reads as many articles as possible before trading [17:15].
- Chart themes: his real chart is white ("swag chart"); black for camera; also an "upscale" layout [12:12], [20:17].
- No moving average or other indicator mentioned in this video.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Assess the macro/news backdrop first. In crisis periods (Brexit, the pandemic, Russia–Ukraine) the market trends in one direction for weeks; technical levels still matter but "don't expect it to just hold up like it would during a regular week" [15:14].
2. Read FXStreet/DailyFX articles and what leaders/analysts say (sentiment analysis) and form a directional bias [15:14–17:15].
3. Check the weekly chart for the big picture level-to-level path (e.g. gold 1870 → 1915 → 2000) [12:12].
4. On the daily, identify the pullback: a 50 or 61.8 Fib retracement, then a break of the counter-trendline, then a move to the next level, retest of the broken level, next level, etc. [13:14].
5. Compare with similar past cycles (March 2020 pandemic: "the pairs that went down are going down again; the pairs that shot up are going up again") [17:15].
6. If there is a real retracement, trade the counter-trendline break in the trend direction (AUDUSD example) [20:17].
7. If there is no real retracement, drop to the 15m/5m and look for "the smallest chance of retracement" to enter [20:17].

## 6. Entry rules
- With-trend entry after a pullback: daily 50/61.8 retracement, then a **counter-trendline break** in the trend direction [13:14], [20:17].
- No-pullback markets: "You got to go to a 15 minute, five minute time frame and just make sure you are seeing like where there's the smallest chance of retracement" [20:17].
- Fundamental filter: trade in the direction the news/sentiment supports (buy gold, buy AUD, sell GBP and EUR in this crisis).
- Order type: not covered.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
Not covered in this video.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
Targets stated as outlooks (no specific trade):
- Gold: break above 2075 (the August 2020 all-time high), "I do think we will see gold reach 2100" [14:14].
- GBPAUD: still falling; a "barrier" lower [shown on chart, not stated]; recalls the 2020 drop "from 2.05 to 1.70, about 2,500 to 2,700 pips" [17:15].
- GBPJPY: down to a marked level [shown on chart, not stated] [17:15].
- GBPUSD: 1.27 [18:17].
- EURUSD: possibly parity 1.00 "maybe even 0.99" [19:17].
- AUDUSD: 0.77 [20:17].

## 9. Trade management
- Hold the directional bias until price says otherwise: "continuingly going to buy gold until the market tells me not to anymore" [14:14].
- Expect gold to correct when the crisis eases (as in 2020 when vaccine news arrived) [14:14].
- No specific break-even/partial rules.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
Not covered in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: Gold (XAUUSD) long bias (12:12–17:15)
- Recalls his earlier call: from 1870 he said gold would reach 2000; at 1915 he repeated it; it reached 2000 (video "Forex is Art, change my mind").
- Weekly: clean level-to-level steps [levels shown on chart, not stated].
- Daily: up-move, pullback to a 50 or 61.8 retracement (Fib not drawn on screen), break of a small counter-trendline, run to the next level, retest, next level, retest, next level — the latest leg hasn't retested yet.
- Fundamental reasoning: safe-haven demand during the war/inflation uncertainty.
- Plan: keep buying; targets above 2075, ~2100. Notes one analyst sees 2100 but not 2800.
- Entry/stop/result: not given.

### Trade 2: GBPAUD short bias (17:15–18:17)
- Still heading down, "a clear-cut free-for-all" like March 2020 when it dropped from ~2.05 to ~1.70. AUD strong with gold, GBP sinking. No entry/stop.

### Trade 3: GBPJPY short bias (17:15)
- "I have a barrier down here that I do think GJ will come down to" [level shown on chart, not stated].

### Trade 4: GBPUSD short bias (18:17)
- He had called GU to 1.40 before the war — "the one thing I was wrong about." Now dropping; he sees 1.27.

### Trade 5: EURUSD short bias (18:17–19:17)
- Euro weakened by dependence on Russian gas; articles see no recovery until the war ends; possible 1.00 or 0.99.

### Trade 6: AUDUSD long via counter-trendline break (20:17)
- Unlike others, AUDUSD had "a real-life retracement." Look for a break of the counter-trendline and trade it upward; target 0.77. Entry/stop not given.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- In crisis markets, fundamentals dominate: "During Brexit or during a pandemic or during Russia and Ukraine, the market is literally 99.9999 [%] affected by fundamentals" [15:14] (he later softens it to say sentiment analysis is what factors in fundamentals [16:14]).
- Levels still count but may not hold as usual: "draw all your borders and draw trend lines ... but don't expect it to just hold up like it would during a regular week" [15:14].
- Read the news first: "I read as many articles as I can before I trade, and then I trade" [17:15].
- No pullback → lower timeframe: "you got to go to a 15 minute, five minute time frame" [20:17].
- Trade the counter-trendline break after a real retracement [20:17].
- Follow the market: "buy gold until the market tells me not to anymore" [14:14].
- 30 minutes a day is enough to analyze, place trades and let them run [4:07].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- No explicit strategy change stated. He says he takes "about 30 minutes or less" a day [4:07], consistent with fewer, longer-held trades rather than screen-watching.
- His academy content is updated yearly because "things change ... markets change, sentiments change" [2:07–3:07]; the 2022 program adds gold, US30, funded accounts/prop firms and "institutional trading."
- "I took my full year of 2021 off" [33:25] — said about stepping back from the industry/public role; not clearly a trading break.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- No entries, stops, lot sizes or results — this is market commentary, not trade execution.
- The Fib on gold isn't drawn ("I'm just not going to place it on"); he says "50 or 61.8" without specifying which.
- Exact levels for GBPAUD/GBPJPY barriers and gold's weekly steps are only on the chart.
- Filming date likely early March 2022 (gold at 2000, war just started) despite the April upload date.
- "Upscale" and "trading floor" are names of his TradingView layouts [?].

## 15. Key quotes
- "All I need every single day is about 30 minutes or less, and I'm fine." [4:07]
- "Any trader can clearly see that is a 50 or 61.8 retracement, goes up, breaks through this little counter trendline, then it goes all the way up to this next level." [13:14]
- "As long as there's a lot of uncertainty in the world ... gold will continue to go up." [13:14]
- "I am continuingly going to buy gold until the market tells me not to anymore." [14:14]
- "Look at FXStreet and DailyFX and click the little news tab ... this is your best friend." [15:14]
- "The market is literally 99.9999 [%] affected by fundamentals." [15:14]

- "You got to go to a 15 minute, five minute time frame and just make sure you are seeing where there's the smallest chance of retracement." [20:17]

# 2022-02-20 — Hitting a 10 Year Goal 🥺 Secure The Swag (Episode 2)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dWM8liTEXhA |
| Uploaded | 2022-02-20 |
| Length | 37:37 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 2:02–3:02 GBPAUD trade call; related trading remarks 3:02–5:03 and 15:28–18:29) |

## 1. What this video covers
A vlog episode (profits/withdrawals montage, 10-year goal, academy features, merch, teeth whitening, penthouse incident). The strategy content is short: a GBPAUD short he called on a webinar — wait for a **real candle close** (not just a wick/break) below a level, confirming a **lower high** that doesn't take out the prior high, stop "a few pips above" that high, fixed take-profit — plus trading-mindset rules (trust your analysis but stay flexible, don't dismiss small profits). He gives a GBPUSD 1.40 call (4–8 weeks), and says the academy's "student of the month" will be judged on % growth with good R:R and no over-leveraging. He also summarises how 2020, 2021 and 2022 differed for him.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Directional calls held for weeks: GBPAUD "going down for a very, very long time"; GBPUSD to 1.40 "sometime in the next four to eight weeks" [3:02].
- Timeframe of the GBPAUD entry: a candle close below the level — "not just below an hour ago but actual legitimate close" (suggests he wanted at least an hourly close, possibly higher) [2:02]. Exact timeframe not stated.
- Profits range from $250 to $25,000 per day in his account [4:03–5:03].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- GBPAUD ("GA") — main trade; GBPUSD ("GU") outlook; gold (his "Gold Club"/"Gold VIP" group).
- Sessions: not covered.
- New in 2022: group trading with members on webinars — "we really try to trade together, which is something that is new that we did not do in 2020 or 2021" [4:03].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Horizontal level/structure only; no indicators mentioned.
- Journal/tracking: Myfxbook mentioned elsewhere; academy leaderboard software in development [14:25–15:28].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Hold a higher-timeframe directional bias (GBPAUD down) [3:02].
2. Mark the level below current price and the last high (the potential lower high) [2:02].
3. Wait for a legitimate candle close below the level, with price not taking out the last high → confirms "a great lower high" [2:02].
4. Enter; stop a few pips above the last high; take profit at a fixed level [2:02–3:02].
5. If price instead reverses up, switch to trading the bullish move — be flexible [3:02].

## 6. Entry rules
- Close, not break: "If price can give me a close below here I'd be very, very happy — not a break below, but ... actual legitimate close below here" [2:02].
- The close must come without price taking out the previous high (the lower high stays intact): "if price breaks below here and does not take this high out" [2:02].
- Order type: not stated (reads as market entry after the close).

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- "This would be my stop loss, just maybe a few pips above that last [high]" [2:02].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- GBPAUD: a single TP level; the caption says "0.7250," which does not fit GBPAUD (≈1.8x at the time) — probably misheard [?] (see Gaps). "It did both" — he says the trade hit the TP (and the setup played out) [3:02].
- GBPUSD outlook: 1.40 within 4–8 weeks [3:02].

## 9. Trade management
- Take every profit size seriously: "stop negating the lower profits just for that home run and try to manage both of them" [5:03].
- No break-even/partial/trailing specifics.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Academy "student of the month" will be judged not by most money but by "who literally grew from this percentage to this percentage with a good risk to reward ratio and didn't try to over leverage, didn't get margin called" [17:29]. He criticises putting "a 10 lot on a trade" and losing the account [17:29].
- Withdrawals shown: $25,000, $3,000, $1,000 [1:02]. Goal: $1.7M trading for the year "up until 3 million" [14:25].
- Risk %: not stated.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPAUD short (webinar call) (2:02–3:02)
- Context: called on a webinar "last night" (days before recording); GBPAUD in a long-running downtrend he'd been calling.
- Setup: wait for a legitimate candle close below a level [price shown on chart, not stated] while the last high is not taken out → a lower high.
- Entry: after that close [price not stated].
- Stop: a few pips above the last (lower) high.
- Target: one fixed TP — caption "0.7250" [?].
- Result: "it did both" — he says it reached the target (screenshots shown, figures not read out). He adds "obviously there's more trades than that."

### Trade 2: GBPUSD long outlook (3:02)
- "GU ... consolidating right now, going down a little bit, but ... sometime in the next four to eight weeks GU will be close to 1.40." No entry/stop. (In Episode 3 he admits this call was wrong after the Russia–Ukraine war started.)

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Wait for a real close: "not a break below ... but actual legitimate close below here" [2:02].
- Stop above the lower high: "my stop loss, just maybe a few pips above that last [high]" [2:02].
- Trust your analysis: "doesn't matter if you're right or wrong, just trust your analysis" [3:02].
- Be flexible: "I don't care if you are right in your analysis as long as you are right when it comes to the actual trade" [3:02].
- Don't dismiss small profits: "do not discriminate on the type of profits" [4:03].
- No over-leveraging; judge success by % growth with good R:R [17:29].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- "2020 was spent teaching you guys ... 2021 I spent over analyzing things, trying to be a perfectionist and then getting mind-boggled by all the things that other people were saying, and now in 2022 all I'm focusing on is trading and transparency" [15:28].
- New in 2022: trading together with members live on webinars — "something that is new that we did not do in 2020 or 2021" [4:03].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- GBPAUD TP "0.7250" cannot be a GBPAUD price; may be a mishearing (e.g. "1.8250"?) or a different pair [?].
- The timeframe of the "legitimate close" is unclear ("not just below an hour ago" is garbled).
- Entry level, stop price and pip result are only on screen.

## 15. Key quotes

# 2021-03-06 — An EASY One Hour Japanese Candlestick Guide For Beginners (Full Forex Guide)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dIhU-khdVYw |
| Uploaded | 2021-03-06 |
| Length | ~62:00 (last caption marker [61:53]; exact length not in transcript) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | Early 2021, intraday/swing level-to-level (psychological levels + candlestick confirmation on 1H-4H, entry refined on 15m) |

## 1. What this video covers
His dedicated candlestick video. It is less a pattern catalogue and more a set of personal rules for **where and how** he uses candles. He only reads candlestick patterns at a **psychological level, trend line or Fibonacci retracement**. He wants the pattern confirmed on a **higher timeframe (1H/2H/4H)**, then drops to the **15-minute for an entry** on structure or a second pattern. He trades **level to level** (e.g. 0.78 → 0.79) in the direction of the 1-2 week outlook. He also covers the body-to-wick ratio as a momentum read, stop placement with "breathing room", and using **price alerts** at every psychological level instead of watching charts. This reflects his early-2021 intraday/swing level-to-level approach. The first few minutes (open/high/low/close, wick vs body) are textbook basics.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **Higher timeframe for confirmation:** a pattern on the **1H or 2H** "tells me exactly what price is going to do for the next few days". On the **4H or daily** it tells him "what price is going to do for the next week or week and a half". [13:18–14:19]
- **His working flow:** confirmation on the **2H or 4H** (sometimes 1H), then "scale down to a 15 minute time frame and see the structure being created". [29:32–30:32, 54:47]
- He won't act on a 15m pattern at a psych level alone: "it's easy to get faked out." [29:32]
- **Direction horizon:** he wants to know what price will do over the next 24h, 48h, week, two weeks or month, and then trades only in that direction. "Some trades I may hold for a week straight." [54:47–55:48]
- He says he's not someone who trades "every 10 minutes". He places the trade with stop and target and walks away. [53:41–54:47]
- He started trading in 2015, and early on was "obsessed" with 1m/5m/15m candles. [6:11]

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **AUDUSD** examples at 0.80, 0.79, 0.78 and the 0.77500 mid/quarter level.
- **GBPAUD** ("GA") example at 1.77/1.78-1.81. He mentions GA 1.80 and GU 1.40 as major levels he talks about often. [32:33, 34:35]
- GBPJPY is mentioned only as a pair where a 5-pip stop is unrealistic. [40:37]
- **News:** don't trade around fundamental announcements, "because it can easily stop and wick you out". [18:20]
- Sessions/times of day: not covered.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Psychological levels** are his core framework. He trades between whole-number levels (e.g. 0.77, 0.78, 0.79). [9:12, 33:34]
  - **"New set of 10 digits"** levels (1.70, 1.80, 1.90; 0.70, 0.80, 0.90) are "a lot stronger" than 1.76/1.77-type levels. Price will "stop… consolidate… move around" there. [34:35]
  - **Quarter levels** are every **250 pips** (e.g. 1.10000, 1.12500, 1.15000; 0.70, 0.72500…), and he also uses their **mid-levels** (e.g. AUDUSD 0.77500). [37:36–38:36]
- **Trend lines and Fibonacci retracement** (38.2/50/61.8) as the other "major areas". [9:12, 22:25]
- **Moving averages:** "the MA crossover" is a confluence ("chances are the MA has done crossed over"). Periods are not stated here. He references his previous MA video (8/14 EMA).
- **TradingView alerts** at every nearby psychological level. When price consolidates he draws a box and sets alerts about **10 pips above and below**. [49:39–52:41]
- **Favourite patterns:** he uses "the same 10 or 11" patterns only. Named in this video: bullish/bearish engulfing, morning star, evening star, hammer, shooting star, doji, tweezer top/bottom, marubozu, head and shoulders/inverse, plus a "momentum candle". [10:13, 12:16, 48:39]

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Mark accurate **psychological levels / support-resistance, trend lines (and Fib)**. "You can know all the candlesticks in the world… if you don't have the correct levels on… you will be faked out." [35:35]
2. Work out the **overall direction on the higher timeframe** (4H/daily, weekly), e.g. "from 1.76 to 1.80… 400 pips". Expect it to come in steps such as "up 100 pips, down 40, up 50, down 20". [15:19]
3. Ignore candles "in the middle of nowhere". If price is already mid-move between levels, wait for the next level. [9:12–11:13, 45:38–46:38]
4. Set **alerts** at the surrounding psychological levels and walk away. [49:39]
5. When price reaches a level, look for a **candlestick formation** there. Single, double or triple doesn't matter. Use the **2H/4H** (sometimes 1H) for the confirmation. [12:16, 29:32]
6. Check the candle's **body-to-wick ratio**. Little or no wick means strong momentum. [41:37–43:38]
7. Get a third confluence, e.g. an MA crossover. "I only need three confirmations." [12:16]
8. **Drop to the 15m** and look for **structure** (a lower high/higher low, or a break of the last lower high/higher low) and/or another candlestick formation for the entry. [30:32]
9. Enter, put the stop a few pips beyond the pattern, and target the **next psychological level**. [31:32–33:34]
10. If the level just consolidates, box it, set alerts ~10 pips beyond the box, and re-assess when it breaks. [50:40–52:41]

## 6. Entry rules
- Enter only when a candlestick formation prints **at a psychological level, trend line or Fib retracement**, combined with another confluence (e.g. an MA crossover). "That's enough of a confirmation for me to get in, period. I only need three confirmations." [12:16]
- "Any type of bullish candlestick formation at these levels, I'm getting in", as long as it is "strong enough and not on like a one minute time frame". [19:21]
- **React, don't anticipate.** Don't wait for a specific pattern (e.g. an evening star) to form at a level, because you may get a bearish engulfing instead and miss the move. [13:18]
- **Higher-TF pattern, then 15m entry.** Confirm on 2H/4H, then enter on the 15m on structure (a lower high for shorts) or a second pattern (e.g. a 15m bearish engulfing). [30:32]
- **In-trend re-entries:** pullbacks to a psych/mid-level or a Fib 38.2/50/61.8 within the bigger move are counter-trends to buy or sell into. Example: a morning star at the 50% retracement means "we're looking to get back in". [16:20–17:20, 22:25–23:25]
- **Aggressive vs conservative:** enter on the confirmation candle right after the pattern (with a stop that has breathing room), or wait for price to **break the pattern's low/high (break, retest, continuation)**. [40:37–41:37]
- **Missed entries:** don't chase (no FOMO, e.g. after a friend says GBPUSD dropped 100 pips). Wait for price to create more structure, such as a lower high plus a bearish pattern, then a break. [45:38–48:39]
- **Scaling in:** "every single retracement, scale in, scale in… partial profit, get in, get out", in line with the trend found on the 4H. [55:48]

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Put the stop **beyond where the pattern printed**: below it for longs, above it for shorts. (He misspeaks "below an uptrend… above… on an uptrend".) [32:33]
- AUDUSD example: "your stop loss is a few pips above here, or even above here to be safe", i.e. above the 15m entry structure or above the 4H tweezer top. [28:29, 31:32]
- **No 2-5 pip stops:** "do not have a two pip stop loss or a five pip stop loss." "You can't have a five pip stop loss and then get wicked out every time… on GBPJPY, be more realistic." [31:32, 40:37]
- He mocks the idea that "if you have a stop loss over 10 pips you don't know how to trade". [56:49]
- Expect drawdown after entry: "there's no such thing as an absolute perfect entry… we have to anticipate some form of drawdown." [39:36]

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- **Level to level:** "All I do is trade the levels", e.g. 0.78 → 0.79. He won't assume a 300-pip run. [33:34]
- If price is still strong as it approaches the next level, he expects it to continue to the next level (e.g. "maybe that 500 level or the 1.80 level"). [33:34–34:35]
- After a TP, go back to the 4H and read the next candle at the level, e.g. a shooting star with no lower wick at 0.79 means "it's going to break past this .79 level and at least get to 0.78". [28:29–29:32]
- Fixed R:R, partial percentages: not specified (see §10).

## 9. Trade management
- **Alerts instead of screen-watching.** Alerts sit at every psych level. If consolidation follows, box it and set alerts about 10 pips above and below. When an alert fires he checks the candles. [49:39–52:41]
- "I'm placing my trade, I'm putting my stop loss and take profit and I'm walking away." [53:41]
- **Hold through retracements.** A pullback to 38.2/50/61.8 inside the move is "simply retracing… we're not looking to get out or get scared". [22:25]
- **Scale in on retracements and take partials**, in the direction of the 4H trend. [55:48]
- Some trades are held "for a week straight". [55:48]
- Break-even, trailing and weekend rules: not covered.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- He says stop loss, take profit, R:R and **lot size** deserve "the same level of importance" as entries. He warns about **over-leveraging**, "a way bigger lot size than you should have", and blowing the account. [55:48–57:49]
- The R:R remark is garbled in the captions: "your risk to reward ratio isn't 1 to 3 every single time, so that means you have to win you know 80% of your trades". He seems to be saying that a poor R:R forces a very high win rate, which implies he favours about **1:3** [?]. [56:49]
- Small accounts ($300/$500) are no excuse to trade low timeframes. Still analyse on the 1H+ to avoid being wicked out. [59:50]
- No risk % or lot numbers are given.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: Unnamed pair, longs at psychological levels (9:12–11:13)
- 4H chart, several bounces off psychological levels (pair not named; levels [shown on chart, not stated]):
  - a **bullish engulfing** right on a psych level, then "all the way up";
  - a **morning star** at a psych level, then up;
  - a **tweezer bottom followed by a momentum candle**, then up;
  - consolidation then a **hammer**, then up.
- Price moves "in the middle of nowhere" between levels are shown as places he would not enter.
- No entry, stop or target numbers are given.

### Trade 2: Whiteboard long, bullish engulfing at key level, re-entry at 50% (20:22–23:25)
- Drawn scenario. A bearish candle closes into a key level, then a bullish engulfing prints at the level (with "the MA… crossed over"), giving a long entry.
- Price rises and retraces to the 38.2/50/61.8. Hold the first trade.
- A **morning star** (red candle, doji, green candle of similar length) forms at the **50% retracement**. Re-enter / add, aiming for the higher-timeframe target.

### Trade 3: AUDUSD short, 4H tweezer top at 0.80 → 15m entry → 0.79 (26:29–29:32)
- 4H: a clean **tweezer top** at **0.80000** (no pattern had formed at 0.79/0.78 on the way up; price "bulldozed" through those).
- The tweezer top's level is given as **0.79730**. He drops to the **15m**, where a **bearish engulfing** forms at about 0.79730 and price then makes a **lower high**. Entry: short there, after the tweezer top completes.
- **Stop:** "a few pips above here, or even above here to be safe", i.e. above the 15m structure or above the tweezer top.
- **Target:** **0.79000**. It hit.
- Follow-up: back on the 4H, a **shooting star with no lower wick** near 0.79 gave strong momentum, so he expected a break of 0.79 and a move to at least **0.78**.
- Pips/$: not stated. About 73 pips from 0.79730 to 0.79000 (calculated, not stated).

### Trade 4: GBPAUD long, hammer at psych level, 1.78 → 1.79 and beyond (32:33–34:35)
- He starts with "GU" and switches to "GA" (GBPAUD). The downtrend made lower highs and lower lows. The first reversal sign is price **taking out the last lower high** (a structure break).
- At the **1.78 level** (captions also say "177 level" [?]) price printed a **hammer** and "bulldozed above".
- He trades level to level, **1.78 → 1.79**, and does not assume 1.78 → 1.81. On the 15m the momentum kept growing approaching 1.79, so he expected "maybe that 500 level [1.7950] or the 1.80 level".
- Stop and exact entry: [shown on chart, not stated].

### Trade 5: AUDUSD short, evening star at 0.77500 mid/quarter level (37:36–45:38)
- An **evening star** at **0.77500** (mid-level of the 250-pip quarter grid), followed by a bearish confirmation candle. Then price **immediately retraced**.
- **Aggressive:** short on the confirmation candle with a stop above the pattern with breathing room. A 2-pip stop there would have been taken out before the move.
- **Conservative:** wait for the **break of the original low** (break, retest, continuation).
- The breakdown came on a strong, nearly wickless bearish candle that broke a small neckline after a **lower high**. That was the "get in" signal. He says to keep re-entering if missed ("get in, get in, get in").
- Targets/result: not stated (implied next level down).

### Trade 6: Whiteboard alert workflow at 0.77 (50:40–52:41)
- An alert sits at the 0.77 psych level. When it fires he checks for a bullish pattern.
- If there is only small, choppy candles, he boxes the consolidation and sets an alert just above it (e.g. 0.7710) and about 10 pips below. When the box breaks, he looks for a higher low or trend-line setup.
- Applied back to the AUDUSD 0.79 example, an alert at the level would have shown him the tweezer top and shooting star.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Don't read every low-timeframe candle: "do not get caught up in every single candlestick." [8:12]
- Only read candles at major areas: "I'm only looking for one thing… what it does on a major area, a psychological level, a trend line or fibonacci retracement." [9:12]
- No mid-range entries: "If price is in the middle of those levels and it's already moving… I'm not looking to enter." [9:12]
- Candles need support/resistance: "Candlestick patterns are a main confluence with me but… they're nothing without support and resistance." [11:13]
- Three confirmations are enough: "I only need three confirmations for me to get in." [12:16]
- Don't anticipate a pattern: "you can't anticipate that… all you can do is react to the market." [13:18]
- Confirm on the higher timeframe: "I need that confirmation on a two hour or a four hour time frame, once I get that then I can scale down to a 15 minute." [29:32–30:32]
- Trade level to level: "all I do is trade the levels." [33:34]
- Respect round numbers: levels like 1.70/1.80 are "a lot stronger" than 1.76/1.77. [34:35]
- Give the stop room: "do not have a two pip stop loss or a five pip stop loss." [31:32]
- Read the wick: "the less of a wick you see, the more confirmation that it's going to go up." [42:37]
- Don't chase: "you got in when the move was already created." [47:38]
- Use alerts: "a way I can combat this is by setting alerts at every single psychological level around me." [49:39]
- Avoid news: don't trade around fundamental announcements. [18:20]
- Don't over-leverage: watch your lot size and R:R as closely as your entries. [56:49]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- In 2015, when he started, he was "so obsessed" with 1m/5m/15m candles that his bias flipped every 15 minutes. He now looks for confirmation on the 1H-4H and only uses the 15m for entries. [6:11]
- He has moved to long-form, detailed academy videos and is rebuilding the academy. That is a content change, not a strategy change.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Prices for the 4H level examples (Trade 1) and the GBPAUD entry and stop are chart-only.
- **GBPAUD level:** the captions say both "177 level" and ".78". The hammer level is uncertain [?].
- **AUDUSD Trade 3:** he calls 0.79730 where the tweezer top "stopped at". It is unclear whether this is the pattern's low or the entry area.
- **R:R statement** at [56:49] is garbled. The 1:3 / 80% win-rate link is uncertain.
- MA periods are not stated here (presumably 8/14 EMA from the MA video).
- The stop-placement sentence at [32:33] misspeaks the directions. The intended meaning is below the pattern for longs and above it for shorts.
- No risk %, lot sizes, break-even or partial percentages.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I mainly pay attention to candlesticks when it's near a psychological level or a trend line, that is it." [8:12]
- "If I see some type of formation printed on a psychological level or a trend line or a fibonacci retracement mixed with another type of confluence like a moving average crossover… that's enough of a confirmation for me to get in, period." [12:16]
- "I like to find a confirmation on a higher time frame… it tells me where price is going to head for the next few weeks." [13:18]
- "If price for the next… two weeks is going up… I'm going to only look for buys, and if price is retracing that is only just counter trends for me to look for more entries." [14:19–15:19]
- "Find a candlestick pattern on a higher time frame and then once I have that confirmation, head down to a lower time frame for an entry." [30:32]
- "All I do is trade them levels, so 0.78 to 0.79." [33:34]
- "When price is near a new set of 10 digits like 1.70, 1.80, 1.90… those are a lot stronger." [34:35]
- "Quarter levels… it's basically just every 250 pips." [38:36]
- "Do not have a two pip stop loss or a five pip stop loss." [31:32]
- "The less of a wick you see, the more confirmation that it's going to go up." [42:37]

# 2021-02-22 — 39 Minutes of The Greatest Moving Average Video on YouTube (Full Guide)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UsbZgSzvpJI |
| Uploaded | 2021-02-22 |
| Length | ~39:00 (last caption marker [38:23]; exact length not in transcript) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | Early 2021, intraday/swing level-to-level (8/14 EMA crossover for entries, 20/50 EMA for bias, psychological levels and trend lines) |

## 1. What this video covers
A long moving-average guide. The first ~15 minutes are textbook material for beginners: what an MA is, SMA vs EMA, and the arithmetic for both. The rest covers how **he** uses MAs. He uses an **8 and 14 EMA crossover** (8 blue, 14 red) for entries and reversal spotting, judged on the **4H or daily**, and a **20 and 50 EMA** for long-term direction. MAs are always combined with psychological levels/support-resistance, trend lines and strong candles, and are only used in trending markets. Targets come from levels, never from MAs. This is his early-2021 approach. It already includes the 8/14 EMA pair that the project's era summary associates with 2023.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- He reads the **daily and 4H first** to get the direction "for the next week, the next two weeks", then trades in that direction. [19:16]
- **MA crossovers are judged on the 4H or daily**, "because it gives me an accurate confirmation on a higher time frame". [18:15–19:16]
- He warns against 15-minute crossovers, which give fake-outs. His example: entering on the 15m with a **10-pip stop** gets you stopped out by a retest. [19:16, 21:17]
- **Holding period:** "not that I'm going to hold it for three weeks. I personally like to hold some trades." For intraday traders, he says know the 1-3 week direction and "get out every two days, every three days, whenever price starts to retrace… take partial profit, take full profit, get back in". [20:17]
- He describes himself as an intraday trader "to a certain extent" still. As an intraday trader he holds for 1-4 hours ("I'm only trading for two hours… an hour… four hours, I'm not trading for four days or five days"). [27:18]
- If the weekly candle is expected to close bullish, he doesn't sell that week. [22:18]
- Daily charts in the video are used "only… because it's a lot easier… to give an example". [18:15, 29:19]

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- No pairs are named for the chart examples. The prices mentioned (1.34→1.29; 1.2145→~1.23-1.24; targets 1.19, 1.18) suggest a USD major such as GBPUSD or EURUSD [?]. GBPAUD and AUDUSD appear only in the arithmetic examples.
- Sessions: not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **His entry MAs:** **8 EMA (blue)** and **14 EMA (red)**, Exponential, on TradingView, saved as an indicator template ("8 and 14"). "I use a 8 and a 14 moving average… doesn't matter, I use it 8 and 14." [16:13] He says 9/15, 7/13, 10/20 and 20/50 are also fine for others.
  - Blue (8) crossing above red (14) means uptrend. Red crossing above blue means downtrend. [16:13]
- **His bias/direction MAs:** **20 EMA and 50 EMA**, "for a longer term direction of the market and to see where my key levels are going to be". He says 50/100 or 50/200 also work ("it's all preference"). [25:18, 30:20, 36:22]
- **Single-EMA option:** he also has a one-line template (captions: "aema"). Price above it means up, below means down. He says one line vs a crossover "is just a personal preference". [17:14]
- **Why EMA over SMA:** EMA reacts faster. On his example the 8/14 SMA crossed so late that price had already dropped "from 1.34 to 1.29… 500 pips you just missed". [24:18–25:18, 28:19–29:19]
- On longer settings he found the 20/50 **EMA** gave a cleaner trend read, while the 20/50 **SMA** gave false crossovers during consolidation. [25:18–27:18, 28:19]
- **Textbook-only (not his edge):** SMA = sum of closes ÷ periods. EMA = (today's close − yesterday's EMA) × multiplier + yesterday's EMA, with multiplier = 2 ÷ (periods + 1) (0.3333 for 5, 0.1818 for 10), seeded with an SMA. He works examples (16.6 → 16.73 → 18.46; GBPAUD 1.35440 SMA → 1.35293 EMA). [5:06–15:11]
- He uses the Fib tool only to find the **50% midpoint** of a range for a target, "not to use fibonacci at all". [35:21]

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Read the **daily and 4H** for direction over the next 1-3 weeks. [19:16]
2. Check the **20/50 EMA** for the long-term trend (e.g. blue over red since May 2020 = up, despite retracements). [25:18, 31:20]
3. Mark **psychological levels/support-resistance** and **trend lines**. A third bounce validates a trend line; two touches are only a "potential trend line". [31:20, 34:21]
4. Use market structure (break-of-structure ideas). A pop below a trend line that does not break the last higher low is not a break. A real break needs price to break the trend line, retest it, and take out the low. [32:20–33:20]
5. Treat moves against the 20/50 EMA bias as **retracements, not reversals**, and use them as entry areas. [36:22]
6. At a level or trend line, wait for the confluence stack (§6) and use the **8/14 EMA crossover** as the trigger or scale-in signal.
7. Set targets at levels: the prior high, the range midpoint, the next psychological levels (§8).
8. Flip bias only after structure breaks: lower highs and lower lows after a retest, and price going below the trend-line-break point. [37:22]
- Don't trade consolidation. When MAs are tangled in price, "there's no trend right now and you shouldn't be in a trade". [24:18]

## 6. Entry rules
- **MAs are never the sole reason:** "you shouldn't get in the trade just because there's a moving average crossover." They are combined with a trend-line bounce, a support/resistance bounce, or a bullish engulfing. [17:14–18:15]
- **The entry recipe:** "We're looking at a bounce off of support or resistance, or even a breakthrough… we're looking for a strong candlestick, not small candlestick… and then we mix the moving average with it." [23:18]
- **His three main confluences:** "trend line, support and resistance, and candlestick formations". [33:20]
- **Aggressive entry (trend-line long):** (1) a bounce off a valid trend line (third touch), (2) a bullish candle such as an engulfing or morning star, (3) an 8/14 EMA bull crossover. Enter there. [33:20]
- **Conservative entry:** wait for price to break above the nearby resistance/psych area and **retest it as support**, then go long. [34:21]
- **Scaling in:** on each pullback in the direction of the 20/50 bias, wait for a fresh 8/14 crossover in the trend direction and add. "The moving average just helps to scale in." [35:21–36:22] In a downtrend, a break of a counter-trend line is "another entry right here, scale in and then sell". [30:20]
- He ignores 8/14 crossovers against the 20/50 bias: "the reason we're not looking for it down here… based on the 20 and the 50 EMA… we know that price coming down is mere retracements." [36:22]
- Order type: not stated (examples read as entries after confirmation).

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- For a long after a bounce off support: "let me place my stop loss below it" (below the support). [20:17]
- For a counter-trend-line break sell: "stop loss above the last high". [30:20]
- Avoid tight 15-minute stops. A 10-pip stop on a 15m entry gets taken out by retests. [19:16]
- No fixed pip number of his own is given.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- "Moving averages don't tell you where your take profit should be… for take profit moving averages don't help with that. What helps with that: support and resistance, psychological levels." [30:20–31:20]
- **Prior high:** "price is so close to the high it made last month, that is a take profit area… we always want to make sure we take profit at the high, or partial profit at the high." [34:21–35:21]
- **Range midpoint:** use the Fib tool on the range to find the 50% point and take profit or partial profit there. Example: a range of 1.2145 to ~1.23-1.24 (captions: "1.23 about 430"), called "about a 200 pip range", gives a TP about **100 pips** from entry. [35:21]
- **Short targets after a bias flip:** psychological levels, plus "the trend line break's point B and point A", then lower psych levels such as **1.19 and 1.18**. [37:22–38:23]

## 9. Trade management
- Scale in on pullbacks with 8/14 crossovers in the trend direction (§6). Scale out and take partial and full profits. "I like to hold some trades and scale in, scale out, partial profit, full profit." [22:18]
- For intraday holders: exit when price starts to retrace, then re-enter in the bigger direction. [20:17]
- Don't close a good trade in panic on a lower-timeframe pullback to near break-even. That is a retracement, not a reversal. [21:17]
- Bias flip rule: if price breaks structure, takes out the low, makes lower highs and lower lows after a retest, and goes below the trend-line-break point, he switches to sells and waits for the 8/14 red-over-blue crossover to enter. [37:22]
- Break-even, alerts and weekend holding: not covered in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
Not covered in this video. The only figure is the 10-pip 15m stop he uses as a bad example.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: Unnamed pair long, daily 8/14 EMA crossover off a psychological level (17:14–23:18)
- Daily chart (used for ease; he would judge the crossover on the 4H or daily).
- Setup: a bounce off "this psychological level, this key area of support", a bullish engulfing-type candle, a doji, then a **marubozu** breaking above the MAs, then the **8 over 14 crossover**. "That signifies an entry." [18:15]
- The crossover never crossed back "until it was clearly heading down". He compares this with 15m crossovers in the same period, which faked out twice. [21:17–22:18]
- Stop: below the support. Target: not stated. Prices: [shown on chart, not stated].
- A single-EMA version of the same trade shows the same bounce, marubozu, and price closing above the EMA. [24:18]

### Trade 2: Unnamed pair short, EMA vs SMA comparison (28:19–30:20)
- Daily screenshot. The 8/14 EMA crossed bearish near the top. The 8/14 SMA crossed only after price had dropped "from 1.34 to 1.29… 500 pips you just missed". [29:19]
- The 20/50 EMA stayed bearish (red over blue) through the counter-trend retracement. On a 1H/2H version he would sell the **break of the counter-trend line** as a scale-in, with the **stop above the last high**. [29:19–30:20]
- Exact entry and targets: [shown on chart, not stated].

### Trade 3: Unnamed pair long, trend-line third-bounce plan (31:20–38:23)
- Context: the 20/50 EMA is bullish (blue over red, uptrend since May 2020). Two swing lows form a potential trend line, and he waits for the **third bounce**.
- A pop below the trend line that does not take out the structure low is not a break. He keeps a long bias. [32:20–33:20]
- **Aggressive entry:** trend-line bounce, then a bullish engulfing or morning star, then an 8/14 bull crossover. **Conservative entry:** break above the nearby psych/resistance area, then a retest as support. [33:20–34:21]
- **Targets:** last month's high (full or partial TP). Alternatively the midpoint of the 1.2145-~1.23/1.24 range ("about a 200 pip range"), giving TP or a partial about 100 pips in. [34:21–35:21]
- **Management:** scale in on each pullback when the 8/14 crosses up again. Ignore the bearish 8/14 crosses because the 20/50 bias is up. [35:21–36:22]
- **Alternative scenario:** if price fails, breaks structure, makes lower highs/lows after a retest and goes below the trend-line-break point, flip to short on a bearish 8/14 cross. Targets: psych levels, trend-line-break points B and A, then 1.19 and 1.18. [37:22–38:23]
- Result: hypothetical. Not stated.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Use EMA, not SMA: "I personally like exponential moving averages only because they put a clear emphasis on what price has done recently." [16:13]
- MAs are confluence only: "Moving averages are not to be the main confluence." [17:14]
- Judge crossovers on the higher timeframe: "I personally like to look at a moving average crossover on a four hour or a daily." [18:15]
- Don't trade chop: "During consolidation you will see moving averages trade in between price, that means there's no trend right now and you shouldn't be in a trade." [24:18]
- Use the 8/14 and 20/50 for different jobs: "I use an 8 and a 14 EMA for entries… but I use a 20 and a 50 EMA for a longer term direction." [30:20]
- Targets come from levels: "Moving averages don't tell you where your take profit should be." [30:20]
- Validate trend lines: "a third bounce actually validates the trend line." [31:20]
- Take profit at the prior high: "we always want to make sure we take profit at the high, or partial profit at the high." [34:21]
- Don't fight the weekly candle: "if you know that weekly is going to close as a bullish candle there's no point in selling during that week." [22:18]
- Don't call it a reversal on the 15m: "they think it's a reversal because they're too busy analyzing on a 15-minute time frame." [20:17–21:17]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- "When I was an intraday trader, and I'm still an intraday trader to a certain extent… I liked EMAs a lot better" because SMAs are too slow for 1-4 hour trades. [27:18] This suggests he was already moving away from pure intraday trading by early 2021.
- He says 2021 videos will be long and detailed. No other strategy changes are stated.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- **Pairs:** none of the chart examples name the pair. The prices suggest GBPUSD or EURUSD [?].
- **Range example:** "1.2145 to 1.23 about 430" does not match "about a 200 pip range" cleanly. The upper bound could be ~1.2343 or ~1.2430 [?].
- **Long-term SMA:** at [26:18] he says "a longer term SMA may have some fake outs" and "a shorter term SMA… doesn't really have those fake outs because… it's slower to react". This conflicts with his own EMA-fake-out summary at [27:18]. Treat it as loose speech.
- **Single-EMA template:** its period is not stated ("aema" in the captions).
- No pip stops, risk %, or lot sizes are given.
- The candle pattern names in the examples are as spoken. Exact candles and prices are chart-only.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I would click moving average exponential… and I use a 8 and a 14 moving average." [16:13]
- "Anytime the blue crosses over the red, price is in an uptrend… anytime the red crosses over the blue, price is in the downtrend." [16:13]
- "Moving averages are meant to be combined with other forms of technical analysis like a trendline bounce." [17:14–18:15]
- "I personally like to look at a moving average crossover on a four hour or a daily because it gives me an accurate confirmation on a higher time frame." [18:15–19:16]
- "I look at the daily and the four-hour first, I see the overall direction of where the market is going to go for the next week, the next two weeks." [19:16]
- "We're looking for a strong candlestick, not small candlestick… and then we mix the moving average with it." [23:18]
- "Moving averages only work during a trending market." [24:18]
- "I use an 8 and a 14 EMA for entries… but I use a 20 and a 50 EMA for a longer term direction of the market and to see where my key levels are going to be." [30:20]
- "For take profit, moving averages don't help with that. What helps with that: support and resistance, psychological levels." [30:20–31:20]
- "My main confluences that I consider are trend line, support and resistance, and candlestick formations." [33:20]
- "Remember the moving average just helps to scale in." [35:21]

# 2021-02-15 — Building a 6 Million Dollar Home & Changing My Forex Day Trading Strategy... Secure The Swag (Ep. 5)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nOK64T2hLe8 |
| Uploaded | 2021-02-15 |
| Length | 22:09 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segment: 2:04–6:05) |
| Strategy era | Transition: 2020 intraday/swing (close every Friday) → 2021 fewer, larger, longer-held trades (1–3 weeks) |

## 1. What this video covers
A vlog episode (Christmas, new studio, Mexico trip, merch, a possible move to Texas). The trading content is a four-minute segment, apparently filmed around late December 2020 / January 2021, where he says he is **thinking about changing his overall strategy**: instead of being in the market every day and closing every Friday, he wants to place a few trades in the direction of a longer-term target and **hold them for one to three weeks**, because banks and funds work toward monthly and quarterly goals. He would keep intraday trading on volatile pairs (GBPAUD, GBPJPY) and hold longer on majors (AUDUSD, EURUSD, USDCAD) and pairs tied to precious metals. He uses AUDUSD 0.70 → 0.78 and GBPUSD 1.28 → his 1.40 target as examples. He stresses this is a direction he's heading in, "not my overall strategy fully right now."

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
**Before (his "usual strategy"):**
- Intraday + swing; never a scalper. "I've always swung for four days, a week, a week and a half" [5:05] — but in practice closing every Friday and re-entering Sunday/Monday toward the same target [3:04–4:04].

**What he noticed (self-audit):**
- "I've actually audited myself and caught myself placing only one or two trades a week but having a 15 lot size or 8 lot size or 20 lot size, even a 10, and just letting it go as high as it possibly can or as low as it possibly can" [2:04].

**New direction (being backtested "in the last few weeks"):**
- "Get in the trade, place a few trades and just hold them for two weeks, three weeks" [3:04]; also "one week" [4:04].
- Still "going to be an intraday trader and be in the market here and there" [2:04], and some pairs must stay intraday (hold 1–3 days) [4:04].
- He accepts the weekend gap risk: "I know I'm going to be subjected to a little bit of risk when the market is closed on Saturdays and when it opens back up" [3:04].
- Timeframes: not stated in this segment.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **Hold longer**: majors — AUDUSD, EURUSD, USDCAD — "and pairs that are affected by precious metals" [4:04]. GBPUSD also used as an example.
- **Keep intraday (1–3 days)**: GBPAUD and GBPJPY — "it moves too much and it's too volatile ... you got to be in, you got to be out" [4:04].
- Sessions: not covered.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
Not covered in this video. (He mentions his new studio can show TradingView, his phone (linked to TradingView) and an iPad for noting key economic events [6:07].)

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Form a longer-term directional target from fundamentals and levels (e.g. AUDUSD to 0.75; GBPUSD to 1.40 if the UK leaves the EU without further delay) [3:04–4:04].
2. Think in institutional time frames: banks and funds "have a common goal to reach at the end of the month, at the end of the quarter" (Q1–Q4); FOMC meets eight times a year; NFP is monthly [2:04].
3. Instead of trading the daily fluctuations, place "maybe three or four trades" in that direction and hold them toward the target for 1–3 weeks [3:04].
4. Choose the pair type: majors/metal-linked pairs for holding; volatile GBP crosses for intraday only [4:04].

## 6. Entry rules
- "Get in a trade right here, maybe three or four trades, and just hold it for the next month, the next four weeks" (AUDUSD hindsight example) [3:04].
- Specific entry triggers: not covered.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
Not covered in this video.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- The longer-term level target (AUDUSD 0.75, which was later exceeded at 0.78; GBPUSD 1.40) [3:04–4:04]. Exits otherwise not specified.

## 9. Trade management
- **Old**: "Knowing it would get to 0.75, and then I would close on Friday and then open back up on Monday, and close on Friday, then open back up on Sunday, then close on Friday, trying to get to that 0.75 goal" [3:04–4:04].
- **New**: hold through weekends for one to three weeks, accepting gap risk [3:04].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- His observed behaviour: 1–2 trades a week at **8, 10, 15 or 20 standard lots** [2:04]. (No account size or risk % given.)
- New plan: a few trades (three or four entries) per directional idea, held for weeks [3:04].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: AUDUSD long, 0.70 → 0.78 (hindsight example) (3:04–4:04)
- Context: AUDUSD went from 0.70 to 0.78 "in a matter of two months" (roughly Nov–Dec 2020). He had been calling 0.75 for months in videos and his academy.
- What he did: traded it with his usual approach — in during the week, close Friday, re-enter next week, repeat toward 0.75.
- What he'd now do: "get in a trade right here, maybe three or four trades, and just hold it for the next month" [entry level shown on chart, not stated].
- Result: not given for his trades; the move was 800 pips.

### Trade 2: GBPUSD long from ~1.28 toward 1.40 (4:04–5:05)
- On Nov 1–2 (2020), GBPUSD was ~1.28. His long-standing call: 1.40 if the UK transitions out of the EU without further delay. Used as another example of a move that could be held as a few longer trades. No entry/stop/result given.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Never scalp: "I'm not a scalper whatsoever ... it works for some people, never worked for me" [5:05].
- Think like institutions: "Every bank and institution has a common goal for Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4" [5:05].
- Hold majors, not volatile crosses: "GBPAUD, it moves too much and it's too volatile ... GBPJPY, too volatile to just hold" [4:04].
- Accept weekend risk on long holds [3:04].
- Not final yet: "That's not my overall strategy fully right now, that's just the direction I think I'm heading in" [5:05].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
| | Before (2020) | New direction (early 2021) |
|---|---|---|
| Holding period | Intraday to "four days, a week, a week and a half"; closed every Friday and re-entered after the weekend | Hold 1–3 weeks (up to a month), through weekends |
| Trade frequency | In the market daily, catching the fluctuations | A few trades (3–4 entries) per idea; he already found himself taking 1–2 trades a week |
| Size | — | 8–20 lots per trade observed |
| Logic | Level to level, daily fluctuations | Institutional monthly/quarterly goals (Q1–Q4), FOMC 8×/year, monthly NFP |
| Pairs | All pairs traded the same way | Hold majors (AUDUSD, EURUSD, USDCAD) and precious-metal-linked pairs; keep GBPAUD/GBPJPY intraday (1–3 days) |
| Intraday | Main style | Kept "here and there" |

Key quotes: "I'm thinking about switching my overall strategy" [1:04–2:04]; "I just never swung for three weeks. I've always swung for four days, a week, a week and a half. Now I'm going to try something where I can swing it for a longer period of time" [5:05].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- No entry triggers, stops, targets method, timeframes or risk % for the new approach — only the concept and holding period.
- Status: still being backtested; he says it's "the direction I think I'm heading in," not a finished strategy.
- Filming date likely late Dec 2020 / early Jan 2021 (Christmas content), uploaded Feb 15, 2021.
- "AUD USC" and "USC CAD" in the captions read as AUDUSD and USDCAD; "aw usd" = AUDUSD.
- Lot sizes (8–20) are what he caught himself using, not a stated rule; account size not given.
- GBPUSD "128" on Nov 1–2 is approximate (it was around 1.29–1.30 then) [?].

## 15. Key quotes
- "I'm thinking about switching my overall strategy, and I'm still going to be an intraday trader and be in the market here and there." [1:04–2:04]
- "I've actually audited myself and caught myself placing only one or two trades a week but having a 15 lot size or 8 lot size or 20 lot size, even a 10." [2:04]
- "Banks and funds ... trade every single day, but they have a common goal to reach at the end of the month, at the end of the quarter." [2:04]
- "Get in the trade, place a few trades and just hold them for two weeks, three weeks." [3:04]
- "I know I'm going to be subjected to a little bit of risk when the market is closed on Saturdays." [3:04]
- "I would close on Friday and then open back up on Monday ... trying to get to that 0.75 goal." [3:04–4:04]
- "GBPAUD, it moves too much and it's too volatile ... GBPJPY, too volatile to just hold." [4:04]
- "The major pairs like AUDUSD, EURUSD and USDCAD and pairs that are affected by precious metals ... you're more likely to hold these pairs successfully." [4:04]
- "That's not my overall strategy fully right now, that's just the direction I think I'm heading in." [5:05]
- "I just never swung for three weeks. I've always swung for four days, a week, a week and a half." [5:05]

# 2021-02-14 — A 30 Minute Masterpiece on Trading Fibonacci in Forex (The Best Tool)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3jF0xjtV6zk |
| Uploaded | 2021-02-14 |
| Length | ~30:00 (last caption marker [29:23]; exact length not in transcript) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | Early 2021, intraday/swing level-to-level (1H/2H/4H Fibonacci combined with key levels, MA crossover/retest and candlestick patterns) |

## 1. What this video covers
A "remastered" version of his Fibonacci retracement video from about 9-10 months earlier. He skips the maths and gives his personal list of Fib rules: which timeframes he uses it on, which levels he draws, how he picks the swing, what has to happen at a level before price reverses, his two stop-loss options, and his take-profit (the -27 extension, sometimes the -61.8 extension). He works through EURUSD examples with the bar-replay tool, mostly on the daily chart so the examples run faster, while saying he really trades it on the 4H/2H. This is his early-2021 intraday/swing approach: Fib is one confluence that has to line up with a key level, the moving averages or a candlestick pattern.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- **His Fib timeframes:** only the **4H, 2H and 1H**. "I don't use a fibonacci retracement on anything lower than an hourly time frame. I only use it on the four hour, the two hour and the one hour." [3:05]
- **Daily Fibs:** he uses them rarely and accepts that they take **about 2 to 3.5 weeks, up to a month,** to reach the target. Example: the swing started 14 September, retraced from 28 September, hit the 50% on 1 October and the -27 extension on 22 October ("about a full month") [4:05]. The year is not stated, probably 2020.
- When he trades a daily Fib he holds it "till Friday", closes, and re-enters on Sunday or Monday until the move completes. He calls these "rare instances". [4:05]
- He says intraday traders should stick to 1H-4H, and that "the daily works as well" for swing traders [4:05]. (The same passage also says swing traders get less use from Fib because they are in and out fast, so it contradicts itself; see §14.)
- He calls himself someone who monitors an open trade every 1-2 hours. If he holds a trade overnight, the first thing he does on waking is check whether it was stopped out or hit target. [14:11]

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **EURUSD:** "a pair that likes to use the fibonacci a lot". All the main examples are on it. [22:16]
- **AUDUSD:** he gives it as a pair where Fib is not useful, because "it just flies straight up" and there is nowhere to draw one. [22:16]
- He mentions a first quick example on what the captions write as "tpp aud" [6:06], most likely **GBPAUD** [?].
- General rule: some pairs respect Fib more than others, so test it pair by pair.
- No sessions or times of day are covered.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **TradingView Fib retracement tool** with these levels: **0%, 38.2, 50, 61.8, 78.6, 100**. He has removed **23.6**: "23.6 is irrelevant." [4:05–5:05]
- **Extensions:** he targets the **"27 extension"** (the -0.27 / 127.2% extension) and the **"61.8 extension"** (the -0.618 / 161.8% extension). The exact extension values in his template are not read out. They are "[shown on chart, not stated]" beyond the names he uses.
- **The three "safe" reversal zones** are 38.2, 50 and 61.8. He says 50 is not a true Fibonacci ratio but sits midway between 38.2 and 61.8. 78.6 is "never irrelevant" but he uses it mainly for the conservative stop. [5:05–6:06]
- **Moving averages:** he refers to an MA crossover and MA retest as confluence, and the chart shows MAs. No periods are stated in this video. (His MA video from the same month gives 8/14 EMA and 20/50 EMA.)
- **Bar replay** is used for teaching, and a **counter-trend line** is drawn on the retracement.
- He keeps his charts clean. He criticises charts with "10 support and resistances… 20 lines… it looks like a coloring book". [16:12]

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Mark the major key levels and support/resistance (he keeps a few "major areas" on the chart and nothing else). [24:20]
2. Make sure the Fib **starts from a confluence**, not "the middle of nowhere": a key level, an MA crossover, an MA retest, a trend-line break, a double bottom/top, etc. "Fibonacci has to be at a low level or a high level… it has to be in confluence with something else." [6:06–7:07]
3. Draw the swing (A→B). In a downtrend draw from the top down to the last low before the retracement. In an uptrend draw from the bottom up to "the highest point" before a **full** retracement. One small opposite candle does not count. A real retracement is about **4-6 candles** against the trend; 1-2 candles that only reach 23.6 are not one. [2:04, 6:06, 7:07–8:07]
4. Watch for the retracement (point C) to reach **38.2, 50 or 61.8**.
5. Wait for reversal evidence at that level. Either **"four to five hits"** on the level, or a **double bottom/double top** ("it's one or the other"). [8:07–9:08, 13:11]
6. Look for more confluence at C: a break of a counter-trend line drawn on the retracement, an MA crossover or MA retest, a candlestick pattern (e.g. bullish engulfing), C retesting the **point B of an earlier Fib**, or a pattern such as an inverse head and shoulders. [12:10–13:11, 26:21–27:22]
7. Enter. Place the stop (conservative or aggressive, §7) and target the -27 extension (§8).
8. When the target is hit the Fib is "done". Remove it and draw the next one, e.g. from the next MA retest or swing low up to the new high. [8:07, 12:10]

## 6. Entry rules
- Enter at a 38.2, 50 or 61.8 retracement **only** with confluence. Fib on its own is not enough: "you can't use fibonacci on its own, it's already affected in support and resistance." [25:20]
- Reversal confirmation means the level gets about 4-5 touches, or a double bottom/top forms there. [8:07]
- Example trigger: "you get your entry, you do a long position, you get in a mix of this moving average crossover and its bullish engulfing." [11:08–12:10]
- Counter-trend line: draw a counter-trend line on the retracement. Price should respect it ("boom boom boom") and then "finally break above, retest above, then head all the way up". [13:11]
- Order type (market vs limit) is not stated. The examples read as market entries after confirmation.
- Scaling in is not covered in this video.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
He has two options and says the choice "really depends on the trade". [10:08–11:08]
- **Conservative:** beyond the **78.6** retracement, which gives "all the breathing room in the world". If price goes through 38.2, 50, 61.8 and 78.6 and keeps going, "I'm cool with getting stopped out". [10:08–11:08]
- **Aggressive:** beyond the **last lower high (downtrend) or higher low (uptrend)**, "maybe even a few pips below to give it some breathing room". [11:08]
- If price reverses from 38.2 or 50 off a clear low/double bottom, the stop goes a few pips below that low rather than below 78.6. [11:08]
- He notes that other courses use other stops ("very tight above the last swing high/low", "above the 78.6", "always 20 pips"). [10:08]

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- **Main target: the -27 extension.** "When price hits the 27 extension I usually get out the trade." [13:11] "For me personally I am done with the trade, I am gone." [14:11]
- **Extended target: the -61.8 extension**, used only when momentum is strong at the -27. If he sees "huge candles being thrown at the market at the 27 extension" he holds for the 61.8 extension. If he sees small candles there he takes profit. [13:11–14:11]
- **Example R:R** for a long from an MA crossover and bullish engulfing with the stop a few pips below the low: the -27 extension gave about **2.1:1** and the -61.8 extension about **3:1** (captions say "30.21" [?]). [12:10]
- He explains why price reverses at the -27/-61.8: traders close positions there, so price "should instantly consolidate and retrace once they hit the 27 extension or the 61.8". [18:15–19:15]

## 9. Trade management
- **Early exit near 0%:** after point C, price either goes **straight to the -27 extension with no heavy retracement**, or it stalls and slides just past the **0% level** and retraces. If it is slow and choppy around 0% ("it's doing too much"), he gets out and does not wait for the -27. If it leaves C "with so much force and velocity and momentum", he leaves the trade on. [20:15–21:15]
- **Monitoring:** he checks an active trade every hour or every couple of hours, and first thing in the morning after holding overnight. [14:11]
- **Daily-Fib trades:** he closes on Friday and re-enters Sunday/Monday until the move completes (no weekend holding in that case). [4:05]
- Break-even, partials and trailing are not covered. "Play it by ear" is his stated general rule. [21:15]

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
Not covered in this video, beyond the example R:R figures in §8 (about 2.1:1 to the -27 extension and about 3:1 to the -61.8 extension).

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: GBPAUD [?] short, basic illustration (2:04–3:05)
- Bar-replay example. The captions call it "tpp aud" [?] in a later reference [6:06]. The timeframe is not stated.
- Downtrend. Fib drawn from the top down to the last low before the retracement.
- Price retraced to the **50%**, then fell to the **-27 extension** "or the 61.8 extension".
- Entry, stop and pips: not stated.

### Trade 2: Daily Fib example, timing (3:05–4:05)
- Pair not stated. Daily chart. A-B-C to the 50% and on to the -27 extension.
- The swing started 14 September, the retracement began 28 September, it hit the 50% on 1 October and the -27 extension on 22 October (year not stated), which is about a month.
- He uses this to show why a daily Fib requires holding for weeks.

### Trade 3: EURUSD long from minor key level / double bottom (6:06–9:08)
- He shows it on the daily for speed and says he would normally use the 4H/2H. He places a "minor key level" at a double bottom ("let's just imagine this is a key area").
- Fib drawn from that bottom up to the highest point before a full retracement. Price retraced to the **50%** and then ran to the **-27 extension** (D), where the Fib is finished.
- On the 4H the retracement shows a clear **double bottom** at the level: "bottom, up, came back, retested and then it pulled right up". [8:07–9:08]
- Stop illustration: the conservative stop is below 78.6, "way down here". Since price made a low and retested it, the actual stop would go a few pips below that low. Long trigger: MA crossover plus bullish engulfing. Targets: -27 extension (about 2.1:1) or -61.8 extension (about 3:1). [11:08–12:10]
- Exact prices: [shown on chart, not stated].

### Trade 4: EURUSD long, second leg from MA retest (12:10–14:11)
- After the -27 extension was hit, price came down and **retested the moving average**, then pulled back up. A new Fib was drawn from the MA retest up to the new high.
- Retracement of about 4 daily candles. On the 4H/2H it is clearer, with a **counter-trend line** being respected and then broken and retested.
- At the **38.2** level price hit the counter-trend line and formed a **double bottom**, then rallied to the **-27 extension**. Exit there, unless candles are large, in which case hold for the -61.8 extension.

### Trade 5: EURUSD, end of the uptrend / reversal and new Fib down (14:11–19:15)
- Daily. The uptrend had a series of retracements: "retrace, uptrend, moving average retest, uptrend, retrace to the 38.2".
- Reversal signal: a **bearish engulfing** that followed through, plus an **MA crossover "and then another one"**. That is how he tells a full reversal from a retracement. [15:11]
- Price fell, he drew a minor key level where it stopped, and it bounced. A-B-C reached the 38.2. On the 4H there were **about 8 hits** at the level before price "finally came back down and crashing down". [17:14–18:15]
- Price reached the -27 extension, was then "done" and retraced as traders took profit. [18:15–19:15]
- Entry, stop and target prices: [shown on chart, not stated]. The direction of this last Fib is somewhat ambiguous from the audio.

### Trade 6: EURUSD long, multi-confluence 50% retracement (22:16–27:22)
- Daily/4H. He shows that a retracement bounced off the **38.2** and also off the **same high from a few days before** (an earlier Fib's point B). There were "five to six bounces on a four hour and two hour", plus a cross below the MA. [23:16–24:20]
- He then sets up the next swing. Before drawing a Fib he asks "where do you think it's going to retest next?" and answers that it's the same major area.
- Confluences for the long at C:
  1. Point C retests **point B of the previous Fib**.
  2. A **50% retracement** bounce.
  3. An **inverse head and shoulders** (shoulder, head, shoulder) forming at the 50%.
- Price then ran "all the way back to 1.22" [EURUSD 1.2200; captions say "122"] before consolidating. [27:22]
- Entry, stop and target prices: [shown on chart, not stated].

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Only use Fib on the 1H, 2H and 4H: "I don't use a fibonacci retracement on anything lower than an hourly time frame." [3:05]
- Expect a daily Fib to take weeks: "it's going to take about two or three and a half weeks to actually fulfill." [3:05]
- Ignore 23.6: "23.6 is irrelevant. It's not a real retracement." [5:05–6:06]
- Fib needs confluence: "It has to start at a key level, start at a moving average crossover or moving average retest, break of a trend line… you can't just be in the middle of nowhere." [6:06–7:07]
- Draw to the true swing extreme: "You need an actual full-on retracement." [8:07]
- Confirm the reversal: it "needs about four to five hits on that level… or it has to be a double bottom or a double top." [8:07]
- Conservative stop: "your stop-loss should either be above the 78.6 retracement for a conservative stop-loss." [10:08]
- Aggressive stop: "above the last lower high or higher low." [11:08]
- Default exit: "When price hits the 27 extension I usually get out the trade." [13:11]
- Hold for -61.8 only on momentum: "if I see huge candles being thrown at the market at the 27 extension… I'm going to hold off of it to the 61.8 extension." [14:11]
- Exit if price stalls around 0%: "it's doing too much, I'm getting out the trade." [20:15]
- Not every pair suits Fib: "you can't use fibonacci for everything." [22:16]
- Keep charts clean: "I have to keep my charts really clean." [16:12]
- Play it by ear: "the most important advice I can give you guys is to play it by ear." [21:15]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- He presents this as a "remastered version" of his Fib video from 9-10 months earlier (about April-May 2020) and says he keeps refining it: "this is why I have to refine it and remaster it because nothing is guaranteed." [0:04, 21:15]
- He says he is returning to "heavy chart work" for the academy and Secure the Swag. No specific rule changes from the old video are listed.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- **Extension naming:** "27 extension" and "61.8 extension" are taken to mean the -0.27/127.2% and -0.618/161.8% extensions. The exact values on his tool are not read out.
- **R:R figures:** captions say "30.21 risk reward" for the 61.8 extension, which most likely means 3:1 [?].
- At [11:08] the captions say "this is a 17.6", which is probably 78.6 [?]. At [13:11] "38.2 extension" most likely means the 38.2 retracement.
- **Swing traders:** at [4:05] he says swing traders get less use from Fib because they're "in and out of the market so fast", then says "if you're a swing trader the daily works as well". The terms look swapped; the intended meaning is unclear.
- Direction and exact levels for Trade 5 are hard to follow from audio alone.
- MA periods are not given in this video (see his 2021-02-22 MA video: 8/14 EMA, 20/50 EMA).
- No risk %, lot size, break-even or partial rules.
- Order type is not stated.
- The trade and level prices are mostly chart-only.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I only use it on the four hour, the two hour and the one hour." [3:05]
- "These are my levels: zero percent, 38.2, 50, 61.8, 78.6 and 100." [5:05]
- "23.6 is irrelevant. It's not a real retracement." [6:06]
- "Fibonacci has to be at a low level or a high level, like it has to be in confluence with something else." [6:06]
- "It needs about four to five hits on that level… before reversing, or it has to be a double bottom or a double top." [8:07]
- "Your stop-loss should either be above the 78.6 retracement for a conservative stop-loss." [10:08]
- "The other way, which is a more aggressive stop-loss, is if it's above the last lower high or higher low." [11:08]
- "When price hits the 27 extension I usually get out the trade." [13:11]
- "If I see huge candles… at the 27 extension… I'm going to hold off of it to the 61.8 extension." [14:11]
- "If it's slow and it goes to zero and it comes up a little bit… I'm not about to wait for it to get to the 27 extension." [20:15–21:15]
- "Price should instantly consolidate and retrace once they hit the 27 extension or the 61.8." [19:15]
- "You can't use fibonacci on its own, it's already affected in support and resistance, but it's just an extra tool." [25:20]
