# 2020-12-12 — How to Trade Support and Resistance in Forex For Beginners (Full Breakdown)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XpPde1tTIO0 |
| Uploaded | 2020-12-12 |
| Length | 23:17 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing, level-to-level trading |

## 1. What this video covers
He explains psychological (round-number) price levels, which he calls "the main way I trade," using GBPUSD on the 4H chart from late October to mid-December 2020. He draws every whole-figure level (1.29, 1.30 ... 1.35) and shows price moving from one round number to the next, consolidating at them and reversing at them. The video is mainly about using those levels as **take-profit targets** ("trading from level to level"), with one worked entry example (higher low + moving average break on the 4H). It reflects his 2020 intraday/swing style: 1-3 pairs, ~100-pip moves held hours to a couple of days, a single moving average and otherwise pure price action. The section on "quarter levels" was moved to his paid academy and is not in this video.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- He calls himself both an intraday trader and a swing trader, not a scalper: "I'm not a scalper, I'm an intraday trader and I'm a swing trader, I'm both" [20:20]. He can close the same day or the next day.
- Typical holding period for one level-to-level move: "it may take a day or two" — he lists "15 hours, 10 hours, 32 hours" as examples [20:20]. He does not hold for weeks: "would I just hold it over the course of a few weeks? No, get in, get out by the next day" [21:21].
- Holding over the 5 pm (New York) "market reset" / rollover is fine; swap is only "10 cents, a dollar, 50 cents, two dollars, three dollars" [20:20–21:21].
- Timeframes: the **4-hour** for the overall view and levels; **entries on the 1-hour and 15-minute** [19:19]. Reason: on the 4H "you may miss a move" because a 4H candle takes long to form.
- For the daily/weekly view, the broader "quarter levels" (e.g. 1.30, 1.325, 1.35) matter more; for intraday, every whole figure (1.29, 1.30, 1.31 ...) matters [4:10].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Example instrument: GBPUSD (4H; he also flips to the daily for one comparison).
- Pair count: "you really can just trade one, two or three pairs at the most and not have to juggle six to ten pairs because you kind of know the levels" [19:19–20:20].
- Sessions: no specific session rule. He mentions not entering just before major news (hypothetical 2 pm release, entry at 1:45 pm) and notes that 1:45 pm is "closer to the market reset, so nobody usually gets in that late" [18:19] (implying he does not usually enter late in the New York afternoon).

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **One moving average** — "the only indicator I have is a moving average" [21:21]. Period/type **not stated** in this video [shown on chart, not stated]. Sometimes he removes even that and reads "pure price action."
- Horizontal lines/rays at round numbers. On his live chart he keeps only the 2-3 levels nearest price and deletes the far ones; the fully lined chart in the video was made for illustration [2:08], [16:18].
- Trendlines (drawn off higher lows / lower highs).
- Chart patterns used as context: inverted head and shoulders, consolidation, doji, evening star [?] (probably meant morning star; see Gaps).
- News: TradingView's "News" tab (third tab) for GBPUSD/Brexit updates and Kitco. He does **not** use Forex Factory [12:13–13:13].
- Uses TradingView's drawing/box tools to measure.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. On the 4H chart, mark the whole-number psychological levels around current price (e.g. 1.31, 1.32, 1.33 on GBPUSD). Keep only the levels near price; delete those far away [16:18].
2. Look back in history to see how price reacted at each level (e.g. GBPUSD 1.35 rejected repeatedly; it had not been broken since May 2018) [14:14].
3. Read market structure at the level: consolidation, higher lows / lower highs, patterns like an inverted head and shoulders at a level ("we are only looking for buys right now") [5:11–7:12].
4. Draw a trendline off the higher lows/lower highs; note when price respects it or breaks it.
5. Check the moving average: break above/below it and retests of it are used as confirmation.
6. Check fundamentals/news (TradingView news tab, Kitco) for scheduled events and the big story (Brexit). A level that has held for years needs "something monumental" to break [14:14–15:16].
7. Set the target at the next round number (~100 pips on GBPUSD) and look for an entry on the 1H/15m [16:18], [19:19].
8. Stay flexible: if price stalls before the expected level (doji, consolidation, lower high), take profit and consider trading the other direction [8:13–9:13].

## 6. Entry rules
- This video is "a take profit level [video]; it's not so much a sniper entry level" [9:13–10:13]; entries are covered in his "sniper entry" videos (parts 1–4).
- Example entry pattern given [10:13]: a higher low at the level, a small doji/star reversal candle, then a "blue" (bullish) candle that pushes **above the moving average** → market entry (example: 1.32103 on GBPUSD).
- Break-and-retest of a round number is a recurring pattern, but "sometimes price won't re-test the level" [9:13].
- Entries are taken on the 1H or 15m chart after the 4H sets the scene (e.g. consolidation at a major psychological level, then a break above the MA) [19:19].
- Avoid entering just before major news [18:19].
- Order type: not specified (example reads as a market entry on candle confirmation).

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Example: "stop loss a little bit below, like 30 pips below, of the last low" [10:13]. No other stop rule stated.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- "My take profit for me is always the next level, because it's 100 pips" [16:18] — the next whole number on GBPUSD.
- Price often stops a few pips short of or beyond the exact round number (e.g. 1.33977 for 1.34; 1.3213, 1.3210, 1.31998) — treat these as the same level [11:13].
- Example R:R: he first said 1:3, then corrected himself to **1:2** (≈30-pip stop, ~100-pip move to 1.33 — he says "a little bit below"; the numbers he gives don't fully match 1:2, see Gaps) [10:13].

## 9. Trade management
- Trade one level at a time; don't hold the whole multi-week move: "You wouldn't hold this trade all the way to here, no. We get it from one level to the next" [20:20].
- Be willing to switch sides: close buys at a level when the market turns and then sell — "it's okay to be on both sides of the market" (he means sequentially, not hedging, which he says is illegal in the US) [8:13].
- Hold through the daily 5 pm reset if needed; swap cost is small [20:20–21:21].
- No break-even/partials rules mentioned.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Risk % / lot sizes: not covered.
- R:R example 1:2 [10:13].
- Prefers ~100-pip targets over chasing 20–30 pips: "people are trying to get 20, 30 pips ... a hundred is an intraday slash swing style of trading" [16:18].
- Trade only 1–3 pairs [19:19].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPUSD down-move into 1.29 and reversal (late Oct – early Nov 2020) (5:11–7:12)
- 4H chart. Price drops, retests 1.31, drops to 1.30, retests it along with a trendline, breaks below and falls to 1.29.
- At 1.29 it consolidates about five days (around Oct 27/28 to Nov 2/3) and forms an inverted head and shoulders — "a major key area"; at 1.30 there was only quick consolidation and lower highs.
- He says the US election (Nov 3) spikes should not be studied too much, but price still respected 1.30 and 1.31 and then made a higher low, from which a rising trendline can be drawn. Conclusion: "we are only looking for buys right now."
- No specific entry/stop given — context example.

### Trade 2: GBPUSD level-to-level climb 1.31 → 1.35 (Nov – Dec 2020) (7:12–12:13)
- After the election: breaks 1.31, retests, consolidates, breaks to 1.32, pulls back, breaks to 1.33, pulls back to 1.32 (lower high, retest of the moving average), drops to 1.31, then back up to 1.32, retest, up to 1.33; then above 1.33 to 1.34 "on the dot" (1.33977); a news wick, ~a week to 10 days of consolidation, then ranging 1.33–1.34 and on to 1.35, retested twice.
- His explanation: traders take profit at each round number, so price stalls/pulls back there.
- Each leg is a separate trade lasting from ~10 hours to ~2 days [20:20].

### Trade 3: GBPUSD long from 1.32 to 1.33 (10:13–11:13)
- 4H. Setup: higher low at 1.32, a small doji / reversal candle, then a bullish candle pushing above the moving average.
- Entry: 1.32103 (market, on the confirmation candle).
- Stop: about 30 pips below the last low.
- Target: 1.33 (next round number).
- Result: hit target with "literally zero drawdown except for ... a little bit of a wick." He calls it 1:2 R:R (after first saying 1:3).

### Trade 4: GBPUSD short from 1.35 (Brexit call) (13:13–15:16)
- Context: price reached 1.35, a level not broken since May 2018 and that "every time price got to 1.35 it just collapsed." He checked this on the daily.
- He predicted in an earlier "Secure the Swag" video: if Brexit talks fail → drop to 1.32; if they succeed → 1.36–1.37. Talks stalled; GBPUSD "dropped dramatically."
- Entry/stop not stated; target 1.32 as predicted.

### Trade 5: GBPUSD current outlook (12:13, 16:18)
- Last day of the trading week; price near 1.32/1.31. He expects it may rise a bit next week "maybe even 1.33" and then possibly collapse if Brexit news gets worse; keeps 1.31 on the chart as the next downside level. No entry given.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Use whole-number levels as targets for intraday: "these simple round numbers ... 129, 130, 131 ... are very very very important and they can help you figure out your take profit" [4:10].
- Target the next level: "my take profit for me is always the next level because it's 100 pips" [16:18].
- Keep your chart clean: "I always ... delete them when [price is] not close" [16:18].
- Trade few pairs: "you really can just trade one, two or three pairs at the most" [19:19].
- Enter on lower timeframes: "I enter on the one hour and a 15 minute chart because on the four hour you may miss a move" [19:19].
- Don't hold for weeks: "get in, get out by the next day" [21:21].
- Be on the right side, not right in your prediction: "it's okay to be on both sides of the market" [8:13].
- Strong historical levels need major news to break: "regular retail sales and policies ... is not strong enough to break a level that hasn't been broken in three years" [14:14–15:16].
- Don't enter right before major news [18:19].
- Minimal indicators: "the only indicator I have is a moving average" [21:21].
- Don't use Forex Factory; use TradingView's news tab and Kitco [13:13].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Moving-average period and type are not stated (shown on chart only).
- R:R arithmetic: he says a ~30-pip stop and "100 pips up," first calls it 1:3, then corrects to 1:2 after looking at the drawn tool. Exact stop/target distances are only on the chart.
- "Evening star doji" at a higher low is probably a slip for a morning star [?].
- The quarter-levels section (how 1.325/1.35 quarter levels combine with the whole numbers) was cut and moved to his academy — not in this video.
- Entry details are deferred to his "sniper entry" series and a promised top-down analysis video.
- The chart for the Brexit short is shown, not described (no entry or stop).

## 15. Key quotes
- "That is obviously the main way I trade overall." (on psychological key levels) [0:03]
- "On ... an intraday trading level these simple round numbers ... are very very very important and they can help you figuring out your take profit in the market." [4:10]
- "It's not so much of a sniper entry level, it's more so just understanding the key levels and trading from level to level." [9:13–10:13]
- "Stop loss a little bit below, like 30 pips below, of the last low." [10:13]
- "My take profit for me is always the next level because it's 100 pips." [16:18]
- "A hundred is an intraday slash swing style of trading." [16:18]
- "I enter on the one hour and a 15 minute chart because on the four hour you may miss a move." [19:19]
- "You really can just trade one, two or three pairs at the most." [19:19–20:20]
- "You wouldn't hold this trade all the way to here, no. We get it from one level to the next." [20:20]
- "I'm not a scalper, I'm an intraday trader and I'm a swing trader, I'm both." [20:20]
- "The only indicator I have is a moving average." [21:21]
- "It's okay to be on both sides of the market." [8:13]

# 2020-12-06 — A Gold Trade, Surprise From My Wife and TJ Making $50m... Secure The Swag (Episode 2)

| | |
|---|---|

| Uploaded | 2020-12-06 |
| Length | 24:29 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 5:07-9:12) |

## 1. What this video covers
A vlog episode (live-trading launch, wife's surprise, TJ Millionaire Mentor interview clip, his "forex journey" interview clip). The only strategy content is a weekly recap (~2:05 and 5:07–9:12, NFP week, early December 2020) of his trades: a gold long on an MA break that was stopped out and a re-entry that also lost, a GBPAUD short from the 1.82500 resistance with a mid-level 1.81200 idea, a 50-pip AUDUSD long, and his GBPUSD short bias. It reflects his 2020 level-to-level intraday/swing trading.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Intraday/swing, level to level (targets set at levels like gold 1850, GA 1.80, AU 0.74).
- Timeframes: daily close referenced for GU bias [8:11]; other chart timeframes not stated.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Trading AUDUSD ("AU") and gold at the start; GA (GBPAUD), AU, gold, GU discussed [2:05], [4:06]-[8:11].
- Pair characteristics: "GA... all it does is consolidate over and over again between 1.82 and 1.80" [6:08]; GA is a minor pair and "minors move very heavy"; AU moves slower: "80 pips, 60 pips, 100 pips on AU is considered amazing" [7:09].
- Fundamentals: NFP week was "very terrible" (GA chop) [4:06]; UK first to approve a vaccine made AUD look stronger than GBP to him [6:08]-[7:09]; GBPUSD may hold below 1.35 unless major fundamental news [9:12].
- Academy Discord has chats for New York session, Asian, London [10:12] (not a trading rule).

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Moving averages (entry on candle breaking above the MAs). Periods not stated.
- Key levels and mid-levels (GA 1.82500 resistance, 1.81200 mid-level, 1.80 support).
- Lower highs / higher lows for structure.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Not covered in this video beyond the trade recaps (key level -> structure -> MA break -> target next level).

## 6. Entry rules
- Gold: entered on "this candle breaking up the moving average"; re-entered "once gold went back above the moving averages" [5:07]-[6:08].
- GA: sold at the major 1.82500 resistance after a lower high formed [6:08].
- Bias flexibility: if GU rises instead of dropping, "adjust your position and get in for a buy" [8:11].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
Not covered in this video (gold stop placement not stated).

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Gold: "my take profit was 1850" / "ideal target was 1850" [5:07].
- GA short: target 1.80 [6:08]; expected retrace to mid-level 1.81200 then down again.
- AU: target the 0.74 area (highest since ~2018; September 1 high) [7:09]-[8:11].
- GU: potential collapse to 1.33500 [8:11].

## 9. Trade management
- Took early profit on an earlier gold trade at ~1830 instead of holding to 1850 "because I was already 210 pips in profit" [5:07]-[6:08].
- GA second trade became "more so a break even position" after price made a higher low [7:09].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- On the live account he notes being "down about 200" just after opening two positions because of spread — "not really a big deal" [2:05]. No sizing or % stated.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: Gold (XAUUSD) long — earlier in week (5:07–6:08)
- Entered ~1807, rode to ~1830 (~210 pips [as stated; $23 move would be 230 pips — he says 210]), closed there rather than holding to 1850 target. Win.

### Trade 2: Gold long — MA break, stopped out (5:07)
- Entry on a candle breaking up through the MAs; in profit briefly; came down and stopped him out; price went to 1823, later rose as high as 1848 (his TP 1850 not hit). Loss.

### Trade 3: Gold long — re-entry above MAs (6:08)
- Re-entered once gold went back above the MAs; "it kicked my ass". Loss. Gold at 1842 at time of recording.

### Trade 4: GBPAUD short (6:08–7:09)
- Start of week at major resistance 1.82500, lower high formed, sold; some drawdown; crashed to 1.80. Win (pips not stated, ~250 implied [?]).

### Trade 5: GBPAUD short re-entry at mid-level (7:09)
- Expected retrace to mid-level 1.81200 then crash; price reached the mid-level and started down, he re-entered; price made a higher low and went back up — closed around break-even.

### Trade 6: AUDUSD long (7:09–8:11)
- Week started above a major area; entered long, rode to the 0.74 area (Sept 1 high, highest since ~2018). "A quick 50 pips." Win. Bias: retrace, then new highs.

### Trade 7: GBPUSD — short bias (8:11–9:12)
- "100% confident" it's about to drop after the daily close; target 1.33500; 1.35 area stopped price repeatedly. Will reverse to a buy if it rises.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Adjust bias: "You don't got to be right... if you see it start to rise, be smart, adjust your position and get in for a buy" [8:11].
- Know pair speed: "80 pips, 60 pips, 100 pips on AU is considered amazing" [7:09].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Gold pip math: 1807→1830 is 230 pips (at $0.10/pip), he says 210 [?].
- No stops, lot sizes, or timeframes stated for any trade.
- The two open positions at [2:05] are "AU and ga" or "a unga" [?] — likely AU and GA or AU and gold; he says GU is on the right screen.
- GA trade 4 entry price is just "at" 1.82500 [shown on chart].

## 15. Key quotes

- "I was going to hold it to 1850 but I did not because I was already 210 pips in profit." [5:07]

# 2020-11-11 — The Reality Check All Day Traders Need...

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7OW1HZl08p8 |
| Uploaded | 2020-11-11 |
| Length | 17:12 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 3:01-4:02; 12:08-16:09) |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (level-to-level) |

## 1. What this video covers
Recorded at ~3 a.m. LA time, it uses his Sunday GBPUSD call (no entry until London session Tuesday; target 1.32500) and his GBPAUD call to argue that traders lose because they don't focus on 1–2 pairs, lack risk management and psychology. Strategy specifics: wait for the London session entry, trade level to level on the 1H for 70–90 pip moves, a mid-level 1.32500 identified from a weekend gap, needing a bullish candle close above resistance, wick vs close for structure breaks, GBP news timing, pair selection process (start with 7–10 pairs, narrow down), and not over-risking. 2020 level-to-level era.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Level to level; GU trade described as "not a 200-300 pip swing trade... literally going to be 70, 80 pips" [12:08].
- 1H chart for GU analysis [3:01].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Core pairs: gold, GU (GBPUSD), GA (GBPAUD) — unchanged since March/April/June; also AU (AUDUSD) as a favorite ("slow but the structure of AU is beautiful") [5:03], [8:05]. Doesn't trade EURUSD (only analyzed it on IG Live).
- Pair selection: start with 7–10 pairs and narrow to the ones you trade best; not just one pair, since a single pair can consolidate for two weeks [8:05].
- If none of his pairs presents an opportunity in a week, he doesn't trade that week [5:03]-[6:03].
- Session: GU entry expected "about 2 a.m. Eastern time, London session, on Tuesday" [2:01]-[3:01].
- News: GBP news around 4:30 a.m. New York (1:30 a.m. LA, ~9:30 London [?]) nearly every day; "about an hour into that London session the pound is moving" [14:08].
- Fundamentals: election-day USD weakness drove GU's ~130-pip push on Nov 2–3 2020; didn't trade gold the prior week due to election uncertainty [9:05].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Key levels and mid-levels; 1.32500 GU mid-level reinforced by a weekend gap (Sept 4 close / Sept 7 open) and prior wicks [11:06].
- Rectangles around levels.
- Candle closes (bullish engulfing / closing above resistance).
- Economic calendar for GBP news [14:08].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Sunday analysis posted to Academy: bias and expected timing of entry (London Tuesday) and target 1.32500 [2:01]-[3:01].
2. Mark key levels and mid-levels using previous price action, gaps and wicks [11:06]-[12:08].
3. Trade from one level to the next; skip moves that are too short [12:08].
4. Require a candle close beyond resistance (bullish engulfing / body close) before expecting the next level [12:08].
5. Consider fundamentals/news timing (GBP news ~1 hour into London) [14:08].

## 6. Entry rules
- Wait for the session he expects the entry in (London) rather than trading the Asian chop: GU "did nothing, it was consolidating the entire time" before 2 a.m. [3:01].
- Close above resistance needed: "if we can get a bullish engulf... and it can close above this major resistance" — then GU "is about to start rocking" [12:08].
- A wick below support is not a valid break: "It did not close below, it appears [?] below and wicked below; that does not constitute as a break of structure" [6:03]-[7:04].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
Not covered in this video.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Level to level: "Why would my take profit be higher than 1.32500 right now if this is where the market stopped previously? Just go from one level to the next" [12:08].
- GU leg = "a good 80, 90 pips"; a shorter leg ("too short for me") he didn't trade [12:08].
- GA: if it dropped further it would reach a line ~60-70 pips lower but not break it [6:03].

## 9. Trade management
Not covered in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Don't risk more than you're comfortable losing: "You got a $200 account and you're risking $175, for what?" [15:09]
- Detachment requires proper risk: losing $1,000 on a quarter-million-dollar account "is not that big of a deal" [16:09].
- Stops trading for a while if he loses too much [6:03], [7:04].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPUSD long — London-session call (2:01–4:02, 10:05–12:08)
- Sunday call: no entry until ~2 a.m. ET London session Tuesday; target 1.32500.
- 1H: price consolidated until 2 a.m. Tuesday, then the entry presented and price "shot all the way up". 1.32500 is a mid-level (weekend gap Sept 4→Sept 7, wicks at 1.325/1.32).
- At recording, price pushing through 1.32500; needs a close above to go to the next level. Leg sized at 80-90 pips (70-80 pips realistic). Entry price/stop not stated.

### Trade 2: GBPAUD long — reversal call (6:03–7:04)
- Earlier video (~10:55 mark) said GA won't go lower and will reverse up; if it dips past support it reaches a line ~60-70 pips lower but won't break it. Price wicked below without closing — not a break. Call correct; he says he made money on GA last week.

### Trade 3: Gold — wrong bias, not traded (10:05)
- Expected 2000; reached ~1960-1965 then dropped >1,000 pips. He didn't trade it.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Focus on few pairs: "I focus on these three pairs... one or the other will present an opportunity for me this week; if it doesn't, guess what, I'm not trading this week" [5:03]-[6:03].
- Narrow down: "start off with, let's say, seven to ten pairs and break them down to the pairs that... you trade the best in" [8:05].
- Wick isn't a break: "It did not close below... that does not constitute as a break of structure" [6:03].
- Level to level: "Just go from one level to the next" [12:08].
- Risk: "This is why you don't risk more than you're comfortable with losing" [15:09].
- Full-circle trading: technicals without "risk management... psychology" fails [4:02], [13:08].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- Pairs unchanged since spring 2020 (gold, GU, GA); "next year you see me and maybe gold, GU, GA, one more pair" [8:05]; AU added recently.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Entry price, stop and result for GU not stated.
- GBP news time: "4:30 New York time so 1:30 my time in LA and about 8:30 London time I believe or 9:30" — 4:30 ET is actually 9:30 London; likely he means 2:30 ET UK data [?]. Times inconsistent.
- Segment from 12:08 says rest of analysis continued in the Academy.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I do not think an entry will be present up until 2 a.m. London session on Tuesday. I do think it's going to get to 1.32500." [2:01]-[3:01]
- "It did not close below, it wicked below; that does not constitute as a break of structure." [6:03]-[7:04]
- "Why would my take profit be higher than 1.32500 right now if this is where the market stopped previously? Just go from one level to the next." [12:08]
- "This is not a 200, 300 pip swing trade... this is literally going to be 70, 80 pips, which is great still." [12:08]
- "If it closes above, then we're off to the races on another level." [11:06]
- "About an hour into that London session, the pound is moving." [14:08]
- "You got a $200 account and you're risking $175 dollars, for what?" [15:09]

# 2020-09-22 — A 48 Minute Guide On EVERYTHING You Need To Know About Technical Analysis

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DLpx1log7VU |
| Uploaded | 2020-09-22 |
| Length | 48:45 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing, level-to-level (psychological/quarter levels, one moving average, Fib, trendlines) |

## 1. What this video covers
A long, loosely structured walkthrough (filmed in Mexico) of his TradingView setup and technical approach, split into "beginner," "intermediate" and "advanced" advice. It covers how he marks support/resistance as **boxes around psychological/quarter levels** (GBPUSD 1.25 / 1.275 / 1.30 / 1.325 / 1.35), how to project take-profits by "cloning" a range, where stops belong (beyond the last lower high / higher low, not a fixed 10–20 pips), how intraday traders vs swing traders vs scalpers differ in timeframes and stop size, using an 8 / 14 moving average (or just the 8), black/monochrome charts, counter-trendline breaks, Fibonacci 61.8 and the -27 extension (AUDUSD daily), TradingView alerts and the TradingView news headlines. It ends with a 10-point "how to be the best trader you can be" list. It reflects the 2020 intraday/swing level-to-level version of his strategy. Roughly 18:17–21:19 and 39:33–47:37 are personal/motivational talk.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Pick one style first; it decides your timeframe: "You can't be a scalper trying to just get 15, 20 pips and you're looking at a daily time frame; you also can't be a swing trader and you're looking at a one-minute time frame" [5:05]. Decide through backtesting and trial and error.
- Definitions he gives [26:23–27:24]:
  - **Swing trader**: lets the trade run to the target "over the next couple of days and [has] no worries"; uses the daily; wide stop with target "two, three, four times that" [28:25].
  - **Intraday (day) trader**: "looking to just get in, get their 30 to 100 pips, 30 to 90, 80 pips and get out for the day." Analyzes on the **daily or preferably the 4-hour**, confirms on the **1-hour or 2-hour**, and may hold ~10–11 hours within a 24-hour cycle (in the morning and out before day's end, or in at night and out within 24 hours). "Intraday traders use the five minute for entries but not for analyzing" [27:24].
  - **Scalper**: 1-minute / 5-minute, in and out every 10–30 minutes.
- He trades both swing and intraday (in this example: "if I'm swing trading, my take profit would 100% be right here; if I'm intraday trading I would obviously be scaling down into the two hour time frame or the one hour time frame" [22:20–23:21]).
- His moving averages are shown on the **2-hour** chart in the example [24:22].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Examples: GBPUSD (main), AUDUSD (daily Fib example). Gold mentioned only for chart colors.
- GBPUSD "moves more likely during London and New York, especially the overlap" [22:20].
- Keep all the pairs you trade in your TradingView watchlist [32:27]; "focus on what pair or pairs you're trading" [40:34].
- He sometimes trades "in the middle of the night" (uses a darker chart theme then) [9:05].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Platform**: TradingView. Bar Replay for backtesting (example speed 0.5 s) [6:05].
- **Moving averages**: "is it either the 8 and the 14 or the 8" [5:05]; "I use sometimes the eight and the fourteen, but sometimes ... the eight, where it's just black and just one moving average" [29:25]. Rule: below the MA = still selling, above = buying. Type (SMA/EMA) **not stated** here (refers to his earlier "trail moving average" [?] video). In the 2H example the colors were red and blue MAs [24:22].
- "I only use one indicator ... the moving average, and sometimes I take the moving averages off and just focus purely on price action" [39:33–40:34]. No RSI/MACD/stochastics/Bollinger.
- **Drawing tools he uses** [6:05–7:05]: trendline (heavily), horizontal line, horizontal ray (preferred — "a lot cleaner"), Fibonacci retracement ("from time to time"), rectangle/box, ellipse (to highlight higher lows etc.), text and callout notes (e.g. "price broke CTL" = counter-trendline) [38:32].
- **Box templates** saved by purpose: "breakout region," "liquidity area," red, "cool blue," black areas [11:08].
- **Fibonacci**: 61.8% retracement as the entry/bounce zone; **-27% extension** ("27 extension") as the first target and the -61.8% extension as the next [35:29].
- **Chart styles**: candlesticks (prefers them over Heikin Ashi) [9:05–10:08]; "black charts" (all-black candles) to remove the emotional effect of colors — "use black charts because it takes away the psychology" [29:25]; gold-colored up candles and black down candles when trading gold [8:05].
- **Alerts**: TradingView price alerts sent by SMS/email ("hey stupid, open trade me now") — "alerts are the biggest thing that you can use in trading ... it's definitely a cheat code" [32:27–34:29].
- **News**: TradingView headlines/news tab for the pair, for fundamental and sentiment context [36:30–37:32].
- **Quarter-levels theory**: he used it heavily before; still uses "a little bit of it to this day," "more modern and more to my style" [13:11], [21:19–22:20].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Set up TradingView with your pairs, chosen chart style and your one MA (8, or 8 and 14) [5:05], [32:27].
2. On the higher timeframe (daily/4H), mark where price "usually stopped": horizontal rays at wicks/turning points [12:09].
3. Mark psychological/quarter levels (e.g. 1.25, 1.275, 1.30, 1.325, 1.35 on GBPUSD) and **draw a box around each**, "because price will stop at an area and not a [exact price]" [13:11]. A level is confirmed when price bounces repeatedly, fails to close beyond the box, then breaks and retests it [14:12].
4. Mark the **midpoint** between two quarter levels (e.g. 1.325 between 1.30 and 1.35), and any level price just broke from that caused a sharp move [21:19–22:20].
5. Identify market structure: higher lows/lower highs, the last high/low, and the **break of structure** when price breaks the last swing; watch for the retest and a lower high (for shorts) [7:05–8:05].
6. Project targets: the next key level/box, or "clone" the height of the previous range below/above the breakout (the GBPUSD 1.35→1.30 range projected to 1.25) [14:12–15:14], [21:19].
7. Add confluence: MA position, Fibonacci 61.8 bounce and -27 extension, trendlines and counter-trendlines.
8. Check fundamentals and sentiment from the TradingView headlines [36:30].
9. Drop to the entry timeframe (1H/2H for intraday; 5-minute only for entry timing) and wait for the entry pattern; set alerts at levels instead of watching the chart [24:22], [32:27].

## 6. Entry rules
- Intraday "sniper" example (GBPUSD, 2H/1H): consolidation, then a **lower high** below the MA, then an **evening star** confirmation → enter, stop above the lower high [23:21–24:22].
- Long example: price bounces repeatedly at a support level; a **bullish engulfing above the moving average** and then a **retest of the moving average** → "you should be buying" [29:25].
- **Counter-trendline (CTL) break**: in a trend, on the pullback drop to the 2H and draw a counter-trendline along the retracement; "whenever price breaks this counter trendline, that is your entry" (an additional entry on top of the first) [30:25–31:26].
- **Re-entry on retracement**: set an alert at a retracement level (e.g. GBPUSD 1.28100 / 1.2750); when hit, look for "some kind of bullish [bearish] confirmation" and enter again [33:29–34:29].
- Don't chase (FOMO): entering after a 500-pip drop means your stop would technically need to go above the last high before the drop [16:15].
- Intraday traders and scalpers need precise ("sniper") entries because their stops are tighter; swing traders less so [27:24].
- Order type: not specified (examples read as market entries on confirmation; alerts are used to catch the moment).

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Structural stop: "You need to have it above the last lower high in a downtrend and ... below the last higher low in the uptrend" [28:25–29:25].
- Add buffer: if the entry is close to the last lower high, "for good measure I'd place it above here because this is so close to here" [16:15].
- No fixed small stops: "Don't listen to people out there who say you need a 10 to 20 pip stop loss every time" [28:25].
- Give the trade "enough breathing room"; tight stops lead to being stopped out before price goes your way [17:16], [28:25].
- Swing traders use a large stop so that if hit, "they know it's the absolute wrong trade," with targets 2–4× the stop [28:25].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Take profit at the next support/resistance level/box: "it allows you to know where your take profit is always here" [30:25].
- Intraday: set the TP "at the exact level where price got to" previously (not beyond it) [24:22].
- Measured move: clone the prior range (top to bottom) and project it from the break (GBPUSD 1.35→1.30 → 1.25) [14:12–15:14].
- Midpoint (e.g. GBPUSD ~1.276 / 1.275) is a likely reaction point / alternative target if price doesn't reach the full level [22:20], [24:22–25:23].
- Counter-trendline break targets: "point C, point B and point A" — the points where the trendline started, because after a trendline break price "more times than not" returns to them [31:26].
- Fibonacci: -27 extension, then -61.8 extension [35:29].
- Intraday target size: 30–100 pips [26:23]. Swing: 2–4× the stop [28:25].

## 9. Trade management
- If price gets very close to the TP (e.g. ~10 pips) and stalls and you feel anxious, "feel free to just get out" rather than let it reverse; otherwise let it hit the TP [25:23].
- Swing traders ignore intraday noise; a lower-timeframe mess may just be "one big red candle" on the daily [31:26–32:27].
- Use alerts so you never need to watch the chart [32:27].
- Add a second entry on a counter-trendline break or on a retracement alert [31:26], [34:29].
- Break-even / partials: not covered.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Risk % / lot size: not covered.
- R:R: swing targets are "two, three, four times" the stop [28:25].
- Intraday target 30–100 pips [26:23].
- Rules from his list: have a trading plan, no FOMO, no revenge trading, discipline/patience [39:33–40:34].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPUSD break of structure (example) (7:05–8:05)
- Shown to demonstrate higher lows, highest point, and a break of structure when price breaks the last higher low. Price breaks, retests a little, drops, retests and fails again, makes a lower high and drops. Prices not stated [shown on chart, not stated].

### Trade 2: GBPUSD 1.30 key area and 1.35 → 1.30 drop (13:11–17:16)
- Daily/4H. Box drawn around 1.30. Price bounced there repeatedly, ran up to ~1.35, then dropped about 500 pips to 1.30, where it "doesn't close below this box," then broke below and came back to retest the 1.30 area from below — proof of a key area.
- FOMO lesson: chasing late in the 500-pip drop means the stop must go above the last high before the drop; if you entered at a later lower high, the stop goes above that lower high (with buffer).

### Trade 3: GBPUSD short to 1.25 / midpoint (21:19–26:23)
- Daily. Target derived by cloning the 1.35→1.30 range: 1.25 (quarter level, lines up with earlier reaction points). He also marks the level price just broke and the midpoint ~1.275 (shows 1.276, "about 10 pips above where my mid-level usually would be").
- Swing version: entry [shown on chart, not stated], TP at 1.25 "100%."
- Intraday version: drop to the 2H/1H; consolidation, lower high below the red-over-blue MAs, evening-star confirmation → enter; stop just above the lower high; TP "at the exact level where price got to" — if not 1.25, then near the 1.276 midpoint, where price may make a higher low. Explicitly hypothetical: "this is not a trade that I've gotten in" [24:22].
- Result: not stated (live market).

### Trade 4: Bullish engulfing + MA retest buys (black chart) (29:25–30:25)
- Black-candle chart, timeframe not stated. Price bounces at support five times; bullish engulfing above the MA, then a retest of the MA → buy; repeats in the next leg. Targets = the next resistance level. No prices given.

### Trade 5: Counter-trendline break add-on entry (30:25–31:26)
- Daily trend with a simple retracement; drop to 2H, draw the counter-trendline on the retracement; enter on its break; TP at points C, B, A of the original trendline. No prices.

### Trade 6: GBPUSD alert re-entry (32:27–34:29)
- Alert example at ~1.2975/1.2950 [?] ("129 ... 7.50"), and a retracement alert at about 1.28100 (he mentions 1.28161 and rounds to 1.28100), possibly 1.25 area. On the alert he checks his phone, waits for confirmation and re-enters, targeting lower. Hypothetical.

### Trade 7: AUDUSD daily Fibonacci (35:29–36:30)
- Daily AUDUSD. Fib drawn A→B; point C at the **61.8%** retracement where price "bounced off here several times." Heading for the **-27 extension**; the -61.8 extension is the next target "in case price breaks [the] 27 extension." No entry/stop given; the 61.8 extension "doesn't line up with anything but it's still an extra confirmation."

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Know your style first: "Figure out if you're a scalper, an intraday trader or a swing trader, and that only comes [from] back testing and trial and error" [5:05].
- Mark areas, not lines: "I like to draw a box around it just because price will stop at an area" [13:11].
- Stops go beyond structure: "You need to have it above the last lower high in a downtrend and below the last higher low in the uptrend" [28:25–29:25].
- No fixed tiny stops: "Don't listen to people out there who say you need a 10 to 20 pip stop loss every time" [28:25].
- Don't chase: entering late after a big move means a far stop; FOMO is the biggest killer [16:15].
- MA bias: "any time price is below here I'm still selling" (8 MA) [29:25].
- Use black charts: "it takes away the psychology behind it" [29:25].
- Use alerts: "alerts are the biggest thing that you can use in trading ... it's definitely a cheat code" [34:29].
- Combine technical and fundamental analysis (TradingView headlines) [39:33].
- Intraday: "use the five minute for entries but not for analyzing" [27:24].
- Near the target and stalling: "feel free to just get out" [25:23].
- Keep the chart simple; one indicator only [39:33–40:34].
- His 10-point list [38:32–44:36]: (1) figure out what type of trader you are; (2) figure out your timeframe; (3) have a trading plan; (4) combine technical and fundamental analysis; (5) use indicators or don't (he uses one); (6) focus on the pair(s) you trade; (7) consistency, discipline, patience, no FOMO — psychology; (8) don't give up; (9) double down on what works; (10) have a mentor.

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- Quarter-levels theory: "I used to use the quarters level theory; I use a little bit of it to this day, just not as heavy, because I don't want my charts to be cluttered" [13:11]. He has "made it more modern and more to my style" [21:19–22:20], and now usually spots the midpoints by eye instead of drawing them.
- Non-strategy change: market breakdowns move from YouTube to his paid academy.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- MA type (SMA vs EMA) not stated; "8 and 14" given, plus an earlier video named something like "trail moving average" [?] (possibly "the 8 moving average" or "trailing moving average").
- Most entry/stop prices are on the chart only.
- Alert prices are garbled: "129 ... 7.50 ... I didn't say 700" (≈1.2975 or 1.2950 [?]) and "1.28161, I'll round it to 100 ... 28100."
- "Evening star" used for a lower-high reversal is consistent here (bearish).
- The "-27 extension" is assumed to be Fibonacci -0.27 (127.2%) extension; he says "27 extension."
- No risk % or lot sizes.
- The AUDUSD Fib points A/B/C are on chart only.

## 15. Key quotes
- "You can't be a scalper trying to just get 15, 20 pips and you're looking at a daily time frame; you also can't be a swing trader and you're looking at a one-minute time frame." [5:05]
- "What are my moving averages — is it either the 8 and the 14, or the 8." [5:05]
- "I like to draw a box around it just because price will stop at an area." [13:11]
- "I used to use the quarters level theory; I use a little bit of it to this day." [13:11]
- "An intraday trader is looking to just get in, get their 30 to 100 pips ... and get out for the day." [26:23]
- "Intraday traders use the five minute for entries but not for analyzing." [27:24]
- "Don't listen to people out there who say you need a 10 to 20 pip stop loss every time." [28:25]
- "You need to have it above the last lower high in a downtrend and below the last ... higher low in the uptrend." [28:25–29:25]
- "Any time price is below here, I'm still selling." [29:25]
- "Whenever price breaks this counter trend line, that is your entry." [30:25–31:26]
- "Whenever price breaks a trend line it ... more times than not [goes] to the points that it started on the trend line." [31:26]
- "Alerts are the biggest thing that you can use in trading ... it's definitely a cheat code." [34:29]
- "I only use one indicator ... the moving average, and sometimes I take the moving averages off and just focus purely on price action." [39:33–40:34]

# 2020-09-21 — How To Create a Day Trading Plan For Beginners (Still Works in 2025)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rYF6KfJlUxk |
| Uploaded | 2020-09-21 |
| Length | 13:24 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (4H analysis, 1H/2H confirmation, 15m/30m entry) |

## 1. What this video covers
He lists the mandatory items of a written trading plan and fills in each with his own answers: his "why," three confluences before entry, which sessions and 2–3 pairs to trade, timeframes (top-down 4H → 1H/2H → 15m/30m), exit rules, risk management (max lot size, minimum 1:3 R:R), how to journal trades, and whether to trade news. He ends with a sample sign-off statement of his own plan. This is the 2020 "intraday swing" version of his strategy. He says the plan he has now "is the same trading plan I had when I was broke." The personal content (debt, Mexico trip, people asking for money) at 11:10–13:10 is not strategy.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- He calls himself an "intraday swing trader" [5:05].
- Top-down: "I analyze on the four hour time frame ... I need that confirmation on like a one hour, two hour time frame, then I enter on like the 15 or 30 minute time frame" [5:05].
- In his sign-off: "I only analyze on the four hour and in a one-hour timeframe for confirmation" [8:06].
- The timeframe must match the style; e.g. a scalper analyzes on the 1H and enters on the 5m or 1m [5:05].
- Holding period: not stated in this video.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Pick "two or three pairs you want to trade and try to master," find which session they move in, keep them if it fits your schedule, drop them if not [4:04].
- Plan around your schedule: trade pairs that move when you're available [4:04].
- His own sample plan: "I will only trade Asian session, the end of it, because of gold, and the beginning of London session for Great Britain [GBP pairs]" [9:07]. He also says "I won't trade after 3 p.m." (then corrects himself to the session statement) [9:07]. Time zone not stated (he lived in Los Angeles at the time, so likely Pacific [?]).
- Examples of pairs named: EURUSD, gold.
- Notes that London session is the middle of the night in the US [3:04–4:04].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- His sample plan: "I won't use any indicators" [9:07] (pure price action).
- Fibonacci levels for exits: the "27 extension" (−27%) or 61.8 [6:06].
- Trade journal: automated software such as Myfxbook, or a written record of entry, exit, profit/loss in pips [7:06].
- Candlestick patterns as confluences: evening star, doji, bullish engulfing, shooting star.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
His plan-building order (the items he says every plan must have):
1. State your name and your "why" (bigger than "to make money"); read it daily [2:04].
2. Decide how many confluences you need before entering (his: three) [3:04].
3. Decide when you trade: every day or not, how often, which session(s) [3:04].
4. Choose 2–3 pairs to master, matching their active session to your schedule [4:04].
5. Choose timeframes consistent with your style (his: analyze 4H, confirm 1H/2H, enter 15m/30m) [4:04–5:05].
6. Define exit rules: stop/what makes you exit, and take-profit method (next S/R, Fib −27 or 61.8) [5:05–6:06].
7. Define risk management: starting capital, maximum lot size, minimum R:R [7:06].
8. Decide how you'll document trades (Myfxbook or manual) [7:06].
9. Decide whether you trade the news (fundamental vs technical) [8:06].
10. Write it all down, sign it, read it every day [8:06–9:07].

For chart analysis itself: top-down from the 4H to the 1H/2H for confirmation and the 15m/30m for entry [5:05].

## 6. Entry rules
- **Three confluences minimum**: "I need to see, you know, a trendline break and like, you know, evening star, and then it broke through support at the same time ... I'm not getting in if I see one ... or two confluences; I need three no matter what" [3:04].
- Two-confluence examples he would reject: doji + bullish engulfing; bounce off resistance + shooting star [3:04].
- Reason: "I've seen more profit and better consistency when I had three confluences" [3:04].
- Enter on the 15m/30m after 4H analysis and 1H/2H confirmation [5:05].
- Order type: not covered.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Accept the stop decided before entry: "I'm never gonna risk more than I'm willing to lose, so if it goes against me it's okay ... there's no need for me to close; just let it hit my stop loss or watch it play out" [6:06].
- Stop placement method not specified here; he asks the viewer to decide what makes them exit (e.g. price retracing 20–30 pips against you) [5:05].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Options he lists: "the next support and the next resistance," "the 27 extension on Fibonacci or 61.8" [5:05–6:06].
- Minimum R:R in his plan: "I won't take a trade less than one to three" [8:06–9:07].
- Let trades reach the TP instead of closing early because "I made enough" [6:06].

## 9. Trade management
- Stand firm in drawdown if risk was sized correctly; don't close early out of fear [6:06].
- After a set number of losses: no revenge trading [9:07].
- Weekly and daily goals: aim for them, "if I don't hit it it's okay" [9:07].
- No break-even/partials/trailing mentioned.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- "Probably the most important thing on this list" [7:06]: how much you start with, maximum lot size per trade, R:R on every trade.
- Lot size must fit account size; example of what not to do: a 20 or 30 standard-lot position where 20 pips against you = −$3,000 or −$30,000 [6:06–7:06]. (The math in the audio is loose: 20 lots × 20 pips ≈ $4,000.)
- Example rules to write: "I will only use a 0.10 lot size," "I will only use a standard lot size," depending on capital [7:06].
- Minimum R:R 1:3 [8:06–9:07].
- Track monthly % growth/loss (e.g. +10%, +15%, −5%) in a journal [7:06].
- No specific risk % stated.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
No trades walked through.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Three confluences: "I need three no matter what" [3:04].
- Master few pairs: "figure out what two or three pairs you want to trade and try to master" [4:04].
- Timeframes must match style: "you can't just be like, oh I'm a scalper but you're analyzing on a daily time frame" [4:04].
- Have exit rules: "everybody focuses on entry ... nobody figures out when you're in a trade, you got to have a strategy and rules on getting out" [6:06].
- Respect your stop: "just let it hit my stop loss or watch it play out" [6:06].
- R:R floor: "I won't take a trade less than one to three" [8:06].
- Size to your account: "my risk management is in line with how much money I have in my account" [7:06].
- Journal every trade (Myfxbook or written) [7:06].
- Don't trade news (NFP 8:30 on the first Friday, FOMC) unless you've studied fundamentals [8:06].
- Sample plan: no indicators; only the end of the Asian session (gold) and the start of London (GBP) [9:07].
- No revenge trading, no greed [9:07].
- Stop switching strategies: "if you're one, two years in you already need to have an actual strategy ... and just need to execute it ... if it's failing, adjust a little bit" [10:08].
- Read your plan every day [9:07].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- None — he says the opposite: "My trading plan I have right now is the same trading plan I had when I was broke. I just honed in on it" [10:08].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Time zone for "I won't trade after 3 p.m." and for session hours not given; he immediately rephrases it as session-based.
- He says both "analyze on the 4H, confirm 1H/2H, enter 15m/30m" and, in the sign-off, "only analyze on the four hour and ... one-hour for confirmation" — consistent, but the entry timeframe is only in the first statement.
- No stop-placement rule or risk % in this video.
- He says he'll link a mock trading plan in the description; its contents are not in the audio, and he only shows his signed plan on his phone (not readable on camera).
- "27 extension" assumed to be the Fibonacci −27% (127.2%) extension.
- "Gold" at the end of Asian session and "Great Britain" pairs at the London open are likely his real 2020 focus, but this was given as an example of what to write.

## 15. Key quotes
- "How many confluences do you need to see before you enter a trade? For me it's three." [3:04]
- "I'm not getting in if I see one ... or two confluences; I need three no matter what." [3:04]
- "Figure out what two or three pairs you want to trade and try to master." [4:04]
- "I analyze on the four hour time frame ... I need that confirmation on like a one hour, two hour time frame, then I enter on like the 15 or 30 minute time frame." [5:05]
- "That is a recipe for intraday ... swing trading." [5:05]
- "Is it going to be the 27 extension on Fibonacci or 61.8? You need to figure out where you're going to exit." [6:06]
- "I'm never gonna risk more than I'm willing to lose, so if it goes against me it's okay." [6:06]
- "What's the maximum lot size you will use in a particular trade ... probably the most important thing on this list." [7:06]
- "I won't take a trade less than one to three." [8:06–9:07]
- "I won't use any indicators." [9:07]
- "I will only trade Asian session, the end of it, because of gold, and the beginning of London session for Great Britain." [9:07]
- "My trading plan I have right now is the same trading plan I had when I was broke." [10:08]

# 2020-09-19 — Scalping, Intraday, and Swing Trading

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vHw9Z5q-rqI |
| Uploaded | 2020-09-19 |
| Length | 17:21 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing, level-to-level with Fib legs (61.8 / -27 extension), partials and scaling in |

## 1. What this video covers
He defines scalping, intraday and swing trading by holding time and explains why he is an "intraday swing trader" and not a scalper. He then walks through a 4H chart (apparently AUDUSD [?]) showing how he trades with the trend from level to level: buy the break, take a partial on the pullback, move the stop into profit, scale back in, and close everything by Friday because he doesn't hold over a week. He also shows how he projects targets by cloning the first leg of a trend and using Fibonacci (entry area at 38.2/61.8, target at the -27 extension), and how he uses a side-by-side black 4H chart and 1H/15m chart to time entries on a bullish confirmation candle plus a moving-average crossover. This is the 2020 intraday/swing version.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Definitions [1:02–2:03]:
  - **Scalper**: in the market 5, 15, 30 minutes, up to an hour; wants 20–30 pips (sometimes 40); trades 10–15 times a day on the 15-minute chart; exits at any sign like a doji or morning star [3:03].
  - **Intraday trader**: holds longer than an hour — 1, 2, 3, 4, up to ~12–15 hours.
  - **Swing trader**: holds a full day, 2–3 days, up to a week (he later says 2–4 days, and that swing can technically be months) [7:06].
- Himself: "I'm an intraday and a swing trader where sometimes my trades will last four hours, five hours, two hours, 18 hours, 15 hours, but sometimes my trades last two to three days" [2:03].
- Occasionally exits after ~30 minutes when something moves "ridiculously fast like gold and GA [GBPAUD]," but "my objective is not to get in and get out" [2:03–3:03].
- **Max holding period one week**: "I don't hold trades longer than a week; if it ends this Friday I'm closing my trade" [6:05], then re-enters after the next week opens.
- Timeframes in the example: daily/4H for structure and levels; 1H (60-minute) and 15-minute for the entry [14:10–15:10].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Main example appears to be AUDUSD on the 4H ("I would bet a lot of money that AU would go here" [11:09]; "AUDUSD, two charts" [14:10]). Levels he names: "60 flat, 65 flat as a level, 625 is the mid level" [10:08] and "62-500" [12:09] — these look like 0.6000/0.6500/0.6250 on AUDUSD [?] (see Gaps).
- Gold and GBPAUD named as very fast movers [2:03], [12:09].
- Sessions: not covered.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Moving average** (period not stated) — scalpers flip when price goes above it [4:03]; his entry includes a "moving average crossover" [15:10] (implies two MAs; periods [shown on chart, not stated]).
- **Fibonacci retracement**: A–B–C legs; retracement at **38.2** (first example) and **61.8** (second); target the **-27 extension** [5:04], [8:06–9:08], [13:10].
- **Leg cloning**: copy the length of the first leg of a trend and project it from the pullback low to estimate the second leg's end [8:06–9:08].
- **Levels**: whole/quarter levels and mid-levels (e.g. "60 flat, 65 flat ... 625 is the mid level").
- **Boxes**: consolidation boxes; "breakout region" box template [11:09].
- **Black charts**: a black 4H chart side by side with a coloured lower-timeframe chart to keep emotions in check [14:10–15:10].
- Candle signals: hammer, morning star, bullish engulfing (confirmation).

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. On the daily/4H, identify the trend and the key levels and mid-levels price is moving between [5:04], [10:08].
2. Identify the legs of the trend: first leg (impulse), retracement, second leg; possibly a third push, then the trend "is done" [8:06], [11:09].
3. Draw a Fibonacci from the leg (A→B) and mark the retracement (C) at 38.2 or 61.8; mark the **-27 extension** as the target [5:04], [9:08].
4. Clone the first leg's length from the retracement low; if it lines up with the -27 extension, that is extra confluence for the take-profit [9:08], [13:10].
5. When a leg completes, re-draw: "the new C and D that was the old one becomes the new A and B" [13:10].
6. Plan the invalidation: if price closes below the 61.8 level (and the box/support) and retests, stop buying and look to sell to the next mid-level [11:09–12:09].
7. Open two charts side by side — black 4H and a 1H — then drop to the 15m to time the entry [14:10–15:10].

## 6. Entry rules
- Trade with the trend: "I like to go with the trend ... if I see price breaking this level I'd like to sell all the way down" [4:03].
- Buy the break of a level in an uptrend ("price broke up, broke above, I'm buying, I'm just letting it run") [5:04].
- Re-entry/scale-in: on the pullback to the 61.8 / support level, wait for "some type of bullish confirmation" — a **hammer or morning star**, or close + **bullish engulfing** — plus a **moving-average crossover**, on the 1H then 15m [13:10–15:10].
- Don't enter mid-candle like scalpers; scalpers "don't even wait for price to close" [3:03] (contrast with his approach).
- Order type: not specified.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Stop below the last low (for longs) [6:05].
- After a pullback makes a higher low, move the stop below that new low [6:05].
- "When price makes that high, moving my stop loss into profit, I'm scaling back in" [4:03].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Trade "from this level to this level no matter what; I don't care how long it takes" [6:05].
- Next level or the mid-level between levels [7:06].
- Fibonacci **-27 extension**, ideally matching the cloned first-leg length [9:08], [13:10].
- Target is still valid if price pulls back to 61.8: "don't think it's a failed trade unless price breaks structure" [9:08].
- Riding "the first leg of a trend" is also a valid intraday goal [8:06].

## 9. Trade management
- **Partials and scaling in**: "when it comes back up I take partial profit, but more times than not, when price makes that high, moving my stop loss into profit, I'm scaling back in for another entry and I'm selling it all the way back down" [4:03]. Partial % not stated.
- **Weekly close**: closes all trades on Friday ("I don't hold trades longer than a week"); next week, once the open settles ("when it stops moving around a little bit"), re-enters in the same direction with the stop below the same low [6:05].
- Adapt: if price closes below 61.8 and the support box, then retests, switch to selling toward the next mid-level (about "62-500") [11:09–12:09].
- Rollover fees: holding for months costs ~$2–5/day — "let's say you make 20 grand, two grand may go to the broker" [7:06].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Frequency: "I'd rather get 200, 300, 400 pips in a week and only trade two or three times than trade 10 to 15 times" [4:03–5:04].
- Contrasts with scalpers' "15 trades a week ... 30 to 60 trades a month" [6:05].
- Risk %, lot size and R:R: not covered.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: Scalper's view of a 15m chart (illustration) (3:03–4:03)
- 15-minute chart (instrument not stated). He narrates what a scalper would do: sell a double top on a small doji for 20–30 pips, exit on a doji/morning star, enter mid-breakout of a box without waiting for the close, buy back above the MA, sell the next double/triple top. He uses it to show why scalping is "prone to a lot of fake outs." Not a trade he takes.

### Trade 2: AUDUSD [?] 4H uptrend, level-to-level long with scale-ins (5:04–7:06)
- Daily → 4H. Price bounces off a level, then breaks above it → he buys ("the trade that I definitely made") and lets it run.
- Price pulls back; he moves the stop below the new low and scales in again.
- Fib A→B with C at **38.2**; price went "right to the 27 extension and stop."
- This is "a whole month of movement," traded as a series of trades: close every Friday, re-enter the following week with the stop below the same low, until price reaches the target level.
- Entry/stop prices not stated [shown on chart, not stated]. Result: target reached (-27 extension).

### Trade 3: AUDUSD [?] second leg to the -27 extension (8:06–15:10)
- 4H. First leg identified and cloned; second-leg Fib A–B–C with C at **61.8**; earlier in the chart an A–B–C, lower high, double bottom, then a run to the -27 extension.
- Plan: price may come back to 61.8 (and the low that matches an earlier low); wait on the 1H/15m for a hammer/morning star/bullish engulfing and an MA crossover, then buy, target the -27 extension, which also matches the cloned leg length.
- Invalidation: a close below 61.8 and the consolidation/breakout box, then a retest → stop buying, sell toward the mid-level (~0.6250 [?]).
- Live/forward-looking; no result given. Levels named: "60 flat, 65 flat, 625 mid level."

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Not a scalper: "my objective is not to get in and get out" [3:03].
- Go with the trend: "I like to go with the trend, I don't like to be faked out" [4:03].
- Fewer trades, more pips: "I'd rather get 200, 300, 400 pips in a week and only trade two or three times" [4:03].
- Partials + stop to profit + scale in [4:03].
- Weekly limit: "I don't hold trades longer than a week; if it ends this Friday I'm closing my trade" [6:05].
- Level to level: "I'm trading from this level to this level no matter what. I don't care how long it takes" [6:05].
- A pullback isn't failure: "don't think it's a failed trade unless price breaks structure" [9:08].
- Adapt: "if price breaks below 61.8 and closes ... and a retest and still goes down — hard time buying this pair; adjust to what the market is doing" [11:09].
- Use black charts to keep emotions in check [14:10–15:10].
- Wait for confirmation: "wait for that bullish confirmation and moving average crossover and then you are good to go" [15:10].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Instrument: the caption says "au" and "80 usd" (= AUDUSD [?]). His level numbers "60 flat, 65 flat, 625 mid level" and "62-500" fit AUDUSD 0.60/0.65/0.625, but AUDUSD was around 0.72 in Sept 2020, so the chart may be historical or the numbers misheard [?].
- MA periods for the "moving average crossover" not stated.
- Partial-profit size not stated.
- The 38.2 vs 61.8 retracement: the first Fib example uses 38.2, the second 61.8; he doesn't state a fixed rule for which retracement to enter at.
- "27 extension" taken as the Fibonacci -27% (127.2%) extension.
- He refers to a separate scalping strategy video, not covered here.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I'm an intraday and a swing trader where sometimes my trades will last four hours, five hours ... but sometimes my trades last two to three days." [2:03]
- "A scalper wants 20 to 30 pips, sometimes 40, and they get out." [3:03]
- "When it comes back up I take partial profit, but more times than not, when price makes that high, moving my stop loss into profit, I'm scaling back in for another entry." [4:03]
- "I'd rather get 200, 300, 400 pips in a week and only trade two or three times." [4:03–5:04]
- "I don't hold trades longer than a week. If it ends this Friday I'm closing my trade." [6:05]
- "I'm trading from this level to this level no matter what. I don't care how long it takes." [6:05]
- "If price went this high on the first leg, chances are it'll do the same thing again on the second leg." [9:08]
- "Don't think it's a failed trade unless price breaks structure." [9:08]
- "The new C and D that was the old one becomes the new A and B." [13:10]
- "Wait for that bullish confirmation and moving average crossover and then you are good to go." [15:10]

# 2020-09-16 — The BEST Way For Traders To Grow Their Small Account Quickly

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0Al3RzTNJWY |
| Uploaded | 2020-09-16 |
| Length | 13:55 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 1:05-3:06; 7:11-9:16) |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (small-account rules) |

## 1. What this video covers
A mostly psychological talk for small-account traders. The concrete rules: treat a small account as a small account — take profit when the market gives 30–70 pips (don't try to ride to 200–300 pips); small accounts suit scalping/intraday, not swing trading; have an exit plan with daily/weekly money goals; and don't over-leverage (0.50 lots with a 30-pip stop on $300 risks half the account). He also explains his Academy order (brokers/live accounts only in the last series). Reflects 2020 era.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Small accounts: "your account size is good for scalping and intraday trading", not swing trading [1:05]-[2:05].
- Timeframes: Not covered in this video.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Don't have "a million different indicators on your chart" [10:17]. Otherwise not covered.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Not covered in this video.

## 6. Entry rules
Not covered in this video.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Example: 30-pip stop loss used in the sizing example [7:11]-[8:14]. No placement rules.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Small account: "you need to take profit if the market gives you 30 to 50 pips" [1:05]; "if you are up 30 to 50 to 70 pips and it's X amount of dollars, okay, take profit" [7:11].
- Not on every small profit: "the best way to build a small account is not to just get in a trade and take profit every single time you're in profit, no" — look at the percentages and pip count [6:10]-[7:11].
- Percent-based: e.g. up $500 on a $250 account = 200% gain; take it [3:07].

## 9. Trade management
- Have an exit plan with goals: "how much money do I want to make by the end of the week, by the end of the day" and stick to it [7:11].
- Treat floating profit like cash on the table [3:07]-[4:07].
- Example of greed: weekly goal $1,000, up $1,200, held for $2,500, closed at $400, then lost it [12:17].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- "You cannot use a 0.50 with a 30 pip stop loss with $300 in your account" — that's $150 risk, half the account [8:14].
- Use small lots such as 0.10, 0.05, 0.15 ("a mini lot") instead of 0.50 or 1.00 [8:14].
- Don't over-leverage to make money faster; repeated blown accounts = should not be on a live account [8:14]-[9:16].
- Build in milestones ($1k, $5k, $10k, $25k, $50k, $100k) and celebrate each [10:17]-[11:17].
- His own history: losing in year one; $2,000–3,500/month in late 2016/early 2017 [6:10].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
No trades walked through.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Take 30-70 pips on small accounts: "You need to take profit if the market gives you 30 to 50 pips" [1:05].
- Small accounts aren't for swing trading: "You don't even have the account size to be a swing trader" [1:05]-[2:05].
- Exit plan: "You got to have an actual plan on, like, an exit plan" [7:11].
- Leverage: "You cannot use a 0.50 with a 30 pip stop loss with 300 in your account" [8:14].
- Don't flip: "Build your account, don't try to flip it" [4:07].
- No revenge trading, no greed, few indicators [10:17].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- No account-size threshold defining "small account" (examples: $250, $300).
- No % risk rule given — only the 50%-of-account example as what not to do.
- "Out of the five" [2:05] refers to five Academy videos on this topic [?].

## 15. Key quotes
- "If you have a small account you need to treat it like a small account. You need to take profit if the market gives you 30 to 50 pips." [1:05]
- "You guys aren't swing traders, you don't even have the account size to be a swing trader. Your account size is good for scalping and intraday trading." [1:05]-[2:05]
- "If you are up 30 to 50 to 70 pips and it's X amount of dollars, okay, take profit, because that's 70 pips the market just gave you." [7:11]

- "You cannot use a 0.50 with a 30 pip stop loss with $300 in your account." [8:14]
