# 2020-09-15 — The Best Way to Trade Trendlines in Forex For Beginners (1-2-3 Strategy)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=K3CIP3THGKU |
| Uploaded | 2020-09-15 |
| Length | 33:53 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (level-to-level, structure + trendlines) |

## 1. What this video covers
Using a GBPAUD daily chart (for clarity; he says he actually analyzes and enters intraday), he teaches three linked ideas: (1) "black chart" trading — all candles one color so you focus on structure and aren't scared out by retracement candles; (2) consolidation breakouts — clone the consolidation range and place the copy below/above the "break stop point" to project the target; and (3) the trendline "A-B-C" (1-2-3) take-profit method — when a trendline with points A, B, C breaks, price heads to C, then B, then A, which become TP1/TP2/TP3. He also covers conservative vs aggressive entries on a trendline/consolidation break, stops placed by structure (not fixed 20-30 pips), re-entries on every lower high/higher low, and Fibonacci 61.8 bounce to the 27 extension. Era: 2020 intraday/swing.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- "I'm on a daily time frame, but I'm more of an intraday trader. So I don't really analyze on a daily" — daily used only because it's easier to show [1:07]-[2:07], [26:27].
- Entries on the 15-minute: "my entries on a 15-minute candlestick" [2:07]; entry details deferred to his "sniper entry" videos (parts 1-3).
- Preferred R:R 1:3 ("I prefer my trades to be 1 and three") [19:23].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- GBPAUD (GA) used throughout. Mentions gold has its own chart color scheme [3:08].
- Sessions: Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Black charts ("zero dark 30")**: all candles black so up and down candles look the same; learned from his mentor "Sean [Shalee?]", backtested ~1.5–2 years [2:07], [3:08], [4:08].
- **Moving averages**: two MAs, "blue over the red" = bullish; "blue crossed over to red" signals a turn [5:08], [11:13]. Periods not stated. He says moving averages are the only indicator; no MACD, stochastics or RSI [13:15].
- **Trendlines** with points A, B, C; must touch A and B exactly [17:22]-[18:22].
- **Rectangle clone tool** (TradingView) to copy consolidation range [7:09].
- **Fibonacci**: A-B-C drawn from low (A) to high (B); entries off the 61.8 retracement; targets at the 27 extension (−0.27) [14:15], [16:21].
- Bar replay used for demonstration [14:15].
- Support/resistance key levels (previous stopping points) for targets; chart otherwise "naked" [8:09].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Set chart to black candles to remove emotional reaction to opposite-color candles [3:08].
2. Identify structure: consolidation ranges, trendlines (A-B-C points), higher lows/lower highs, MA alignment (blue vs red) [5:08].
3. Don't trade inside consolidation; wait for a break (and optionally a retest) of the consolidation/trendline [5:08], [10:12].
4. On a break, mark the "break stop point": where the breakout leg stopped before the pullback/retest [6:09]-[7:09], [23:25].
5. Clone the consolidation box and place it just below (downtrend) or above (uptrend) the break stop point — not directly at the box edge — to project the target [7:09], [11:13].
6. For trendline breaks, set targets at the previous trendline's points C, then B, then A [12:14], [24:27].
7. Check confluence with prior stopping points (support/resistance) and Fibonacci extension [8:09], [14:15].
8. Drop to lower timeframes (15m) for the actual entry (not detailed here).

## 6. Entry rules
- **Conservative**: wait for a break, a retest, then a "breakthrough"; mark a line where the break stopped, and sell when price closes below / comes back below that line [6:09].
- **Aggressive**: enter the moment price breaks the consolidation/trendline, stop above the last lower high [6:09]-[7:09], [17:22]. He says he's sometimes aggressive, sometimes conservative, and that conservative entries can miss a lot ("about 500 pips" on one example) [18:22]-[19:23].
- Aggressive double-top entry: after a trendline retest forms a double top / lower high, sell, stop just above the top [18:22].
- Re-entries: "Every time I see like a downtrend, a lower high, I'm entering again. Every time I see a higher low in an uptrend, I'm entering again" [20:24].
- Fibonacci entry: bounce off the 61.8 retracement [16:21].
- Order type: not stated (market implied).

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Stop placed by structure, above the last lower high (shorts) / below the last higher low, a few pips beyond [6:09]-[7:09], [17:22].
- Against fixed small stops: "People may tell you, oh, have a 20 or 30 pip stop loss. That's not how it should be... It needs to be cohesive with market structure" [17:22].
- In a consolidation break, the stop would be "above this whole consolidation range" [4:08].
- Small accounts that over-lever (1.00 lot on $300) are forced into 10-pip stops; that's what he avoids [22:25].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- **Consolidation clone**: target = cloned range placed beyond the break stop point; it often lines up with prior stopping points [7:09]-[8:09], [11:13].
- **Previous stopping points**: "I place my take profits previously where price stopped at" [8:09].
- **Trendline A-B-C**: "When price breaks point C, it is going to point B. Period" [12:14]; TP1 at C, TP2 at B, TP3 at A (start of the trendline), TP4 at further level if price breaks through [19:23]-[20:24], [24:27].
- **Fib**: 27 extension target; in the GA example the next TP is ~1.86510, "150 pips above" [14:15].
- He takes his target and gets out even if price keeps going; may re-analyze to get back in [8:09].

## 9. Trade management
- Hit target -> exit fully: "I know my take profit. I'm getting out" [8:09]; not greedy for extra 200 pips [9:11].
- Black charts help avoid closing early on retracement candles; let price hit the stop or TP [3:08]-[4:08].
- If price reaches C and B but not A: "play it by ear, manually get out" [24:27].
- A losing re-entry after four winners is acceptable ("four winning trades and one losing trade potentially is great") [20:24].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- R:R examples: 1:2 on a daily-chart example (acceptable, "it's not one to one. Good"); 1:4 on a re-entry; preference 1:3 [19:23]-[20:24].
- Warns against using 1.00 standard lot on a $300 account [22:25].
- Risk %: Not covered in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPAUD short — consolidation break, daily (5:08–9:11)
- Consolidation with MAs up (blue over red), then an evening star [shown on chart]. Break below, retest, then down.
- Conservative: wait for break-retest-break; aggressive: sell on the break, stop above the last lower high.
- Target: cloned consolidation range placed below the break stop point; lines up with past stopping points. Range quoted as about 350 pips ("185 and 181.4"), i.e. roughly 1.8500 to 1.8140 [9:11].
- Result: target hit; he exits there rather than chasing extra 200 pips. Price then formed an inverted head & shoulders and rose back to a prior major level [9:11]-[10:12].

### Trade 2: GBPAUD — second consolidation break and reversal (10:12–12:14)
- Lower highs, consolidation; he clones the range below the candle where the retest stopped (break stop point). Price "melts" into the range and stops at an exact pip match of the earlier range.
- Then higher low, another higher low, and blue MA crosses over red -> downtrend done.

### Trade 3: GBPAUD long — trendline A-B-C break (12:14–14:15)
- Downtrend line with points A, B, C; price breaks above at C (higher low forms).
- Entry on lower timeframe [not detailed]. TP1 = point C area; price retests the MA and continues; TP2 = point B (wick-to-body area); after a double top and a trendline retest, TP3 = point A.
- Fib alternative: A–B Fib; TP1 ~1.85 (point A of trendline) [?], next TP 1.86510, ~150 pips higher; price reached the 27 extension then collapsed [14:15]-[15:20].

### Trade 4: GBPAUD long — trendline + Fib 61.8 (16:21–17:22)
- New trendline; once price bounces, get in; Fib A to B, 61.8 bounce, runs to the 27 extension and comes back down. Entries deferred to sniper-entry videos.

### Trade 5: GBPAUD short — trendline break, aggressive vs conservative (17:22–21:25)
- Trendline breaks down; aggressive sells with stop a few pips above the last high ("a little wide stop loss").
- Retest of trendline forms a double top and lower high; aggressive sell there, stop just above the top. Conservative would wait for a break lower and miss "about 500 pips".
- TP1 = lowest point of the move (prior low) ≈ 1:2 R:R on the daily; TP2/TP3/TP4 = points of the previous trendline (C, B, A).
- Re-entries on each lower high: one with ~1:4 R:R; a fifth re-entry could have been a slight loss — "four winning trades and one losing trade".

### Trade 6: GBPAUD short — final trendline run-through (21:25–22:25)
- Trendline A-B connected; break; TP1, retest, TP2, TP3 achieved; TP4 not at first, consolidation, then TP4 achieved. TPs line up with previous trendline levels ("the market is art").

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Black charts to stop fearing retracements: "You're not scared of retracement candles" [3:08].
- Wait for consolidation to break: "Obviously you want to wait for a break and a retest of a trend line and consolidation" [5:08].
- Clone below the break stop point: "I'd place my consolidation range right below it because the real move hasn't happened yet" [7:09].
- Take profits at prior stopping points: "I place my take profits previously where price stopped at" [8:09].
- Trendline targets: "When price breaks point C, it is going to point B. Period. End of story." [12:14]
- Draw trendlines correctly: "Make sure it touches A, B, and then just draw it straight from there" [18:22].
- Stops by structure, not fixed: "It needs to be cohesive with market structure" [17:22].
- Re-enter on structure: "Every time I see a downtrend, a lower high, I'm entering again" [20:24].
- Don't be greedy: "The market gave me 350 pips. I'm not going to be greedy" [9:11].
- No 1.00 lot on $300 [22:25].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- Says he learned black-chart trading from his mentor and has been backtesting it "the last two years, year and a half" and incorporated it [2:07], [4:08]. No other changes stated.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Mentor's name captioned "Sean" / "Sean Sha Lee" [?].
- MA periods/types not stated.
- Prices: "185 and 181.4" read as 1.8500 and 1.8140 [?]; TP "1.8 85" read as ~1.85 [?]; "1.86510" stated.
- Entries deferred to "sniper entry" videos; lower-timeframe triggers not shown.
- All examples are on the daily chart for teaching; actual trading is intraday, so pip figures (350, 500, 150) are daily-scale.
- Most levels/points are [shown on chart, not stated].
- Long announcement section (27:27–end) has no strategy content.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I'm on a daily time frame, but I'm more of an intraday trader. So I don't really analyze on a daily." [1:07]
- "You're not scared of retracement candles... pure price action, pure structure, just letting it hit your take profit." [3:08]
- "A conservative trader would wait for a break, a retest, and then a breakthrough." [6:09]
- "The minute price breaks below here, I am selling and I'm putting my stop loss above the last lower high." [6:09]
- "When you have consolidation, a trick to use is to clone the exact range price consolidated in." [7:09]
- "I place my take profits previously where price stopped at." [8:09]
- "When price breaks point C, it is going to point B. Period. End of story." [12:14]
- "People may tell you, have a 20 or 30 pip stop loss. That's not how it should be... It needs to be cohesive with market structure." [17:22]
- "I prefer my trades to be 1 and three." [19:23]
- "Every time I see a downtrend, a lower high, I'm entering again. Every time I see a higher low in an uptrend, I'm entering again." [20:24]
- "When a trend line breaks, your take profit should be point C, B, and A of previous trend line until price tells you otherwise." [23:25]

# 2020-07-29 — Talking to 2 Millionaires & Buying a Tesla Model X... Secure The Swag (Episode 6)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qGQMJ1R-PTg |
| Uploaded | 2020-07-29 |
| Length | 31:16 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 25:21-29:27; also a short GBPUSD mention at 22:17-23:19) |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (level-to-level with moving averages and candlestick confirmation) |

## 1. What this video covers
This is mostly a lifestyle vlog: buying a Tesla Model X, an Eric Thomas virtual conference, and a talk with Jordan Wiseley [name spelled as heard: "Jordan Wisley"]. The only strategy content is near the end, where he walks through a gold (XAUUSD) long on his small "challenge" account. The setup is a big marubozu confirmation candle, then consolidation, then a pullback that bounced off the moving averages. He entered on a lower timeframe at that bounce. He closed by hand when a long upper wick formed short of his $2,000 target. He also explains how he reads wicks and candle closes, calls moving averages "moving support and resistance", and notes gold's negative correlation with the DXY. This is his 2020 intraday/swing approach.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Intraday to short swing. He held the gold trade about a day: "I just made 15 grand in like 24 hours" [29:28].
- He found the setup on a higher chart (timeframe not stated) and entered "on a smaller time frame" [25:21]. Neither timeframe is named.
- At this point he was trading part-time. He said he would mostly stop live trading until about August 20-25, 2020, while he made around 120 new Swag Academy videos [17:13-24:19]. He calls this his "fourth time" trading the small account [25:21].
- He mentions an earlier GBPUSD trade that he held about 1.5 hours [22:17].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Gold (XAUUSD): "obviously, gold is my favorite pair" [24:19].
- GBPUSD ("GU"): $6,025 in about 1.5 hours on a 5-lot position [22:17].
- Gold vs the US dollar: "as long as there's no confidence in the US dollar and the US economy, gold is going to keep going up because it's negative correlated with the DXY" [29:28].
- Sessions: Not covered in this video. For the GBPUSD trade he only says "that was just me waking up, saw the market looked good" [22:17].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Moving averages.** He uses them as dynamic support and resistance and entered on a bounce off them. The periods and types (SMA/EMA) are **not stated**. He says "moving averages" (plural), so there are probably two or more on the chart [shown on chart, not stated].
- **Candlesticks.** He uses a marubozu as the "confirmation" candle, plus doji, hammer and long wicks. He also reads where a candle closes relative to its full range.
- **Trend lines and support/resistance.** He mentions these as the usual places price bounces. The moving-average bounce is the edge he says people overlook [26:22].
- **Instagram story screen recording** as a record of the trade (not an analysis tool).

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
This is reconstructed from the gold walkthrough [25:21-29:27]:
1. Price reaches a level he has marked ("once it got right here") [shown on chart, not stated].
2. At that level, a large **marubozu confirmation candle** prints in the direction of the trade (up).
3. Price pushes higher, comes back down and **consolidates**.
4. Price pulls back and **bounces off the moving averages**.
5. He drops to a **smaller timeframe** and enters once he sees the bounce actually happen.
6. After entry, price consolidates again ("went up, went down, a little bit of a doji"), then breaks higher.
7. He sets a target at a round number ($2,000 per ounce) and watches candle closes and wicks to decide whether to exit early.
8. For bigger-picture direction he checks the macro view: a weak dollar or DXY means gold keeps rising.

## 6. Entry rules
- Long gold at **1928**, entered "right here once I saw the actual moving average bounce" on a smaller timeframe [25:21]. The order type is not stated. It looks like a market entry once the bounce was confirmed.
- What has to happen before entry: a level, then a marubozu confirmation candle, then consolidation, then a pullback that bounces off the MAs [25:21].
- Why the MA bounce matters: "Once it bounces off of a moving average, that is completely different because people just overlook a moving average bounce." [26:22]
- One entry, on the small account. Lot size not stated.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
Not covered in this video. No stop level is given for the gold trade.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Target: **$2,000 flat** on gold ("I said I was going to go to two flat 2,000 per ounce") [27:24].
- He did not wait for the target. He closed by hand when a long upper wick formed near 1986-1987, which he says was "130 pips shy" of 2000 [28:27].

## 9. Trade management
- **Exit early on a rejection wick.** "Once I saw this wick, get out. Like I literally manually closed" [27:24-28:27]. The candle spiked up and then "closed 10% of the original candlestick's move." In other words, it gave back about 90% of its range and left a long upper wick. He reads that as a sign of a crash: "You should know if price ever does that, it's about to crash all the way down." [28:27]
- What happened next: gold dropped from 1986 to 1904, which he calls "about a 820 pip drop". On gold he counts $0.10 as one pip, so $82 = 820 pips and $13 = 130 pips [28:27].
- He had predicted this in his Instagram caption: "Gold is going to get to 2,000 flat, create a big wick, and then drop all the way down." [28:27]
- Break-even, partials and trailing: Not covered in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **Small challenge account:** started at about $3,000. He says it is now $24,000, "$21,000 profit in one month" from "only traded four times" [26:22-27:24]. He also says "a $14,000 profit" (this is probably the gold trade's P/L) [26:22] and "I just made 15 grand in like 24 hours" [29:28]. The figures don't fully agree; see section 14.
- **Big account:** "my big account has like I think 55 grand in it" [25:21].
- **GBPUSD:** "put a five lot on it", up "$6,025 in about ... an hour and 30" [22:17].
- Risk % for the gold trade: not stated.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: XAUUSD (gold) long (25:21-29:27)
- **Context:** Filmed the day after the trade ("yesterday I was trading") and shown from an Instagram story screen recording. It was on the small challenge account, not his 55K main account. The 1986/1987 high followed by the drop to 1904 fits gold's spike-and-drop of about 28 July 2020. This is an inference from the prices; he does not give a date.
- **Timeframes:** a higher chart for the setup and "a smaller time frame" for entry. Neither is named.
- **Setup:** price reached a level [shown on chart, not stated], then a large marubozu "confirmation" candle, then up, then back down into consolidation, then a pullback that bounced off the moving averages.
- **Reasoning:** moving averages act as moving support and resistance, and traders who only watch S/R and trend lines miss MA bounces. Bigger picture, a weak USD/DXY supports gold.
- **Entry:** 1928 long, on the lower-timeframe MA bounce.
- **Stop loss:** not stated.
- **Target:** 2000.
- **Management:** after entry it consolidated (up, down, a small doji), then "shot up, shot up, shot up". It reached about 1986-1987. A candle wicked up and closed near its low, about 10% of its range, and he closed the trade by hand.
- **Result:** about +$14,000-15,000 on the small account ("$14,000 profit" [26:22], "15 grand in like 24 hours" [29:28]). In pips, 1928 to roughly 1985 is about 570 pips, but the exact exit price is not stated.
- **After the trade:** gold fell to 1904 (about 820 pips), then formed "another long wick, little bit of a doji, confirmation candle shooting up, a hammer", and moved back up. He still expected 2000 "before kind of really resetting itself" [28:27-29:28].

### Trade 2: GBPUSD (direction not stated) (22:17-23:19)
- Mentioned only. "I did make $6,025 in about ... an hour and 30 off GBPUSD." He woke up, "saw the market looked good", "put a five lot on it", checked 1.5 hours later and closed. No setup, levels or direction given.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Use moving-average bounces as entries. MAs are dynamic S/R: "Moving averages act as a moving, you know, support and resistance." [26:22]
- Wait to see the bounce before entering: "I actually got in right here once I saw the actual moving average bounce." [25:21]
- Use a marubozu at the level as confirmation: "it made this huge, you know, confirmation marubozu candlestick" [25:21]
- Watch where the candle closes relative to its range: "This is where you got to focus in on the candlestick." [28:27]
- A candle that spikes up and closes near its low means get out: "You should know if price ever does that, it's about to crash all the way down." [28:27]
- A long wick points the other way: "Once you see like a long long long specific wick, understand the other way. Like they didn't just create that long wick for no reason." [28:27]
- Don't stick to a fixed target when the candles say exit. He closed 130 pips short of 2000 [27:24-28:27].
- Gold vs the dollar: "gold is going to keep going up because it's negative correlated with the DXY regardless." [29:28]
- Only trade with full focus: "when your mind isn't there, it's hard to really trade" [17:13]. This is why he cut back on trading during this period.

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
He describes no change of method. The change he describes is in workload and schedule. Business tasks (hiring, academy videos, taxes) took him away from full-time trading. He planned to "probably won't be trading for the next 3 weeks" [17:13] and return to "full-time trading" around August 20-25, 2020 once things were automated [23:19, 24:19]. He also mentions a planned Swag Academy 2.0 with deeper wick/candle material: "I'll get into more of this deeper in my actual new academy" [28:27].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Moving-average periods and types are not stated. Neither are the higher and lower timeframes.
- The level where the marubozu formed is [shown on chart, not stated].
- No stop loss, lot size or risk % for the gold trade.
- The P/L figures don't agree. He says "$14,000 profit" with the account at $24,000, then "$21,000 profit in one month" from $3,000, then "15 grand in like 24 hours". Most likely the gold trade made about $14-15K, and $21K is the account's total gain for the month.
- He gives the high as both "1986" and "1987".
- "Closed 10% of the original candlestick's move" is read here as: the candle closed only about 10% above its open after spiking much higher. This is a reasonable reading, but the wording is loose.
- "Marbles candlestick" in the captions is taken to mean "marubozu". The Jordan Wiseley conversation [12:09-16:11] is about money and business, not trading method.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I got in at 1928." [25:21]
- "it made this huge, you know, confirmation marubozu candlestick and then it came up here, came down, started to consolidate." [25:21]
- "I actually got in right here on a smaller time frame" [25:21]
- "Moving averages act as a moving, you know, support and resistance. Once it bounces off that, it's very clear where it's going to go." [26:22]
- "people just overlook a moving average bounce. They just expect it to be on support and a trend line over and over again." [26:22]
- "once I saw this wick, get out. Like I literally manually closed" [27:24]
- "This is where you got to focus in on the candlestick. ... You should know if price ever does that, it's about to crash all the way down." [28:27]
- "Once you see like a long long long specific wick, understand the other way." [28:27]
- "gold is going to keep going up because it's negative correlated with the DXY regardless." [29:28]
- "obviously, gold is my favorite pair." [24:19]

# 2020-07-27 — GOLD MAKES HISTORY!!! WE BROKE $1900/oz

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZRMRwGiGkbI |
| Uploaded | 2020-07-27 |
| Length | 20:24 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (level-to-level, MA bounces) |

## 1. What this video covers
A market breakdown of gold at all-time highs (~$1930-1950), DXY, AUDUSD, GBPUSD and GBPAUD, used to teach several rules: match the timeframes you look at (and the closes you wait for) to your trader type; gold's large pip ranges and why small accounts should avoid it; cloning a consolidation range to project moves; and his "moving average bounce" entry (enter when price bounces off/rejects the MA, stop just below). It reflects his 2020 intraday/swing level-to-level approach.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- "I'm an intraday slash swing trader. I don't really scalp" — day trades from day to day and also holds swing trades [7:07].
- Timeframes by trader type [7:07], [17:15]-[18:15]:
  - Scalper: should not look for weekly closes or weekly/monthly charts.
  - Intraday trader (holds ~4-5 hours): glance at the weekly at most; really focus on 4H and 2H, "daily at the highest", plus 1H; enter on the 15-minute.
  - Swing trader: trade 4H and up.
- He wants a weekly close on DXY specifically because it's the dollar index and he wants the long-term picture [6:06]-[7:07].
- Analyzes top-down "start of the daily and you analyze down" to the 2H for intraday [12:11].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Gold (XAUUSD) is his #1 / favorite pair because it's the most volatile he trades (less than US30, which he doesn't really trade) [4:03].
- GU (GBPUSD) is his "second favorite pair" [18:15]; also GA (GBPAUD) and AU (AUDUSD).
- DXY used as correlation: gold negatively correlated with the US dollar [6:06].
- AUDUSD positively correlated with gold (Australia's gold production) — recommended as the beginner alternative to gold [10:09].
- Sessions: Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Moving averages (treated as moving support/resistance/trendline). Periods not stated.
- Key levels / rectangles (zones) on weekly/daily; round numbers (1900, 1950, 2000 gold; 0.70, 0.7250, 0.75 AU; 1.30 GU; 1.80/1.82 GA).
- Rectangle "clone" of a consolidation range to project the next move [8:07].
- Candlestick confluence: marubozu, bullish engulfing, hammer, morning star.
- Trendlines (including counter-trend lines).

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Decide what type of trader you are and look only at the matching timeframes [7:07].
2. Check higher-timeframe context (for gold: monthly all-time high; DXY weekly close below support) [3:03], [6:06].
3. Mark the current consolidation range (gold 1930–1945/1950) and clone it to see where the next leg could go [8:07].
4. On the trade pair, go to the daily and analyze down to the 2H: MAs pointing up, price trending up [12:11].
5. Look for price pulling back to and bouncing off the moving average, with extra confluence (marubozu/bullish engulfing above support) [12:11]-[14:12].
6. Enter on the bounce, stop below it, first TP at the most recent high / next level [13:11].

## 6. Entry rules
- MA bounce: "Whenever price bounces off a moving average, enter, enter, enter... because chances are price is going to continue" [12:11]; "If price bounces off a moving average and rejects it, enter" [14:12].
- Confluence usually present: bounce + a big marubozu or bullish engulfing + above a support level [14:12].
- MA is different from S/R — price always retests S/R, but if it bounces off an MA and can't break it, that's a signal [12:11]-[13:11].
- Don't wait for a box break when there's room to the target: an AU 100-110 pip move — "you can find an entry in between there" [12:11].
- A wick through support or an MA is not a break; the body must close beyond it [18:15].
- Don't second-guess: "enter, don't second-guess yourself" [15:13].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- MA bounce: "your stop loss is right below here" — below the bounce candle/low; "very tight stop loss" [13:11].
- Gold stop sizing: on gold $10 is 100 pips (1935 to 1925 = 100 pips, not 10); ask "can you afford to have that 100 pip stop loss" [9:08]-[10:09].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- AU: first TP 0.72500 (the highest recent point), then looking to 0.75 eventually [11:10]-[13:11].
- Gold: expect a move to 2000 if 1950 breaks; long-term up [8:07].
- GU target 1.30 [18:15]. GA: if it breaks the trendline, it goes "right to 182" (1.8200) — price touches all the points of a broken trendline [19:15].

## 9. Trade management
Not covered in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- AU MA-bounce trade estimated at about 1:8 R:R (very tight stop) [13:11].
- Pip ranges: gold 1930–1945 consolidation = 150 pips; same chart range on AUDUSD would be 15–30 pips [10:09].
- Small accounts ($200) should not trade gold; trade majors such as AUDUSD [10:09], [15:13].
- Don't trade money you can't afford to lose: "if losing 100 or 200 is gonna make or break you, you shouldn't be trading" [15:13].
- Expect 1.5–2 years to become profitable, another year to scale [16:14].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: Gold (XAUUSD) — outlook, no entry (3:03–10:09)
- Monthly: all-time high; expects a big wick eventually then a pullback, but long-term up.
- 4H: consolidating 1930–1945 (150 pips), with ~1h50m left on the current 4H candle. Possibly wicks 1950, maybe wick 2000 on a fundamental announcement, then retest 1950 or the top of the range (1945) or the MAs, then up.
- Clone range for projection; if 1950 breaks, "shoot all the way up to this 2000 level".
- Stop sizing illustration: a stop from 1935 to 1925 is 100 pips.
- He says he traded gold "yesterday" because it "looked too good" [17:15]; details not given.

### Trade 2: DXY — bearish outlook (6:06–8:07)
- Weekly: broke major support and closed below; next zone below [shown on chart]; wants a weekly close (4 days 4 hours left). Target "back to this 89 level" (DXY ~89). Supports gold up.

### Trade 3: AUDUSD long — MA bounce (10:09–14:12)
- Daily: broke and closed above the 0.70 range after repeated rejections. Targets 0.7250 first, then 0.75.
- 2H: MAs up; price stopped at a level, pulled back, bounced off the MA — entry there; stop right below; TP1 0.72500 (highest recent point). Tight stop, about 1:8 R:R.
- Result: not stated (illustrative, after the fact).

### Trade 4: GBPUSD long — outlook (18:15–19:15)
- Repeated bounces off the MA and a support level; wicks below but bodies stay above. Target 1.30.

### Trade 5: GBPAUD — outlook (19:15–20:23)
- Daily: big wick/hammer and mini morning star; trading at the 1.80 level; if it breaks above the trendline, it goes to 1.82.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Match timeframes to style: "If you are a scalper you should not be looking for weekly closes" [7:07].
- Intraday: "you're better off staying on a daily at the highest, but the four hour, two hour and even the one hour... and enter on the 15 minute" [17:15].
- MA bounce entries: "If price bounces off a moving average and rejects it, enter" [14:12].
- "A moving average is a moving trend line... moving support, moving resistance" [14:12].
- Wicks don't break levels: "Just because a wick goes below support... doesn't mean it broke. The body is still above" [18:15].
- No gold for beginners: "Do not trade gold if you are a new trader or you've only been trading for a few months" [9:08].
- Small accounts: "If you have a small account don't be trying to trade gold, focus on the majors" [15:13].
- Trendline breaks: "if price has a trend line... and it breaks it, it's gonna touch all the points" [19:15].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- MA periods/types not stated.
- AU levels captioned "70 to 500" and "72 500" — read as 0.70500 and 0.72500 [?]; "75" = 0.7500.
- DXY "89 level" = 89.00 [?].
- Entry/exit for his own gold trade not given.
- The 1:8 R:R is an estimate he makes on the spot.

## 15. Key quotes
- "Gold is my favorite pair... because it is the most volatile out of all the pairs that I do trade." [4:03]
- "If you are an intraday trader you can look at weekly closes... but really honing in on this four hour, two hour time frame. And if you are a swing trader you should be trading four hour and up." [7:07]
- "For me, I'm an intraday slash swing trader. I don't really scalp." [7:07]
- "The 1935 to 1925 is not 10 pips... that is a hundred pips flat out. Can you afford to have that 100 pip stop loss?" [9:08]
- "Whenever price bounces off a moving average, enter... because chances are price is going to continue." [12:11]
- "Bounce the moving average, enter here, your stop loss is right below here." [13:11]
- "People forget to realize that a moving average is a moving trend line, moving support, moving resistance." [14:12]
- "Stop looking on the weekly and the monthly... enter on the 15 minute." [17:15]
- "Just because a wick goes below support resistance or below a moving average doesn't mean it broke. The body is still above." [18:15]

# 2020-07-15 — Forex Conversations, Praying to God & Talking to Eric Thomas... Secure The Swag (Episode 5)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=m6XLozeBlzc |
| Uploaded | 2020-07-15 |
| Length | 25:30 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 16:20-19:21, emailed Q&A on taking profit) |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (market-structure trade management) |

## 1. What this video covers
This is mostly a lifestyle vlog: a mentoring call with Eric Thomas, a prayer, his brother and best friend visiting, a diamond chain, and a Swag Academy relaunch announcement. The only strategy content is his answer to an emailed question: "If the market is going in your favor and you're so scared you take the money out, but it keeps going your way, should you get back in?" His answer sets out his 2020 trade-management rules. He does not take full profit early. He moves the stop to break-even or a little profit, but only after market structure forms in his favour. He then trails the stop behind the last higher low (in an uptrend) or lower high (in a downtrend). When he is up about $5,000-10,000 he banks 25-50% as partial profit and lets the rest run risk-free.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
Not covered in this video. He does not name timeframes or holding periods. The management rules assume trades are held long enough for new swing structure to form.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
Not covered in this video. The only "tool" is market structure: swing higher lows and lower highs, used to place the stop.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Not covered in this video. He refers viewers to his market structure videos on YouTube and in the academy [17:21].

## 6. Entry rules
Not covered in this video. On the re-entry question, he does not say whether to get back in. He says the trader should accept the profit and next time manage the trade differently instead of closing it all.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- **No break-even straight after entry:** "you don't want to get in the trade and immediately move your stop loss to break even." [18:22]
- **Move the stop only when structure has formed in your favour:** "When the market structure moves in your direction, then you move your stop loss." [18:22]
- **Where to move it:** to "at least break even, if not, you know, in profit, depending on how, you know, market structure is" [17:21].
- **Trailing by structure:** in a downtrend, move the stop above the last lower high. In an uptrend, move it below the last higher low: "in an uptrend, the last higher low, I believe. Yeah, the last higher low. So, just move it with structure." [17:21]. In the captions he says "move it from at the stop loss to and take profit above the last lower high". This is read as "move the stop loss to profit, above the last lower high" for shorts.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- He does not close the whole position early: "I don't take full profit. I move my stop loss in profit." [17:21-18:22]
- Specific TP levels: Not covered in this video.

## 9. Trade management
1. Enter the trade with the initial stop. Do not move it straight away [18:22].
2. Wait for price to form new structure in your favour: a new higher low in an uptrend, or a new lower high in a downtrend.
3. Move the stop to break-even or a little profit, behind that structure. The trade is now "risk-free": "the worst case scenario, I got out the trade the same way I got in with no money or an extra hundred." [18:22]
4. Keep trailing the stop behind each new higher low or lower high ("just move it with structure") [17:21].
5. **Partials:** "if I'm up 5,000 or 10,000, I will like take partial profit. I will take 25%, 40%, 50% of that profit" and move it to his bank account [18:22].
6. Let the rest run with its stop at break-even or small profit: "I would let it run no matter what." That gives "the best of both worlds" [18:22].
- **If you already closed early:** "all profit is good profit" (advice from his friend Shai). Don't beat yourself up about closing too early; it comes with experience. Next time, "trust your analysis" and move the stop instead of closing [16:20-17:21].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- His partial-profit trigger is a dollar amount on his own account: up **$5,000-10,000**, take **25%, 40% or 50%** [18:22].
- He suggests the same approach at any profit size ("whether it's 500, 1,000, 2,000") but doesn't take it all out: put the stop at break-even or better instead [17:21].
- Risk %, lot sizes and R:R: Not covered in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
No trades walked through.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Don't regret taking profit: "understand all profit is good profit." [16:20]
- Don't close the whole trade out of fear. Protect it instead: "I wouldn't recommend taking it all out. I would recommend putting your stop loss at at least break even, if not, you know, in profit" [17:21]
- Don't move the stop to break-even straight after entry: "you don't want to get in the trade and immediately move your stop loss to break even. You don't want to do that." [18:22]
- Move the stop only after structure forms: "When the market structure moves in your direction, then you move your stop loss." [18:22]
- Trail behind the last higher low or lower high: "the last higher low. So, just move it with structure." [17:21]
- Take partials of 25-50% when well in profit: "I will take 25%, 40%, 50% of that profit to bank that into my account" [18:22]
- Let the rest run risk-free: "the other 50% now had its stop loss at break even or a little bit of profit" [18:22]
- Trust your analysis next time: "Now, you know in the future, trust your analysis." [17:21]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
No change in method is stated. He announces that the Swag Academy content is being replaced with new series and tiers, at a higher price later, with current members moved across [20:25-22:35]. He also starts an "Ask Swaggy C" call-in Q&A series [15:19].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- He hesitates over which swing to trail behind ("the last higher low, I believe. Yeah, the last higher low") [17:21]. The rule as given is: uptrend, below the last higher low; downtrend, above the last lower high.
- The captions blur "take profit" and "stop loss" in the trailing sentence ("move it from at the stop loss to and take profit above the last uh lower high"). From context he means moving the stop loss into profit above the last lower high.
- The partial trigger ($5-10K) is tied to his own account size. No percentage or R-based equivalent is given.
- He does not say directly whether a trader who closed early should re-enter.
- The email address is heard as "askswagacademy.com" / "askthewagacademy.com". This is not strategy content.

## 15. Key quotes
- "understand all profit is good profit." [16:20]
- "I would recommend putting your stop loss at at least break even, if not, you know, in profit, depending on how, you know, market structure is." [17:21]
- "in an uptrend, the last higher low ... So, just move it with structure." [17:21]
- "I don't take full profit. I move my stop loss in profit. So, it's a risk-free trade." [17:21-18:22]
- "you don't want to get in the trade and immediately move your stop loss to break even." [18:22]
- "When the market structure moves in your direction, then you move your stop loss." [18:22]
- "if I'm up 5,000 or 10,000, I will like take partial profit. I will take 25%, 40%, 50% of that profit" [18:22]
- "I would let it run no matter what." [18:22]

# 2020-07-08 — Live Trading GBP/USD on Instagram Live & Official Apartment Tour... Secure The Swag (Episode 4)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jD2zaSD7H-U |
| Uploaded | 2020-07-08 |
| Length | 40:15 |
| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 1:19-2:20 trading-psychology rule; 3:23-9:27 live trading clips, mainly 3:23-4:25 and 7:26-8:27) |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (1H leg/retracement structure, Fibonacci, level-to-level) |

## 1. What this video covers
This is mostly a vlog about losing out on a penthouse, touring apartments and moving. The strategy content is a set of live clips from an Instagram Live session where he trades GBPUSD short and also looks at GBPAUD ("GA") and GBPJPY ("GJ"). He explains the setup in terms of market structure: a lower high, and the 1H market moving in "three or four legs" (leg, retracement, leg, retracement). He uses Fibonacci and a "clone the previous drop" projection for targets. He describes his workflow: mark up on TradingView, execute on MT4. He gives his sizing (1.00 lot, about $300 risk) and his management rules: scale in when the trade is going well, and move the stop above the latest lower high once new structure forms. He also states a rule against trading while upset. This is his 2020 intraday/swing approach. The captions in the trading clips are very garbled, so many details are uncertain (marked [?]).

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Intraday to short swing. He says he will report the result of the GBPUSD trade later, so it was not closed within the clip.
- **1-hour** chart for structure: "if you look at the market [scale] back to a one hour time frame, you look at this market comes in three or four legs" [3:23].
- **4-hour** chart for the target projection: "it's a four-hour ... four-hour" while cloning the previous drop [8:26].
- He also says "zoom out" before pulling up Fibonacci on GBPAUD [4:25].
- He mentions a possible "long-term" view of GU: "my stop-loss would have to be on GU up here though, wanna trade it long-term" [5:26] [?].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **GBPUSD (GU):** the main trade. "I'm really focused on GU, GU is amazing lately" [5:26].
- **GBPAUD (GA):** considered and possibly also traded ("I'm getting GA too, actually") [9:27]. He projects GA "heading to 1.77500" with a Fib [4:25].
- **GBPJPY (GJ):** looked at but avoided: "I don't trade GJ ... I don't like Japanese yen" [6:26].
- Other clips that week (per his intro): GBPAUD, GBPUSD and gold [2:20].
- He reads the GBP move fundamentally/thematically: "Great Britain is just dying all of a sudden" [5:26].
- Sessions: Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **TradingView** for analysis and chart markup. **MetaTrader 4** for execution (heard as "mega trade"): "I analyze on TradingView and I mark the charts up on TradingView and I actually make the trade on [MT4]" [7:26].
- **Fibonacci** pulled up on GBPAUD on a zoomed-out chart ("Fibonacci, bring this up, boom") [4:25]. The levels used are not stated.
- **Leg-clone / measured-move projection:** he copies ("clones") the previous drop and pastes it from the current high to project how far the next drop could go. He does this on the 4-hour chart [8:26]. Probably TradingView's copy/clone drawing, but the exact tool is not stated.
- **Market structure:** lower highs, lower lows, legs and retracements.
- **Candles:** he notes "doji, doji" while watching GU [6:26].
- No moving averages are mentioned in these clips.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Reconstructed from the GBPUSD clips [3:23-8:27]:
1. Find a **lower high** on GBPUSD and compare it to the previous high ("we have a lower high and compare it to this high right here") [3:23]. This sets a bearish bias.
2. Drop to the **1-hour** chart and count legs. Price moves in three or four legs: "leg one, retracement, leg two, retracement, counter trend". He waits for the next leg down ("waiting for leg [three] to pull it down") [3:23].
3. Decide whether to enter now or wait for price to clear a level first ("I'm gonna figure if I want to wait for it to clear this and go down here, if I want to get in right now") [4:25]. He enters now because he doesn't want to "miss out on too much profit".
4. Compare related GBP pairs (GU, GA, GJ) for the clearest one. Zoom out and use Fibonacci on GA [4:25].
5. Place the stop above the structure high [shown on chart; level only partly heard, see Trade 1].
6. For the target, move to the **4-hour** chart. Clone the previous drop and project it from the current point. Pick TP near where the cloned leg ends (around 1.2200-1.2170 [?]) [8:26].

## 6. Entry rules
- **Setup:** a lower high, then the 1H leg/retracement count, then wait for the next impulsive leg in the trend direction [3:23].
- **Order type:** market execution on MT4 (implied: "I'm definitely getting [in] GU right now") [6:26].
- **Size:** 1.00 lot initially [7:26].
- **Scaling in:** "if sometime midway in the trade I see it's going well, I will adjust that and add ... scale in another trade and move my stop loss to break-even" [7:26].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- The initial stop goes above the structure (lower high) for a short ("stop-loss right here") [3:23]. The level was heard as roughly 1.2340 (see Trade 1).
- **Moving to break-even or profit:** he does it based on structure, not a fixed pip count. "Once I get like 20, 30 pips in profit — not even 20, 30 pips — more so when the market makes structure, so it makes a lower high and a lower low, I will put it above that lower high" [7:26]. The goal is break-even or a little profit, "so I have a risk-free trade" [7:26].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- GBPUSD TP comes from cloning the previous 4H drop: "I can totally see it going to 1.22, even 1.21700", then "take profit at ... 1.2[?]" [8:26]. The exact TP is not clear in the captions.
- GBPAUD: Fib projection "heading to 1.77500" [4:25].

## 9. Trade management
- **Scale in** when the trade is going well, and move the stop to break-even at the same time [7:26].
- **Trail by structure:** after a new lower high and lower low, move the stop above that lower high [7:26].
- **Early exit on a reversal (not a retracement):** "if it's retraced I'll leave it in there. If I see an actual reversal and I'm up $5, I'm just gonna take the money" [9:27]. Example amounts are $100 or $500 in profit.
- He accepts the outcome either way: "losses and wins and break even" [6:26] [?].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- **1.00 lot** initial size on the GBPUSD trade [7:26].
- **Risk about $300** on the trade: "I will risk ... $300" [7:26]. On GBPUSD, 1 lot is about $10/pip, so this means a stop of about 30 pips (inference).
- Account context: earlier episodes started a $3,000 challenge account. The account used here is not stated.
- **Don't trade when emotionally compromised:** "if you're going through a breakup, your mom dies, your dad dies, you're sick, you just don't trade because trading is 90 percent if not 95 percent psychological" [1:19]. He skipped trading early that week after the penthouse setback [1:19-2:20].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPUSD short (3:23-9:27)
- **Context:** Instagram Live trading session filmed the week before upload (late June / early July 2020). Filmed for "Secure the Swag episode 4, behind the scenes, trading live".
- **Timeframes:** 1H for structure, 4H for the target projection.
- **Setup and reasoning:** "I'm waiting for this to tank". He points to a lower high compared with the prior high. The 1H moves in three or four legs (leg 1, retracement, leg 2, retracement), and he expects the next leg down. GBP was weak across pairs ("Great Britain is just dying").
- **Entry:** market, 1.00 lot, after debating whether to wait for price to clear a level [4:25-6:26]. The entry price is not stated.
- **Stop loss:** heard as "stop-loss ... 1.234[00]" [7:26]. Read as **about 1.2340 [?]**. Captions also have "2073", which is unclear. At $300 risk with 1 lot, the entry would be about 30 pips below the stop (inference, not stated).
- **Target:** 4H clone of the previous drop; "1.22, even 1.21700", then "take profit at 1.2[?]" [8:26]. Exact TP unclear.
- **Management plan:** scale in and move the stop to break-even when it's working. Trail above each new lower high. Close early only on a real reversal [7:26-9:27].
- **Result:** not shown. "I will update you and Austin on what happens with that trade" [9:27].

### Trade 2: GBPAUD short (4:25-5:26, 9:27)
- Zoomed out and applied Fibonacci. "I do have GA heading to 1.77500" [4:25]. He was working out "exactly where it's [going] on the drop", and whether price was retracing or breaking ("oh no it's not retracing, it was breaking, okay great") [5:26].
- At the end: "I'm getting GA too, actually" [9:27]. Entry, stop and result are not stated. The direction is a short (inferred from "drop" and GBP weakness).

### Trade 3: GBPJPY (looked at, not traded) (4:25-6:26)
- Mentioned as moving quickly ("GJ ... really quick"), but "I don't trade GJ ... I don't like Japanese yen".

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Don't trade when upset: "you're sick, you just don't trade because trading is 90 percent if not 95 percent psychological" [1:19]
- Analyze on TradingView, execute on MT4: "I analyze on TradingView ... and I actually make the trade on [MT4]" [7:26]
- Count legs on the 1H: "this market comes in three or four legs: leg one, retracement, leg two, retracement" [3:23]
- Scale in on winners and move the stop: "I will ... scale in another trade and move my stop loss to break-even" [7:26]
- Move the stop on structure, not pips: "not even 20, 30 pips, more so when the market makes structure ... I will put it above that lower high" [7:26]
- Aim for risk-free trades: "so I have a risk-free trade" [7:26]
- Tell a retracement from a reversal: "if it's retraced I'll leave it in there. If I see an actual reversal and I'm up $5, I'm just gonna take the money" [9:27]
- Avoid JPY pairs (his personal preference at the time): "I don't like Japanese yen" [6:26]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- The trading clips are recorded on a phone and the captions are heavily garbled. Entry price, exact stop and exact TP are not reliable. The stop "1.2340" and TP "1.2200 / 1.2170" are best guesses [?].

# 2020-06-30 — The Importance of Closing Candlesticks in Forex For Beginners (FULL Breakdown)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=45UJS0T_dSA |
| Uploaded | 2020-06-30 |
| Length | 23:21 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (level-to-level, MAs, Fib) |

## 1. What this video covers
He teaches why you must wait for candle closes — on the right timeframe and at a key level — before acting, using GBPUSD (and GBPAUD) examples. Key points: plot key levels and mid-levels first; a wick through a level is not a break; candles with far more wick than body signal reversal or consolidation; the higher the timeframe the candle closes on, the stronger the signal. He also shows his 2020 breakout rules with the two moving averages (crossover timing vs consolidation break), the take-profit/break-even decision when price stalls short of target, the "three legs" market structure idea, and a Fibonacci 61.8 rejection + bearish engulfing to the 27 extension. This is his 2020 intraday/swing level-to-level method.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Trades level to level ("I trade levels") [10:19].
- 4H = structure: "the four hours where I look at pure structure" [13:19].
- 1H = entries ("one hour is more for my entries, same as the 15 minute") [13:19]; he notes he doesn't "even get the confirmations" on the 1H [?].
- Wait for the 1H close rather than acting on 15m/5m candles [5:15]-[6:16].
- Daily and 4H closes matter most; daily close below 1.80 on GA used for bias [18:24]-[19:26].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- "I only trade these three pairs anyway": GU (GBPUSD), GA (GBPAUD), and gold [3:14].
- Master one pair: knowing key levels "by heart" [3:14]-[4:14]; "stick to one pair, one or two pairs and just master them" [19:26]-[20:26].
- Session context matters: is GBPUSD consolidating because it's the Asian session, because London just opened, or because London is closing as New York arrives? [6:16]
- Brokers "pushing price" to stop people out — levels where price was pushed to are significant [15:20]-[16:21].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Key levels plus a **mid-level** between each pair of key levels (e.g. 1.24500 between 1.24 and 1.25 [?]) [4:14].
- Advanced traders keep only 1–3 levels on the chart [3:14], [17:21].
- Two moving averages, blue and red; "blue above red" etc.; MA = moving support/resistance/trendline [7:16], [9:19]. Periods not stated.
- Fibonacci: A-B-C; four levels "it moves in one of these four levels" [?]; 61.8 retracement entry; 27 extension target; 50 level mentioned [17:21], [19:26].
- Candlestick patterns: doji, shooting star, pin bar formation (3 candles), bearish engulfing, evening star.
- Bar replay.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Plot key levels (and mid-levels) before anything else [3:14].
2. Identify the 4H trend/structure; trade with it [7:16], [13:19].
3. Note the session/time of day [6:16].
4. Watch for consolidation: MAs tangled together = no clear trend; mark the consolidation box [8:17].
5. Wait for a candle to **close** beyond the consolidation/level on the 1H (wicks don't count) [8:17]-[9:19].
6. Check MA crossover timing relative to the break, and whether price respects the MA as resistance/support [9:19]-[10:19].
7. Enter on the break/retest with candlestick confirmation; target the next level [10:19].
8. Use Fib (61.8 rejection) for re-entries during retracements; target 27 extension [17:21]-[18:24].

## 6. Entry rules
- Wait for the hourly close; don't buy off a big 15m/5m candle into a level with repeated wicks [5:15]-[6:16].
- Breakout + MA crossover: three scenarios learned from his mentor — (a) break and MA cross at the same time; (b) break first, cross later; (c) MAs cross inside the consolidation before the break ("more aggressive") [9:19]. After closure below consolidation, he waits for "confirmation" because the MAs crossed [9:19].
- Retest of the MA / box after the break, then continue [10:19].
- Strong 4H shooting star bouncing off MA resistance: "you better sell" [13:19].
- Example entry after a 3-candle pin bar formation where the third candle breaks below the MA and red crosses above blue at the same time; plus a bearish engulfing that rejects the Fib 61.8 [16:21]-[18:24].
- Re-enter after a retrace only on clear confirmation [11:19], [14:20].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- On a sell after a break: "stop-loss above here" (above the broken consolidation / recent high) [14:20] [shown on chart, not stated].
- Long example: "stop-loss right below the last wick" [5:15].
- No pip size stated.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Next key level: e.g. GBPUSD 1.23000 [?] after the 1.24500 level [10:19]; with strong 4H shooting star, go to "the next level" (1.22) [13:19].
- Fib 27 extension [18:24]; GA target 1.77500 = the 61.8 level [?] [19:26].

## 9. Trade management
- If price doesn't reach the target, two options [10:19]-[11:19]:
  1. Move stop to break-even and let it hit the target eventually (relaxed approach).
  2. Take profit early; "you can never get mad at taking profit" (mentor's advice); re-enter on clear confirmation after the retrace.
- Get out at the 27 extension when candles start shrinking/closing back up: "that tells you 27 is done" [20:26].
- On GA: if a doji appears near target and you're hesitating, get out [19:26].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
Not covered in this video (beyond noting small accounts get stopped out by premature entries).

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPUSD long (losing example) — premature 15m entry (5:15–8:17)
- Context: price at the 1.24500 mid-level [?] between key levels. On the 15m/5m a big bullish candle pushes up after retesting the MA; instinct is to buy, stop below last wick, TP above.
- Result: immediately negative, loses. Reason: didn't wait for the 1H close — 1H had four wicks in a row at 1.24500, body smaller than wick, MAs never crossed (blue not above red) on 1H or 4H; downtrend intact.

### Trade 2: GBPUSD short — consolidation break (8:17–11:19)
- 1H consolidation with MAs together; candles more wick than body (body ~10% of wick). A wick below support doesn't count. Then a close below; MAs crossed over inside the consolidation; clear separation; retest of MA/box; down.
- Target 1.23000 [?]; 1.24500 mid-level for partials [?]. Price didn't reach 1.23 → break-even or take profit early.

### Trade 3: GBPUSD — market-structure outlook (11:19–13:19)
- Price moves in three legs (leg, retracement/consolidation, leg...); expects a fourth leg down. Counter-trend line being broken is just retracement; no HH/HL. Prediction: GBPUSD pushes further down that week.

### Trade 4: GBPUSD short — 4H shooting star (13:19–14:20)
- 4H shooting star closing off MA resistance: sell, target beyond 1.23 to 1.22. Doji clusters on the 4H; entries "right here, stop-loss above here" on the 1H breakdown — "you're never negative".

### Trade 5: GBPUSD short — retest + pin bar + Fib 61.8 (14:20–18:24)
- After retrace: 7 dojis out of last 13–14 1H candles = indecision. Price retests to a tee the consolidation break level (where brokers stopped people out). Pin bar formation (3 candles), third breaks below MA; red crosses above blue at the same time.
- Fib A (top) to B (bottom): exact 61.8 retracement; bearish engulfing close rejects the 61.8. Exit at the 27 extension. Result: target hit.

### Trade 6: GBPAUD short — outlook (18:24–20:26)
- Daily closed below 1.80; shooting stars off the MA and off 1.80 [?] key level. Very bearish. Possible retrace to a Fib 50 level, then drop to 1.77500 = 61.8 level [?] (skipping the 27 extension because he knows how GA moves). Exit if a doji appears and he hesitates.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Plot key levels first: "The first thing I would do is make sure my key levels are on here" [3:14].
- Wicks aren't breaks: "If price wicks below a support but it does not close below, that support still held firm. Period." [8:17]
- Beware wicky candles: "Whenever you see a situation where there's way more wick than body, be very careful" [7:16].
- Trade with the trend: "You got to go with the actual overall trend" [7:16].
- Higher-timeframe closes count more: "The higher the timeframe the candle closes on, the more aggressive you should be" [13:19]-[14:20].
- Take profits without regret: "You can never get mad at taking profit. Period." [11:19]
- Keep charts clean: "I try to keep it clear so my mind can look at it in its purest form" [17:21].
- Master pairs: "Stick to one pair, one or two pairs and just master them" [19:26].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video. (Mentions he started trading in 2015.)

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- GBPUSD levels captioned "120 4 or 500", "123", "122", "2025" — likely 1.24500, 1.23000, 1.22000, and 1.25 [?]. Captions say "1 2300 level... 1 2500" for targets [?].
- "Fibonacci does 104 things, it moves in one of these four levels" [?] — he probably means Fib reacts at one of four levels; which four is not stated.
- MA periods not stated.
- GA "1.77 5 100" read as 1.77500 [?].
- "I don't even get the confirmations [on the 1H]" is unclear — possibly he means confirmations come from 4H [?].

## 15. Key quotes
- "You can know all the patterns in the world; if you don't know what time the pair closed... you don't really know too much about candlesticks." [2:14]
- "What you did was you didn't wait for that hourly closure." [6:16]
- "If price wicks below a support but it does not close below, that support still held firm. Period. End of story." [8:17]
- "Whenever you see a situation where there's way more wick than body, be very careful." [7:16]
- "Understand a moving average is always a moving support or moving resistance or even a moving trendline." [9:19]
- "I do one of two things: either I move my stop loss to break-even and let it hit it eventually... [or] feel free to take profit." [11:19]
- "You can never get mad at taking profit. Period." [11:19]
- "The four hour is where I look at pure structure." [13:19]
- "The higher the timeframe the candle closes on, the more aggressive you should be in your trading." [14:20]

# 2020-06-23 — I Gave $27,000 To My Wife For Her 27th Birthday... Secure The Swag (EPISODE 2)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zLkMavPyrAg |

| Classification | PARTIAL (strategy segments: 1:02-3:02 trading-challenge setup; 12:49-20:52 conversation with Mamba FX) |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (general principles only; little method detail) |

## 1. What this video covers
This is mostly a vlog about his wife's 27th birthday (filmed around 25 May 2020). It has two trading parts. First, at 1:02-3:02 he launches a **$3,000 live "trading challenge" account**, names the instruments he plans to trade, and gives advice on account size. Second, at 12:49-20:52 there is a sit-down with another YouTube trader, **Mamba FX**. They cover simplifying to naked charts, holding to a fixed risk:reward, demo trading, the 1-3% risk rule, small accounts, and avoiding "obvious" support and resistance. The captions have no speaker labels, so each point below is attributed from context with a confidence level. Swaggy C's own method gets very little detail here. The guest's points are kept in a separate subsection.

**How speakers were identified.** Swaggy C is the host and asks most questions. In other episodes he uses the same phrases and stories: "I've studied this for two, three years", "be robotic", trusting your analysis, basketball analogies, "all my students" (Swag Academy). The answer about stripping out indicators and keeping only RSI for divergences is the reply to the opening "when did it get easy?" question. Swaggy's next turn begins "I'm not gonna say that because like naked trading ... definitely helped my game, but ..." That suggests the indicator answer was the guest's, and Swaggy then gave his own answer (psychology).

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
Not covered in this video. No timeframes or holding periods are given by either speaker.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- **Swaggy C (challenge account):** "I'm only gonna trade gold, US30, GA[?]/GU[?], Tesla, Apple ... I'm not entirely sure which two or three pairs I'll focus on" [2:02]. This is gold, the Dow (US30), probably GBP pairs, and the Tesla and Apple stocks/CFDs. He plans to focus on two or three of them.
- Sessions: Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- **Swaggy C:** a public **Myfxbook** link (heard as "FX book") so viewers can track percentage gains per day, week and month [1:02].
- **Mamba FX (guest, likely):** RSI only, for divergences. Otherwise naked charts with support/resistance and break-and-retest trend lines [12:49]. No RSI settings given.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
Not covered in this video as a process. Only the general principles below.

## 6. Entry rules
Not covered in this video. See section 11b for the guest's "avoid obvious support" tip, which is advice on where *not* to enter.

## 7. Stop-loss rules
Not covered in this video. The only stop example is a 20-pip stop and a 40-pip stop, used to illustrate R:R and sizing (attribution uncertain, see section 11b).

## 8. Take-profit and targets
Not covered in this video beyond the R:R discipline point (section 11b).

## 9. Trade management
- **Swaggy C (likely):** put the trade on and walk away. "I'm gonna trust my analysis, I'm not gonna care, this is not gonna make or break me, and put the trade on and walk away or like close my phone or like be robotic" [13:49].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
**Swaggy C (high confidence):**
- Challenge account: **$3,000** live (not demo) [1:02-2:02]. Real main account "has like 54 thousand in it, or like 49" [3:02].
- Account-size advice: don't try to grow $1 or 10-cent accounts. "You should try to, you know, get an investor account and trade their capital or get some capital, a few thousand dollars, and trade" [3:02]. With $500-1,000 you can still make "significant gains" if realistic [3:02].
- "it matters about the skill set and not so much about how much money you have" [2:02].

**Attribution uncertain (probably Swaggy C; either way both agreed):**
- **Risk 1-3% per trade:** "I always tell all my students, 1 to 3%, they don't risk more than 1 to 3%" [16:50]. "All my students" fits Swaggy (Swag Academy), but Mamba FX may also teach.
- **Small accounts:** "trade it like it's a big account" [16:50]. Worked example: a **$50 account**, **0.01 lot**, **40-pip stop = $4** risk, which is too large a percentage of the account [16:50-17:52].
- Don't risk 20% per trade: "nobody really ... don't ever risk that, don't do that" [15:49].
- Expect losses: "you're not gonna win every single trade" [17:52].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
No trades walked through.

### 11a. Swaggy C's own points (high to medium confidence)
- **$3,000 live challenge account** with a public Myfxbook record. Instruments: gold, US30, GBP pairs [?], Tesla, Apple, narrowing to two or three [1:02-2:02].
- **Grow capital realistically or trade investor capital**, not micro accounts [3:02].
- **Psychology was his breakthrough.** He gives naked or simplified charts partial credit: "naked trading in terms of like simplifying stuff, that definitely helped my game, but even when I simplified it ... the minute I took my emotions out of it" [13:49]. He then trusted two to three years of study and was "robotic" about execution [13:49]. (Medium confidence. The phrasing matches his Episode 6 confidence speech.)
- **Demo trading:** a question, probably asked by Swaggy: "what's your opinion on demo trading ...?" [14:49]. The answer "use a demo account ... I blew 10, 12 accounts ... lost thousands" [14:49] is probably the **guest**'s. Swaggy's 2020 story is babysitting and studying, but this is unclear.
- **"No cap" on trading income:** "trading [is] probably the only job in the world where ... there's really no cap ... NBA has a cap" [15:49]. Attribution uncertain (the basketball analogy fits Swaggy).

### 11b. Mamba FX (guest) points, kept separate and not part of Swaggy C's strategy
- **Simplify to naked charts:** "I stopped using any of those indicators ... at this point I use RSI and that's it, just for divergences" [12:49]. He focuses on "supports, resistances and like break and retest trend lines, just like very basic stuff, that's when it got easy" [12:49]. (Likely guest, see attribution note in section 1.)
- **Keep your risk:reward on every trade (attribution uncertain, probably guest):** "as long as your risk to reward ratio is fine on every single trade". Lose three and win one at 1:4 and you are still fine. The mistake: "they use a 1 to 4 but then they start to get impatient". "When you're letting your stop-loss get hit, you have to let your take profit get hit ... you can't just say I'm gonna let this hit my 20 pips stop loss but if it goes up 7 pips I'm taking profits" [12:49]. The other speaker answers: "that exact speech ... that's worth everything" [13:49].
- **Taking profit at 10 pips and watching it run to TP means you aren't ready:** "at that point I feel like you need to go back to demo" [13:49-14:49]. (Speaker unclear.)
- **Avoid obvious support and resistance (stop hunts by "the banks"):** "if you see like a beautiful support ... it's not about to get respected ... the banks are gonna flood that thing out ... no obvious trades" [18:52]. The other speaker agrees ("if it looks so good like this, it's not real") and adds: "doesn't mean sell either ... they'll go down a little bit and then ... take you out" [19:52]. The tip is introduced with "until you start to think like the banks". Who said it is uncertain; both speakers endorse it.
- **Persistence:** "I gave up probably a hundred times just to come back the next day" [18:52]. Speaker uncertain.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- (Swaggy) Prove it on a real small account: "this is a live account ... this is not ... a demo account" [2:02]
- (Swaggy) Don't try to flip micro accounts: "not trying to take a ten cent account or a one dollar account to ten thousand, no, that's not what you should be doing" [3:02]
- (Swaggy, likely) Execute without emotion: "put the trade on and walk away or like close my phone or like be robotic" [13:49]

- (Uncertain speaker) "trade it like it's a big account" [16:50]
- (Uncertain, probably guest) Respect the planned R:R: "when you're letting your stop-loss get hit, you have to let your take profit get hit" [12:49]
- (Both agree) Avoid obvious S/R: "no obvious trades" [18:52]; "if it's a support that the whole damn world sees, it's not gonna be respected" [19:52]

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- (Swaggy, likely) Early on he relied on analysis alone. The change was mental: "the minute I took my emotions out of it" after two to three years of study [13:49]. Simplifying charts "definitely helped" but was not the main change.
- (Guest, likely) Moved from many indicators to naked charts plus RSI divergence [12:49].
