# 2020-06-23 — My 2020 Day Trading Strategy STEP-BY-STEP (Still Works in 2025 For Beginners)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=h_WSHrIzSLU |
| Uploaded | 2020-06-23 |
| Length | 20:10 |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (level-to-level), with a contrasting scalping variant |

## 1. What this video covers
The first ~7 minutes are channel/Academy announcements. From [7:07] he plays a ~10-11 minute excerpt of an Academy video recorded "about a month ago" (so roughly May 2020) in which he lays out his intraday/swing "level-to-level" strategy: key level -> trend direction -> candlestick confirmation (e.g. bullish engulfing) with price above the moving averages on the 1H/2H/4H -> drop to the 15m/5m for a break-of-structure entry -> ride to the next key level / mid-level, with Fibonacci (A-B-C + 27 extension) as an extra target tool. He then contrasts it with a scalping version of the same approach (repeated in-and-out entries with the trend, exiting at every consolidation). He states at [4:05]-[5:06] that, even by mid-2020, he no longer trades "structurally and foundationally" exactly this way, but still uses the same principles.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Self-described: "I'm an intraday slash swing trader, I trade from level to level" [7:07]. Says he combines intraday and some scalping in this video.
- Time split: scalping is "maybe three percent"; "ninety-seven percent I like swinging into intraday trades" [16:16].
- Timeframes:
  - Trend/structure: 4H (the "overall trend on a four-hour" decides scalp direction [16:16]).
  - Confirmation: 1H, 2H, or even 4H (bullish engulfing + price above MAs) [8:07]; he does the write-up on the 2H [16:16].
  - Entry: 15m (break of structure / higher low) [8:07]; "use the fifteen and five minutes to enter" [10:09]; the written step says "scale down to five or two minutes" [17:17] (likely "five or 15 minute" — wording uncertain [?]). Scalping section says "scale down to the five [or] 30-minute [?]" [18:19].
- Holding: ride a trade from one key level to the next (or at least to the mid-level), described as "250 pips or 500 pips" moves [18:19].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Pairs he keeps to: "AU [AUDUSD], gold and GA [GBPAUD]" [8:07] ("I keep to the same pairs"). Charts shown: GA [7:07], a pair with a box breakout, AU, and he mentions going to a quick chart of another pair (caption "USD here" [15:16], unclear which).
- Strong emphasis on mastering one pair: "focus on mastering one pair as opposed to trading different pairs" [14:15]; closing line "try to master that with only one pair" [19:21].
- Says he looks at fundamental announcements as well as technicals [11:11].
- Sessions: Not covered in this video.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Key levels (support/resistance) and mid-levels between them (he expects price to retest "the mid level").
- Moving averages (two MAs; "price goes above the moving averages", "moving average crosses over"). Periods/types not stated in this video.
- Candlestick patterns as confirmation: hammer, morning star, inverted head & shoulders, bullish engulfing, doji + push (morning star) [7:07], [14:15]; marubozu-type full-body candles as momentum [11:11].
- Structure: higher lows/higher highs, "legs" (he draws legs, "not a trend line") [9:07]; consolidation boxes and box breaks; counter-trend lines on lower timeframes [17:17].
- Fibonacci: A-B-C drawn, "drag it straight through price", targets the 27 extension (-0.27) [13:15]-[14:15]; also AB=CD style leg matching ("match to our second leg") [13:15].
- Chart colors: he normally trades on all-black ("black") charts and changes candle colors only so viewers can see: "I'm trying to get you guys to understand black charts but just so you guys can see it better" [11:11].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
His own written summary at [17:17] ("for my strategy, intraday swing but more so intraday trading"), combined with the walkthrough:
1. **Identify key levels.** "First thing you do is see what it does at a key level" [7:07]. Price must be at/near a key level before he analyzes for a trade.
2. **Determine the trend** from those levels/structure (4H) and trade only with it: "we don't counter-trend trade at all" [17:17].
3. **Identify confirmations** on the 1H/2H/4H: a bullish (or bearish) candlestick confirmation such as hammer, morning star, inverted H&S, bullish engulfing, AND price moving above the moving averages [7:07]-[8:07]. For box/consolidation setups, a breakout of the consolidation range and above the MAs [8:07]-[9:07].
4. **Judge candle quality on the breakout**: a full-body, barely-wicked candle breaking the box = real momentum; a wicky candle breaking the box = no confirmation [11:11]-[12:12].
5. **Scale down to the 15m (or 5m)** and wait for a break of structure: a higher low (uptrend) / counter-trend-line break / MAs crossing back up / any lower-timeframe bullish confirmation [8:07], [17:17].
6. **Enter**, stop below the higher low/structure, target the next key level (partials at the mid-level), and use Fib extensions as extra confluence for the target [8:07], [12:12]-[14:15].

## 6. Entry rules
- Must be at a key level first.
- Higher-timeframe (1H/2H/4H) confirmation: bullish engulfing (or similar) + price above the MAs.
- Entry trigger on 15m/5m: "you enter only on a break of structure, which is if you do see a counter trend line like on a five minute time frame, once price breaks the counter trend line or the moving averages cross back up or you get any type of bullish confirmation on a lower time frame, you enter" [17:17].
- On the box-breakout example: enter on the higher lows once price has broken the box: "you're gonna enter any one of these higher lows, especially when it broke the box for sure" [9:07]. Can re-enter each new higher low ("make another higher low, enter") [10:09].
- Only full-momentum breakout candles qualify; a wicky breakout candle is not a valid entry [11:11]-[12:12].
- Order type: market entries implied; not explicitly stated.
- Scalping variant: re-enter every time price breaks out of a small consolidation box in the trend direction [15:16]-[16:16].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- "Stop loss right below here" = below the 15m higher low that triggered the entry [8:07].
- On the full-momentum box breakout: "stop-loss right below here, just low, or even this lower [low] over here" [12:12] — below the most recent low or the lower one beneath it.
- No pip size stated.

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Target is the next key level: "enter to at least... this next level up here, but mainly up here" [8:07].
- Mid-level between key levels is the minimum / partial target: hold "from one level to the next level or at least to the mid level, for 250 pips or 500 pips" [18:19].
- Fibonacci extension: A-B-C Fib, target "27 extension" (-27%), matches the second-leg projection [13:15]-[14:15].
- At a key level he doesn't assume continuation — "see what it does at this key level" [13:15].

## 9. Trade management
- Partial profits at the mid-level or when approaching the next level: "this is what I'm going to take partial profits... for 250 pips you want to... take some money out" [8:07]; "if you want to take a little bit of profit out now and take partial profit you can" [10:09].
- If structure breaks (price breaks below the last higher low) he expects a retest of the mid-level and exits/takes profit; re-enters later back on the 4H when structure resumes [10:09]-[11:11].
- Scalp variant: exit whenever price consolidates; re-enter on the box break [15:16]-[16:16], [18:19].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
Not covered in this video (no % risk, lot sizes or R:R stated). He notes scalping costs more in spread/fees/commissions because of repeated entries [19:21].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video

### Trade 1: GBPAUD (GA) long — template example (7:07–8:07)
- Hypothetical: price at a key level, reversing upward. Waits for a bullish confirmation (hammer, morning star, inverted H&S...), then a bullish engulfing with price above the MAs on 1H/2H/4H.
- Drops to 15m to find a break of structure / higher low; enters there.
- Stop: just below the higher low [shown on chart, not stated].
- Target: next level up, "mainly up here"; partials if 250 pips into the trade; warns price may go to the mid-level and head back down.
- Result: not stated (illustrative).

### Trade 2: Unnamed pair (likely AU or gold [?]) long — consolidation box breakout (8:07–13:15)
- Price consolidating in a box; on the 2H/1H it formed a higher low inside the box. Scaled down to 15m: "nothing but higher lows"; draws a leg, not a trend line.
- Price breaks above the box and above the MAs, starts making HH/HL. Entries on any of the higher lows after the box break, especially after the break.
- Exit: when price breaks below the last higher low he expects a retest of the mid-level; takes profit there; back on the 4H looks to re-enter.
- Second entry comparison [11:11]-[12:12]: an entry off a box break with a full-body, low-wick candle (valid, "full momentum") vs an earlier break made with wicky candles (invalid — price formed another consolidation and came back down).
- Second-leg entry: hammer above, then a full-momentum candle; stop below the recent low or the lower low; take profit up at the level [shown on chart, not stated].
- Fib: A-B-C, "drag it straight through price", second leg matches up to the 27 extension [13:15]. Current price at a key level; may reverse there, retest the mid-level support, then continue toward the 27 extension — "just be mindful" [14:15].
- Prices: none stated.

### Trade 3: AU (AUDUSD) long — morning star at level (14:15)
- Refers to an earlier "scalping one two and three" video call: price came to a level; he got "a Morningstar, this doji, and then it pushes right up". Entry/stop/target not stated; "easy and clockwork when you understand the pair".

### Trade 4: Scalping example (pair unclear, caption "USD") — with-trend scalps (15:16–16:16)
- 4H trend up, so only buys. On 15m (or lower): enter, ride ~30 pips, exit on consolidation; box breaks, re-enter, ~20 pips, exit on consolidation; repeats. A drop inside the trend is not shorted because the 4H trend is up; re-enter when MAs cross back over.
- Results: ~30 pip and ~20 pip legs [15:16].

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Analyze only at key levels: "When price gets to that exact level, then I analyze" [7:07].
- Never counter-trend: "We don't counter trend trade at all" [17:17].
- Need full-candle momentum on a breakout: "This candlestick is full, barely any wick, full momentum" vs "a bunch of wicks... there's no indication" [11:11].
- Use every tool: "I look at everything, look at the candlesticks" [11:11]–[12:12]; also fundamentals [11:11].
- Don't assume continuation at a key level: "Don't just automatically assume price to head up there, see what it does at this key level" [13:15].
- Scalpers must still go with the trend: "If the trend is up, we are looking for buys" [15:16].
- Master one pair: "Focus on mastering one pair as opposed to trading different pairs" [14:15].
- Scalping is costly: "you'll get hit with the same spread... and fees and commissions" [19:21].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- At [4:05]-[5:06] (intro recorded June 2020): "I don't even trade structurally and foundationally like that way fully anymore. I still use those same principles and rules but I don't trade specifically like that. I've adapted and adjusted" — the excerpted level-to-level method is the older Academy version (Academy content made Dec 2019–Jan 2020; this clip was made ~May 2020); his "new trading style" was to go in the new Academy content.
- Title says "Still Works in 2025" (title updated later); no 2025 content in the video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- MA periods/types not stated (other 2020 videos: blue and red MA; check elsewhere).
- Entry timeframe wording: "scale down to five or two minutes" [17:17] and "five 30-minute" [18:19] are probably mis-captioned "5 or 15 minute" [?].
- Pair for the main box-breakout walkthrough (8:07–14:15) is not clearly named.
- No prices, pip-stop sizes, risk % or R:R given. Chart-only details (exact levels, Fib points) are [shown on chart, not stated].
- "250 pips or 500 pips" level-to-level moves are generic figures.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I'm an intraday slash swing trader, I trade from level to level. That means I have my key level set and when price gets to that exact level then I analyze." [7:07]
- "If we see a bullish engulfing and price goes above the moving averages on a one hour or two hours, even a four-hour, I'm scaling down to a 15-minute time frame and seeing if I can find a break of structure." [8:07]
- "If I saw this higher low right here after seeing the bullish engulfing confirmation and it's on key level, I'm entering, with a stop loss right below here." [8:07]
- "Use the fifteen and five minutes to enter. That is the strategy... I ride it to my next level." [10:09]
- "Identify key levels... see what the market is trending so you can trade with it. We don't counter trend trade at all." [17:17]
- "Once price breaks the counter trend line or the moving averages cross back up or you get any type of bullish confirmation on a lower time frame, you enter." [17:17]
- "Instead of holding it from one level to the next level or at least to the mid level for 250 pips or 500 pips, you enter over and over again." [18:19]
- "Maybe three percent [scalping]... ninety-seven percent I like swinging into intraday trades." [16:16]
- "I don't even trade structurally and foundationally like that way fully anymore. I still use those same principles and rules." [4:05]

| Length | ~12:30 (estimated from last caption marker [12:24]) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (level-to-level, MAs, market structure, momentum candles) |

## 1. What this video covers
A short price-action / market-structure lesson on one AUDUSD 4-hour/2-hour example. He shows how he ignores the higher-timeframe (monthly/weekly) trend and trades the most recent structure, waits for a counter-trend pullback to reach a key level and consolidate, and then enters on a stack of confluences (higher low, moving-average crossover, break of the counter-trend line, Marubozu momentum candle). Stop goes below the last higher low and the target is the next key level, giving him a claimed 1:18 R:R. He also explains "mid-levels" (halfway between two key levels) and why he draws zones rather than lines. This is the 2020 level-to-level intraday/swing version of his strategy.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Style for this strategy: "not a swing trade" but "in the middle of intraday and swing trading" — holding "maybe a couple of hours to one or two days" because price may not reach the next level within an hour or two [10:17–11:18].
- He says he also has a separate scalping strategy and an intraday strategy (not shown) [10:17].
- Analysis on the 4-hour chart, drop to a 2-hour to watch the pullback [3:07]. Explicitly does **not** start from monthly/weekly for trend in this example: "We're looking at the most recent price action" [3:07].
- He says he is not a fixed-hours trader: "I'm not like trading randomly… okay I only trade from 6 to 8" — i.e. he waits for price to reach a level rather than trading a time window [4:08].

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Example pair: AUDUSD (captions say "80 USD"; prices around 0.60–0.65 confirm AUDUSD).
- No session/time-of-day rules given.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Moving averages are the only indicator: "I don't use indicators except moving averages; that is the only indicator I use" [3:07]. Periods not stated here (two MAs that cross; colours not stated).
- Horizontal support/resistance key levels, drawn as **zones** (boxes around the line), plus **mid-levels** halfway between two key levels (e.g. 0.6250 between 0.6000 and 0.6500) [8:14–9:16].
- Fibonacci can be used just to measure the 50% midpoint, but he prefers to draw a horizontal mid-level line [9:16].
- Counter-trend trend line (drawn across the pullback) [7:14].
- Consolidation "box" drawn around a ranging area [5:09].
- Candlesticks: Marubozu ("Mero BOSU"/"Amir Bolton" in captions) = momentum candle [7:14].
- Rejects RSI, volume, Bollinger Bands, harmonic scanners, software: "all that stuff is fake" [11:18].
- Pays attention to fundamentals (no specifics) [11:18].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Open the 4-hour chart and read the **most recent** trend from structure and the direction of the MAs (here: uptrend, MAs pointing up) [3:07].
2. Note the last breakout of resistance and that price has not yet retested it [3:07].
3. When price pulls back (counter-trend: lower highs/lower lows), do not trade the counter-trend; expect it to stop and resume the main trend [4:08].
4. Drop to the 2-hour and watch price as it reaches the key level/zone [3:07–4:08].
5. At a major level price will either reverse or break through — wait to see which [4:08].
6. If price consolidates at the level with many wicks (he says 8–15 wicks), expect a reversal, not a break: "price will not get to a level, consolidate, have 8 to 15 wicks and then keep going in that direction" [4:08–5:09]. Draw a consolidation box.
7. Count rejections of the zone (here 8 rejections on 2-hour candles = 16 hours) as evidence the zone holds [5:09–6:11].
8. Look for the higher low (confirming the uptrend resumes) [6:11].
9. Stack confluences: higher low → MA crossover → break of the counter-trend line → break made with a Marubozu momentum candle [7:14].
10. Enter, stop below last higher low, target the next key level (with the mid-level as an intermediate reaction point) [8:14–9:16].

## 6. Entry rules
- Only trade in the direction of the most recent trend; enter after the counter-trend pullback ends at a level [3:07–4:08].
- Confluences listed (in order): (1) structure higher low, (2) MA crossover, (3) break of the counter-trend line, (4) break done with a Marubozu momentum candle [7:14].
- Aggressive entry: as soon as you see the MA crossover plus the higher low [7:14–8:14].
- Patient entry: wait for the Marubozu candle to close above the counter-trend line [8:14]. He calls himself "more aggressive" and enters on the Marubozu close + MA crossover [8:14].
- Market order on candle close (implied; order type not explicitly named).
- Additional entries: "you could have got a second entry right here or right here" on later retracements (e.g. at the mid-level) — details reserved for his paid course [9:16–10:17].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- "Stop-loss below the last higher low" [8:14].
- General: when trading levels "your stop-loss always goes below that level or above that level" (below for an uptrend, above for a downtrend) [10:17].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- "My take profit level is the next level" [8:14]. Here this gave 1:18 R:R.
- Mid-level (halfway between key levels) is where price may retrace on the way — here 0.6250 [8:14–9:16].
- Targets are the mid-level or the very next level, so R:R is "always higher than a one to three" [10:17].

## 9. Trade management
- Expect a retracement at the mid-level on the way to target; it does not invalidate the trade [8:14–9:16].
- Can add a second entry on the retest of the mid-level [9:16–10:17].
- No break-even/partials discussed in this video.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- R:R: example 1:18; "I'm not trading one to one, one to two" [8:14]; with level trading R:R "will always be higher than a one to three" [10:17].
- No risk %, lot size or account examples in this video.
- Says he has been in the market "for six years now" [8:14].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: AUDUSD long (3:07–10:17)
- Context: 4-hour chart, most recent trend up, MAs up; price had broken above a resistance level [shown on chart, not stated] and was pulling back to retest it.
- Timeframes: 4-hour for trend, 2-hour for the pullback and entry.
- Setup: counter-trend pullback (lower highs/lower lows) into the key zone; price consolidated with many wicks; the zone was rejected 8 times on the 2-hour (≈16 hours). Then a higher low formed.
- Confluences: higher low; MA crossover; break of the counter-trend line; break done with a Marubozu momentum candle.
- Entry: on the close of the Marubozu with the MA crossover (aggressive); earlier aggressive option at MA crossover + higher low. Price [shown on chart, not stated].
- Stop: below the last higher low [price not stated].
- Target: next key level [shown on chart, not stated]; stated R:R 1:18.
- Path: price rose, retraced to the mid-level 0.6250 (between 0.6000 and 0.6500 per his description), bounced, retested it again, then reached the target.
- Extra entries: possible second entry on the mid-level retest [location shown on chart].
- Result: target reached (1:18 R:R). No pips/$ stated.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Don't trade counter-trends: "this is the kind of thing we do not trade, counter trends" [4:08].
- Use only recent price action for trend, not monthly/weekly: "No, I'm not doing that, we're looking at the most recent price action" [3:07].
- At a major level price does one of two things: "it is gonna reverse or it's gonna break through it. That is what I trade and what I wait on" [4:08].
- Consolidation with many wicks at a level means reversal: "if price starts consolidating at a level, chances are it's gonna reverse" [4:08].
- Marubozu = continuation: "if you see a Marubozu, chances are it's gonna continue in that same direction" [7:14].
- Draw zones, not lines: "price does not stop at an exact number every single time, it'll stop in the area around it" [9:16].
- Have mid-levels: "my mid levels that I preach that everybody have in the middle of their support and resistance" [8:14].
- Prioritise S/R above all tools: "I place significant importance on support and resistance" [10:17].
- No R:R below 1:2: "I'm not trading one to one, one to two" [8:14].
- No indicators other than MAs: "no like RSI, volume… Bollinger Bands, I don't use any of them" [11:18].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- MA periods/type and colours not stated (he refers viewers to a separate moving-average video).
- All entry, stop and target prices are on the chart only; only the mid-level 0.6250 and the 0.60/0.65 levels are spoken.
- "80 USD" in captions interpreted as AUDUSD from the price levels.
- The "6 to 8" remark is ambiguous — it reads as him saying he does **not** trade a fixed time window, but the caption is garbled [?].
- How to get the "extra entries" is explicitly withheld ("I go into more detail in my Academy") [9:16–10:17].
- The 8–15 wick figure is a rough rule of thumb, not a precise count.

## 15. Key quotes
- "I don't use indicators except moving averages, that is the only indicator I use." [3:07]
- "We're looking at the most recent price action, and right now price is in an uptrend." [3:07]
- "When price gets to a major level it is gonna do one of two things: it is gonna reverse or it's gonna break through it." [4:08]
- "Price will not get to a level, consolidate, have 8 to 15 wicks and then keep going in that direction." [4:08]
- "A Marubozu candle is not a reversal candle, it is… a momentum candle." [7:14]
- "Stop-loss below the last higher low… my take profit level is the next level, 1 to 18 risk reward ratio." [8:14]
- "Price does not stop at an exact number every single time, it'll stop in the area around it." [9:16]
- "Your stop-loss always goes below that level or above that level… and your take profit is either the mid level or that very next level." [10:17]
- "I play support and resistance way up here." [10:17]

# 2020-05-02 — The Best Forex TimeFrames For Beginners To Trade On (Still Works in 2025)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AiFxhnrtHwA |
| Uploaded | 2020-05-02 |
| Length | ~14:30 (estimated from last caption marker [14:17]) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (top-down, level-to-level, MA crossover sniper entries) |

## 1. What this video covers
His top-down timeframe workflow in 2020: daily (sometimes weekly, only the last 2–3 weeks) for bias, 4-hour and 1-hour for confirmation and levels, then the 5- or 15-minute for a "sniper" entry with a tight stop, targeting the next key level. He walks through a GBPAUD short (claimed 1:10), a GBPAUD long reversal from a level (1:4.5), and a EURUSD long off a 4-hour hammer with a 5-minute entry (around 1:17–1:23). He stresses that MAs are short-period (not 50/100), that anything above 1:3 is good, and that getting stopped out on a tight stop is fine — just re-enter. This is the 2020 level-to-level intraday/swing version.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Timeframes he keeps highlighted: 1m, 5m, 15m, 30m, 1h, 2h(?), 4h, daily, weekly, monthly, and 3-month/quarterly [1:05–2:05] (captions: "1 minute 5 15 31 to for daily weekly monthly and 3 months").
- Analysis starts on the **daily**; looks at weekly/monthly only sometimes and only at the last 2–3 weeks: "I don't look too much at the weekly" [2:05, 7:10, 12:16].
- 4-hour (and 1-hour) = confirmation of the daily bias and where levels are [3:05].
- Entry on the **5- or 15-minute** [5:09, 12:16].
- Summary rule: "look at the daily and the four hour for the overall trend… look at the one-hour, get the confirmation you need and then scale down to the 5 or 15-minute timeframe for your perfect entry" [11:15–12:16].
- He re-assesses every time price reaches a new level (trades back and forth level to level) [5:09, 7:10].
- Holding period not stated explicitly; trades go from one level to the next.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- GBPAUD (captions "GBP AUD", "GP AUD") and EURUSD.
- No sessions or times stated.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Moving averages — "relatively close" short periods, "not a 50 or 100 EMA"; exact periods given in his separate MA video, not stated here [2:05]. MA crossover is his entry trigger; MA also acts as "a moving support or resistance" [5:09].
- If price is "in the middle of the moving average" (MAs tangled/price chopping through them) it is still consolidating — don't trade [9:12].
- Horizontal key levels; trades "level to level and mid level" [3:05].
- Consolidation boxes [4:07, 6:09].
- Candlestick patterns: bearish engulfing, bullish engulfing, hammer, Marubozu ("mr. disclose"), shooting star mentioned [4:07, 6:09, 8:10].
- Market structure (higher highs/lows, lower highs/lows).

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Start on the daily: check MA direction and how many consecutive days price has moved one way (e.g. 13 straight bearish days on GBPAUD = sell-only bias) [2:05–3:05].
2. Optionally glance at weekly — but only the last 2–3 weeks matter [7:10, 12:16].
3. 4-hour: confirm same structure (lower highs/lower lows) and mark the levels you will trade between [3:05].
4. 1-hour: same, for confirmation [3:05].
5. If price is sitting at a level and the daily trend is choppy (e.g. 6–7 days down, 6 days up), hold no bias and wait for a candlestick signal at the level (e.g. 4-hour hammer) [7:10–8:10].
6. Scale down to 5- or 15-minute; draw a box around the consolidation; wait for a break of the box, retest, MA crossover, and a lower high (shorts) / higher low (longs) [4:07, 9:12].
7. Enter, stop at the nearest structure, take profit at the next level seen on the 4-hour [4:07–5:09].
8. When price reaches the level, exit and reassess for the next trade (which may be the opposite direction) [5:09–6:09].

## 6. Entry rules
- Direction must agree with higher-timeframe bias; with a sell bias "I'm only looking for sells" [3:05].
- Shorts on the 5-minute: price breaks the support box, retests it, comes back down → entry; confluences: MA crossover, bearish engulfing, Marubozu close, lower high/lower low [4:07].
- Patient alternative: wait for the next MA crossover + another lower high [4:07].
- Longs after 4-hour bullish engulfing: 1-hour MA crossover, break above the box, higher low, retest of MA that can't break → entry [6:09].
- Reversal at a level: 4-hour hammer at the end of a downtrend → mark it on the 1-hour (1-hour MA crossover) → scale to 5-minute, only looking for buys → wait for break of resistance + retest + MA crossover + price trading above the MAs (not inside them) → enter long [8:10–9:12].
- "Price has to break a level, retest it and continue" [9:12].
- Order type not stated (entries on candle close implied).

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Place the stop so it's tight, at the structure that defines the entry: above the lower high for shorts; below the higher low / below the candle that tested the MA / below the box for longs [4:07, 6:09–7:10, 10:13].
- "You can't put a stop loss 5 pips below your trade, you'll get stopped out every time" — it must be at a real structure point (e.g. the higher low that bounced off the MA) [12:16–13:16].
- If a tight stop gets hit, "get back in" with a similar stop [12:16–13:16].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- "Your take profit is the next level" [4:07]; "go back to a 4-hour timeframe" to see it [4:07–5:09].
- R:R: "always 1:3, 1:4, 1:5", sometimes "1:12, 1:15, 1:20" [3:05]; "anything over 1:3 in my eyes is good" [7:10].
- Exit at the level: "the minute price gets to a level I'm out the trade" [5:09].

## 9. Trade management
- On reaching the level, close and reassess; look for the next trade (e.g. after a short hits the level, a 4-hour bullish engulfing gives a long) [5:09–6:09].
- Pullbacks that respect the MA are normal; MA = moving support/resistance [5:09].
- Warns against closing early out of emotion when a big wick appears; let it run to the level [11:15].
- No break-even/partials discussed here.

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- R:R minimum ~1:3; examples 1:10, 1:4.5, ~1:17–1:23 [3:05, 5:09, 7:10, 11:15].
- Losses accepted: "I got stopped out for a hundred or 200 dollars, if I end up with $5,000 that day I won" [13:16]. "You can shoot 9 for 23 as long as you get the win at the end of the day" [13:16].
- Says he made about $4,000 on the GBPAUD sell "last week" [2:05].
- No risk % or lot sizes stated. Mentions a future video on building a trading plan [12:16].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: GBPAUD short (2:05–5:09)
- Context: daily bearish for ~13 consecutive days (≈two weeks), MAs down; 4-hour and 1-hour confirm lower highs/lower lows. He says he made ~$4,000 on this sell "last week".
- Entry timeframe: 5-minute. Price broke a support box, retested it, came back down → entry [price shown on chart, not stated]. Confluences: MA crossover, bearish engulfing, Marubozu close, lower high/lower low. Patient alternative entry at the next MA crossover + lower high.
- Stop: above the lower high [shown on chart].
- Target: next level on the 4-hour [shown on chart]; 1:10 R:R.
- Path: small retrace that didn't get past the MA, then reached target. "Barely ever in drawdown."
- Result: target hit (1:10).

### Trade 2: GBPAUD long (5:09–7:10)
- Context: after Trade 1 reached the level, a 4-hour bullish engulfing formed.
- Entry: scaled to 1-hour (says he could have used 5-minute): MA crossover, break above the consolidation box, higher low retesting the MA (wick) that couldn't break → long [shown on chart].
- Stop: below the candle that tested the MA or below the box; he'd use below the candle [shown on chart].
- Target: next level; 1:4.5 R:R.
- Result: target hit.

### Trade 3: EURUSD long (7:10–11:15)
- Context: daily choppy (≈7 days bearish after ≈6 days bullish) — no bias at the level. 4-hour hammer at the end of the downtrend at the level → bullish signal; 1-hour MA crossover at the hammer close.
- Entry: 5-minute, buys only. Price broke resistance, retested, MAs crossed, price failed to break a level (wicks), then started trading above the MAs → long [shown on chart].
- Stop: very tight below the MA retest / higher low [shown on chart].
- Target: next level [shown on chart]. Measured ~1:17 after he adjusted; "probably 1 to 23" with the original tighter stop. Later says "1 to 20, 30".
- Result: went to target "slowly but surely", never in drawdown.
- Note: overall EURUSD structure later described as going up [13:16].

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Recent price action over long-term trend: "I pay attention to the most recent price action, which is why my moving averages are where they are" [2:05].
- "I'm only looking for sells" when daily/4h/1h are all bearish [3:05].
- Get in low-timeframe, plan on high-timeframe: "get in on a lower time frame based on a higher time frame" [3:05].
- "Anything over 1 to 3 in my eyes is good" [7:10].
- "The minute price gets to a level I'm out the trade and I try to reassess" [5:09].
- "Anytime price gets to a level I am stopping and analyzing" [7:10].
- Don't trade when price is inside the MAs: "it's still consolidating, I wouldn't trade it" [9:12].
- Don't put meaningless tight stops: "you can't put a stop loss 5 pips below your trade" [12:16].
- If stopped out, re-enter: "if it were to stop me out, guess what, I'd get right back in with a similar stop-loss" [13:16].
- Follow your trading plan: "as long as you are following your rules you are fine" [12:16].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video. (The YouTube title says "Still Works in 2025", added later; the content itself is 2020.)

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Exact MA periods/type not stated (refers to his MA video); only that they are not 50/100.
- List of timeframes in captions is garbled ("31 to for"); likely 30m, 1h, 2h, 4h [?].
- All prices for entries, stops, targets are chart-only.
- First pair identified as GBPAUD from his later statements ("whereas GBP AUD for 13 days straight came down").
- R:R on Trade 3 is ambiguous (1:17 measured, "probably 1:23", later "1 to 20, 30").

## 15. Key quotes
- "The first thing I do is start on a daily timeframe." [2:05]
- "It's not a 50 or 100 EMA… they're relatively close and recent." [2:05]
- "I trade level to level, and mid level as well." [3:05]
- "My risk reward's always 1 to 3, 1 to 4, 1 to 5; I'll be having 1 to 12, 1 to 15, 1 to 20 sometimes." [3:05]
- "On a five minute time frame I'm looking for a lower high where I can execute my entry as close to my stop-loss as possible." [3:05]
- "Your take profit is the next level." [4:07]
- "The moving average is obviously a moving support or resistance." [5:09]
- "Look at the daily and the four hour for the overall trend… look at the one-hour, get the confirmation… then scale down to the 5 or 15-minute timeframe for your perfect entry." [11:15–12:16]
- "If my stop loss is too tight it may stop me out real quick — guess what, get back in." [12:16]

# 2020-04-16 — How To Get Sniper Entries in Trading (Forex For Beginners)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XiqDimyXScA |
| Uploaded | 2020-04-16 |
| Length | ~16:40 (estimated from last caption marker [16:17]) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (S/R key levels, MA crossover, candlestick patterns, scaling in) |

## 1. What this video covers
Sniper entries part 2. He restates that his strategy is "purely price action and market structure": he trades only from support/resistance (supply/demand) key levels. Entries come at a rejection of a key level or at a break-and-retest; he then scales in on each new lower high (downtrend) or higher low (uptrend), moving all stops to make earlier positions risk-free. Examples on EURUSD, GBPUSD and GBPAUD, plus Fibonacci ABCD/-27 extension as an alternative target and the rule to exit manually if structure breaks before target. This is the 2020 intraday/swing version.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Level-to-level trading from key levels [1:01].
- Example charts: 1-hour (GBPUSD), 4-hour pattern then 15- or 5-minute entry (GBPAUD) [9:14, 12:14].
- "I'm immediately looking to scale in on a 15 or 5 minute time frame" after a 4-hour pattern [12:14].
- Holding period not stated.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- EURUSD (captions "your USD"), GBPUSD ("GU"), GBPAUD ("GA"); he shows a random pair (GBPNZD?) only to illustrate first step [1:01].
- He made "the 5k" on the GBPAUD move shown [11:14].
- No sessions stated. Notes that price can behave abnormally ahead of "some fundamental announcement" [5:07, 10:14].

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Support/resistance key levels ("supply and demand zones"), "the ones the banks use" [1:01–2:01].
- Moving averages (crossover is a confluence; "really tight" MAs = consolidation). Colours mentioned: "blue crossed over to red" [5:07]. Periods not stated.
- Fibonacci retracement with levels incl. 61.8 and the **-27 extension** (ABCD): used as an alternative/confirming take-profit [6:11–7:12, 14:15].
- Candlestick patterns: doji, Marubozu (momentum candle), shooting star, bearish engulfing ("big red over a small green/blue"), evening star, hammer [3:05, 4:06, 8:14, 12:14, 13:15].
- Consolidation boxes [5:07].
- Price alerts on levels (e.g. orange alert line at 1.95 on GBPAUD) [13:15–14:15].
- "I don't have a lot of stuff on my charts" [7:12].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. On a blank chart, the first thing: find the key levels (S/R) [1:01–2:01].
2. Only look to trade when price reaches a key level, not in between [2:01].
3. At resistance/support price either breaks through or rejects — read which: many dojis/consolidation = rejection ("it won't place eight dojis in a row and then break the level") [2:01–3:05].
4. Draw a box around the consolidation; watch for tight MAs (consolidation) [5:07].
5. Look for structure change (higher low / lower high), MA crossover, momentum candle → entry [3:05, 5:07].
6. For higher-timeframe patterns (e.g. 4-hour evening star), drop to 15m/5m to time the entry [12:14].
7. Target the next key level (or Fib -27 extension); stop at last structure point beyond the level.
8. Scale in on each new lower high/higher low and trail stops [4:06–5:07].

## 6. Entry rules
- "Your entry should be always at a rejection of a key level or a breakthrough and a retest" [4:06–5:07].
- Rejection entry confluences: consolidation box at the level, MA crossover, momentum candle (Marubozu) [3:05, 5:07].
- Uptrend example confluences numbered: (1) consolidation, (2) higher low / MA crossover ("blue crossed over to red"), then a huge momentum candle = entry [5:07–6:11].
- Bearish engulfing after a lower high forms = short entry [8:14].
- Evening star on 4-hour at a level → 15m/5m: MA crossover off a doji = entry [12:14–13:15].
- Scaling in: "if you're scaling in a downtrend, every time price makes a lower high, scale in, and in an uptrend every time it makes a higher low, scale in" [5:07]. Second entry e.g. at the close of a shooting star; third on next MA crossover + lower high [4:06].
- No bias: if market breaks resistance he buys, if it rejects he sells — "I adapt" [9:14–10:14].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Stop beyond the level/structure: above the rejection area for shorts, "right below" the higher low for longs [3:05, 6:11].
- On GBPAUD evening-star short, stop above the level price was failing to break [12:14].
- Align stops (and targets) with structure, not anywhere: "they place their stop-loss anywhere and take profit anywhere as opposed to aligning it with structure" [7:12].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Next key level ("level to level") [3:05, 6:11].
- Examples: 1:13, 1:4, 1:4, ~1:8 [?], 1:5.5 [3:05, 6:11, 9:14, 13:15, 14:15].
- GBPUSD next level 1.2250 ("122 five flat") [9:14]. GBPAUD target "two flat" = 2.0000 [14:15].
- Alternative: Fibonacci -27 extension of the A-B-C swing (C at 61.8) [6:11–7:12, 14:15]. If price gets within a few pips of the target, close manually [6:11].
- Minimum: "I don't trade any reward that's less than one to three" [9:14].

## 9. Trade management
- When price moves in favour, pulls back and continues, move the stop into profit to make it "risk free": "I'm looking to do two things: I'm gonna move my stop loss into profit… [and] I'm looking to scale in" [3:05–4:06].
- Trail the stop to each new lower high (downtrend) / higher low (uptrend) [6:11, 11:14].
- With multiple scaled positions, all stops move together to the latest structure point; earlier trades become risk-free, the newest one may still be at risk [4:06].
- Exit manually if structure breaks before target (e.g. in a downtrend price fails to make a new lower low and forms a higher low): "we are ditching out of the trade, closing it in profit" [10:14–11:14].
- At target, close and watch whether the level rejects or breaks [13:15].
- Uses alerts at levels [13:15].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Never below 1:3 R:R [9:14].
- Worst case after moving stops: "I won a few hundred dollars" depending on size [4:06].
- No risk % or lot sizes given.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: EURUSD short (2:01–4:06)
- Price reached resistance [level shown on chart]; consolidated (dojis) → rejection. MA crossover then a Marubozu momentum candle → short entry.
- Stop above resistance [shown on chart]; target next level down; 1:13 R:R.
- Management: after the first leg and a pullback, stop moved into profit; second entry at the close of a shooting star; third entry on MA crossover + lower high; all stops moved to the newest lower high.

### Trade 2: EURUSD long (5:07–7:12)
- At support: consolidation box, tight MAs; higher low; MA crossover (blue over red); huge momentum candle → long.
- Stop right below the higher low; target next level; 1:4 R:R.
- Management: after pullback and continuation, stop moved to make it risk-free, then trailed below each higher low; closed manually a few pips short of target.
- Alternative target: Fib from swing A to B with C at 61.8 → -27 extension lined up with the target. Second Fib ABC "a little bit short of D" was "good enough". Scale-in points along the way [shown on chart].

### Trade 3: GBPUSD short (8:14–11:14)
- 1-hour. Price broke resistance [shown], retested, made a lower high, broke support, made another lower high; doji then bearish engulfing → short. He says a lower timeframe would give a more sniper entry.
- Stop above [the lower high, shown on chart]; target next level 1.2250; 1:4 R:R.
- Management: trail stop to each new lower high; exit manually if price fails to make a lower low and forms a higher low (structure break).

### Trade 4: GBPAUD short (11:14–13:15)
- The move he says he made "the 5k" on. 4-hour evening star at a level ("big green candle, small doji, red candle follows"). Scaled to 15m/5m: price not breaking the level, MAs crossed off a doji → short.
- Stop above the level; target next level down [shown]; "1 to 8" [? captions "one day a"].
- Management: stop moved into profit (risk-free) on pullback; hit the target level and was closed.

### Trade 5: GBPAUD long (13:15–15:15)
- At the target level a hammer formed; alert at 1.95 not crossed (no break below). Higher low formed; MA crossover → long.
- Stop below the higher low [shown]; target "two flat" (2.0000); 1:5.5 R:R.
- Fib ABCD: waiting for C at 61.8; -27 extension aligns with the 2.00 target. Outcome not shown (live/unfinished at recording).

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Trade only levels: "I literally trade off support and resistance… I don't trade anything else" [1:01].
- Entry location: "your entry should be always at a rejection of a key level or a breakthrough and a retest" [4:06–5:07].
- Rejection tell: "it won't place eight dojis in a row and then break the level" [3:05].
- Scale in with structure: "every time price makes a lower high, scale in" [5:07].
- Move stop to risk-free: "a lot of people forget to use that trick" [6:11].
- Minimum R:R: "I don't trade any reward that's less than one to three" [9:14].
- No fixed bias: "I don't have a preconceived notion of the market… I just adapt and react to it" [10:14].
- Exit on structure break: "the minute it does this and breaks structure… we are ditching out of the trade" [11:14].
- Target isn't sacred: "that's just my take profit… no, it does not have to go there" [10:14].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video. He credits his mentors (Sony and Nat from "Ashville FX" [?]) for teaching him [16:17].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- MA periods not stated; only colours (blue/red) mentioned.
- Nearly all levels/prices are chart-only; spoken prices: 1.2250 (GBPUSD), 1.95 alert and 2.00 target (GBPAUD).
- R:R for Trade 4 garbled ("one day a tryst award") — possibly 1:8 [?].
- Exact mentor names uncertain ("Sony and NAT from Ashville FX" [?]).
- Trade 5's outcome not shown.

## 15. Key quotes
- "My strategy is purely price action and market structure… I trade in levels." [1:01]
- "The first thing I would do is find my key levels." [1:01]
- "When price gets to resistance it can only do one of two things: it can break through it or it can reject it." [2:01]
- "Your entry should be always at a rejection of a key level or a breakthrough and a retest." [4:06]
- "In a downtrend every time price makes a lower high scale in, and in an uptrend every time it makes a higher low scale in." [5:07]
- "Stop-loss is getting moved… so now it is a risk free trade." [6:11]
- "I don't trade any reward that's less than one to three." [9:14]
- "I don't have a preconceived notion of the market… I just adapt and react to it." [10:14]
- "The minute it… breaks structure… we are ditching out of the trade, closing it in profit." [11:14]

# 2020-04-04 — How I Get Sniper Entries When Trading as a Day Trader (25 Years Old)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sXKLyERv90c |
| Uploaded | 2020-04-04 |
| Length | ~19:35 (estimated from last caption marker [19:25]) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday (day trading, level-to-level, consolidation box + break of structure + MA crossover) |

## 1. What this video covers
Sniper entries part 1, done in TradingView bar-replay. He explains why he does not wait for a full break of a consolidation box: when price consolidates at resistance (or support), he expects the opposite direction and, on the 15-minute, enters on the first break of structure inside the box confirmed by an MA crossover. Stops go above the last lower high (e.g. ~10 pips), targets go to the next key level (1:13, 1:7, 1:3, 1:7 examples). He then trails the stop to each new lower high to make the trade risk-free, and scales in on later lower highs/MA crossovers. Examples on EURUSD (resistance 1.1250) and GBPAUD (round-number levels 1.85/1.80/1.75). This is the 2020 day-trading version.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Day trader (title); level-to-level.
- Level/context: 2-hour (and 1-hour) chart [3:10, 10:14].
- Entry: 15-minute ("you need to be on a 15-minute timeframe") [8:11]; sometimes 30-minute to check [5:10].
- Holding period not stated; trades run until next level.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- EURUSD and GBPAUD.
- No sessions stated (markets closed at recording, weekend).

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Key horizontal levels at round numbers: EURUSD resistance 1.12500 [1:05]; GBPAUD 1.85 [?], 1.825 [?], 1.80, 1.75 ("drop down in round numbers") [12:18].
- Price alerts at levels [1:05, 12:18].
- Consolidation box drawn around ranging price [1:05, 3:10].
- Moving averages: crossover = confirmation; MAs "apart" vs "close together" used to judge crossover [4:10]. Periods not stated.
- Candlesticks: "Japanese candlesticks"; bearish engulfing; doji; "little candlesticks" (shrinking candles) as a tell before a drop [9:12, 13:19, 16:23].
- TradingView bar replay for demonstration.

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Mark the key level (e.g. 1.1250 resistance on EURUSD; round numbers on GBPAUD) and set an alert [1:05, 12:18].
2. On the 2-hour, see price consolidating at the level; draw a box around it [1:05].
3. Infer direction: consolidation at resistance → expect down; at support → expect up [2:07].
4. Wait for a break of structure (a lower high and lower low) within the box — not a full break of the box [2:07–3:10].
5. Scale to 15-minute; draw a smaller box around the local consolidation; watch for fake-outs of the box [3:10–4:10].
6. Wait for MA crossover plus break of structure; he waits for "one more" confirming candle [4:10].
7. Enter; stop above last lower high; target next level from the 2-hour [4:10].
8. Watch for "little candlesticks" forming at the top/bottom of a range as a sign of an imminent move [13:19].

## 6. Entry rules
- Don't wait for the box to break: "a lot of traders will say wait for it to break the box and then get in — that's not a sniper entry" [1:05–2:07].
- Need: consolidation at the key level + break of structure (lower high/lower low for shorts; higher low for longs) + MA crossover [3:10–4:10, 9:12].
- Fake breaks of the small box are ignored; "I'm waiting for a moving average crossover but also a break of structure" [3:10].
- Also valid: bearish engulfing followed by an MA crossover [13:19].
- Re-entry/scale-in: after the trade moves and price makes a new lower high and the MA crosses back down, take a second trade [8:11, 16:23].
- Re-entry after a stop-out in profit: wait for the retest of the box, 15-minute consolidation, MA crossover + break of structure [10:14].
- A lower high that fails to exceed the prior lower high = short entry [14:20–15:22].
- Be aggressive: "you cannot be afraid to get in" [17:24].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- "Stop-loss is above the last lower high" [4:10].
- Example size: "you'd have like a 10 pip stop loss and a 90 pip profit" [11:18].
- "A few pips above" the lower high, or above the box [9:12, 15:22].
- Stops must sit at structure, not at random points: "you can't just move it to a random point" [7:10].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Next key level from the 2-hour [4:10].
- Can use two targets: first TP at a nearer level (1:3), second TP at the next round-number level (e.g. 1.75) [13:19–14:20].
- Minimum R:R 1:3: "I don't trade 1 to 1… or 1 to 2" [5:10]; "anything less than 1:3 I'm not trading" [14:20].
- May close manually close to the level [17:24].

## 9. Trade management
- Trail the stop: once in profit and price retraces then continues, move the stop to the high of that retracement (new lower high); repeat at each new lower high [5:10–7:10, 17:24].
- Rationale: price "can't break structure" — it won't go above that lower high and still continue down [6:10].
- Move it from "-$200 or -$1,000 depending on your account" to locked profit [6:10].
- Once stop is above entry the trade is "risk free" [8:11].
- Scale in on later lower highs + MA crossovers; multiple positions share the trailing stop [8:11, 16:23].
- After a TP, wait and "play it by ear" whether price breaks the level or reverses [14:20].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Lot size example: "with a two or three or four lot size" a locked-in worst case is "probably a thousand, two thousand dollars"; best case "five grand, six grand" [6:10–7:10].
- Win rate: "if you trade a 1:3, 1:4, 1:5 risk reward ratio you can lose 70% of your trades and still make thousands every single week" [5:10].
- No risk % stated.
- Says he has been trading "five years" [2:07].

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: EURUSD short (1:05–9:12)
- Context: 2-hour; resistance 1.12500 with alert; price consolidating in a box at resistance → bearish.
- Entry: 15-minute; smaller box; fake-out below ignored; waited for break of structure + MA crossover + "one more" candle → short [price shown on chart].
- Stop: above the last lower high [shown].
- Target: next level on 2-hour [shown]; 1:13 R:R.
- Management: price dropped; on the first pullback and continuation, stop moved to the pullback high (worst case ~$1–2k profit with 2–4 lots); then trailed at each new lower high. Second entry possible on the next lower high + MA cross back down.
- Result: eventually "price stopped me out" in profit — "I made a couple grand."

### Trade 2: EURUSD short re-entry (9:12–11:18)
- Price retested the box (last low); 15-minute consolidation; MA crossover + break of structure → short.
- Stop: just above [shown]; 10-pip stop vs 90-pip target described for the sniper entry [11:18].
- Target: next level; 1:6.7 (~1:7) R:R. "Never really negative"; target hit.

### Trade 3: GBPAUD short (11:18–14:20)
- Blank chart; levels drawn at round numbers: 1.85 [?], 1.825 [?] ("25 flat"), 1.80, 1.75; alert created.
- Price consolidated; "little candlesticks" at the top; bearish engulfing, then MA crossover → short.
- Stop just above [shown]. TP1 at 1:3 [level shown]; possible TP2 at 1.75.
- Result: TP hit; then he waited to see whether price would break 1.80.

### Trade 4: GBPAUD short (14:20–17:24)
- Price failed to exceed prior lower high → new lower high → short.
- Stop a few pips above the lower high (or above the area). Target 1.75; 1:7 R:R.
- Management: after price broke below 1.80, stop moved above the whole move (now in profit); scale-in entry when a doji consolidated, broke below with an MA crossover (two trades open, risk-free); stop then moved to each new lower high ("chop-chop-chop").
- Result: target reached around 1.75 (he says he'd probably have closed manually near it).

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Don't wait for the box break: "that's not a sniper entry" [1:05].
- Consolidation at a level = opposite move: "if it's purely consolidating at resistance or at support, chances are it's going the opposite way" [2:07].
- Trigger: "I'm waiting for a moving average crossover but also a break of structure" [3:10].
- "Stop-loss is above the last lower high" [4:10].
- "I don't trade 1 to 1 risk to reward ratio or 1 to 2" [5:10].
- Trail at structure: "you keep moving your stop-loss every time it retraces" [6:10].
- Not random stops: "you can't just move it to a random point" [7:10].
- "Anything less than 1 to 3 I'm not trading" [14:20].
- "You have to be aggressive when you see price break structure… you cannot be afraid to get in" [17:24].
- No perfect entries: "there's no such thing as a perfect entry" — get "as close to perfect as possible" [2:07].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- He used to use the "more relaxed" approach of waiting for a full box break; "it took me a while to get… sniper entries" [1:05–2:07].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- MA periods not stated.
- GBPAUD levels partly garbled: "1 need 5 flat… 25 flat" — likely 1.85 and 1.825 [?]; 1.80 and 1.75 clear.
- Entry/stop prices on chart only; the 10-pip/90-pip example is the only stated pip sizes.
- "We go back to a 15 minute or even 30 minute" — exact timeframe used at points unclear.

## 15. Key quotes
- "Wait for it to break the box and then get in — that's not a sniper entry." [1:05]
- "If it's purely consolidating at resistance or at support, chances are it's going the opposite way." [2:07]
- "I'm waiting for a moving average crossover but also a break of structure." [3:10]
- "Stop-loss is above the last lower high… our take profit is the next level." [4:10]
- "You can lose 70% of your trades and still make thousands every single week because your reward ratio is so high." [5:10]
- "Move your stop loss to where it went up before going back down, because price won't go back up to that level." [6:10]
- "You'd have like a 10 pip stop loss and a 90 pip profit." [11:18]
- "Anything less than 1 to 3 I'm not trading." [14:20]
- "Keep moving your stop-loss every time it creates structure." [17:24]

# 2020-04-03 — How to Trade With Fibonacci Retracement (The Best Forex Tool To Trade With)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0z39pJSwy2o |
| Uploaded | 2020-04-03 |
| Length | ~17:40 (estimated from last caption marker [17:17]) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (S/R + MA + Fibonacci retracement/extension confluence) |

## 1. What this video covers
How he uses the Fibonacci retracement tool in 2020: never on its own, always in confluence with support/resistance, trend lines and candlestick patterns. He draws it on a significant impulse (top-to-bottom in a downtrend, bottom-to-top in an uptrend), waits for price to react at 38.2/50/61.8/78.6 with a clear candle, then drops to the 15- or 5-minute for the entry. Targets are the -27 extension (first) and -61.8 extension (second/scale-ins), as an intermediate target before the next key level. He lists his whole toolset: S/R key levels, trend lines, one MA, Fibonacci, Japanese candlestick patterns. This is the 2020 version; note the contrast with his 2025-26 use of Fib 50/61.8 limit orders.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Fib drawn on the 1-hour/2-hour chart; entries on 15-minute or 5-minute ("I go to the 15-minute timeframe or 5-minute timeframe and enter") [5:09–6:09, 8:09, 15:15].
- If you "scale in" on 4-hour [? "40 min timeframe"] the stop must be much wider (1:4 instead of 1:26) [12:13].
- Holding period not stated.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Pair not named (bar-replay examples). No sessions stated.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- His full toolset: support/resistance ("key levels, key areas"), trend lines, moving average (the only indicator), Fibonacci retracement + extension, Japanese candlestick patterns [0:05, 16:17]. Hates other indicators/software: "they're all fake, they don't work" [0:05].
- **Fibonacci settings he says to copy**: 0, 38.2, 50, 61.8, 78.6 (captions "70.6"/"seventy twenty six"/"seventeen point six"), 1 (100), and extensions **-27** and **-61.8** [5:09–6:09]. ("You can copy the prices, the levels, everything I have here.")
- Drawing: downtrend — from swing high (100 at top) to swing low (0 at bottom); uptrend — from swing low to swing high [5:09, 14:15].
- ABCD labelling: A = swing high, B = swing low (0), C = retracement reversal point, D = extension target [10:11].
- Counter-trend line drawn on the retracement for confluence [10:11].
- Consolidation box [7:09].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Have key S/R levels on the chart first — needed to distinguish a **reversal** at a key level from a mere retracement [3:08–4:09].
2. Wait for a significant impulse move away from the level [4:09].
3. When price starts to pull back, draw the Fib (high→low in downtrend) [5:09].
4. Do not blindly enter at 38.2/50/61.8; wait for a significant/"overbearing" candle showing the reaction [5:09, 7:09].
5. Drop to 15m (then 5m) — draw a box around consolidation, wait for a box break or lower highs [7:09].
6. Enter; stop above the box (or tighter above a lower high for a sniper entry); target the -27 extension [8:09].
7. At target, exit; delete the Fib; reassess and redraw a new Fib if a new swing/pullback forms [9:09–10:11].
8. Add a counter-trend line on the pullback; when it breaks with lower highs and failure at the MA, enter/scale in [10:11–11:12].

## 6. Entry rules
- Fib is never traded alone: "Fibonacci is not to be traded by itself, it's meant to be… in confluence with support, resistance, the trend lines and major market patterns" [3:08].
- Enter on the pullback, never chase the impulse: "don't even try to enter, just wait for it to retrace" [15:15].
- Confirmation needed at the Fib level: a strong candle / candlestick pattern (doji, hammer, shooting star, evening/morning star) printing on 38.2/50/61.8/78.6 "is telling you to get in" [7:09, 16:17].
- On 15m: lower high + break of structure/box → short (mirror for longs) [7:09–8:09].
- Also: counter-trend line break + price failing at the MA + lower highs [11:12].
- Scale in on further retracements [12:13, 14:15].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Above the consolidation box, or tighter above the lower high for a sniper entry [8:09].
- Sniper entry on 5m/15m → very tight stop (example 1:26 R:R) versus a much wider stop if entering on higher timeframe (1:4) [11:12–12:13].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Normal take profit = next key level; the Fib extension gives an earlier target when the next level is far away ("no man's land") [1:06–2:06, 6:09].
- First target: **-27 extension**; if price goes beyond it, **-61.8 extension** [8:09–9:09].
- With multiple positions: first trade TP at -27, scale-ins at -61.8 [12:13].
- If the next support is very close, target it instead [11:12].
- Sometimes price stops at 0 (the prior low) but more often reaches the extension [9:09].

## 9. Trade management
- At target, close: "the minute it does that, get out the trade" [8:09–9:09].
- Once in, don't micromanage — set stop properly and let it play out; closing on consolidation made you miss the move [13:14].
- Redraw Fibs as new swings form; "don't force it" [9:09].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- R:R examples: 1:3 (first trade), 1:26 (sniper version) vs 1:4 (higher-TF entry) [8:09, 11:12–12:13].
- No risk % or lot size.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: Unnamed pair short (3:08–9:09)
- Context: price reversed at a resistance key level [shown on chart] — a reversal, not a retracement. Significant drop.
- Fib drawn from swing high to swing low on the pullback. Price touched 38.2 first then pushed higher (blind 38.2 sellers would have lost); then a strong bearish candle.
- Entry: 15m; consolidation box; lower high → short [shown on chart].
- Stop: above the box (sniper option tighter).
- Target: -27 extension; 1:3 R:R. Price went "right to" the -27 extension; closed.

### Trade 2: Same pair short / scale-in (9:09–13:14)
- New Fib drawn on the next swing (ABCD). Counter-trend line on the pullback; price failed at the MA and made lower highs → short.
- Stop: sniper on 5m/15m very tight → 1:26 R:R; higher-timeframe entry would need a stop "way up here" → 1:4.
- Target: near the support level / extensions; first trade TP at -27, scale-ins at -61.8.
- Result: dropped to target area after some consolidation.

### Trade 3: Uptrend long (13:14–15:15)
- Fib drawn bottom to top; price bounced at the 50; extension -27 above = TP. Price reached the -27 extension "clockwork". Entry details not given ("I'm not gonna talk about entries right now").

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Never trade Fib alone: "Fibonacci is not to be traded by itself" [3:08].
- Don't blindly enter at a level: "I'm not a trader who just tells you it'll reverse off 38.2 or 50 or 61.8" [5:09].
- Wait for the retrace: "price always has to retrace no matter what" [15:15].
- Don't FOMO into impulses: "don't have FOMO and try to get in the trade" [15:15].
- Exit at the extension: "the minute it does that… get out the trade" [8:09].
- Don't force Fibs: "the Fibonacci retracement should be constantly added if you see it fit, don't force it" [9:09].
- Let trades play out: "once you're in a trade relax… have your stop someplace, have it in a proper place and let it play out" [13:14].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
Not covered in this video.

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Pair and all prices are chart-only (bar replay).
- 78.6 level rendered variously in captions ("seventy twenty six", "70.6", "seventeen point six") — almost certainly 78.6.
- "If you did scale in 40 min timeframe" probably means 4-hour [?].
- Extension levels written "negative 27" and "negative 61.8" — in TradingView these are -0.27 and -0.618.
- MA period not stated.

## 15. Key quotes
- "The only indicator I use is a moving average and of course the Fibonacci retracement and the Fibonacci extension tool." [0:05]
- "Fibonacci is not to be traded by itself, it's meant to be… in confluence with support, resistance, the trend lines and major market patterns." [3:08]
- "For a downtrend you click it and drag it from the top, the swing high, to the bottom, the swing low." [5:09]
- "These levels below zero are our take profit every single time." [6:09]
- "Take profit way down here… negative 27 extension." [8:09]
- "Once you see it slowly reversing, scale down to the 5-minute or 15-minute, enter, and have your take profit at the negative 27 extension or the negative 61.8." [15:15]
- "Price always has to retrace no matter what." [15:15]
- "Use trend lines, use support and resistance, use the Fibonacci, use a moving average… and know the Japanese candlestick patterns." [16:17]

# 2020-03-04 — 23 Mins of Value On When To Buy & When To Sell When Trading Forex

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-GyagNA9Hdg |
| Uploaded | 2020-03-04 |
| Length | ~23:20 (estimated from last caption marker [23:17]) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (enter near levels/trend lines after a higher low/lower high, target next key level) |

## 1. What this video covers
His main point: traders lose because they enter too early or too late — chasing big candles in "no man's land" leaves no logical stop. He teaches to wait for price to come back to a key level, trend line or Fib area, see a candlestick signal and a higher low/lower high, then scale to the 1-hour/15-minute and enter with a tight stop under the structure, targeting the next major level (often a weekly level with a history of reversals). Examples on USDJPY (110 and 112 levels) and GBPAUD (1.94/1.95/1.9750). He also shows exiting manually when price stalls (dojis + lower high, or no new low). This is the 2020 intraday/swing version.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Weekly/daily to analyse and find major target levels; 4-hour and 1-hour, even 15-minute, for entries [5:05–6:06].
- Entry examples on 1-hour and 15-minute [5:05, 11:10, 16:14].
- Holding period not stated.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- USDJPY ("UJ"), GBPAUD ("GA").
- Notes forex runs Sunday–Friday; if you miss a move, "it's only Monday", wait for the retrace [21:15].
- No session times.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Support/resistance key levels and areas (drawn as grey zones/boxes) [12:12].
- Trend lines [2:03, 3:03].
- Moving average(s) — break above MA or doji on MA as entry trigger [4:04, 6:06]. Period not stated.
- Fibonacci retracement with ABCD; 50% bounce and -27 extension target [11:10, 14:13].
- Candlesticks: hammer (bullish reversal), doji, tweezer top, Marubozu ("Mirabeau zoom"), shooting star, engulfing [4:04, 6:06, 18:14, 19:15].
- Consolidation rectangle [6:06].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. On weekly/daily, find the major levels and their history (e.g. USDJPY reversed at 112 eight times on the weekly) [7:07].
2. Identify trend and the tools in play: trend line, key level, Fib [3:03, 11:10].
3. If price is far from any level/trend line ("no man's land"), do nothing — wait for price to come back [3:03, 11:10].
4. As price approaches the level/trend line, watch: it will bounce or break [4:04].
5. Look for a candlestick reversal signal (hammer/doji/tweezer) and a higher low (uptrend) or lower high (downtrend) [4:04, 15:14].
6. Scale down (1-hour → 15-minute) and enter on the trigger (break above MA, engulfing, breakout candle) [11:10–12:12, 16:14].
7. Stop below the last low / box; target next major level [5:05].
8. At the target, wait to see break or rejection and look for the next trade [5:05].

## 6. Entry rules
- Only near a level, trend line or Fib area: "if we can get in anywhere else, do not even think about getting in" [11:10].
- Break trades: "get in once it's breaking, after it holds and retests… but get in near that level" [3:03].
- Trend-line bounce: hammer on the trend line → higher low → "most likely wait to cross above moving average" → long [4:04].
- Consolidation at a level: box; doji placed on/above the MA = get in at the close of the doji [6:06–7:07].
- 15-minute trend-line bounce: candle engulfs the prior red candle and breaks above MA → long [11:10–12:12].
- Fib: price stops at the 50% and makes a higher low → enter at the close [14:13].
- Retest of a broken level: after break of 1.95, retest, higher lows on 15m, break above MA → long [15:14–16:14].
- Sell at a major level with multiple confirmations: tweezer top + Marubozu + shooting star + close below MA ("four different confirmations") [18:14].
- Never chase big candles (FOMO) [1:02].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- "If price is going up your stop-loss has to be below the last higher low… if it's going down, above the last lower high" [1:02–2:03].
- Below the consolidation box, a little lower because price wicks out of boxes; "below the last low" [7:07].
- Put stops at a level that, if broken, means the trade is invalid: "if it hits our stop loss it's definitely going in the other direction" [9:09–10:10].
- Too tight is dangerous; give room to breathe, but at the right structure [9:09].
- Bad entries force huge stops that make the trade not worth taking [8:08].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- "Our take profit level is always the next key level or key area" [19:15].
- Use history: USDJPY TP at 112 because it reversed there 8 times on the weekly; don't set TP beyond it [7:07–8:08].
- "Always get out at a major support or resistance level; don't try to hold it unless you see it break through" [12:12].
- TP is an area/zone; anywhere within the grey box is acceptable [12:12].
- Fib -27 extension as TP [14:13].
- R:R examples: 3:1, 5:1, 3:1, 5:1, 5:1, 3:1 (he says "five to one" meaning reward:risk) [5:05, 7:07, 14:13, 16:14, 18:14, 20:15].

## 9. Trade management
- Manual early exit when price stalls: several dojis (he counts 5) at a level then a lower high → "get out, manually take the money" [10:10].
- Exit a short when price prints a hammer, consolidates and doesn't make a lower low — "price is stalling, the trade is done, get out" [19:15–20:15].
- Don't panic-close on normal drawdown; good entries mean little drawdown [13:13].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
- Illustration: "if you're risking $100 your reward will be $500; risking $200 you'll be a thousand" [7:07].
- Win rate: "nobody is ever a 100 percent profitable trader… 80/20, 70/30, 90/10" [22:15].
- Weekly P&L framing: lose $1,000, make $8,000 → net $7,000 [22:15].
- Says he's traded five years; first two years losing [1:02].
- No risk % or lot sizes.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: USDJPY long — trend-line bounce (3:03–5:05)
- 4-hour; rising trend line; major resistance at 110.
- Hammer on the trend line → higher low → (likely) cross above MA → long [price shown].
- Stop just below the last low; TP 110; 3:1. Never went negative.

### Trade 2: USDJPY long — consolidation at 110 (5:05–10:10)
- 1-hour; price consolidating at 110; rectangle drawn. Price rejected the box low, then placed a doji on the MA → enter at close of the doji.
- Stop below the box (a bit lower, below last low).
- TP 112 — weekly level hit ~8 times historically; 5:1. Price hit 112 and reversed.
- Alternative exit: five dojis + lower high at the level → close manually.

### Trade 3: GBPAUD [?] long — 15m trend-line bounce (11:10–12:12)
- He says "GA in particular" with trend line and Fib ABCD; mid-sentence says "I get it on UJ" [?]. Price returned to the trend line; 15m candle engulfed the red candle and broke above MA → long.
- Stop below the candle; TP 1.95 ("195"). Price hit the zone, then broke above.

### Trade 4: GBPAUD long — Fib 50% (13:13–15:14)
- 4-hour: between 1.94 and 1.95 (100 pips); price stopped at the Fib 50 (ABCD) and made a higher low → enter at close.
- Stop below the box; TP the -27 extension; 3:1.

### Trade 5: GBPAUD long — retest of 1.95 (15:14–17:14)
- After break above 1.95, retest; 15m higher lows, consolidation, break above MA → long.
- Stop a little below 1.95; TP 1.9750; 5:1. Hit after hesitation.

### Trade 6: GBPAUD short at 1.9750 (18:14–20:15)
- Tweezer top + Marubozu + shooting star + close below MA → short below the Marubozu/middle of the shooting star.
- Stop above the area (tight); TP next key level [shown]; 5:1.
- Price never reached TP: hammer, consolidation, no lower low → closed manually in profit.

### Trade 7: GBPAUD long from the box (20:15–21:15)
- Box held; first higher low; breakout candle → long. Stop below box; TP 1.9750; 3:1. Target hit.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- Core: "you don't know when to correctly get in… if you know when to correctly get in the market, you will make money" [0:01].
- Don't chase: "you'll see this big-ass candle and have FOMO" [1:02].
- No man's land: "when price is way up here in no-man's land you can't get in, you have to be patient" [3:03].
- Stops: "your stop-loss has to be below the last higher low… above the last lower high" [1:02].
- TP: "always get out at a major support or resistance level" [12:12].
- Patience: "price will come back to the level you're looking at" [11:10].
- Retrace: "you have to wait for a retrace, price will always retrace" [17:14].
- Manual exit: "five dojis and a lower high, get out" [10:10].
- You will lose: "you will always lose money in the market no matter what; the goal is to make way more money than you lose" [22:15].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- His first two years he did exactly the wrong-timing entries he criticises [1:02].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- GBPAUD levels heard as "195", "105", "one on four/one on five", "197 50" — interpreted as 1.95, 1.94, 1.9750 [?]. "I get it on UJ" at 11:10 contradicts "GA" [?].
- Entry/stop prices chart-only.
- MA period not stated.
- He states R:R as "five to one" etc. (reward first).

## 15. Key quotes
- "The reason why a lot of you lose money in the market is because you don't know when to correctly get in." [0:01]
- "If price is going up your stop-loss has to be below the last higher low, and if it's going down… above the last lower high." [1:02]
- "Get in once it's breaking, after it holds and retests… but get in near that level. You can't just get in in no-man's land." [3:03]
- "Price can do two things… bounce off the trend line… or break through." [4:04]
- "Our take profit level is 112… in the past it constantly stopped at 112." [7:07]
- "If we can get in anywhere else, do not even think about getting in, just wait, be patient." [11:10]
- "Always get out at a major support or resistance level; don't try to hold it unless you see it break through." [12:12]
- "Our take profit level is always the next key level or key area." [19:15]
- "Nobody is ever a 100 percent profitable trader." [22:15]
