# 2020-02-11 — How to Backtest in Forex So Live Trading Becomes Easier (Step By Step Guide)

| | |
|---|---|
| Video | https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pfoK9v7j7hY |
| Uploaded | 2020-02-11 |
| Length | ~19:15 (estimated from last caption marker [19:14]) |
| Classification | CORE |
| Strategy era | 2020 intraday/swing (round-number key levels + consolidation boxes + candlestick confirmation) |

## 1. What this video covers
Despite the title, this is a bar-replay walkthrough of his price-action strategy (the backtesting is shown by replaying a GBPAUD 4-hour/1-hour chart). He says to trade only a few pairs — his non-negotiables are gold, GBPAUD and GBPUSD, plus AUDUSD (swappable with EURUSD) — and to know each pair's key levels and fundamental drivers. Key levels are round numbers ("where the banks and government like their levels"), drawn as boxes. He trades consolidation at levels, mini evening stars, dojis and confirmation candles that break the MA, targets the next round number or Fib -27 extension, and adjusts targets to structure. This is the early-2020 version.

## 2. Trading style, holding period and timeframes
- Bar replay on 4-hour, using 1-hour "to make it easier/faster"; real execution always on 15-minute (or 5-minute for sniper) [3:03, 7:04].
- "I will be scaling down to a 15-minute time frame any time I'm about to trade and about to execute" [7:04].
- Holding period not stated.

## 3. Markets, pairs and sessions
- Keep pairs few: "try to only keep your pairs to a few" [0:02].
- His: "GU, GA and AU, and AU can be swapped out sometimes with EU" — "my three non-negotiables are gold… GA… and GU" [1:02]. Gold: very fast, volatile; GA: fast, balanced; GU: major, "more slow, more relaxed" [1:02].
- Know your pairs: fundamental announcements that move them, historical price pattern, key levels "off the top of your head" [0:02].
- Example pair: GBPAUD.
- No session times.

## 4. Tools and indicators (with exact settings)
- Key levels at round numbers (banks/government prefer 1.900, 1.850, not 1.83225) [2:02].
- GBPAUD levels: 1.9000, 1.9250 (mid), 1.9500, 1.8750 (mid), 1.8500 [2:02, 8:05].
- Weekly/monthly levels stay forever; mini daily/4-hour levels deleted as they're used [9:06].
- Levels as thin boxes (key area, not exact number) [2:02–3:03]; consolidation boxes from wick top to body top [15:09–16:11].
- Moving average — break below/above as confirmation. Period not stated.
- Candlesticks: mini evening star, doji, confirmation candle [5:04–6:04, 10:07].
- Fibonacci: 61.8 rejection and "1.27" extension (= his -27 extension) as TP [12:08, 14:09].
- Says he did not use a single trend line in this video [17:11].

## 5. Analysis process, step by step
1. Limit yourself to a few pairs and learn their levels and drivers [0:02].
2. On a blank 4-hour chart, mark only the round-number key levels near price, box them [2:02].
3. When price reaches the level, draw a consolidation box; read direction from small candles not breaking the MA [3:03].
4. Scale to 15m and wait for breakout of a mini consolidation in the expected direction [3:03–4:03].
5. Enter; stop above the area; TP next level [4:03].
6. After a break, expect a retest of the box ("it will come back to be tested always") [5:04].
7. Look for mini evening star + confirmation candle below MA for the next short [5:04–6:04].
8. Adjust target to structure (last place price launched from) [8:05].
9. Delete used boxes; keep chart clean [11:08].

## 6. Entry rules
- At a level, sell only if consolidation with "little candlesticks" isn't breaking above the MA [3:03].
- Breakout of mini-consolidation in the expected direction [4:03].
- Retest of box + mini evening star + confirmation candle breaking below MA [5:04–6:04].
- Further shorts only after "a full break below" a level, judged by candle body close (not wick) [6:04, 9:06].
- Long: three touches of a low that won't break + doji + confirmation candle; or doji at a Fib 61.8 rejection breaking above MA [10:07, 14:09].
- Long after five rejections of a box low [16:11].

## 7. Stop-loss rules
- Above the consolidation area (shorts) [4:03].
- "Above the last high if it's a downtrend, or below the last low in an uptrend, to give you the most accurate, reasonable room" [7:04].
- Above-level move invalidates the trade [5:04].

## 8. Take-profit and targets
- Next round-number level (e.g. 1.9000) [3:03, 6:04].
- Don't over-extend: keep 1.90 as TP until it fully breaks [6:04].
- Adjust: target 1.8750 moved ~40 pips to the last area price launched from [8:05].
- Fib -27 extension ("1.27") [12:08, 13:08]. Long TP 1.8950 [11:08].

## 9. Trade management
- Exit at target; reassess [9:06, 12:08].
- Exit early if price shows it won't reach target [8:05].
- Keep weekly levels; delete mini levels/boxes to keep chart clean [9:06, 11:08].

## 10. Risk management and position sizing
Not covered in this video.

## 11. Trades walked through in this video
### Trade 1: GBPAUD short to 1.9000 (3:03–5:04)
- 4-hour at a level above 1.90 [shown]; consolidation, small candles, not breaking MA. 1-hour mini consolidation breakout → short. Stop above the area; TP 1.9000. Won.

### Trade 2: GBPAUD short — retest + mini evening star (5:04–6:04)
- Box retest; mini evening star; confirmation candle below MA → short. TP 1.9000.

### Trade 3: GBPAUD short below 1.90 (6:04–9:06)
- After full body close below 1.90 → short on 15m; stop above last high. TP 1.8750 adjusted ~40 pips to last launch area. Hit target.

### Trade 4: GBPAUD long (9:06–12:08)
- Three touches of a low; doji/confirmation; mini box; TP 1.8950 / Fib "1.27" extension. Won.

### Trade 5: GBPAUD long to 1.90 (13:08–15:09)
- Doji after 61.8 rejection; break above MA on 15m/5m → long. TP Fib extension aligned with 1.90; hit.

### Trade 6: GBPAUD long — box bounce (15:09–17:11)
- Box from wick top to body top; five rejections → long; Fib 61.8 bounce; exited [details shown on chart]. Mentions 1.9250 as next mid-level and an evening star that would invalidate.

## 12. Rules, do's and don'ts
- "Try to only keep your pairs to a few" [0:02].
- Know your pairs: "you got to understand what fundamental announcements will push that pair" [0:02].
- Round numbers: banks prefer "1.900… as opposed to 1.83225" [2:02].
- Don't clutter levels: "you're gonna have a cluttered chart… price is not gonna respect any of these levels" [1:02–2:02].
- Retests: "whenever price breaks through a level of consolidation… it will come back to be tested always" [5:04].
- Body closes: "the wick is just where it went… the bodies are where it closes" [6:04].
- Execute on 15m: "any time I'm about to trade… I scale down to a 15-minute time frame" [7:04].
- Keep chart clean; delete old boxes [11:08].
- Pure price action: "there's no indicator except moving average… no fancy scanner" [12:08].
- React to signals: "if it gives me a doji and it's supposed to go up… I'm buying; if it gives me an evening star… I'm selling" [17:11].

## 13. Changes from his earlier approach (as he states them)
- Implies he once drew too many levels: "like I said I've been here before" [2:02].

## 14. Gaps and ambiguities
- Title promises backtesting steps; transcript is a replay demo, no explicit backtesting procedure (no logging, sample size, metrics).
- Pair list garbled ("UGA and a you… EU"); read as GBPUSD, GBPAUD, AUDUSD/EURUSD plus gold [?].
- "1.27"/"one point two seventy" read as his -27 extension [?].
- Trade 6 exit details unclear.
- No risk sizing.
- MA period not stated.

## 15. Key quotes
- "Try to only keep your pairs to a few." [0:02]
- "My three non-negotiables are gold… GA… and GU." [1:02]
- "It's much easier for them to say the currency rate is 1.900 or 1.800 or 1.850." [2:02]
- "The resistance is not a number… you draw a box because it's that area, a key area." [2:02–3:03]
- "Whenever price breaks through a level of consolidation anywhere it will come back to be tested, always." [5:04]
- "Any time I'm about to trade and about to execute, I scale down to a 15-minute time frame." [7:04]
- "Make sure about the last high if it's a downtrend, or the last low if it's an uptrend." [7:04]
- "It's purely price action… there's no indicator except moving average." [12:08]
- "If it gives me an evening star and a validation that it wants to go down, I'm selling." [17:11]

# Swaggy C: visual chart notes from the two newest strategy videos (April 2026)

This is a frame-by-frame visual review of what is on his TradingView screen. The transcripts miss this. The source is 480p video (742x480). Frames were upscaled and cropped to read the labels. The working frames are in the session scratchpad (`scratchpad/vid/frames/`) and are **not** copied into the repo. Their names are given as `787_<sec>.png` / `ouz_<sec>.png`.

How to read the numbers below:
- **Read** means the value was legible on screen.
- **Derived** means the label was too blurry to read. The value was computed from the Fibonacci levels that could be read. Every derived value matched every legible label it could be checked against.
- **[unreadable at 480p]** means the value could not be read or derived.

Platform: TradingView, light theme. Broker feed: OANDA for USDJPY and XAUUSD, FOREXCOM for GBPAUD. Watchlist: XAUUSD, BTCUSD, USDJPY, GBPUSD, GBPAUD, 6J1! (yen futures).

> Scalping over a 2 year period = 1-3%
> Day Trading over a 2 yr period = 5-10%
> Swing Trading over a 2 yr period = 15-20%
> Position Trading over a 2 yr period = 25-30%
>
> LOOK AT WHERE YOU BELIEVE PRICE WILL BE 3 MONTHS FROM NOW...

>
> and the market went 700 pips = $21,000
>
> If you're on a PROP ACCOUNT =
>
> 1) You should only be a 6 figure account only beCAUSE of the daily loss
> 2) Make some money, and with your first WD, put some of that into your brokerage account so you can SCALE and position trade on your own.

### Where the visuals clarify or contradict the audio (video 1)
- **"8 and 14 EMA"**: the chart always shows exactly **two thin MAs**. The faster one is **blue/blue-grey** and the slower one is **red/salmon**. The indicator legend is hidden, so the type and period cannot be confirmed on screen [unreadable at 480p]. The settings are spoken only.
- **Psych levels are not exact**: he says to type 160.000 exactly, but the 155, 150 and 145 lines sit at 155.080, 150.154 and 145.046. The purple rectangle absorbs the error.
- **Fib is redrawn on the latest swing**: the first Fib runs 146.409 → 159.425. The working Fib runs 146.662 → 159.458 (Jan 2026 high). The replay lesson uses the second one. It was drawn with hindsight: the 0% is a high that formed later than some of the replayed bars.
- **"Stop in the middle between 78.6 and 100"**: on screen the USDJPY stop is 148.518, which is between the two levels but closer to 78.6. The GBPAUD stop is also between 78.6 and 100.
- **"Move stop into profit … 400 pips in profit"**: on screen he drags the stop to **break-even (entry, 0.001 away)**, not 400 pips above it. The 400 pips refers to open profit.
- **Fixed $ risk**: every position tool shows **"Amount: 750"** (USDJPY, GBPAUD, and also USDCAD in video 2). That is consistent with risking a fixed $750 per trade. He never says it.
- **TP ladder from the audio, shown as Fib levels**: TP1 0%, TP2 -27%, TP3 -61.8%, TP4 = next big psych number (170.048 on screen).

### Where the visuals clarify or contradict the audio (video 2)
- **"80-pip stop loss … over a 300-pip TP"**: the screen shows **74.4-pip stop, ~292-pip target, R:R 3.92**.
- The entry is a **limit at the 61.8%** of a small daily swing (~296 pips), **not** a breakout of the trendline. Price is still under the descending trendline at entry.
- The bar-replay games use the current drawings and add no new method.

---

## What the charts show about his current method (April 2026)

- **Timeframes**: analysis on **Daily and Weekly**. Weekly gives the 3-month bias and the big Fib. Daily is used to place the Fib, zones and limit orders. The **15m** appears only to show intraday noise or to zoom into an open trade. No 1m/5m/1h/4h setups are shown.
- **Instruments shown**: USDJPY (main), GBPAUD (short), USDCAD (long), XAUUSD (levels only).
- **Indicators**: two thin moving averages, fast = blue/blue-grey and slow = red/salmon. He says they are the 8 and 14 EMA; the legend is hidden, so the type and period are [unreadable at 480p]. No oscillators or volume.
- **Level drawing**:
  - Horizontal black lines at **round psychological numbers**: every 5.00 on USDJPY (160/155/150/145); every 0.025 on GBPAUD (2.050, 2.025, 2.000, 1.975, …); 1.400 / 1.375 on USDCAD; every $100 (and some $50) on gold.
  - **Thin purple rectangles** (±~0.2–0.3 JPY, ~±20 pips on GBPAUD) wrap the main lines as zones.
  - A thin box outlined in light blue/black marks prior swing highs/lows that flipped.
  - A big **beige rectangle** marks a base/range.
  - A **yellow box** marks the swing low that anchors the Fib.
- **Trendlines**: thick blue main trendline plus thinner "inner/outer" lines. Longs use a rising line from the major lows; shorts use a falling line from the major highs. The **third touch** is used as an entry confluence.
- **Fibonacci settings** (identical on every chart): **-61.8, -27, 0, 23.6, 38.2, 50, 61.8, 78.6, 100**. Labels show percent and price, on the right of the drawing. Colours: 0/23.6/100 grey, 38.2/50 red, 61.8 green, 78.6 light blue, extensions yellow/orange. The tool shows no 0.705/0.886/1.618/-1.0.
  - **Anchoring**: longs go **swing low (100%) → swing high (0%)**; shorts go **swing high (100%) → swing low (0%)**. The extensions are therefore beyond the 0% in the trade direction. He anchors on the latest completed impulse on the Daily/Weekly (USDJPY Sep-2025 low → Jan-2026 high; GBPAUD 2.015 → 1.871; USDCAD 1.367 → 1.397).
- **Entries**: **limit orders spread across a purple box covering 38.2–61.8** (USDJPY replay), or the 50–61.8 box on USDCAD. The biggest lot size goes at the deepest level. Actual position-tool entries: USDJPY 152.835 (between 50 and 61.8); GBPAUD ≈ 61.8; USDCAD 1.37835 (= 61.8).
- **Stops**: always **beyond 78.6% and before 100%** (USDJPY 148.518; USDCAD 1.37091; GBPAUD between 1.984 and 2.015). Widths shown: 431.7 pips (USDJPY W), 327 pips (GBPAUD W), 74.4 pips (USDCAD D). After price moves, the stop goes to **break-even** (shown on screen).
- **Targets**:
  - The first TP is **0%** (prior high).
  - The others are the **-27%** and **-61.8%** extensions, then the **next big psych number** (USDJPY 170.048).
  - The USDCAD single target, 1.40752, sits between -27 and -61.8.
- **R:R shown**: USDJPY **≈3.4** (TP at -61.8%) → **≈4** (TP 170); GBPAUD **≈5.6**; USDCAD **3.92**. **Fixed risk "Amount: 750"** on every position tool.
- **Directional filter**: every example trades in the direction of his 3-month projection: USDJPY up to 163–167 (cursor on Jul 10 2026), USDCAD up to 1.405–1.415, GBPAUD down to 1.78. His 3-month projection targets are the Fib extensions.

# Swaggy C — strategy content in YouTube Shorts

## Intro

This file reviews **53 YouTube Shorts** (31–123 seconds each) from Chris "Swaggy C" Williams' channel, uploaded between 2021-09-23 and 2026-10-06. They were chosen from the full Shorts list because their **titles hinted at strategy** (stops, sessions, swing/position trading, trade recaps, trading plans, risk). Every transcript was read in full (YouTube auto-captions).

- **38 Shorts** have at least some concrete strategy content (rules, numbers, timeframes, entries, stops, targets, sessions, holding periods, trade examples). They are listed below, newest first.
- **15 Shorts** are motivation or mindset only. They are listed at the end.

Notes on reading this file:
- Caption markers fall about every 60 s, so a timestamp such as `[0:02]` marks the start of the caption block the quote comes from, not the exact second.
- Caption errors have been fixed in quotes ("GU" = GBPUSD, "hikanashi" = Heikin Ashi, "agent session" = Asian session). Uncertain words are marked [?].
- Several 2025 Shorts are **re-cuts of 2023–2024 clips** (same words). For those, the era given is the era of the **original recording**, and the duplicate is named.
- Many 2023 Shorts are clipped from the long video *"CHART WORK: The 2023 Strategy That Made Me Over $100K Last Month"* (MEgXUxqSPQ4, 2023-09-23). Where that applies, it is noted.

---

# Shorts with strategy content

## 2026-10-06 — A girl whose life changed on a trade she forgot 🤯 Position Trading is the WAY. (etjvn5pccyM)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/etjvn5pccyM

- He is a position trader with wide stops and wide targets: "I'm a position trader. I have a pretty big stop-loss and a pretty big take-profit. Just leave it there." [0:02]
- Rule: set the stop and target before you enter, then do not touch the trade: "When you get in a trade, it's going to do one of two things. Hit your stop loss or hit your take-profit. We set them before the trade for a reason." [0:02]
- Expected holding time is weeks: "place a trade, leave it there, live your life … and then come back to it four, five, six, seven, eight, nine weeks later" [1:02]
- He compares this with how he used to trade: "what I used to do six, seven years ago, wasting my time day trading all day, every day" [1:02]. He says day trading "made me money, but I have done more with less position trading." [1:02–2:02]
- **Trade example (group call, about 12 weeks before upload, so around mid-July 2026):**
  - He took the trade live in front of his group (about 100 people on the call). The instrument is **not named**.
  - Week 1: "did absolutely nothing". Week 2: nothing. Weeks 3, 4 and 5: in drawdown ("we're losing money"). He told people to "just leave it there."
  - Only about 3 of the 100 left the trade untouched. About 5.5 weeks after that (about **10 weeks in total**), the market rallied to the target.
  - Result: one member was up "well over $170,000" [1:02]. Her lot size, entry, stop and target are not given.
- Strategy era: **2025–26 position trading.** This confirms his current approach: wide stop and target fixed at entry, no management, and trades held for about 4–10 weeks with several weeks of drawdown accepted. It also says plainly that he moved away from day trading.

## 2026-08-10 — This Mistake Is Making Traders Quit (mLT7cdcn5Tg)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/mLT7cdcn5Tg

- Day traders get "stopped out by two or three pips and then [price] goes in that direction." [0:02] His diagnosis: "You know how to trade. You just don't know how to day trade."
- Not every day, or even every month, is a trading day: "everybody knows every single day is not a trading day. There may even some months where the market is trash." [0:02]
- The number of trades he aims for: "Start learning how to swing to where, oh, I can actually take **14 trades in a quarter** and actually make more money." [0:02] That is about one trade a week.
- Strategy era: **2025–26 swing/position.** It gives a trade-frequency figure of about 14 trades per quarter.

## 2025-09-23 — GOLD is Going to Make A LOT of Traders Profitable 🤯🤯🤯 (tXz6sPJASKE)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/tXz6sPJASKE

- His gold thesis on the upload date: gold at about 3,700, with Goldman Sachs forecasting 5,000. "Do you know how much money you can make if you find a good entry and you **position trade it**?" [0:02]
- He focuses on one instrument: "a lot of people try to focus on 30 different things, 10 different pairs. All you need to focus on is gold to change your life." [1:03]
- The rest promotes a free 3-day gold boot camp (Sept 23–25, 7 pm, 90 minutes).
- Strategy era: **2025–26 position trading.** Gold is the main instrument, held long-term for a move toward 5,000.

## 2025-08-23 — SwaggyC: The 2 Types of Traders (cYn8GKyO48w)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/cYn8GKyO48w

- This is a rule for **withdrawing profits** instead of compounding. He calls it "the method to success in this industry." [0:02]
  - Trader 1 compounds a $10K account: 15K → 23K → big loss to 13K → 18K → 13K → 27K → 45K, and only then thinks about withdrawing.
  - Trader 2 (his method) starts with the same $10K. "If you get it to 15K, you have a small withdrawal of $1,500. So now you're at 13.5K. Scale it up to 22K … another withdrawal … 5K." [0:02]
  - His reasoning: a smaller balance means losses stay smaller in dollar terms ("it's not a massive loss because you don't have as much in your account" [1:02]). Regular withdrawals add up ("you get more money when you take it out and take it out" [1:02]).
- Strategy era: **2025–26** (money management). It does not depend on any particular setup.

- Judge results by quarter or year, not by month: "Why do you think hedge funds pay people out every quarter, semi-annually or even annually? Because it's hard to generate the same returns every single month." [0:00]
- Strategy era: The stats come from the **2024 swing era**. The advice to judge by quarter is from 2025.

## 2024-02-06 — My 2024 Day Trading Routine (fmr7RJ0gqnM)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/fmr7RJ0gqnM

- Routine: "my day usually started around **Asian session**. I look for pairs, I look for trades. If I see a good setup that I feel is going to move later, I will **enter during Asian session** because I don't want to miss the trade." [0:01]
- He doesn't watch the trade after entry. The trade will "only hit my stop loss or hit my take profit or hit my **trading alert**." By the time he wakes up he is "either massively in profit or a little bit in drawdown." [0:01]
- Exit: "I usually **take profit at New York session**. Sometimes these moves may take **2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 days** and then I'll hold it." [0:01]
- Style: "I'm a swing trader now. I don't get sniper entries. I get the most pips. I don't miss trades as much as I did in the past." [0:01]
- Strategy era: **2023–24 Asian-session swing** (enter in the Asian session, exit in New York, hold up to about a week). The title says "day trading," but he calls himself a swing trader.

## 2024-02-01 — How To Never Miss A Trade (lMn2Om15nd4)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/lMn2Om15nd4

- Rule: when he sees the setup, he enters straight away instead of waiting hours for the price: "I'll get in right then and there." [0:01] Otherwise you miss the move while you are at the gym or asleep.
- **Example: gold entry "at that 1943 level"**. "The market will definitely draw down." His alerts to the group say "I'm expecting a little bit of drawdown. If you guys aren't swing traders maybe be a little bit more patient." [0:01]
- "No matter how long it consolidates it can only hit that take profit or hit that stop loss." [0:01]
- Strategy era: **2023–24 swing** (early market entry, accepting drawdown).

## 2023-11-07 — Small Stop Loss Explained (qJN-NsW9vvM)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/qJN-NsW9vvM

- He rejects tiny stops: "I'm going to have a five pip stop loss but I'm going to have a 100 pip reward … you're not going to convince me that's going to work [over] 1,000 trades." [0:01]
- "These five, seven, even 10 pip stop losses I don't adhere to." [0:01]
- Stop and R:R rule: "some trades … require a **20 pip stop loss** and there's some that's going to require **50**. I don't care as long as my reward is let's say **one to three**." [0:01]
- Examples of wider stops: "all you had to do was have **17 pip stop loss, 27, 37** and you'd have had some drawdown." [0:01]
- Strategy era: **2023** (stops of 20–50 pips depending on the trade, at least 1:3).

## 2023-10-28 — 8 Trading Hacks (RhdVk13qJlE)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/RhdVk13qJlE

- Sessions: "I don't even trade one session. I make sure if my trade is moving during any session, I am trading it." [0:01]
- He uses alerts: "I literally have alerts all through **TradingView**." [0:01]
- Pairs: "I focus on **one to two pairs**. That is it." [0:01]
- Tools he rejects: "harmonic scanners and bat [?] patterns and Elliott … they don't work … all that is based on one thing: simple price action." [0:01]
- Getting started: learn MetaTrader on a demo account, "then instantly open a live account with **$100**" to experience real emotions. [0:01]
- He also says to get a mentor and says it is unrealistic to trade a $200 account like a $200,000 one.
- Strategy era: **2023.**

## 2023-10-14 — How To Navigate Markets with News (5-OHCX7yQaU)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/5-OHCX7yQaU

- He checks the fundamental sentiment of a pair before entering: "you guys need to understand the **sentiment** of pairs before you actually get in." [0:00]
- Tool: the **news icon (lightning bolt) on TradingView**. It shows news for that pair's currencies (pound / US dollar in his example). He reads the analysts' views "like you're reading a book." [0:00]
- His example of a headline: the Fed decision means analysts expect "USDJPY to drop a little bit and retest 146.700." He says "this is where it comes into the strategy." [0:00]
- Strategy era: **2023** (fundamental sentiment filter on top of technicals).

## 2023-10-11 — Complete Guide On How I Analyse My Trades (Z-4JYTyX6Ic)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/Z-4JYTyX6Ic

- **Chart example: GBPUSD ("GU") short, a planned sell** (clipped from MEgXUxqSPQ4, 2023-09-23).
  - Level: "place a little bit of a **horizontal** here because price clearly reacted off of that, so if price would have come anywhere near this level I may get a **sell limit**." [0:01]
  - Confluences: price retested and "created another **lower high**. I can get a **trend line** right here. I can even get a **Fibonacci**." [0:01]
  - Timeframe: "go back to **daily** time frame. TP2 kind of lines up with this **61 extension** [?, likely −61.8% Fib extension]." [0:01]
  - Target: "if price gets to **1.23 flat** I would take profit." [0:01]
  - Invalidation: if price "comes up and then … starts to come back up and break through this, get out." If it makes a lower low and then a lower high, "we're golden." [0:01]
  - Stop: "Stop will be here … this will be the last higher low [? for a sell he likely means the last lower high / swing high]." [0:01]
  - Management: "price gets to 1.23 flat, **take 60 to 75% of the trade off and let the other 25% run**. It's risk-free at that point." [0:01]
  - Moving the stop: "when I'm in a trade and I see that I'm in profit **100 pips, 90 pips, 120 pips**, chances are structure was created and I'm able to move my stop into profit." [0:01]
- Strategy era: **2023** (horizontal level + lower high + trendline + Fib; sell limit; take 60–75% at TP1, then a risk-free runner).

## 2023-10-09 — $100K/Month: My Day Trading Strategy REVEALED (Jb4vs_HZ8u4)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/Jb4vs_HZ8u4

- "A detailed version of my strategy in **2023**." Clipped from MEgXUxqSPQ4.
  1. "Take **60 to 75%** of the trade off and let the other 25% run. It's risk-free at that point." [0:03]
  2. Entry time: "I usually enter my trades between **6:30 and around 9:30 p.m.**" [Eastern, i.e. early Asian session]. He accepts drawdown: "I'm always comfortable with a little bit of drawdown. Once it gets to London session … I already forgot about the trade." [0:03]
  3. "I will get in **earlier** because I'm so confident it's going to go down." [0:03]
  4. "**Take profit if it's close to it** … people [say] I'm three pips away, two pips away from my take profit, I'm going to let it play out … take the profit." [0:03]
  5. Technicals: "have your **site [? key] levels** because you need reasons to get in the trade." [0:03]
  6. "Understand the **candlesticks**, how they close … the type of **patterns**," then "factor in **moving averages**." [0:03] (The long video names these as the 8 and 14 EMA.)
- Strategy era: **2023** 8/14 EMA + Asian-session swing.

## 2023-09-28 — The Biggest Mistake Day Traders Make (TlQGD7GQISE)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/TlQGD7GQISE

- "You have this **5, 10 pip** stop loss and gives your trade no room to breathe." [0:02]
- Fix: "if you just gave it an extra **20, 25, 30 pips** of a stop loss and … move the take profit to an area where you can hold it over a span of **one, two, three days**." [0:02]
- Strategy era: **2023** (wider stops, hold 1–3 days).

## 2023-09-21 — How To Get Into Big Trades With a Small Account (fW9SshhCTh4)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/fW9SshhCTh4

- Lot sizing for small accounts: "slowly scale that account and slowly raise the lot size every now and then." [0:01]
- Example: a **$300 account** making about **$150 with a 0.04 lot** "over the span of a few days." With a standard lot on a $400 account, "literally all it takes is **40 pips** and your account is blown." The market "can move 20 to 40 pips in two minutes." [0:01]
- He suggests buying a prop-firm account to get more capital instead of oversizing.
- Strategy era: **2023.**

## 2023-09-11 — How Much Time Should You Spend Day Trading? (3YQJVpihLMs)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/3YQJVpihLMs

- Analysis time once experienced: "you're able to look at the market and try to analyze it within **15–20 minutes** and you can have a **bias**." [0:03]
- Process: combine fundamentals/news with technicals into a prediction ("you match this, okay the technicals say this, boom, it's going to drop"). [0:03]
- Alerts: if your pair moves while you are at work, "you can set an **alert** that'll … get you a text message … and you can get in based on that alert." [0:03]
- Strategy era: **2023.**

## 2023-08-25 — Should You Always Set Up a Specific Take Profit 💰 (FXUqAIjBHPw)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/FXUqAIjBHPw

- "I **always set a take profit no matter what**," because otherwise "you are not accurately calculating risk to reward." [0:02]
- Place the TP "based off all the **confluences**" where you predict price will go. [0:02]
- Always use a stop: "if it hits my stop loss I'm out [of the] trade, but don't trade with no stop loss." [0:02]
- Strategy era: **2023** (TP/SL always fixed, so trades can run unattended).

## 2023-08-22 — How To Choose The Perfect Time To Day Trade (dP2m2NVUDfI)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/dP2m2NVUDfI

- The original recording of the sessions clip (re-cut 2025-03-31 as zOjyAQjM8UY). Times are US Eastern:
  - **New York 9 am–5 pm**: "when the USD is moving … a lot of the news regarding the US dollar tend to come out." [0:01]
  - **Asian 7 pm–3 am**: Australia, New Zealand, **gold**, Japan are most volatile. [0:01]
  - **London about 2 am–noon**: "the most volatile session … Euro, GBP pairs are moving heavily." [0:01]
- Sessions overlap. Find out when your pair moves.
- Strategy era: **2023.**

## 2023-07-30 — How To Create Trading Plan In 8 Steps (E0gTYjT0g2k)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/E0gTYjT0g2k

- What his trading plan must contain [0:01]:
  1. Your name and your "why."
  2. "How many **confluences** do you need to see before you enter a trade."
  3. "How often are you going to trade and what **session**." This needs your **pairs** and **timeframe**.
  4. "What would make you **exit** a trade."
  5. Risk management: starting capital, "the **maximum lot size** you will use in a particular trade," "your **risk-reward ratio** every single trade."
  6. How you document trades: **Myfxbook** (automated) or a hand-written journal, with monthly growth reviewed (e.g. "10% growth, 15% growth, I lost 5%").
  7. Sign it, and read it.
- Strategy era: **2023** trading-plan mechanics.

## 2023-07-25 — How To Stop Blowing Trading Accounts FOREVER!!! (bMT9VGvyIj4)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/bMT9VGvyIj4

- Oversizing is the main reason people blow accounts. On a $300 account, people put on a standard lot or 2 lots and "risk 50% of your account." [0:01]
- His path: "I didn't start out with standards and two lots and five and ten lots." Use small lots for about **a year**, even if 40–50 pips only makes $20. [0:01]
- Strategy era: **2023** (risk/lot sizing).

## 2023-06-06 — 5 Tips For Small Account Traders 💎 (ycUdcJTyZEg)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/ycUdcJTyZEg

- For accounts under $1,000: "you do not have to stay up for every single session." Execute what is in your trading plan. [0:01]
- Rule: "if the markets are clearly **consolidating** and they don't look like they're about to break out anytime soon, **walk away**." [0:01]
- "Study risk management as if your life depends on it." [0:01]
- Strategy era: **2023.**

## 2023-06-04 — 4 Trading Rules NOBODY Has Told You Yet! (gvFSTN7JK_c)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/gvFSTN7JK_c

- **Offset your take profit**: "whatever your take profit is, move it up and move it down [? '2 to 5 [pips]' — garbled '259'] for buying or selling. Everybody has the same take profit, why would the market reward every single person?" [0:02]
- **Market execute early**: "I market execute when I think the market is not ready to move yet … I don't want to miss the trade, so I can afford to be in drawdown." [0:02]
- **Heikin Ashi candles as confirmation**: "I use **Heikin Ashi** candlesticks as confirmation … they show you what the trend is and it helps you to stay in the trade." You won't close because one candle goes the other way. [0:02]
- After placing the trade, close your phone so you don't cut it during a $20 drawdown. [0:02]
- Strategy era: **2023** (matches the Heikin Ashi and early market execution in the 2023 long video).

## 2021-09-23 — LIVE TRADING Forex $500 to $10,000 Challenge (QOaL1Lwmoj0)
https://www.youtube.com/shorts/QOaL1Lwmoj0

- **Trade example: GBPUSD ("GU") short in a $500 → $10,000 small-account challenge** (the account had reached about $3,000 within a few days).
  - In floating profit "down 50 or 60 pips." [0:02]
  - Partial: "I'm probably going to close a little bit more than half, maybe **1.4, 1.5** [lots]" [0:02]. This locked in about **$800** ("at least 800 is guaranteed"). About **0.50 lot** was left running because "GU has a little bit more of a push to go down."
  - Stop management: "put our **stop loss into take profit** [i.e. into profit] so no matter what it is officially a **risk-free trade**." [0:02]
- Rule: "if you're up a couple thousand dollars or a few hundred dollars … there's no reason why you should go back to break even or negative." [0:02]
- Lot sizes are aggressive for the account size (about 2 lots on about $3K).
- Strategy era: **2021** (intraday, aggressive small-account challenge, partials of more than half + stop into profit). This is before the move to fewer, longer trades.

# What the Shorts add

Compared with keyword searches of the 113 long-video transcripts in `sc/txt/`:

**Not found in the long videos (as far as searched):**
- **14 trades per quarter** as a frequency target for swing/position trading (mLT7cdcn5Tg, 2026-08-10). No long video says "14 trades."
- **Win/loss streak stats**: 31/31 wins in January 2024 and 6 losses in a row in March 2024, with the advice to judge results by quarter (W_cn01-q2BY).
- **Withdrawal rule**: withdraw about 10% at +50% (10K → 15K, take 1.5K) and keep the balance low instead of compounding (cYn8GKyO48w). Long videos mention withdrawing but not this rule.
- **Take-profit offset**: move your TP a few pips away from the obvious level, because "everybody has the same take profit" (gvFSTN7JK_c). No long-video equivalent was found.
- **4 months on / 2 months off** trading schedule, with December treated as a non-trading month (u1vraMaMgdc). This is only briefly hinted at in long videos.
- **Partial at 60–70 pips + TP1–TP4 ladder**, never partials at 10–30 pips (OmMedf1LWLA). Long videos say "take 60–75% at TP1" but not the 60–70-pip threshold.
- **Emotion journaling** (GMNJlEl-wok): log how you felt before each trade and find patterns over 3–6 months.
- **Trade recaps**: USDJPY "crash" +$30K day (UeDTR8M9DwE), gold long from about 1924/1943 to 1947 for +$40K (MyK2U3B7zcI) and GBPUSD trendline-break short for +$23K (FuAl1RyUEkI). These exist only as Shorts. They show market execution before the move, accepting 25+ pips of drawdown, adding with a buy limit, and **closing early when about 30 pips from TP**.
- **Heikin Ashi**, the **6:30–9:30 pm entry window** and **taking 60–75% off at TP1** do appear in the long videos (MEgXUxqSPQ4 2023; koKRQ_pPhjQ). The Shorts confirm them but add nothing new.
- **Broker set-up** (24K Markets on TradeLocker, FundedNext, forex.com) is also in the long video Q6WiIOUcR8k (2024-11-23).

**About his latest approach (2025–2026):**
- **2026-10-06 (etjvn5pccyM)** is the clearest statement: "I'm a position trader. I have a pretty big stop-loss and a pretty big take-profit." Stop and target are set before entry and never touched. A group trade sat flat for 2 weeks and in drawdown for 3 weeks, then hit its target after about 10 weeks. He says he does "more with less" than with the day trading he did 6–7 years ago.
- **2026-08-10**: swing/position, about 14 trades a quarter; "not every day is a trading day."
- **2025-09-23**: gold is the main instrument. Position-trade it toward Goldman Sachs' 5,000 forecast (gold was about 3,700 then). "All you need to focus on is gold."
- **Evolution seen in the Shorts:**
  - **2021**: intraday, big lots, partials at 50–60 pips.
  - **2023**: stops of 20–50 pips at 1:3, Asian-session entries from 6:30–9:30 pm, take profit in New York, hold 1–3 days, Heikin Ashi + moving averages, take 60–75% at TP1.
  - **2024**: swing trades held 1–2 weeks with 40–50 pip stops, 1–3 trades per two weeks.
  - **2025–26**: position trades held for weeks, wide stops and targets set and forgotten, gold-focused.
- **Re-uploads to note**: in 2025 he re-posted the 2023–2024 stop-loss, session and swing clips (61Kzz2vU94A, cyv8O-VMw48, zOjyAQjM8UY, DVqFGEt2q0I, 3FuiL5n84co). The 20–50 pip stop figures in them are **not** current position-trading numbers.
